# BUILD PLAN — #76 + #65 approval lifecycle, phases A–D

- **Revision r5.8** (2026-10-03; (r5.8, critique of the fold) a two-critic pass on the r5.8 fold folded in place, §12.13.1).
- **Date:** 2026-10-03.
- **Repo:** this repository.
- **Grounded at:** `fb515f9` (r2–r4.1) and `53c2ed6` (r5). `53c2ed6` is `fb515f9` plus WORKLOG-only commits, so the code is identical. (r5.2) r5.2's cites were re-read at `4314626`; `git diff 53c2ed6 4314626 -- internal pkg src/signer src/mcp` is empty, so they hold at `53c2ed6` too. (r5.3) r5.3's new cites were read at `4314626`. `git diff 4314626 366ba07` (#22 phase 1, now on the branch) touches nothing in `internal`, `pkg`, `src/signer` or `src/gate/verify.go`, but it moves `src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate/main.go` (the `gate.VerifySigned` call is at `:216` and its exit-65 return at `:225`; `:213` and `:222` at `4314626`) and edits `src/mcp/server.go` and `src/mcp/tools/{denial,denial_shape_test,run}.go`, which A0 re-grounds (A13b's Denial count is corrected below). (r5.4) r5.4's cites were read at `366ba07`. (r5.5) So were r5.5's. (r5.6) So were r5.6's. (r5.7) So were r5.7's. (r5.8) So were r5.8's.
- **Design:** `local-workspace/release-2026-07-04/SPEC-76-65-approval-lifecycle-2026-07-19.md` (v5). The owner ratified it on 2026-10-02 with K1–K20 unchanged.
- **State model:** `local-workspace/release-2026-10-02/STATE-MODEL-76-65.md` (how r4.1's state actually worked, and why three reviews kept finding defects in it).
- **Constraint source:** the Unit-3 design v21 (`…/UNIT3-HOSTED-POLICY-DESIGN-2026-08-16-v21.md`).
- **Out of scope:** #22 and design Phase E. This plan neither analyses nor plans them.

**Status:** this is the binding build plan. Where they conflict, **§1.5 (the state architecture) overrides everything else in this plan**, §1.1 errata override the spec text, and nothing changes a K-ruling. **The build implements the ratified K-ruling behaviour everywhere.** §1.2 lists optional owner amendments, the only items that would change a ruling. None of them is built, flagged or defaulted on; each lands only on the owner's explicit word, as its own named commit with its own tests. (r5.2) OQ3, also in §1.2, decides frozen v1 behaviour rather than amending a K-ruling. The owner ruled it (b), so it is built, as its own named commit in A7.

**Revision r5 (2026-10-03): redesign of the durable state, not another patch round.** Three independent reviews of r2, r3.1 and r4.1 each returned NO-GO (11, 8 and 7 findings). Nearly all were about durable-state recovery, crash cutpoints, idempotency, ownership and retention across r4.1's 13 durable record types, and five seams drew a finding in every round. Under the owner's rule (after a third fix round on one subsystem, stop, write down how it actually works, and redesign), `STATE-MODEL-76-65.md` wrote the system down and three state architectures were proposed and judged. r5 restructures the plan around the winner, **"write-once facts, derived state"**, with grafts from the other two (§10.2):
- **§1.5 is replaced.** r4.1's durable state contract (SC-0 to SC-7, about 330 lines) gives way to one state architecture of about 175 lines: five rules, three record types, pure views, one commit function and one reconciler per store, one audit rule, one recovery rule and one retention table.
- **Errata rewritten to agree with it:** E4 (c), E7, E8 (b), E9, E18, E19, E21, E23, E24, E25, E29, E41, E48, E51, E52, E54, E59, E61, E63, E67, E68, E72, E73, E74 and E75; small edits to E26, E27, E28, E31, E34, E38, E49, E50, E55, E62, E64 and E71. **E66 is deleted.** §1.3 item 20 is withdrawn and items 21–26 are added.
- **Sub-phases re-derived:** A1 splits into A1a (contract types) and A1b (the pure state core plus its property and model harness); A6a and A6b merge into one engine, A6; A8 becomes the notifier reconciler; A9's migration 7 is `approvals_v2` plus two columns on `requests`, with no vote, challenge or snapshot tables; A11 uses MAC challenges; A12 shrinks to a stateless forwarder; A13a adds `registry.ReadCurrent`; new B0 adds two `securestate` methods; B1 builds the per-record store; B2 is "Startup = Next"; C1 and C2 build the job record and `JobNext`; D1a and D2 keep snapshots in memory.
- **§10 maps every finding of all three reviews, and the six items STATE-MODEL found, to where r5 resolves it.** §11 lists what critique pass 1 must check against the code.
- No K-ruling changes, and no owner amendment becomes a default. The base is `53c2ed6` and A0 has not started, so nothing has to be unwound.

**Revision r5.1 (2026-10-03): critique pass 1 folded in place.** Two critiques checked r5 against the code (both GO-WITH-FIXES; 3 blockers and 11 majors between them, several shared). Every item was re-checked against the code at `53c2ed6` and the spec; §12 records each verdict. The architecture of §1.5 is unchanged (three records, write-once slots, pure views, one commit function and one reconciler per owner). The fixes are rules inside it, each marked "(r5.1)":
- `JobNext` is single-flight per job and treats a current-epoch marker without a result as in flight, so a tick or waiter nudge can never dispatch a step twice (SA-5).
- Grant liveness reads a per-(principal, alias) newest-grant mark that Shed and Delete never lower, so pruning can never revive a superseded grant; v2 grants cover only their own principal, and v1 `sign` never spends a v2 grant (SA-6.4, E9, E24).
- Stamps are the wall clock; a memory-only high-water mark only guards and refuses, so a clock error can never put a signature timestamp in the future (SA-1 rule 3).
- Operator surfaces address v2 records by a derived record handle, never by a bare `request_id` (SA-2, SA-6.6).
- Record bounds are on the encoded bytes, and executor bodies are `[]byte` (SA-3).
- A live Telegram send failure is `error` as K19 says; `uncertain` comes only from a crash (SA-4, E50).
- Every duplicate vote is audited (K15); the assist view's synthesized `unavailable` snapshot hashes the same as the one Clock writes (K8); Claim no longer races the outbox flush on the synchronous path.

**Revision r5.2 (2026-10-03): independent review 4 and the OQ3 ruling folded in place.** independent review 4 of r5.1 (review note 4-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)) returned NO-GO with 9 findings (3 P1). Each was checked against this plan and the code at `4314626`. All 9 hold, and §12.5 records each one. The §1.5 architecture is unchanged: still three durable record types, write-once slots, pure views, and one commit function and one reconciler per owner. No record type is added. The fixes are rules inside that architecture, each marked "(r5.2)":
- **Clock break (P1).** A wall-clock regression of more than 5 min no longer resets the mark silently. The owner first commits, at `t = mark`, every expiry fact the views already showed. Clock also gains one materialization: mint `{error: window_closed}` for an approved record nobody claimed. The local signer then restarts its lifecycle under a new epoch, so every grant dies. Startup applies the same floor, per record, from the stamps it loads (§12.7). An expired grant, an expired request or a closed claim window can never come back (SA-1 rule 3, SA-4 Clock and Claim, SA-6.2, SA-8).
- **Caller-fixed submit window (P1).** The MCP sets `submit_not_after` in the `exec_submit` input, inside `input_hash`. The executor carries it unchanged and refuses a new id after it. A replay of an old `exec_submit`, after both stores have pruned the original, is therefore refused (SA-2, SA-3 R2, SA-5, E73, C1–C3).
- **Store ownership for the store's whole life (P1).** The local signer and the executor each hold an exclusive, non-blocking flock on their store directory's own descriptor before they load it, and keep it until exit, across reopens. Two processes with different sockets can no longer load independent caches of one directory (SA-1 rule 1, SA-8, B1, C1).
- **Edits after a crash (P2).** An unconfirmed edit entry from an earlier epoch is retired, and a fresh edit entry is appended in the same commit, because only the challenge's hash is stored. Within one epoch the actor re-runs the edit from memory (SA-4 step 3, SA-8, E48, B2).
- **R2 bound from the whole encoded record (P2).** The non-body allowance is measured by A1a from the worst escaped draft (about 144 KiB of `<` alone). It is no longer a fixed 64 KiB. Admission reserves each job's own encoded header plus its encoded output worst case (SA-3 R2, SA-9, E56, E75, A1a, C1, C2).
- **One deletion predicate per job file (P2).** A job file is deleted only when the job is final, its bodies are gone (or it never had any), its report is settled or was never owed, and `submit_not_after + 24 h` has passed. Then the 30-day age limit or count pressure applies. A definitive report refusal settles the report as `refused{code}` (SA-3 R2, SA-5 step 7, SA-9, E75, C2).
- **Unsigned steps need no signature window (P2).** `JobNext` Claims only before a signed step, and before a job's first dispatch. K16 applies only to signed steps. So an unsigned read after the last signed window still runs, as `run_batch` runs it today (SA-5 step 6, E74, C2).
- **Advisory binding in the header (P2).** The decision slot records the assist state, revision and digest the View showed when it committed. The header keeps the redacted reason, so a shed hosted row still holds spec §8.3's audit fields (SA-3, SA-4 Vote, E23, A10, A11).
- **DAG (P2).** Two edges are added: `A1b → A9` and `A1b → A13a` (§5, A9, A13a).
- **OQ3 ruled (b) by the owner and built.** v1 `sign` no longer auto-signs under any grant: a grant-covered v1 request prompts, and the v1 response bytes are unchanged. This closes the pre-existing cross-host grant gap. (a) is no longer the build default (§1.2 OQ3, E8b, E9, E24, SA-6.4, §1.3 items 27 and 33, §1.4 item 4, A7, D3, C-36).
- **(r5.2) Fold critique.** Two critics checked this fold (both GO-WITH-FIXES; 5 majors and 10 minors, three shared). All hold and are fixed in place; §12.7 records each. The clock mark now survives an in-process reopen, the Startup floor is per record, and the executor keeps a K16 floor across a clock break; A7 also owns the one `src/signer` test that hung under OQ3 (b); the submit window has a 5 s local allowance; route snapshots are bounded; both local stores require a local filesystem. §12.5's stop-rule argument is withdrawn, and the §4.4 ruling goes to the owner before the re-review (§12.6).

**r5.2 addendum (2026-10-03): the scoped stop rule was applied, and the time model needs a redesign. This is not r5.3.** Two independent re-scores scored STATE-MODEL §5 against r5.2, and every gap they claimed was checked against this text and the code at `4314626`. The record architecture holds. SA-1 rule 3's time model does not: this is the fourth consecutive round of findings on it, with eight defects verified in this round. So r5.2 is **not patched**. `STATE-REDESIGN-NEEDED.md` records the flaw, the redesign's scope (time only) and the constraints it must meet, plus the non-time gaps to fold with it (OA-5, OD-3) and the owner items. **Appendix L** writes down how the clock mark, the executor K16 floor, the store-ownership lock and `submit_not_after` actually live under r5.2. §1.5 stays binding until the redesign lands.

**Revision r5.3 (2026-10-03): the time model is redesigned, and the memo's non-time gaps are folded.** `STATE-REDESIGN-NEEDED.md` found SA-1 rule 3's time model structurally flawed (defects D1–D8) while the record architecture held. Three time-model proposals were scored against its constraints C-a to C-g and judged. The winner keeps time at the edges ("edge-anchored", Proposal 3), with grafts from the other two (§12.8). The record architecture is unchanged: no record type, slot, writer, durable object or wire field is added, and the change mostly deletes r5.1 and r5.2 clock code. Each change is marked "(r5.3)":
- **Views derive no closure from time.** Expiry, assist `unavailable`, a closed claim window and erased bytes are write-once slots that only Clock writes. Every R1 command except Delete applies Clock first, in its own commit, at its own wall-clock `now`, and the tick applies it each second. A time-based refusal is therefore a committed fact before its answer leaves (SA-1 rule 3.3, SA-4, SA-6).
- **Held authority runs on two clocks.** A v2 grant, a claimed signature set and the sync wait budget each end at the first of their wall-clock end and an elapsed end measured from acquisition on a new `internal/elapsed` clock (`CLOCK_BOOTTIME` on Linux), and the first finding is latched (SA-1 rule 3.4). The MCP synchronous coordinator is now a holder (D5). K16 is judged only for the step about to be dispatched (D3).
- **Withdrawn:** the high-water mark, `t`, `clock_regressed`, the 5 min band, the clock break, the carried mark, the per-record Startup floor and the executor's K16 floor (D1–D7). A reopen carries only the ownership descriptor.
- **Residuals are stated once,** at the end of SA-1 rule 3 (R-1 to R-5). A step back stretches deadlines nobody has judged yet; a forward step ends them early; a deleted id can be replayed as a new request after a step back of more than a day (D8, R-2, an owner item).
- **A gate future bound is proposed** (§1.4 item 5), and **a v1 grant suspend bug was found** in shipped signers (§1.4 item 6). Both need the owner's word.
- **Non-time gaps folded:** OA-5 (a local challenge's card-entry revision must equal the View's, SA-6.1); OD-3 (one definitive/transient split for every signer answer `JobNext` consumes, SA-5); OR-5 (the executor reservation lifecycle, SA-9, E75); and the first-window note (`claim_end`, SA-6.2, SA-6.5). OI-4: the owner accepted the residual (§11.2).
- **Appendix L** describes r5.3's lifecycles, STATE-MODEL §5 gains OC-1, and §12.8 records every disposition with its acceptance test.
- **(r5.3) Critique pass 1 of r5.3, folded in place.** Two critics checked r5.3 against the code (both GO-WITH-FIXES; 9 majors and 17 minors between them, one shared). All hold and are fixed in place; §12.8.1 records each. None changes the time model's shape: no record type, slot, writer or durable object is added, and no clock-held state returns. The fixes: windows exist only for signed steps and end at whole seconds; a held pair is acquired once per step per process; `next_due_at` covers the Erase and the retention ages; hosted migration 7 gains a `mint_state` slot column so the `live` count stays slot-derived; E64's command gate drops redelivered commands by `update_id`, not by the signer's start time; the clock-based refusals are listed once (3.1); `submit_window_ahead` is transient; Report uses SA-5's one split; and #22's read-jail exit 77 is `failed_not_started`. The proposals and the judge are filed at `TIME-MODEL-JUDGE.md`.

**Revision r5.4 (2026-10-03): independent review 5 folded in place.** independent review 5 of r5.3 (review note 5-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)) returned NO-GO with 7 findings (1 P1). Each was checked against this plan and the code at `366ba07`, and all 7 hold; §12.9 records each with its acceptance test. The §1.5 architecture and r5.3's time model (SA-1 rule 3) are unchanged: no record type, writer or durable object is added. The fixes are rules inside that architecture, adding one monotone counter and one write-once field to R1 and one write-once slot to R2, each marked "(r5.4)":
- **Effective vote policy (P1).** A v2 Vote decides with `Decide(EffectivePolicy(rec, flags), votes)`. The threshold comes from the record's frozen `required_approvals` and the requester from the record key's authenticated identity, as `approval_engine.go:197-204` does for v1 today. Only the deny-veto and self-approval flags come from configuration, and `sub_principal` never enters the requester (SA-3, SA-4 Vote, A1b, A10, C-49).
- **Re-notify is a durable authorization.** R1 gains the monotone counter `renotify_gen`, and each burst records the `gen` it was sent under. Renotify raises the counter in its own commit, and notify step 1 sends exactly when no burst carries the current `gen`. So a crash after a confirmed `/renotify` resends once, and a crash after that burst's append does not (SA-3, SA-4 steps 1 and 5, SA-6.3, SA-8, B2, C-50).
- **Executor completion time.** R2 gains the write-once slot `final_at`, the wall clock of the commit that first makes the job final. Body removal and the 30-day age count from it (SA-1 rule 3.1, SA-3 R2, SA-9, E75, C2, C-51).
- **K16 inside `RunPhased`.** `RunPhased` takes a pre-start guard that it calls after key load, dial, handshake and session setup, immediately before `Session.Start`. The runner's guard is the K16 re-check, so a slow handshake can no longer spend a window that an outer check passed (E10, E28, SA-1 rule 3.4, SA-5 step 6, E74, A13a, C2, C-52).
- **A delivered card has an outcome.** Notify step 2 commits `msg_id` and `outcome = sent` together. Only the burst and the send entries (`first`, `replacement`) feed `sending` and `uncertain`; edit entries never do (SA-4 step 2, SA-6.3, SA-8, E50, C-53).
- **Every non-resolving legacy tap is audited (K15).** A tap on a local legacy v1 card that does not resolve the request (a duplicate, an opposite replay, a tap after the deadline, or a tap on an unknown handle) writes one fail-open audit line. The tap's answer, the v1 response and the first-of semantics are unchanged (E8b, E23, E61, SA-10, A6, A7, A8, C-54).
- **Replay properties are per command.** SA-11's "every command is idempotent" is replaced by one replay row per command. A repeat Claim returns byte-identical signatures, never re-mints and adds 1 to `claim_count`; exact record idempotence is kept only where intended (SA-11, A1b, C-32).
- **(r5.4) Critique of the fold (§12.9.1).** Two critics found 16 issues (4 majors), all fixed in place: refused local v2 requests are audited again as `respondError` audits them today (SA-7, C-55); hosted stale-challenge POSTs append `vote_refused` (E7 step 5, C-56); the hosted worker refreshes stale cache columns (SA-1 rule 3.3, C-57); `RunPhased` refuses a signed command without its K16 guard, and every signed coordinator, both transfer legs included, passes one (E28, C-52); and the notify, view, replay, retention-age, `JobView` and legacy-cap rules are tightened.

**Revision r5.5 (2026-10-03): independent review 6 folded in place.** independent review 6 of r5.4 (review note 6-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)) returned NO-GO with 5 findings (4 P2, 1 P3). Each was checked against this plan and the code at `366ba07`, and all 5 hold; §12.10 records each with its acceptance test. The §1.5 architecture and r5.3's time model (SA-1 rule 3) are unchanged: no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object, wire field or clock-held state is added. The fixes are rules inside that architecture, plus one pure function and one pure predicate in `approvalplan`, one hosted partial index and one store primitive, each marked "(r5.5)":
- **Outbox retries have their own schedule.** `next_due_at` stays time-driven only, and an unflushed outbox is not on it, so a post-commit Flush that failed once waited for the record's next deadline, often `claim_end`. Now each owner keeps an unflushed list (locally an in-memory projection; on hosted the existing `audited_complete` slot column, with a new partial index and an `UnflushedV2` primitive), and the flusher (local) and the worker (hosted) retry every listed record each second, independently of `DueV2`, nearest `next_due_at` first, stopping a pass at its first sink failure. So a recovered sink drains with no further command, and flush retries never starve Clock work (SA-7, SA-1 rule 3.3, SA-8, A1b, A6, A9, A10, C-58).
- **Local payload admission reserves each record's whole-life worst case.** The 32 MiB budget was checked only at Submit, while an admitted record still gains its assist, card entries, bursts, status edits, signatures and outbox events and cannot be shed while live. Each full-payload record now counts at `approvalplan.R1Reservation(rec)`, a pure function of its frozen header and immutable payload (plan and ReviewSnapshot) that bounds every state the record can reach; Submit admits only within the budget, and only Shed or Delete releases a reservation. So no admitted record's transition can exceed the budget or be refused for it (SA-3, SA-9, E54, E75, A1a, A1b, A6, B1, C-59).
- **An approved synchronous request whose Claim window closes is `expired_unexecuted`, not `approval_timeout`.** SA-4 answers Claim `expired` at `claim_end` ahead of the flush gate, so with a failing audit sink a timely approval with TTL 60 ends about 60 s after the decision, not at the 300 s budget. The coordinator tells the two `expired` meanings apart by the decision it already saw (write-once): `expired` from Submit, Wait or Get is the pending request's expiry (`approval_timeout`, as before); `expired` from a Claim after `approved` is the window's close, which ends the run `expired_unexecuted` with a negative report and the non-retryable `expired_unexecuted` Denial. The budget-end `approval_pending` wording stays for a late approval whose window outlasts the budget (SA-4 Claim, SA-5, E25, E26, A13b, Appendix L.2, §1.3 item 35, C-60).
- **The executor's leak test separates signature material from the journalled draft.** R2's header keeps the draft and its exact commands for recovery, so C2 no longer forbids command text in the job file. It forbids signature material in every executor artifact and command text in every diagnostic field, and checks that the stored draft stays byte-exact (C2, C-27).
- **The registry compatibility test matches today's `Get`.** A malformed, unreadable or insecure file keeps the last-known entry; an absent file empties the registry (`servers.go:98-107`); a valid changed file is reloaded (`servers.go:255`). `ReadCurrent` refuses in all five cases (A13a, C-30).
- **(r5.5) Critique of the fold (§12.10.1).** Two critics returned GO-WITH-FIXES with 14 issues (1 major), 11 distinct after overlaps; all hold and are fixed in place, inside §1.5 and r5.3's time model: an in-epoch edit that ends in a possible send now has an in-memory retry driver, 1 s doubling to 30 s (SA-4 step 3, E48, SA-8, SA-11, A6, A8); every in-process coordinator, `revoke_server` included, maps a Claim `expired` after `approved` to `expired_unexecuted` (A13b, C-60); C-27 lets the MCP live log and the signer AuditLog keep command text, as they do by contract; a final executor job counts at `R2FinalCharge` (SA-9, E75, C2); SA-3 states the outbox bound `OutboxMax`; the drain property reads ⌈N / `FlushBatch`⌉ + 1 passes; hosted Flush runs in one flusher goroutine (SA-7, A10); legacy taps keep today's one string, "expired or already resolved", with the `sync.Once` loser's answer named as the one change (E8b, A6–A8); E26's quoted sentences are per-instance messages beside class-level Denial text; a shed record is bounded by `ShedMax` and a quarantined file counts in the budget (SA-3, SA-7, SA-9, A1a, B1); `ListV2`, `DueV2` and `UnflushedV2` are plain reads outside any transaction (A9, A10).

**Revision r5.6 (2026-10-03): independent review 7 folded in place.** independent review 7 of r5.5 (review note 7-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)) returned NO-GO with 3 findings (all P2). Each was checked against this plan and the code at `366ba07`, and all 3 hold; §12.11 records each with its acceptance test. The §1.5 architecture and r5.3's time model (SA-1 rule 3) are unchanged: no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object or clock-held state is added. The fixes are one wire error code, two constants and four pure functions in `approvalplan` (`VoteCap`, `VoteRoom`, `ExecutedOrdinals`, `Partial`), one report-acceptance check and one startup check, each marked "(r5.6)":
- **Hosted `busy` has a wire and a retry contract.** E21 promised a retryable 503 "busy" for `SQLITE_BUSY` at `BEGIN` (`pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/sqlite.go:192`, `_txlock=immediate` with a 5 s busy timeout), but A5's closed code set had no `busy`, SA-5 calls every unlisted code definitive, and A13b retried only `storage_uncertain` and `not_ready`. So a literal build either broke the wire or ended a recoverable job with `error{busy}`. `busy` is now a member of A5's closed set with a fixed envelope (HTTP 503), the client's typed `ErrBusy`, the A12 proxy passes it through, and it is transient in SA-5's one split and in A13b's synchronous rule. It means the command was not applied, so a retry of the same bytes is always safe (E21, SA-5, A5, A10, A12, A13b, C2, C-61).
- **The vote set can always hold the quorum.** SA-3 refused the 65th vote while the hosted threshold is unbounded above (`pkg/signerkit/hosted/config.go:113`; the roster check, `src/signer-server/cmd/sshgate-signer-server/main.go:845`, accepts any N the eligible operators cover), and votes that do not count (the requester's own with self-approval off, a deny with veto off) also fill the set. Now the vote bound is per record, `VoteCap(rec) = required_approvals + 64`; a vote that neither counts toward the quorum nor decides is accepted only while it leaves room for every approval still needed plus one, so a counting or deciding vote is never refused for room; and the threshold is bounded at `MaxRequiredApprovals = 256`, the policy plane's existing ceiling (`pkg/signerkit/policystore/config.go:32,72`): the signer-server refuses to start with `--required-approvals` above it, so no v2 record freezes a quorum its vote set cannot hold. The quorum is never lowered (SA-3, SA-4 Vote, A1a, A1b, A10, A11, §1.3 item 37, C-62).
- **`partial` is one pure function.** E29 defines `partial` as some step executed and some step did not, and counts `indeterminate` as executed, yet A13a expected `partial` for the whole two-step job `[failed read, indeterminate write]`, where no step is established as unexecuted. The fixture is corrected (`partial: false`), a three-step case with a later `not_run` step pins `partial: true`, and every step status now falls in exactly one of two sets. `approvalplan.ExecutedOrdinals(steps)` and `approvalplan.Partial(steps)` are the only definition. The runner, `JobView` in recovery, the executor's report body, the synchronous coordinator, the MCP output and the signer's report acceptance all use them, and a report whose `executed_ordinals` or `partial` disagrees with its steps is `invalid_report` (E29, E25, SA-4 Report, A1a, A1b, A13a, A13b, C2, C-63).
- **(r5.6) Critique of the fold (§12.11.1).** Two critics returned GO-WITH-FIXES with 14 issues, 8 distinct after overlaps. Seven hold and are fixed in place; one holds in part. The contention tests move to the sub-phases that can build them (A12 at client level; A13b, C2, A11 and D2 for the sync coordinator, the executor, the vote POST and cancel/deny-all); the human plane's `busy` is spelled out per transaction, including the TOTP step a busy vote may already have spent; the hosted assist commit is resent until its deadline; the sync Submit phase gets a retry bound and its own ending; the MCP read tools map `busy`; a report must list every ordinal in E29's shape, and `JobView` gives every unrun step of a final job a status; `decided_by` enters the R1 header bound; the freeze clamps N ≤ 0 to 1; and the 256 startup refusal is filed as owner item OI-5 with its built default. Still no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object or held deadline is added.

**Revision r5.7 (2026-10-03): independent review 8 folded in place.** independent review 8 of r5.6 (review note 8-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)) returned NO-GO with 4 findings (all P2). Each was checked against this plan and the code at `366ba07`, and all 4 hold; §12.12 records each with its acceptance test. The §1.5 architecture and r5.3's time model (SA-1 rule 3) are unchanged: no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object, held deadline or clock-held state is added. The fixes are one wire error-code member, one pure mapping in `jobplan`, one reworded Denial summary, one error-detection seam and corrected test placement and expectations, each marked "(r5.7)":
- **Stderr is never execution evidence on a signed route.** r5.3 made exit 77 with the gate's `read jail unavailable` line `failed_not_started` on `signed_reveal` too. But `run(reveal:true)` sends writes down that route, the gate classifies the inner command itself and runs a write unjailed with the reveal flag (`src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate/main.go:257,278`), and a command can print that line itself (`src/mcp/tools/run.go:313-316`). So a revealed write could run and be recorded as never executed. Now only `unsigned_read`, which the gate can never run as a write (`main.go:273-276`), maps the line to `failed_not_started`; on every signed route the step is `failed` and in `executed_ordinals`, and the line selects only the remediation (`read_jail_unavailable`, not retryable, its summary reworded to be true on both kinds of route). `jobplan.StepOutcome` is the one mapping, used by the sync coordinator and the executor (E10, E28, A13a, A13b, C2, §1.3 item 38, C-64).
- **`signer_incompatible` is a wire code.** A12 had to answer an older hosted server's non-envelope 404 as `signer_incompatible`, but A5's closed set had no such code and the client knew only the legacy `unsupported kind` and a direct HTTP 404. The code joins A5's closed set as a local-only frame with fixed bytes, the proxy sends it with one `refused{signer_incompatible}` audit row, and the client types it `ErrSignerIncompatible`. A golden and an older-hosted-server → current-proxy → MCP test pin it (E13, E69, A5, A12, A13b, C-65).
- **A10 no longer tests A11's routes.** A10's acceptance used the v2 vote POST, approve-POST-driven flushing and `/ui/audit`'s `v2_entries`, all built by A11, which depends on A10. A10's tests now vote through the engine's Vote entry point; the HTTP and UI forms move to A11 (A10, A11, C-58).
- **(r5.7) Critique of the fold (§12.12.1).** Two critics returned GO-WITH-FIXES with 14 issues, 10 distinct after overlaps; all 10 hold and are fixed in place. `run_batch` can never carry a reveal, so the batch half of the read-jail test uses a `signed_write` and the no-bulk-reveal test is ported, not dropped; C2's async case drives `exec_submit` directly, and the `await_approvals` form moves to C3 and C5; `StepOutcome` is keyed on the step kind (route plus admin verb or transfer leg) and on whether an earlier step executed, so `update_gate`, `revoke_server` and `transfer` keep today's exit texts and E10's retry advice is limited to `signed_write` and `signed_reveal`; a jail-refused read that ends a mixed batch is a named change (§1.3 item 39); §1.3 item 38 and AGENTS.md cover every signed `run`; the approvals plane's 404 is pinned as the `not_found` envelope; and four test expectations are corrected (the assist deadline plus one tick, Wait under the lock, the unsigned `run` read's `err == nil`, A11's labels). Still no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object, held deadline or clock-held state is added.
- **Two contention fixtures now expect what their setups produce.** A 6 s lock cannot release before an assist deadline of at most 5 s (E68), so that case now expects Clock's `unavailable`, and an injected early busy shows the in-time retry. A WAL reader is not blocked by a writer's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (`pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/sqlite.go:104,187,193-194`), so read-side `busy` is injected, and separate tests show reads still answer under the lock. E21's detection now matches any error with a SQLite `Code()`, because modernc's `*sqlite.Error` cannot be built in a test (E21, A10, A11, D2, C-61).

**Revision r5.8 (2026-10-03): independent review 9 folded in place.** independent review 9 of r5.7 (review note 9-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)) returned NO-GO with 3 findings (all P2). Each was checked against this plan and the code at `366ba07`, and all 3 hold; §12.13 records each with its acceptance test. The §1.5 architecture and r5.3's time model (SA-1 rule 3) are unchanged: no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object, wire field, held deadline or clock-held state is added. The fixes are one inert audit encoding, one memory-only stop latch per owner, and ownership and tests moved between sub-phases, each marked "(r5.8)":
- **Hosted v1 replays on resolved rows are audited (K15).** E7 step 3 answered a resolved v1 row 409 before `SubmitVote`, and the unchanged engine also returns before any audit on a resolved row (`pkg/signerkit/hosted/approval_engine.go:147-149`). So an N=1 approve followed by a second approve or a deny, and any POST after expiry or cancel, wrote nothing, and step 5's challenge refusals were audited only for v2. Now every authenticated v1 POST refused at step 3 or step 5, or by `SubmitVote`'s `ErrAlreadyResolved`, writes one inert line before today's unchanged answer: `vote_duplicate` when the operator holds a vote on the row, otherwise `vote_refused{reason}`. The line is an `AuditVerdict` with `Approval` metadata, which the sinks encode as kind `verdict_inert` (append-only) or status `approval-<transition>` (`AuditLog`), never as a resolution. It is fail-open, and the engine is untouched (E7, E23, E61, SA-10, SA-11, A6, A11, D2, C-66).
- **An owner's stop is not a notification failure.** SA-4 terminalized every live send failure and B2 required SIGTERM to leave records unchanged, but §1.5 overrides B2, and cancelling an actor mid-request produces exactly the transport failure SA-4 terminalizes. SA-8 now defines the owner stop (SIGTERM, and the local signer's uncertain-commit reopen). Intake stops first. Then a memory-only `stopping` latch, set under the store lock, makes the commit function refuse every later commit, and the effect context, which only `Close` cancels, is cancelled and the effects joined. Anything an effect observes after the latch is an interruption, never a live failure: it commits nothing and makes no further external call (no INCOMPLETE edit, no status edit, no retry). The interrupted send is then a crash cut that the existing rules recover: `uncertain` under the next epoch and resent only by `/renotify`, an edit superseded by a fresh one, a status edit repeated once, assist filled by Clock. The same rule covers the hosted assist job and the executor's `JobNext` (SA-4, SA-8, E18, E50, E63, A6, A8, A10, B1, B2, C2, Appendix L.0, C-67).
- **Every sub-phase passes on its own predecessors.** B1's uncertain-commit reopen ran "Startup (B2's code path)", and B1's tests needed it, while B2 depends on B1. So `startup.go`, the one Startup for process start and for the reopen, moves to B1, and B2 keeps shutdown, `/renotify` and the restart matrix. C1's tests asked its fake signer to see one Submit, and needed restart recovery and the bodies-dropped retry of a result commit, all built by C2; and C1 owned `exec_submit`'s idempotency while C2 owned its job commit. Now C1 owns `exec_submit` up to and including the job commit (window, admission, `ReadCurrent`, the `jobplan` draft, the commit and the answer). Its tests stay at the store and protocol boundary, with results committed through `approvalplan`'s pure transitions and no signer client, and the signer-facing assertions move to C2. §0.5 states the rule. A scan of every sub-phase's text for later sub-phases found no other case (§12.13) (§0.5, §2, A6, B1, B2, C1, C2, C-31).
- **(r5.8, critique of the fold) Two critics, 19 issues, 16 distinct after overlaps; all 16 hold and are fixed in place (§12.13.1).** The SIGTERM stop had no owner in code (`Service.Close` never touched the lifecycle; A7 now closes it first), Startup moves again, to A6, whose own gate already restarts the memory store, interruption is judged by the latch alone (a send budget built as a context deadline is still a live failure), an executor `exec_submit` whose job is committed answers with the job at a stop, hosted's stop cut and the assist pool's `closed` answer are defined, the inert-verdict predicate is `Approval.replay` and hosted v2 events go through `Call`, and B2, C2 and A11 test text now matches what the records hold.

**Earlier revisions (history; their state mechanisms are superseded by §1.5):** r2 folded two critiques (§7). r3 folded independent review 1 (§8). r3.1 was an independent check of r3 (§8.1). r4 folded independent review 2 and added r4.1's §1.5 (§9). r4.1 was an independent check of r4 (§9.1). Markers such as "(r3)" or "(r4)" inside text that r5 kept say where that text came from.

**How to read the cites:** every `file:line` was read at `fb515f9` or `53c2ed6`, which hold the same code. #22 lands first (WORKLOG 2026-10-03) and will move some lines. Builders grep by symbol and never trust a line number blindly. Cites of the form `PLAN:n` in the reviews and in STATE-MODEL point at r4.1's line numbers. r4.1's verbatim text is filed at `local-notes/archive/2026-10-03/BUILD-PLAN-76-65.r4.1.md` (the r5 lane left a copy for the orchestrator to file there).

## 0. Ground rules for every builder

**0.1 Base and branches.**
- The base is `main` after #22 merges. If the owner orders #76/#65 first, the base is `53c2ed6` (the same code as `fb515f9`).
- The integration branch is `feat/76-65-approval-lifecycle`.
- Each parallel lane gets its own worktree `../SSHGate-wt-<lane>` on branch `lane/<id>`, cut from the current integration tip.
- Only the orchestrator creates branches and worktrees, and only the orchestrator merges. It merges one lane at a time and re-runs §0.8 after each merge.

**0.2 Working tree.** At plan time `go.sum` carries an uncommitted 6-line addition of `/go.mod` hashes for six modules: `cloud.google.com/go/compute/metadata v0.3.0`, `github.com/fxamacker/webauthn v0.6.1`, `github.com/kr/pretty v0.1.0`, `golang.org/x/net v0.54.0`, `golang.org/x/text v0.37.0` and `gopkg.in/check.v1`. This plan did not make it. The orchestrator must find out who owns it (probably the #22 lane or a tool run) before A0. It must never ride inside a #76/#65 commit.

**0.3 Tests are not concurrency-safe.**
- Only one test-running process may run at a time across all lanes. Wrap every `go test`, `make test`, `make preflight` and `make test-integration` call in `flock local-workspace/release-2026-10-02/.test.lock <cmd>`. This plan calls that `$LOCK`.
- Integration and e2e own host port 2222, and only the orchestrator runs them.

**0.4 Never do any of these:**
- push, tag, merge to main or release;
- start, stop, restart or reinstall the operator's live `sshgate-signer-telegram` system unit, or install or enable any unit on the operator's box (design rule D11);
- run `make release-gate`, which writes `dist/gate`;
- hand-edit `dist/gate`;
- weaken a frozen test.

A golden or ratchet test may change only where §3 names the change as spec-mandated. The commit message must name both the test and the K-ruling or erratum behind it.

**0.5 Commits.**
- Each sub-phase is one commit or a few, and every commit is green.
- Commit trailers follow the contributor's own attribution policy.
- No session links. Check with `git show -s --format=%B HEAD`.
- Code-writing subagents get the coding-agent prompt pasted into their prompt.
- The orchestrator appends a publishable WORKLOG entry at the end of each phase.
- **(r5.8, review 9 #3) Each commit passes on its own predecessors.** A sub-phase's tests and acceptance use only code it owns and code its §5 predecessors have merged. An assertion that needs a later sub-phase's code belongs in that later sub-phase's tests. An implementation that an earlier sub-phase's acceptance needs moves into the earlier sub-phase, and its ownership and acceptance move with it.

**0.6 Gate closure.**
- No new package may appear in `go list -deps ./src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate`. The `verify-gate-closure` target is added in A1a.
- An edit inside the gate closure must prove the gate bytes are unchanged. The closure is `src/gate`, `src/redact` together with `src/redact/rules`, `src/redact/rules/gitleaks` and `src/redact/rules/sshgate`, plus `src/classify`, `src/sigwire`, `src/hostkey`, `src/xfer`, `src/xferwire` and `src/gatever` (checked with `go list -deps ./src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate`). The proof compares GATEHASH (below) against the baseline measured in A0.
- At `fb515f9` (and so at `53c2ed6`) the baseline is `f4b8b82bb8044af08bd9c2d128dc3977df0787d20c83afa8c27118beebf22cb5`. I verified it by a reproducible rebuild for this plan.
- The committed `dist/gate` (`146bf158…`) is already stale (§1.4). Compare against the rebuilt baseline, not against the committed binary.

**0.7 GATEHASH.** These are the same flags as `Makefile:21` and the `release-gate` recipe (`Makefile:86-119`). The output goes to the scratchpad, never to `dist/`.

```
TC=$(awk '/^toolchain/{print $2}' go.mod)
GOTOOLCHAIN=$TC CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 GOAMD64= GOEXPERIMENT= GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly \
  go build -trimpath -ldflags "-s -w -buildid= -X main.versionMarker=SSHGATE_GATE_VERSION{$(cat VERSION)}" \
  -buildvcs=false -o "$SCRATCH/gate" ./src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate && sha256sum "$SCRATCH/gate"
```

**0.8 Acceptance sets.**

STD runs on every sub-phase:

```
make vet
go vet -tags integration ./...                      # integration files are invisible to make vet/test (E58)
flock $LOCK make test                                # go test -race ./...
make build
make verify-no-sqlite-local verify-no-humanauth-local
make verify-gate-closure verify-mcp-closure verify-lifecycle-closure   # only once A1a has merged; A0, and A2 or B0 if they merge first, skip this line
GOOS=darwin GOARCH=arm64 go build -o /dev/null ./src/mcp/cmd/sshgate-mcp
GOOS=darwin GOARCH=arm64 go build -o /dev/null ./src/signer/cmd/sshgate-signer-telegram
```

Do not add the darwin build of `sshgate-signer-server` to STD: it is already broken (§1.4).

Frozen selectors, run explicitly so a reviewer can see the "must not touch" proof:

```
FROZEN-LOCAL : flock $LOCK go test -count=1 -run 'TestSocketGolden_|TestEnvelopeGolden_|TestSignerSeam_|TestOrdinaryAuditEventJSONRemainsByteFrozen|TestBaseManifest' ./pkg/signerkit/ \
               && flock $LOCK go test -count=1 -run 'TestDaemon_' ./src/signer/ && flock $LOCK go test -count=1 ./src/sigwire/...
FROZEN-HOSTED: flock $LOCK go test -count=1 -run 'TestWireFrozen_|TestUnit3WireRatchet|TestDirtyPolicyPaths|TestOrdinaryDirtyPath|TestPolicyMachine|TestMigrationLedger' \
               ./pkg/signerkit/hosted/ ./pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/
FROZEN-MCP   : flock $LOCK go test -count=1 -run 'TestServe_RegistersExactlyAgentTools|TestNewDenial_ShapePinned|TestSignTimeoutOutlastsSignerHandler' ./src/mcp/...
```

PHASE-END runs at the end of each phase:
1. STD and all three FROZEN selectors.
2. `flock $LOCK make preflight` (this includes `test-refapp-js`, gitleaks, `verify-dist`, `verify-versions` and `verify-repro`).
3. One independent read-only review, following the reviewer recipe. Fix every NO-GO and re-review.

## 1. Drift resolutions

### 1.1 Binding errata (spec corrections; none changes a K-ruling)

Each erratum names the spec section, the evidence in code, and the binding resolution. Duplicate drift findings are merged into one erratum each.

**E1 — Status, ordering and cites (header, §1, invariant 10, §9–§13 baseline).**
- The spec header now reads "v5 ratified 2026-10-02 (K1–K20 unchanged)".
- The roadmap cites are `docs/ROADMAP.md:178-185` (approval plane), `:471-494` (async lifecycle) and `:496-509` (LLM assist).
- #22 lands before this build. A0 re-greps every symbol this plan cites, and re-reads invariant 10 and the Phase-E preconditions against what #22 shipped, before A1a freezes `ApprovalPlanV1`.
- `exact_command` stays the plan-v1 identity. #22's argv identity arrives as `ApprovalPlanV2` in Phase E.
- §1 is otherwise unchanged.

**E2 — §2 citations.** The behaviour holds, but the locations have moved:

| Spec cite | Current location |
|---|---|
| `socket.go:139-167` | accept loop `pkg/signerkit/socket.go:142-160`, `serveOne` `:167-205` |
| `telegram.go:34-41,94-113` | struct `src/signer/backend/telegram.go:42-104`, pendingState `:115-121` |
| `telegram.go:520-597` | `Request` `:532-619` |
| `telegram.go:529-530` | explainer call `:541` |
| `telegram.go:747-768` | `resolve`/`timeout` `:762-795` |
| `telegram.go:858-882` | `runExplainer` `:870-899` (raw `c.Cmd` at `:889-894`) |
| `telegram.go:914-927` | `redactForDisplay` `:934-940` |
| `telegram.go:1031-1099` | `formatApprovalMessage` `:1043-1130` |
| `hosted/handlers.go:187-315` | `handleSign` `:120-254`, `handlePoll` `:262-316` |
| `approval_engine.go:140-280` | `SubmitVote` `:122-230`, `approve` `:257-281` |
| `run.go:31-43` | `RunInput` `:31-51` |
| `client.go:96-105` | `:99-106` |
| `client.go:148-279` | `Sign` `:156-280` |
| `backend.go:81-107` | `CommandReq` `:198-212` |
| `request_grant.go:86-96` | call at `:91` |
| `hosted.go:114-215` | `Request` `:129-222` |
| `daemon.go:561-578` | `Backend.Request` + wait `:608-625` |
| `store.go:45-68` | `Status` `:24-44`, `Request` `:52-68` |

One cite is **wrong**, not just moved: the free-form `context` map is `signRequest.Context` at `pkg/signerkit/hosted/handlers.go:34-41`, not `hosted/server.go:120-149`.

**E3 — `/v2` routing (K13, §10.1).**
- *Evidence:* with the policy plane attached, `Server.ServeHTTP` (`hosted/server.go:251`) sends every path that trims to `v2` or `v2/…` to the policy handler (`isPolicyV2Path` `:278-287`). That handler 404s everything except `/v2/policy/base-manifests[/pm_*]` (`policy_runtime.go:76-96`).
- *Resolution:*
  - Narrow `isPolicyV2Path` to `v2/policy` and `v2/policy/…`, on both the decoded and escaped forms, keeping the same `TrimLeft("/")` normalization.
  - `dirtyPolicyPlane` (`:262-276`) is already scoped to `/v2/policy` and stays unchanged.
  - Mount `/v2/approvals` through a new `approvalsMachine` handler routed in `ServeHTTP` exactly like `policyMachine`: its own strict path grammar, its own frozen JSON 404, `withAuth`, and its own limiter. It does not go through `http.ServeMux` patterns, which redirect non-canonical paths.
  - Bare `/v2`, `/v2/` and any non-policy, non-approvals `/v2/…` path get the approvals plane's 404. No test pins that path today.
  - (r5.7, critique of the fold) **The approvals plane's 404 is the `approvalwire` `not_found` envelope**, never the policy plane's `{"error":"not found"}` (`writeJSONError`, `hosted/handlers.go:372-373`; `policy_runtime.go:79-93`). Every 404 the `approvalsMachine` answers (a path its grammar does not match, an empty or malformed id, an unknown or foreign id, bare `/v2`, `/v2/` and any non-policy, non-approvals `/v2/…` path) is HTTP 404 with the body byte-equal to the `not_found` envelope an unknown id gets (A5 golden). So a v2-capable server's 404 is always an envelope, and E69's non-envelope rule can match only a server without migration 7.
  - Every case pinned by `policy_dirty_paths_phase_f_test.go:11-55` and `policy_routes_phase_d_test.go:43-118` must route identically. Those cases include `//v2/policy/…`, `/v2//policy/…` (handled by `dirtyPolicyPlane`) and `%62ase-manifests`.

**E4 — Hosted expiry and the legacy v1 TTL (K12, §6.1, §10.1, §11).** (c) rewritten in r5.
- *Evidence:*
  - There is no ordinary-plane worker. The only workers are the two policy workers, ROSTER and RECOVERY (`policy_runtime.go:139-161`, `Add(2)` at `:157`), and they start only with the policy plane.
  - `StatusTimeout` and `StatusError` are never written; the sole `UpdateStatus` caller is `approval_engine.go:303`.
  - Quotas count only `status='pending'` (`sqlitestore/sqlite.go:42-43`, `~286-299`), so abandoned rows hold quota forever.
- *Resolution:*
  - **(a)** Add a new ordinary-plane LIFECYCLE worker owned by `hosted.Server`. `StartOrdinaryWorkers` starts it in both policy and non-policy modes. It is cancelled and joined before the DB closes (v21 R69 ordering). `StartPolicyWorkers`, its `Add(2)` and its tests (`policy_engine_test.go:894,1343`; `policy_f15_effects_test.go:60-100`) are untouched. The documented roster becomes "two policy workers + one ordinary lifecycle worker". In r5 the worker is the hosted store's reconciler (§1.5 SA-1 rule 4, SA-8): it runs `Next` over due v2 rows and `ExpireLegacy` over v1 rows.
  - **(b)** Migration 7 adds `requests.request_expires_at` and `requests.terminal_reason` (NULL, `expired` or `canceled`). The expiry is frozen at intake as `created_at + --legacy-v1-pending-ttl` (default 24h). Existing pending rows are backfilled with `created_at + 86400`, so rows already older than that expire on the first sweep.
  - **(c) (r5)** `ExpireLegacy` is a pending-only CAS with its own SQL (`UPDATE requests SET status='error', terminal_reason='expired', resolved_at=? WHERE request_id=? AND status='pending' AND request_expires_at<=?`). It deliberately does **not** carry `UpdateStatus`'s unaudited-vote barrier (`sqlite.go:352-360`), so an interrupted v1 vote can never block expiry. The admin's `CancelLegacy` (D2) has the same shape and writes `status='denied'`, `terminal_reason='canceled'`. A `SubmitVote` that races either CAS loses its flip: its `UpdateStatus` affects no row, `persistedOutcome` reads the terminal row, and the signatures it minted before the flip are discarded (`approval_engine.go:257-281`). Its unaudited vote row stays as evidence. The worker never decides or mints a v1 row. **(r5.1)** Each `ExpireLegacy` and `CancelLegacy` CAS that changes a row writes one ordinary audit line (request ID, `terminal_reason`, the admin for a cancel, and the operators and decisions of any vote rows left as evidence), so the hosted audit stream never shows an audited approve vote with no terminal fact after it. r3–r4.1's worker audit completion for v1 votes, and the rule that expiry and cancel wait for it, are withdrawn (R1.3, R2.6 and R3.2 all sat on that seam).
  - The v1 `Status` vocabulary (`store.go:26-44`), the `/v1/poll` and `/v1/audit` bytes, and `storetest` all stay unchanged. The frozen client reads `denied` as a non-retryable denial and `error` as terminal (`pkg/signerkit/hosted.go:333-351`).
  - Nothing ever writes `timeout`. Only v2 resources spell `expired` and `canceled`.
  - The UI labels v1 rows "legacy v1" and shows `terminal_reason`.

**E5 — Scope of the K12 local-v1 sentence.** "Frozen local-socket v1 … cannot later become approved" holds for native local backends (`telegram.go:774-795`). With `[backend] type=hosted`, the local legacy wait is `HostedServerBackend`'s budget: 60s by default (signer `main.go:426-429`; `hosted.go:157-159`). An abandoned hosted row then stays pending until E4's legacy TTL sweeps it. The v2 proxy (A12) must never copy the v1 hosted `error`→timeout collapse (`hosted.go:333-339`).

**E6 — Roles and admin (K18, §9.3).**
- *Evidence:*
  - `users.role` is free TEXT (`sqlitestore/migrations.go:82-90`).
  - Only `operator` is ever created (`src/signer-server/cmd/sshgate-signer-server/main.go:649`).
  - `PolicyVoterRole="operator"` is digest-bound (`main.go:571`; `sqlitestore/policy_binding.go`).
  - `validateApprovalRoster` counts operators only (`main.go:831-853`).
  - The ordinary vote path has no role check (`approval_engine.go:122-230`).
- *Resolution:*
  - Migration 7 closes `users.role` to `{operator, admin}` with BEFORE INSERT and BEFORE UPDATE OF role triggers that raise `RAISE(ABORT,'sshgate: users.role must be operator or admin')`. The migration first counts any rows outside the set and fails with that same named error.
  - `admin` authority is cancel and snapshot deny-all only, which is force-deny.
  - An eligible voter, on ordinary v1 rows and v2 records alike, is `role='operator'`. Adding that check is a named behaviour change.
  - The policy voter predicate, ConfigDigest and roster validation stay byte-identical. Admins are therefore separate accounts, created by a new offline `--bootstrap-admin` that mirrors `--bootstrap-operator` (`main.go:105-106,632-706`).
  - `sqlitestore/operators_test.go:187` already stores `Role: "admin"` and stays green.
  - On local Telegram, the single `allowed_user_id` is both the N=1 approver and the admin.

**E7 — A challenge on every displayed decision (K15, §9.2, §9.3; rewritten in r5).**
- K15 applies to every decision a surface displays, including requests that originate from v1.
- **Binding.** A challenge is bound to `{plane, record key, plan_hash, operator, session or card, assist_revision, assist_digest, allowed_actions}`. The session is required by spec §5 ("operator/session-bound") and §9.3.
- **Telegram (local).** The record's notifier actor mints a random 64-bit challenge for each card entry and stores only its hash in that entry (§1.5 SA-3). The callback carries it (E47). Validity is derived, never stored as a state (§1.5 SA-6.1): the record's decision slot is empty after Clock (r5.3); the entry is the record's latest unretired card entry; (r5.3, OA-5) the entry's `rev` equals the assist revision of the record after Clock; its message ID is recorded and equals the callback's (an `edit` entry must also be confirmed); the hash matches; the action is allowed at that entry's revision; this operator has no vote. Rotation is a new entry (E48). Legacy v1 cards use the same callback form against the in-memory `LegacyRequest` (§1.5 SA-10).
- **Hosted (both planes): a stateless MAC challenge.**
  - `challenge = nonce(16 B) ‖ expires_at ‖ HMAC-SHA256(K, canonical{plane, record key, plan_hash or LegacyHostedPlanHash, operator, session_binding, assist_revision, assist_digest, allowed_actions, expires_at, nonce})`, compared in constant time.
  - K is 32 random bytes generated at process start and never persisted, so a server restart invalidates every open view (§1.3 item 15).
  - `session_binding = SHA-256("sshgate-vote-session-v1"‖session_id)`. The raw session ID is never stored or put in a challenge. `expires_at` never exceeds the session's own expiry. (r5.3) It is judged at the wall clock, so a step back stretches it (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3, R-1).
  - **Nothing is stored.** r3–r4.1's `vote_challenges` table, its partial unique index, its GC and the logout deletion are withdrawn. A logged-out or expired session fails `withSession` with 401 before any vote check, and a new session's binding differs, so its challenge fails the MAC (409 "view changed").
  - **Authentication context (A11).** `withSession` (`human_handlers.go:117-131`) today passes only `sess.UserID` (`:129`). It additionally stores `{session ID, session ExpiresAt}` in the request context under an unexported key (r5.1: capping `expires_at` at the session's expiry needs `ExpiresAt`, which `ValidateSession` already returns, `auth.go:547-559`). `next`'s signature does not change, so no other handler moves.
  - **(r5.1) Addressing.** v1 rows keep `GET /ui/request/{id}` and `POST /ui/request/{id}/approve|deny` with their `r_…` IDs. A v2 record is addressed only by its **record handle** (§1.5 SA-2): `GET /ui/v2/request/{handle}`, `POST /ui/v2/request/{handle}/approve|deny` (and D2's `/cancel`). A bare `request_id` is unique only per principal (E19), so it never names a v2 record on a human surface; an `a2_…` ID on a v1 route is 404, and a handle that matches more than one record is refused (409 "ambiguous handle").
  - The detail GET returns an additive `vote_challenge` object: `{challenge, plan_hash, assist_revision, assist_digest, allowed_actions, expires_at}`. The GET never writes (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3).
  - The approve and deny POSTs require `challenge`. `voteRequest` (`human_handlers.go:723-725`) gains the field, decoding stays strict, and the response bytes are unchanged. This ends the frozen "empty body is allowed" contract (`human_handlers.go:750-757`), as K15 requires, and it is a named change.
  - v1 rows bind `approvalplan.LegacyHostedPlanHash(request_id, client_id, commands blob, created_at, required_approvals)`, revision 0, an empty digest and actions `{approve, deny}` (legacy rows have no assist, E36).
  - **Order of checks in `handleVote`, both planes.** For v1 rows every check is at the HTTP layer; `approval_engine.go` and `approval_engine_test.go` are not touched, because that test file votes 24+ times as users it never creates.
    1. `withSession` fails → 401;
    2. unknown id → 404. For v2 (r5.1), the record handle resolves to exactly one record key and the record is loaded by that key (§1.5 SA-6.6's operator carve-out); no match is 404 and more than one is 409 "ambiguous handle";
    3. the row is resolved → 409 "request already resolved". An overdue v1 row (`now ≥ request_expires_at`) is first expired through `ExpireLegacy` (E4c), which always succeeds on a pending row. **(r5.3) For v2** this step runs Clock on the row in its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3.3) and answers 409 when the decision slot is then filled; a POST refused here appends its `vote_refused` noise event (E61) in that same transaction. It stays before the role, MAC and step-up checks, so a stale view still never spends a TOTP step. **(r5.8, review 9 #1) For v1**, a POST refused here by (r5.8, critique of the fold) an operator-role session (`Store.GetUser`, the read step 4 makes; another role's POST is not a vote, and gets today's 409 with no line, as it gets today's 403 on a pending row) writes one v1 inert line (step 7) before today's 409: `vote_duplicate` with the decision posted when `Store.ListVotes` lists a vote by this operator on the row (an N=1 approve followed by a second approve or a deny lands here, because the first vote resolved the row), otherwise `vote_refused{resolved | expired | canceled}` from the row's `status` and `terminal_reason` (E4c; `expired` also when this POST's own `ExpireLegacy` has just expired the row). r5.1–r5.7 audited v1 only at step 7, which a resolved row never reaches;
    4. the voter's `role` is not `operator` (via `Store.GetUser`) → 403;
    5. **(r5.1, moved before step-up)** `challenge` missing → 400; MAC invalid, expired, or bound to another session, revision, digest, plan or request → 409 "view changed". The MAC check is stateless and answers only valid or invalid for a view the session can fetch anyway, so it is no oracle; running it first means a stale view never consumes a TOTP step (`ConsumeTOTPStep`), which would otherwise make the operator wait up to 30 s to retry. **(r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-2) For v2** a refusal here is audited as E61 requires: the handler runs one noise-only commit in its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` on the record step 2 resolved (Clock first, as every command; SA-1 rule 3.3), appends `vote_refused{stale_challenge | expired_challenge | session_mismatch}` subject to the 32-event fold, commits, and then answers 409 "view changed". The MAC verification itself stays stateless and runs before the transaction; the comparison of the bound revision, digest, plan and actions with the row after Clock is judged inside it, so a stale-revision refusal and the Clock fact that made it stale are committed in that same commit (SA-11 time property 3). Step 5 still consumes no TOTP step. A challenge replayed by the operator who already voted passes this step and reaches step 7, where it is `vote_replay` (E61). **(r5.8, review 9 #1) For v1** a refusal here writes one v1 inert line (step 7) before today's 409 "view changed"; nothing is stored. (r5.8, critique of the fold) A v1 row binds only fixed values (`LegacyHostedPlanHash`, revision 0, an empty digest, `{approve, deny}`), so a MAC failure has no finer cause: another session's challenge, one from before a server restart (a new K) and a forged one look the same. The v1 reasons are therefore `vote_refused{expired_challenge}` when the MAC verifies and the challenge's `expires_at` has passed, and `vote_refused{stale_challenge}` for every MAC failure (E8b's spelling for a hash that does not match); `session_mismatch` is v2-only. A missing challenge (400) is not audited on either plane;
    6. step-up, unchanged from today (`handleVote`: `allowAuth` then `Auth.StepUp` when `RequireStepUp`, `human_handlers.go:763-773`) → 401 "step-up required";
    7. **v1:** the unchanged `SubmitVote`. A same-decision duplicate resumes the interrupted audit and finalization exactly as today (`sqlitestore/operators.go:354-392`); an opposite decision returns `ErrVoteConflict` → 409 "operator already voted" (E61). **(r5.1) HTTP-layer additions, engine untouched:** before `SubmitVote`, `handleVote` reads `Store.ListVotes` (audited votes only); if this operator already holds an audited vote, the POST is a duplicate and gets one ordinary audit line (`vote_duplicate`, with the decision posted) before the unchanged `SubmitVote` answer (200 for the same decision, 409 for the opposite); an `ErrVoteConflict` against a vote not yet audited (which `ListVotes` hides) gets that same single line. After `SubmitVote`, an outcome with `flipped=false` whose re-read row is no longer `pending` and does not hold the decision posted (an `ExpireLegacy` or `CancelLegacy` won the race, and `persistedOutcome` would answer `pending` for an `error` row, `approval_engine.go:234-256`) answers 409 "request already resolved" instead of 200 (with no extra line: `SubmitVote` audited that vote, and the expiry or cancel line names it, E4c). **(r5.8, review 9 #1)** A `SubmitVote` that returns `ErrAlreadyResolved` (the row resolved after step 3: `approval_engine.go:147-149`, or `PrepareVote`'s `ErrRequestResolved` at `:165-166`) gets step 3's line before today's 409 (`human_handlers.go:781-782`), (r5.8, critique of the fold) unless this POST already wrote its pre-`SubmitVote` `vote_duplicate` line: a POST writes at most one line (an operator whose approve is audited posts again under N=2 while another vote, `ExpireLegacy` or `CancelLegacy` resolves the row between the pre-check and `SubmitVote`'s `GetByID`: one `vote_duplicate`, not two). **v2:** the Vote command (§1.5 SA-4), one transaction.
    - **(r5.8, review 9 #1) The v1 inert line.** Each HTTP-layer line of steps 3, 5 and 7 is one `AuditVerdict` written through the hosted core's `Verdict`, the sink that `SubmitVote`'s own vote audit uses (`approval_engine.go:178`). `Operator` is the session's operator with this POST's authn method, `CommandSHA256` is the row's, `Approved` is the decision posted, and `Approval` is `{transition, decision, replay}`, with `transition` = `vote_duplicate` or `vote_refused{<reason>}` (spelled as E8b spells its legacy lines) and `replay` set. The sinks encode it as inert, never as a resolution (E23): kind `verdict_inert` on the append-only sink, and status `approval-<transition>` on `AuditLog`. So a reader that counts `verdict` lines, or `approved` and `denied` statuses, never counts a replay. The line is written before the answer and is fail-open: a sink error is logged and never changes the answer, whose bytes stay today's at every step. There is (r5.8, critique of the fold) at most one line per POST, written only for the refusals named in steps 3, 5 and 7 (a 401, the 400 for a missing challenge, a 403 and a step-up 401 write none, as today), only for an operator-role session, and no fold, because a v1 row has no outbox. Like SA-7's local refusal lines these are uncapped: each answers one authenticated operator's HTTP request. `approval_engine.go` and `approval_engine_test.go` stay untouched.
  - **(r5.6, critique of the fold) Hosted contention at each step, v2 records only (E21):** a busy `BEGIN` at step 3 or at step 5's refusal commit → 503 "busy — retry", before step-up, so no TOTP step is spent; a `SQLITE_BUSY` from step 6's `ConsumeTOTPStep` → 503 "busy — retry with a fresh code", never 401; a busy `BEGIN` at step 7 → 503, "busy — retry with a fresh code" when step 6 consumed a step, otherwise "busy — retry". v1 rows keep today's answers at every step.
  - r3–r4.1's step 3 (the `GetOperatorVote` pre-check) and step 6 (a CAS consuming a stored challenge) are withdrawn. A replayed challenge is a duplicate vote, which is inert and audited (E61), so a stored consumed flag adds nothing. K15's "consuming it appends at most one immutable vote for that eligible operator" holds through the one-vote-per-operator guard (§11 asks critique pass 1 to confirm this reading).
- **Named, spec-mandated test changes in A11:**
  - `unit3_wire_ratchet_test.go:100-110` sends the challenge on approve and deny.
  - `wire_frozen_test.go`'s `/ui` approve step (`:70`, nil body today) fetches the detail and sends the challenge.
  - Both still assert byte-identical responses.
  - In `human_handlers_test.go`, every vote POST sends a challenge fetched from the detail GET: `:273` (nil body), `:544`, `:575` (the second approve still expects 409; step 3 produces it, because the row is already resolved), `:596`, `:630`/`:645` (step-up; r5.1: they carry a valid challenge, because the challenge is now checked before step-up) and `:674`.
- **Telegram:**
  - Uses a new callback namespace (E47).
  - Legacy `approve:`/`deny:` data (`telegram.go:558-559,638-639,719-720`; parsed at `:817`) is emitted only by the direct `Backend` methods, which production never calls (E8e). Lifecycle and legacy cards never emit it.
- **Not copied:** the policy journal's clear-text challenge (`policy_journal_linux.go:129`).
- **Replays:** E61.

**E8 — Legacy v1 requests (K3, K12, K13, §9.1).** (b) rewritten in r5.
- **(a)** Frozen v1 kinds keep their wire bytes; the socketrpc goldens prove it.
- **(b) (r5)** When the daemon has a lifecycle with a Telegram notifier, v1 `sign`, `request_grant`, `transfer` and `register_xfer_key` approvals become **in-memory `LegacyRequest`s** (§1.5 SA-10). That is a separate small type, never a lifecycle record:
  - `rationale_origin=synthesized_legacy_v1` (`register_xfer_key` follows E39);
  - the card goes through the shared renderer with K3's legacy label and a `v:` challenge (E7), so production has one approval surface (E8e);
  - **(r5.1) assist, as today's explainer:** the `LegacyRequest` runs one assist attempt through `assist.Run`/`Pool` under the K7 gate (E15) **before** its card is sent, exactly where today's `Request` calls `runExplainer` before the send (`telegram.go:541`), bounded by the same 5 s watchdog (E36). Alignment is forced to `not_assessed` (K3, spec §9.1: "no rationale-alignment assessment"); summary, effects and concerns render as AI advisory. A full pool or the watchdog gives `unavailable` and the card is sent anyway. Because the card is sent only once assist is terminal, Approve is never withheld (K6's withholding is v2-only). The result lives only in the in-memory request, like everything else about it. r5's "no assist" would have dropped today's explainer summary from v1 cards, an unlisted regression;
  - resolution keeps today's semantics exactly: the first of {an allowed tap, the deadline} wins once (`sync.Once`, `src/signer/backend/telegram.go:595-606,774-795`), with deadline `min(connection ctx end, submitted_at + sigwire.ApprovalWindow)`. A tap after resolution is answered "expired or already resolved", today's one string for it ((r5.5, critique of the fold) `telegram.go:484-490`), and changes nothing. K12's "cannot later become approved" holds by construction;
  - **(r5.4) Every tap that does not resolve the request is audited (K15: "duplicate challenges/votes are inert and audited"; E61).** r5–r5.3 said such a tap "records nothing", and today's handler only answers it (`telegram.go:484-490`, "expired or already resolved"), so local v1 duplicates went unaudited although E7 applies K15 to v1 decisions. The rule:
    - after resolution, the legacy table keeps the request as a memory-only **tombstone** `{handle, chal_hash, request_id, resolution, resolved_by}`; at most 256 tombstones, oldest evicted first, all lost at restart like the request itself;
    - every authenticated `v:` tap that does not resolve a live `LegacyRequest` writes exactly one audit line: `legacy_vote_replay` with the decision posted (the request was already resolved by a tap, same decision or opposite, including the tap that loses the `sync.Once` race to another tap), or `legacy_vote_refused{reason}` with reason `expired` (the deadline or the caller's disconnect won), `stale_challenge` (the hash does not match), or `unknown_card` (the handle is in neither the legacy table, its tombstones, nor the v2 card-handle index, for example after a restart or an eviction; A8 uses the same line for a v2 handle whose record is gone);
    - the line is an `AuditEvent` carrying `Approval` metadata (`transition` and `replay`, E23), status `approval-<transition>`, written through the lifecycle's `AuditWriter` after the callback is answered and fail-open, exactly like today's v1 audit (`daemon.go:674-743`). It never feeds back: (r5.5, critique of the fold) every tap that does not resolve the request is answered "expired or already resolved", today's string (`telegram.go:484-490`), whatever the reason (the deadline, an earlier tap, a stale challenge or an unknown handle); r5.4 called "expired" and "already resolved" today's strings, which today's handler never sends. One answer changes deliberately: today the tap that loses the `sync.Once` race has already loaded the pending entry, so it is answered with its own verb and edits the card footer again (`telegram.go:508-523`), and an approved card can end reading "Denied by …"; now it is answered "expired or already resolved" and edits nothing, and the card's terminal edit comes from the resolution alone. The v1 response, its resolution and its timeout semantics are unchanged;
    - the lines are bounded like the v2 noise fold (SA-7): at most 32 per request, (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C2-6) counted over its whole life, live `LegacyRequest` and tombstone alike (a stale-challenge tap on a live request counts), and 32 per minute for `unknown_card`. A tap beyond a cap adds 1 to **one** process-wide legacy-dropped counter, which the next legacy line written for **any** request reports and resets; when the counter is non-zero and no legacy line has been written for 60 s, the tick writes one `legacy_audit_dropped{count}` line and resets it. r5.4's per-tombstone counter could never be reported once its tombstone had hit the cap. Like the tombstones, the counter is memory-only and a restart loses it;
  - the v1 response and its audit are today's: signed at response time (`signAll(now)`, E21), audited after the response, fail-open (`daemon.go:674-743`). That includes the tap;
  - nothing is journaled; a restart loses the request, as today;
  - **(r5.2, OQ3 (b), owner ruling) No grant matching for v1 `sign`.** v1 `sign` matches no grant at all. The short-circuit at `daemon.go:604-606` (`if id, ok := d.matchGrant(req.Commands); ok { return d.respond(… ApprovedBy: "grant:"+id) }`) is removed, so every v1 `sign` reaches `legacyApprove` and prompts. A v1 grant still in the frozen map never covers a v1 request. A v2 grant record never covers one either (E24). The v1 response bytes are unchanged: a prompted, approved `sign` gets today's human-approved response;
  - **Mechanism:** each of the four v1 call sites calls a new `d.legacyApprove(...)` helper in `daemon_approvals.go`, which picks the `LegacyRequest` path or `d.Backend`. The call sites are `Backend.Request` (`daemon.go:608`), `Backend.RequestGrant` (`:818`), `Backend.RequestTransfer` (`:1147`) and `Backend.RequestRegisterKey` (`:1333`). A `Backend` decorator is **not** allowed. It would hide the optional `BaseManifestApprovalBackend`/`HostedBaseManifestApprovalBackend` interfaces that the policy path type-asserts on `d.Backend` (`daemon_policy_linux.go:68,91,143,959`).
  - r4's SC-1.L deadline CAS, r4.1's reduced legacy entries and its audit-first legacy vote (r4.1 §1.3 item 20) are withdrawn. R2.6 is closed because a legacy tap is no longer a lifecycle vote at all.
- **(c)** The v1 per-command `reason` (`daemon.go:256-290`) is not the request rationale:
  - on reveal steps it renders as **"Reveal justification (agent-stated, unverified, legacy per-command field)"**;
  - on an `SSHGATE_UPDATE` step it renders as **"Build metadata (v1 client-supplied)"**;
  - both are redacted and neutralized. Today they render raw (`telegram.go:1080`, `:1017`).
- **(d)** Backends without the lifecycle capability keep the frozen `Backend.Request` path byte-for-byte: `StubBackend`, `MockBackend` and `HostedServerBackend`. **`MockBackend` must not gain the capability**, because the socket and envelope goldens depend on its unchanged path and nonce sequence.
- **(e)** `TelegramBackend`'s four `Backend` methods (`telegram.go:532-760`) remain as library and test surface only (their pinned format tests stay green). **Production never reaches them.** From A8 on, signer `main.go` always constructs the lifecycle with the Telegram notifier, and `[approvals]` has no switch that disables the lifecycle or routes v1 around it. **(r5.1) The one routing rule:** `legacyApprove` routes a v1 request through a `LegacyRequest` only when the daemon has a **local lifecycle with a `LegacyNotifier`**; otherwise it uses `d.Backend` byte-for-byte. "Otherwise" means library composition, the (d) backends, and `backend=hosted`, where A12's stateless proxy is the daemon's v2 implementation and there is no `LegacyNotifier`, so hosted-mode v1 `sign`, `request_grant`, `transfer` and `register_xfer_key` stay on `HostedServerBackend` exactly as today (A12 tests it). r5's "only when the daemon has no lifecycle" would have sent hosted-mode v1 into a `LegacyRequest` with no notifier. So every production Telegram approval, v1 or v2, uses the shared renderer, K3's legacy label for v1, a K15 challenge and `v:` callbacks, and no production card carries `approve:`/`deny:` data (`telegram.go:558-559`). r2's group-linked-chat fallback to the direct path is withdrawn (E64). The direct methods' explainer call still gets the K7 gate (E15). Between the A7 and A8 merges, production `main.go` builds no lifecycle, so v1 keeps today's direct path and v2 kinds answer `lifecycle_unavailable`. That interim state is today's production behaviour, except that (r5.2, OQ3 (b)) v1 grants no longer auto-sign, because A7's hunk 5 lands with A7; no binary is released from it (§0.4, §1.2 OQ3 "Timing").

**E9 — Grant-covered submissions and grants as records (invariants 1 and 5, §5–§7, §11; rewritten in r5).**
- *Evidence:* `matchGrant` auto-approves without calling the backend (`daemon.go:604-606,1456-1500`). The grant ID and expiry are minted at a memory install after approval (`daemon.go:851-868`), and revoke deletes the map entry by alias (`:900-904`). r4.1 made the grant *request* durable but left the capability in memory, with no rule for which side wins after a restart, a revoke or a supersession (R3.5, F-4).
- **A v2 grant is its R1 record (§1.5 SA-3).** A `standing_grant` request is an approval record with `execution_applicable=false`. Its grant ID is its `request_id`, and its expiry is `decided_at + duration_seconds`; Get and Wait return both. Liveness is the pure view of §1.5 SA-6.4: approved, decided in the current store epoch, not revoked, unexpired by either clock of SA-1 rule 3.4 (r5.3: the grant projection drops G at the first finding and never re-adds it in that epoch), and the newest approved grant for its `(principal, alias)` in this epoch (r5.1: read from the newest-grant mark, which Shed and Delete never lower, so pruning a superseder can never revive an older grant). The grant's alias, duration and host fingerprint are header fields (r5.1), so a shed grant record still answers Get with `grant_id` and `grant_expires_at`. Revoke writes the record's `revoked_at` slot. The grant projection used for matching is a rebuildable index of live records, so there is no separate install step that a crash could split from the approval. A grant dies with its epoch: a signer restart or an uncertain commit ends every v2 grant, which matches today's memory-only grants.
- **(r5.1) A v2 grant belongs to its principal.** It covers only v2 Submits from the same principal (the record-key owner, `local-uid:<uid>`), never another uid's and never a v1 request. The MCP, the executor and the agent shell share one uid (E17), so in-tree use is unchanged; another local uid in the `sshgatesigner` group, including one that `[approvals] allowed_uids` excludes, gets nothing from it. This follows spec §7.4 ("group membership permits a dial, not the right to claim another principal's request").
- **Grant-covered Submit.** A v2 `command_job` whose every signed step is covered by a live, host-bound (E24) grant G of the same principal is decided in its freeze commit:
  - decision `{approved, decided_at = submit time, decided_by = grant:<G>, epoch}`; the audit event names G's original approver, read from G's decision slot;
  - no vote, challenge or notification (notification view `not_applicable`), and assist `not_applicable`;
  - signatures are minted at the **first Claim** (E21), which re-checks that G is still live. If it is not (revoked, superseded, expired, or a new epoch), that Claim writes mint `{error: grant_not_live}` and answers `materialization_error`, so a revoke reaches grant-approved requests not yet claimed (§1.3 item 21);
  - the K3 reason is still required, because the MCP cannot know about coverage before submit and K3 covers "ordinary writes".
- Invariant 5 reads "terminal approval (a human tap, or a live human-approved standing grant)".
- Reveal, admin and transfer steps never match a grant (unchanged). The check is on command bytes and route `signed_reveal`, outermost, before any grant lookup (E71).
- v1 `request_grant` keeps the frozen memory map and its wire bytes unchanged. **(r5.2, OQ3 (b))** v1 `sign` consults no grant, so a v1 grant, once approved, auto-signs nothing. It stays listable and revocable. `revoke_grant` and `list_grants` cover both sources as E24 states.
- Hosted v2 has no grants.

**E10 — K16 on the batch path.**
- *Evidence:* a later expired signature returns gate exit 65, which maps to `bad_signature` with "Resubmit … ONCE" (`src/mcp/tools/denial.go:128-138`; AGENTS.md says "65 = … retry once"). That re-runs earlier writes.
- *Resolution:*
  - Before dispatching each signed step, sync or async, the runner requires `now + 2s < decided_at + step TTL`. **(r5.3)** This is judged on the held (wall, elapsed) pair of §1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4, acquired once per step in each process, at the first Claim whose answer carries step k's window, and never at a repeat Claim (r5.3, critique pass 1): `wall + 2 s < decided_at.Unix() + TTL_k` and `E + 2 s < elapsed_end`. It is judged only for the step about to be dispatched (the first step with no result, when it is signed), before the marker and again just before `Start`. (r5.4) The second check is `RunPhased`'s pre-start guard (E28): it runs after key load, dial, handshake and session setup, immediately before `Session.Start`, so a slow handshake cannot spend the window after an outer check passed. A guard failure sends no exec request.
  - If that fails, the step becomes `expired_unexecuted` and every later step becomes `not_run`. The Denial class is `expired_unexecuted` (`stop_do_not_retry`), and its message lists the executed ordinals.
  - A post-dispatch exit 65 or 77 on a signed step is `failed`, annotated "gate refused (signature)", with retry advice only when it was the job's first executed step. (r5.7) A 77 whose stderr carries the gate's read-jail line keeps the status `failed` but takes the annotation and remediation of the read-jail bullets below. (r5.7, critique of the fold) That annotation, its retry advice and the read-jail remediation apply only to `signed_write` and `signed_reveal` steps. `signed_admin` steps (`SSHGATE_REVOKE`, `SSHGATE_UPDATE`) and the transfer legs keep their tool's own exit texts with no retry advice (`revoke_server.go:116-121`, `update_gate.go:190-215`, `transfer.go:208-237`): `update_gate`'s 65 is a deterministic binary-check refusal, and it is always the job's first executed step.
