A13a's `StepOutcome` holds both sets of rows.
  - (r5.3, critique pass 1; narrowed to `unsigned_read` in r5.7, review 8 #1) **Exception: the read jail (#22 phase 1, `366ba07`), on route `unsigned_read` only.** An unsigned dispatch can never run a write: the gate classifies the command itself and refuses an unsigned write with exit 77 before anything runs (`src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate/main.go:257,273-276`; the Tier-1 path likewise, `:182-191`), and it runs an unsigned read only through `execRead`, in the read jail the host supports (`:267`, `:493-503`). So on `unsigned_read`, exit 77 with the gate's `read jail unavailable` stderr line (`main.go:328,345,499`; the MCP's `readJailDenied`, `src/mcp/tools/run.go:317-319`) means the read never ran: that step is `failed_not_started` (E28), so it is not in `executed_ordinals` (E29), and it carries the `read_jail_unavailable` Denial (`escalate_to_human`) with no retry advice, on the sync and async paths alike. A read that printed the line itself could hide only that a read ran, which has no side effect to repeat.
  - **(r5.7, review 8 #1) On every signed route, `signed_reveal` included, stderr is never execution evidence.** Reveal is not read classification. `run(reveal:true)` sends any command, write or read, down the signed reveal route (`run.go:254-261`), and the gate classifies the verified inner command on its own: a write runs through `execAndAudit`, unjailed, with the reveal flag set (`main.go:257,278`), and the child's stderr reaches the session (`main.go:289-292`). A revealed write can therefore perform its side effect, print `gate: read jail unavailable`, and exit 77; the MCP's own helper says a command can forge that line (`run.go:313-316`). So on `signed_write`, `signed_reveal`, `signed_admin` and each transfer leg, a post-dispatch exit 77 is `failed` (executed, in `executed_ordinals`) whatever its stderr says, as the signed-step bullet above says. The line selects **remediation only**, and (r5.7, critique of the fold) only on `signed_write` and `signed_reveal`: the gate dispatches the admin verbs and the transfer legs before it classifies anything (`main.go:232-254`), so it never prints the line for them, and their 77 keeps the tool's own text. On those two routes: the step is annotated "gate reported read jail unavailable (unverified on a signed route; counted as executed)", and the Denial it gives is `read_jail_unavailable` (`escalate_to_human`, `Retryable: false`, no retry advice even on the job's first executed step), as today's `runWrite` already picks it on every signed route (`run.go:402-404`). Its message lists the executed ordinals and never says that nothing ran. A revealed *read* that the jail really refused is therefore reported as possibly run: the conservative reading, and the only one the MCP can justify from a signed route. **`jobplan.StepOutcome` (A13a) is the one mapping** from (step kind, whether an earlier step executed, `RunPhased` result) to step status, annotation and remediation; (r5.7, critique of the fold) the step kind is the route refined by the admin verb or the transfer leg, because one row per route cannot hold both `revoke_server`'s and `update_gate`'s exit texts, and the first-executed rule needs to know whether an earlier step ran. The synchronous coordinator (A13b), the transfer coordinator (A13b, for each leg) and the executor (C2) all use it, so the paths cannot disagree.
  - The AGENTS.md exit-65 guidance is updated in A13b.

**E11 — Blocking-path consequences of K3 + K13 + K19.**
- *Evidence:* (r5.3, critique pass 1) the Denial set is closed at 11 classes at `366ba07` (`denial.go:21-33`; `denial_shape_test.go:29`), because #22 phase 1 added `read_jail_unavailable`.
- *Resolution:*
  - A13b adds 9 classes: `reason_required` (`provide_reason`), `request_too_large` (`stop_do_not_retry`, remedy "split into smaller requests"), `request_unrenderable` (`stop_do_not_retry`, E16), `expired_unexecuted`, `approval_canceled`, `stale_target` (`escalate_to_human`), `execution_indeterminate` (`stop_do_not_retry`), `signer_incompatible` (`escalate_to_human`) and, in r3, `approval_pending` (`stop_do_not_retry`, E26).
  - C3 adds `executor_unavailable` (`escalate_to_human`). It also covers the executor's `executor_full` refusal (E75), with the remedy "collect finished results with await_approvals".
  - No new actions are added.
  - `denial_shape_test.go` moves (r5.3, critique pass 1) from 11 to 20 classes (A13b), then to 21 (C3). Both are named changes.
  - The K19 bounds apply on the synchronous path too (§1.3). The MCP validates them before any signer contact, and the signer validates again.
  - `commands/run.md`, `run_batch.md`, `revoke.md`, the skills and `serverInstructions` all pass and explain `reason`.

**E12 — The name `reason` (K2).**
- Output `reason` keeps its current meaning as the classifier or denial reason (`run.go:83`, `run_batch.go:60,74`, `server.go:130-140`).
- Input `reason` gets this description: "Agent-stated intent for the whole request (required for any approval; 1–500 bytes, one line). Shown to the approver as unverified."
- `serverInstructions` states the input/output distinction.
- Reveal uses the same single request-level reason. There is no second field.

**E13 — Local kinds (K13).**
- *Resolution:* there are six new kinds, `approval_{submit,get,list,wait,claim,report}_v2`. That adds `wait` (≤55s) and `claim` (E25) to K13's submit/get/list/report.
- Each frame carries `approval_proto: 1`, the family's own version in `src/approvalwire` (the `policywire` precedent). Frames carry no `proto_version`, so `kindPeek`'s global check (`daemon.go:496-500`) passes.
- Old daemons answer `unsupported kind` (the default case, `daemon.go:519`). The MCP maps that to `signer_incompatible`. (r5.7, review 8 #2) A current daemon in front of an older hosted server answers the v2 frame with code `signer_incompatible` (A5's closed set, E69), which the MCP maps the same way.
- The kinds are dispatched in the `HandleSignRequest` switch after the policy scan (`:470`), the audit-nil guard, the proto check and the backend-nil guard, each with a strict per-kind decode.
- **No socket kind exists for Vote or AdminCancel.** Operator actions stay on the human surfaces (K13).
- `sigwire.ProtoVersion` stays 1, and no socketrpc golden moves.
- A wait connection lives at most 55s plus the write grace, which fits inside `SignerHandlerTimeout`.
- The lifecycle is a new package plus an optional capability. It is never added as methods on `Backend` (`backend.go:285`) or `Store`.

**E14 — The Unit-3 policy family stays outside this lifecycle.**
- `base_manifest_sign_v1`, `p:` callbacks, the policy journal, `/v2/policy` and `/ui/policy` keep their own state machines.
- Policy requests are excluded from `/pending`, `/deny_all`, `/cancel`, `list_pending_approvals`, assist and the v2 pending lists.
- `TestTelegramPolicyNeverInvokesConfiguredExplainer` (`telegram_policy_test.go:478`) stays green.
- v2 multipart bursts take the existing `t.policyNotifyMu` (`telegram.go:100-103`), so policy and v2 bursts never interleave. The policy code itself is not edited.

**E15 — K7 fail-closed redaction.**
- *Evidence:*
  - `RedactString` returns the raw input with `ok=true` when the rules are empty (`src/redact/scrub.go:28-31`). It has no `recover`, and every caller falls back to the raw text.
  - The explainer receives raw `c.Cmd` (`telegram.go:541,889-894`). I probed `PASSWORD=hunter2secretvalue` with nil rules and got the raw text back with `ok=true`.
- *Resolution:*
  - Provider input goes only through `assist.RedactForProvider`. It returns text only if `len(rules)>0`, `ok` is true and no panic occurred.
  - Any failure skips the provider call. Assist becomes `unavailable` with the reason "redaction unavailable".
  - Provider input replaces each hidden span with `⟦hidden N bytes⟧`, built from E16's `RedactSpans`. It carries no `[SSHGATE_REDACTED key=…]` marker keys. If `RedactSpans` returns `ok=false`, the provider call is skipped, as above.
  - This covers commands and reason, v2 and legacy records, local and hosted.
  - Display redaction is unchanged: nil rules still display verbatim, which `telegram_secretredact_test.go:90` pins.
  - The legacy direct `runExplainer` path (`telegram.go:870-899`) uses the same gate. Named change: the explainer tests in `telegram_test.go` that run with nil rules (around `:802-955`) now expect the skip footer or configure rules.
  - "Configured redaction policy" means the built-in `redactrules.Combined()` until the redactor control plane (`feat/v1.2-redactor`, kept on purpose) lands.
  - The setup docs disclose that unmatched text still reaches the provider.

**E16 — Hidden spans (K20, §9.1, §13).**
- *Evidence:* `ExactRedactedBytes` rejects any overlapping raw matches (`scrub.go:46-80`, check at `:70`). Under `Combined()`, an AWS access key and `PASSWORD=…` each match two rules over the same span; my probe got `exact=false` for both.
- *Resolution:*
  - Add `redact.RedactSpans(s, salt, rules) (redacted string, spans []Span, ok bool)`, built on the existing union-merge in `dedupMatches` (`src/redact/scanner.go:193-223`).
  - `Span` is `{Start, End, RuleIDs []string, Bytes}`.
  - A property test proves `redacted == RedactString(s, …)` on every corpus input.
  - **PEM is computed explicitly, not left to `ok=false`.** PEM handling exists only in the Writer's accumulator (`writer.go:170-201` `Close`, `:208-285` `feedPEM`; `pem.go`), not in `scanner.rawMatches`/`dedupMatches`. `spans.go` therefore replays the same segmentation in the same package, using the unexported helpers `findPEMBegin`, `findPEMEnd`, `isPrivateKeyBegin`, `isPublicPEMBegin` and `pemMaxBuffer`:
    - a complete non-public block is one span (rule ID `pem-block`);
    - a complete public block (certificate, public key, CSR) is scanned with the named rules only;
    - an aborted private-key BEGIN gives a span over the buffered bytes;
    - an aborted non-key BEGIN is scanned normally;
    - an unterminated private-key BEGIN at EOF gives a span to EOF.
    The bytes after a PEM block are scanned normally, so a destructive tail stays visible.
  - `ok=false` is left only for nil-safe failure cases: a reconstruction mismatch, a recovered panic, or input over the writer's 64 KiB ring (`writer.go:22-26`). A v2 step can never exceed that ring (K19: 4 KiB). With nil rules, `RedactSpans` returns the input unchanged with no spans and `ok=true`, matching `RedactString` and E15's verbatim display.
  - **On `ok=false` the renderer never hides bytes that no rule matched.** This follows §9.1: "refuse the request if a faithful safe display cannot be produced".
    - **v2:** freeze refuses the request before any notification with the typed error `request_unrenderable`. The MCP Denial class is `request_unrenderable` (`stop_do_not_retry`, remedy "the command cannot be displayed safely for approval; restructure it, for example move key material into a `transfer`").
    - **Legacy v1:** the card shows the `RedactString` output, which replaces only matched spans. It is escaped through A3's neutralizer and labelled "Display-redacted command — hidden-byte count unavailable" next to the K20 label.
  - Marker-forgery rewrites do not count as hidden bytes.
  - `ExactRedactedBytes`, `LiteralPreview` and the `policyreview` goldens stay untouched.
  - GATEHASH must be unchanged (§0.6).

**E17 — Principal and invariant 3 (K10, §7.4, §13).**
- *Evidence:* no peer-credential code exists. `RequestHandler` takes an `io.ReadWriter` (`socket.go:25-26`), although `serveOne` holds a `net.Conn` (`:167`).
- *Resolution:*
  - Add an optional `PeerAwareHandler` interface. `serveOne` type-asserts it and reads peer credentials from the `*net.UnixConn`:
    - Linux: `unix.GetsockoptUcred(SOL_SOCKET, SO_PEERCRED)`;
    - darwin: `unix.GetsockoptXucred(SOL_LOCAL, LOCAL_PEERCRED)`, which already exists in x/sys v0.45.0 (`syscall_darwin.go:488`);
    - any other OS: no peer, and v2 kinds are refused.
  - `RequestHandler` and its implementers stay unchanged.
  - The local principal is `local-uid:<uid>`. An optional `[approvals] allowed_uids` setting restricts the v2 kinds.
  - Get, Wait, Claim and Report all require a principal match.
  - The MCP, the executor and an agent shell share one uid and therefore one principal. Invariant 3 is an interface property: tool outputs never carry signatures. The claim token separates other principals only; the human tap plus the gate remain the security boundary.
  - THREAT-MODEL and design docs state this (D3).

**E18 — Local record store and recovery (K11, K14, §10.2; rewritten in r5).**
- The v2 store is built on `internal/securestate` (`store_linux.go`: `OpenAt`/`Update`, 0700 directory, 0600 files, flock, fsync+rename, `ErrCommitUncertain`). It holds **one file per record** (§1.5 SA-3), not one snapshot. Listing and deleting records need two additive methods, `ListNames` and `Remove` (B0); today the transaction has only `ReadFile`/`WriteFile` and their canonical-JSON forms (`internal/securestate/store_linux.go:241-370`).
- Its default root is `filepath.Dir(Paths.Key)/approvals`, which follows the policy-journal precedent (`resolvePolicyJournalRoot`, signer `main.go:355-360`). In production that is `/var/lib/sshgatesigner/keys/approvals`, inside the unit's `ReadWritePaths=/var/lib/sshgatesigner` (`scripts/install.sh:191`). In `--dev` it follows the key under `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/signer-<pid>` (`resolveInitPaths`, `:753-772`). `doInitFlow` writes it into the generated config. It never goes under `/run`.
- `!linux` builds use the memory store and log a warning. On darwin, synchronous v2 works, but nothing survives a restart (as with v1 today), and async is Linux-only (K10).
- **An uncertain commit is a crash of the store's owner (§1.5 SA-8).** The store poisons itself under its operation mutex, exactly as the policy journal does (`pkg/signerkit/policy_journal_linux.go:159-167,313-316`); every command answers `storage_uncertain`; the lifecycle cancels and joins its actors ((r5.8) by §1.5 SA-8's owner stop, so an interrupted send makes no INCOMPLETE edit), discards all lifecycle memory, reopens with a new epoch and runs Startup. r4.1's poison-and-continue with in-memory undone-effect facts is withdrawn.
- **Restart is Startup (§1.5 SA-8):** list and load every record, validate it, build the projections, mint a new epoch, and run `Next` on every record. There is no ordered recovery list, no card reattach and no "signer starting" gate; the Telegram poller and the socket listener both start after Startup returns. **(r5.1)** Today the poller starts inside `buildTelegramBackend` (`tb.Run`, signer `main.go:606`), before `signerkit.New` (`:275`) and the socket `Listen` (`:321`), so A8 moves `tb.Run` out of `buildTelegramBackend` and after Startup.
- Do **not** copy the policy journal's interrupt-on-restart (`policy_journal_linux.go:1327-1343`).
- On SIGTERM, v2 records stay as they are, the actors are cancelled and joined, and no v2 card is edited. Legacy requests end as today (E8b). (r5.8, review 9 #2) "As they are" means as of the last commit before §1.5 SA-8's `stopping` latch. Intake stops, the latch refuses every later commit, and only then are the actors cancelled and joined. A send, edit or assist call the stop interrupts is not a live failure: it commits nothing, sends no INCOMPLETE edit, and is recovered at the next start by the crash rules (`uncertain`, a superseding edit, Clock's assist fill).
- Poll starts at offset 0 (`telegram.go:339`) and redelivers old taps. A redelivered `v:` callback is an ordinary, idempotent Vote command judged by the derived validity of §1.5 SA-6.1. Redelivered slash commands are dropped by E64. (r5.1) A vote's `ts`, and so `decided_at`, is the signer's processing time, because callback queries carry no date; `answerCallbackQuery` on an old query fails ("query is too old") and is best effort, never fatal.

**E19 — Hosted principal (K17, §5; rewritten in r5).**
- *Evidence:* `MachineClientID` is required only with the human plane (`hosted/config.go:109-112`; signer-server `main.go:209-210`). `withAuth` injects one `MachineClientID` for every request (`server.go:310-325`), so hosted authentication cannot tell the local daemon's uids apart.
- *Resolution:*
  - `AttachApprovals` refuses an empty `MachineClientID`.
  - The v2 principal is `(client_id, sub_principal)`. `client_id` comes only from the `withAuth` context. `sub_principal` is the optional `requester_sub` field of the unfrozen v2 wire, grammar `^local-uid:[0-9]{1,10}$`; absent means the empty namespace. The local daemon's proxy (A12) always sets it from `SO_PEERCRED`; a direct bearer client normally omits it.
  - `sub_principal` is a **partition inside one client, not an authentication boundary**: any holder of the bearer can name any `sub_principal`. K17 makes that one client, and the bearer is readable only by the signer uid, so this narrows the per-client filter and never widens it. THREAT-MODEL says so (D3).
  - Every v2 row is keyed on `(client_id, sub_principal, request_id)` (§1.5 SA-3), so another uid's id and a missing id are the same miss (§1.5 SA-6.6). This replaces r3–r4.1's proxy ownership index (E66, deleted).
  - v1 is unchanged. `/v1/audit`'s exposure of all rows is accepted under K17 (single client) and documented.

**E20 — Async output shape (K9, §7.1).**
- `RunOutput` and `RunBatchOutput` gain omitempty `request_id`, `approval_status`, `execution_status` and `notification_status`, plus (r3) `executed_ordinals` and `partial` (E29), and run_batch results gain a per-step `status`.
- The required sets are unchanged: `stdout, stderr, exit_code, kind, approved` (`run.go:56-64`) and `server, results, approved` (`run_batch.go:65-69`).
- The text summary says "NOT EXECUTED YET — collect with await_approvals".
- The field descriptions say `exit_code` is meaningful only when `execution_status` is a terminal known result.

**E21 — Mint at the first Claim (invariants 4–5, §6.1, §10.1; rewritten in r5).**
- *Evidence:*
  - The hosted engine mints before its CAS (`approval_engine.go:257-281`, `approvedAt` at `:263`) and infers the winner by read-back.
  - `storetest` treats any non-pending tuple as final.
  - The local daemon signs inline at `d.now()` (`daemon.go:1548-1565`).
- **Spec-body erratum (not a K-ruling).** Spec §6.1's "threshold crossing commits … a `minting` claim; only that claimant mints" becomes: **the first Claim mints inside its own commit, at TS = `decided_at`, and that one durable set is what every later Claim inside the window returns** (§1.5 SA-4). Invariants 4 and 5 hold unchanged: nothing is signed while pending, and the signature timestamp is the terminal human approval. There is no `minting` state, no claim ID and no stale-claim recovery (r3–r4.1's SC-1.M is withdrawn).
- **Hosted:**
  - v2 records live only in `approvals_v2`, never in `requests`.
  - **(r5.1) Mint outside the transaction, then CAS the slot** (v1's mint-before-flip precedent, `approval_engine.go:257-281`). A first Claim reads the row with a plain WAL query, re-runs E71 on the stored plan, and calls `Service.SignApproved(cmds, decidedAt)` (`pkg/signerkit/hosted_sign.go:56`, already timestamp-parameterised) with no transaction open. It then runs the Claim command in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction: re-read the row, (r5.3) apply Clock first, re-check the record (still approved, mint slot empty, and SA-1 rule 3.2's `decided_at ≤ now + 5 s`), and pass the minted set to `Apply` as its impure input. (r5.3) If Clock closes the window or rule 3.2 refuses, the pre-minted set is discarded and the Claim answers from the committed facts (`expired`, or `materialization_error{clock_skew}`). If the mint slot is still empty, the slot and `claim_count` are written and committed, and only then do the bytes leave; if another Claim filled it first, the minted bytes are discarded and the stored set is returned as a repeat Claim. Signing therefore never holds SQLite's write lock: `Custody`'s `crypto.Signer` seam (`custody.go:70-91`) may be an external signer with no context, which inside the transaction would hold the RESERVED lock unboundedly.
  - (r5.1) Every read inside a v2 transaction goes through that `tx`: the `:memory:` store runs one connection (`sqlite.go:205-209`), so an `s.db` call inside a transaction (an E6 `GetUser` role check, for example) would deadlock the tests. Reads outside a command (Get, List, Wait, the detail GET) are plain WAL queries, never `BeginTx`, because under `_txlock=immediate` every `BeginTx` takes the write lock.
  - An error from `COMMIT`, or from the driver after the statement was sent, answers 503 "storage uncertain — retry" and returns nothing. (r5.1) An error at `BEGIN` (`SQLITE_BUSY` after the 5 s busy timeout) is a definite failure: a retryable 503 "busy — retry", without the uncertain path.
  - **(r5.6, review 7 #1) The `busy` contract.** r5.1–r5.5 named the answer but gave it no code, so A5's closed set and SA-5's split would have turned it into a wire violation or a definitive `error{busy}`. Now:
    - *Envelope.* HTTP 503 with `Retry-After: 1` and the body `{approval_proto:1, ok:false, error:{code:"busy", message:"busy — retry"}}`. `busy` is a member of A5's closed code set. It means the command was **not applied**: nothing was read into a decision, nothing committed and no effect ran, so a retry of the same bytes is always safe. That is the difference from `storage_uncertain`, where the commit may have landed.
    - *Detection.* (r5.7) An error in which `errors.As` finds `interface{ Code() int }` with primary code (`Code() & 0xff`) `SQLITE_BUSY` (5), returned by `BeginTx`, or by a plain read outside any transaction. modernc.org/sqlite's `*sqlite.Error` (`go.mod:17`, v1.50.1; `Code()` at `error.go:21`) satisfies it. Its fields are unexported (`error.go:12-15`), so a test cannot build one; the interface lets the store wrappers of A10, A11 and D2 inject a busy error of their own type, as their tests require. r5.6 named the concrete type, so no injected case could be built. A `SQLITE_BUSY` from `COMMIT`, or from the driver after a write statement was sent, stays `storage_uncertain` (the rule above).
    - *Where.* Every hosted v2 machine route answers it the same way: Submit, Claim (its out-of-transaction mint is discarded, as when another Claim wins, so no signature leaves), Report, and a Get, List or Wait whose plain read ends in `SQLITE_BUSY`. On the human plane, the vote POST, cancel and the deny-all snapshot answer 503 "busy — retry" (r5.6, critique of the fold: spelled out per transaction, and owned where the routes are built). **Vote POST (A11), in E7's order, v2 records only:** a busy `BEGIN` at step 3's Clock transaction or at step 5's refusal commit answers 503 "busy — retry" before any step-up, so no TOTP step is spent and the same challenge and code work on the retry (no `vote_refused` is appended, because no transaction committed); a `SQLITE_BUSY` from step 6's `ConsumeTOTPStep` (an autocommit upsert, `pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/operators.go:255-265`, reached through `VerifyTOTP`, `pkg/signerkit/hosted/auth.go:315-318`, which wraps it with `%w`, and `StepUp` returns it unwrapped, `auth.go:598-603`) answers 503 "busy — retry with a fresh code", never today's 401 "step-up required" (`human_handlers.go:769-770`): it is a write statement, so by the detection rule above it may have landed and the code may be spent; a busy `BEGIN` at step 7's Vote transaction after step 6 consumed the step answers the same 503 "busy — retry with a fresh code": nothing of the vote was committed, but the TOTP step is spent, so the retry needs the next code (up to 30 s). With step-up off, step 7's 503 reads "busy — retry" and the same challenge works at once. `request.js` treats a 503 as "busy, tap again", and shows "enter a fresh code" for the second message. **Cancel and deny-all (D2):** a busy cancel answers 503 and the admin resends it; the snapshot POST is in-memory plus a plain read, so a busy read answers 503 and creates nothing ((r5.7, review 8 #4) a plain read rarely meets `SQLITE_BUSY`: the file-backed store runs in WAL mode (`sqlite.go:104,187,193-194`), where a writer's lock does not block readers, so the read-side cases are tested by injection, and a separate test shows reads still answer while a writer holds the lock); the confirm applies each item as its own AdminCancel commit (D1a), so a busy item is reported "not canceled (busy)", like the existing skipped items, the remaining items still run, the items already canceled stay canceled, and the admin takes a new snapshot for the rest (the snapshot is discarded at confirm, SA-3). **Hosted assist commit (A10):** the assist job retries its `AssistResult` commit on a busy `BEGIN` with E25's backoff until `assist_deadline`; the provider is not called again (K8's one attempt is the provider call), and a retry after Clock filled the slot is dropped as any late result is (SA-4 AssistResult). The worker and the flusher treat a busy `BEGIN` as "not this pass": the row is retried at the next pass, logged as contention, never as uncertain. v1 handlers are unchanged, step 6 included: a v1 row's vote keeps today's 401 for any step-up error.
    - *Local.* The local lifecycle serialises commits under its own mutex and never produces `busy`. The local daemon emits `busy` only by forwarding a hosted answer (A12).
    - *Retry.* The MCP client returns a typed `ErrBusy`, distinct from `ErrStorageUncertain` and `ErrResponseLost` (A5). `busy` is transient in SA-5's one split (C2) and in A13b's synchronous rule; both retry the same request bytes (the same Submit tuple, the same claim token, the same canonical report body) with E25's bounded backoff (250 ms doubling to 2 s). Inside the synchronous budget the retries end at the budget; a Claim still answering `busy` at the window's end answers `expired` on the first retry that gets through, because Clock commits `window_closed` in that Claim's own transaction. (r5.6, critique of the fold) **The synchronous Submit phase has its own retry bound,** because the wait budget is acquired only when Submit returns: the coordinator stops resending a Submit once its wall reading (`internal/elapsed` `Wall()`) passes the draft's `submit_not_after` (Submit + 120 s, E73), or 125 s of E have passed since the first attempt, whichever comes first. The bound confers no authority, because the signer judges the window itself at its own wall clock (SA-1 rule 3.5), so it is a retry bound like a backoff, not a held deadline. Its ending is E26's Submit-phase row. **The MCP's read tools** (C3) treat `busy` from List or Get as transient too: `list_pending_approvals` resends List once after 250 ms, `await_approvals` resends Get inside its await deadline, and an answer still missing then reads "unknown (signer busy — retry)" for that item or column, never a tool error.
  - v1 rows keep today's engine, `UpdateStatus` and `storetest`.
- **Local:**
  - The daemon gains `signAt(cmds, ts)`, and `signAll` becomes `signAt(cmds, now)`, so v1 bytes stay identical.
  - Likewise `signTransferLegs` (`daemon.go:1221-1222`, which signs at `d.now()`) becomes `signTransferLegsAt(..., ts)`, and the v1 path passes `d.now().Unix()`.
  - v2 mints at the persisted `decided_at`, with Host and Reveal, through the lifecycle's `Minter` inside the Claim's critical section. Both refactors are proven byte-identical by FROZEN-LOCAL plus a fixed-clock equality test.
- **One set leaves.** The payload carries a nonce (`src/sigwire/payload.go:29`), so a re-mint after a commit that did not land produces different bytes. That is safe because bytes leave only after the mint commit is confirmed: an uncertain first mint never left the signer.

**E22 — Renderer (K19, K6, §9.2).**
- Copy the policy pre-render discipline into `src/approvalreview` and a new Telegram v2 notifier; do not mutate it. The sources are the bounds at `telegram_policy_review.go:18-31`, the packer at `:201-243` and the send flow at `telegram_policy.go:64-157`.
- Add `command i, chunk j/M` continuations that repeat the command's SHA-256.
- Add best-effort INCOMPLETE edits.
- Add the edit that adds Approve.
- `formatApprovalMessage` (`telegram.go:1043`) and its tests stay as they are for the direct path, which production no longer reaches (E8e).

**E23 — Audit (K8, §8.3; rewritten in r5).**
- *Evidence:* `AuditEvent` is byte-frozen (`service_test.go:211`). `AuditCall.Reason` is custody-only (`audit_sink.go:61-82`), and `CommandReq.Reason` is overloaded (`backend.go:203`).
- *Resolution:*
  - Append `Approval *approvalplan.AuditMetadata` (E62) with tag `json:"approval,omitempty"` as the last field of `AuditEvent` (`audit.go:36-47`), and likewise on `AuditCall` and `AuditVerdict`.
  - Its fields: `{plan_hash, rationale_redacted, rationale_origin, assist{state,revision,digest}, transition, decision, decided_at, decided_by, execution{state,class,client_reported}, replay, event_key}`. `event_key` is `<record key>:<outbox seq>` (§1.5 SA-2), so a re-run of the flusher writes a recognisable duplicate rather than a second fact.
  - Never reuse either existing `Reason` field.
  - Local status strings are `approval-<transition>`.
  - **v2: outbox, then flush (§1.5 SA-7).** Every v2 event is appended to the record's outbox in the same commit as its transition. The flusher writes it to the sink and then commits `audited_through`. Until the outbox is flushed through the decision, no signature leaves the signer; until it is flushed completely, the record is neither shed nor deleted. A failing sink therefore delays Claim (the record may end `expired_unclaimed`), but it never changes approval state. (r5.5) Once the sink recovers, the outbox drains with no further command: each owner retries every unflushed record each second, independently of `next_due_at` (§1.5 SA-7). r3–r4.1's "audit before the state change", and r4.1's competing prepare-first vote rule, are withdrawn (R3.2): there is one ordering rule.
  - **Local v1** keeps its after-response, fail-open audit (`daemon.go:674-743`), taps included (E8b); (r5.4) a tap that does not resolve the request writes one fail-open `legacy_vote_replay` or `legacy_vote_refused` line (E8b).
  - **(r5.8, review 9 #1) Inert verdicts.** An `AuditVerdict` whose `Approval.transition` is `vote_duplicate` or `vote_refused{…}` (hosted v1's HTTP-layer lines, E7 steps 3, 5 and 7) is encoded as an inert line: kind `verdict_inert` by `appendOnlySink`, the hosted core's sink (signer-server `main.go:251`; today it writes kind `verdict`, `audit_sink.go:309-311`), and status `approval-<transition>` by `AuditLog.Verdict` (today `approved` or `denied`, `audit_sink.go:399-428`). Every verdict without that metadata keeps its bytes. The policy plane's durable sink never receives one. A6 builds it. (r5.8, critique of the fold) **The predicate is `Approval != nil && Approval.replay`, not a list of transition names.** `replay` is set on exactly the noise lines and events: hosted v1's inert lines, E8b's local legacy lines, and v2's `vote_replay`, `vote_refused`, `invalid_claim` and `invalid_report`; it is never set on an event that counts. **Which sink method carries what:** `Verdict` carries only hosted v1 votes (`SubmitVote`'s own, unchanged) and hosted v1 inert lines; the hosted v2 flusher writes every v2 outbox event through `Call`, as an `AuditCall` with `Lifecycle = "approval-<transition>"` and `Approval` set (append-only kind `lifecycle`; `AuditLog.Call` writes status `approval-<transition>` for it, with no `custody:` prefix), never through `Verdict`, so no v2 event, noise or not, reaches a sink as an ordinary verdict. **Embedding apps:** `signerkit.Config.Audit` accepts any `AuditSink` (`service.go:33-37`), and such a sink cannot learn the new encoding, so A6 updates the doc comments of `AuditSink.Verdict` (today "a single operator's resolution", `audit_sink.go:42-45`), `AuditVerdict` and `AuditCall.Lifecycle`: a `Verdict` whose `Approval.replay` is set is not a resolution, and a `Call` whose `Lifecycle` starts with `approval-` is an approval-lifecycle event, not custody; D3's integrator docs and the release notes state the same.
  - **Hosted:** the committed `approvals_v2` row is itself the durable audit fact. The ordinary stderr sink (`signer-server main.go:251`) stays a non-durable projection in v0.2 (§1.3 item 5; this addresses STATE-MODEL F-5).
  - **(r5.1) Hosted v2 audit stays visible and is not pruned by default.** Because the row is the audit fact: (1) the decision slot and the vote list live in the R1 header (§1.5 SA-3), so a shed row still holds who decided, how and when; (2) hosted Delete is off by default (`--approval-max-age` defaults to 0, meaning never), which matches today's never-pruned v1 rows; when `--approval-max-rows` is then reached, a new v2 Submit gets `queue_full` and the worker logs that the ceiling needs raising or a retention set (§1.3 item 29); (3) `/ui/audit` (`human_handlers.go:799-809`, which reads only `requests` through `RecentAudit`, `sqlite.go:447-453`) gains an additive `v2_entries` list built from `approvals_v2` headers, leaving `entries` byte-identical (A11). `/v1/audit` is frozen and stays v1-only (E31).
  - **(r5.2) The advisory binding survives Shed.** Spec §8.3 audits "the plan hash, redacted reason, assist status/revision/digest, decision, approver, execution transition and final result class". r5.1's header held the plan hash, decision, approver, votes and final class. The redacted reason and the assist binding were only in the sheddable snapshot and outbox, and on hosted the stderr line is only a projection. So after a Flush and a Shed, a hosted row could no longer show which advisory the decision was bound to (K8). Two fixes, both inside the existing header and slots: (1) the header keeps `rationale_redacted`, the snapshot's redacted reason, which is immutable from freeze; (2) the write-once decision slot gains `assist_bound {state, revision, digest}`, the assist that the View showed at the deciding commit. The decision event carries the same digest (SA-4 Vote). For a grant-decided record or a Clock expiry it is the View's assist at that commit, `not_applicable` for a grant. `/ui/audit`'s `v2_entries` shows both. No audit table is added.

**E24 — Host-bound grants (§5 `standing_grant`; rewritten in r5).**
- *Evidence (pre-existing):*
  - The grant struct is `{id, alias, scope, commands, expiry}` with no host (`daemon.go:53-59`).
  - `matchGrant` checks only alias and command (`:1478-1497`).
  - `signAll` copies the host the requester supplied (`:1563`).
- *v2 resolution:*
  - The `standing_grant` payload adds `trusted_host_fingerprint` and `registry_entry_digest`, and the grant card shows both.
  - v2 matching runs only over live grant records (E9, §1.5 SA-6.4) and requires `step.trusted_host_fingerprint == grant.host_fp`.
  - Host-less grants created through v1 never cover v2 submissions.
  - **(r5.1) v1 `sign` never spends a v2 grant.** v1 `matchGrant` (`daemon.go:1456-1500`) consults only the frozen v1 map, unchanged ((r5.2) and is then removed altogether by the next bullet). r5's bridge (v1 `sign` falling through to the v2 projection with a host check) is withdrawn: through it any `sshgatesigner`-group process, including a uid that `allowed_uids` excludes, could get tap-free signatures under a grant a human approved for one principal, on a path with no reason, no v2 record, no claim count and no report, audited only after the response and fail-open. After A13b no in-tree client sends v1 `sign`, so the bridge served only stale clients. The v1 `grant` struct and every v1 wire byte are unchanged. A7 tests that a live v2 grant never auto-signs a v1 `sign`, on any host.
  - **(r5.2, OQ3 (b), owner ruling) v1 `sign` spends no grant at all.** The v1 short-circuit (`daemon.go:604-606`) is removed (E8b), so host-less v1 grants no longer auto-sign. That closes the pre-existing gap where a group process sent `{kind:sign, server:<granted alias>, host:<any registered fingerprint>}` and got a tap-free signature bound to a different server (`matchGrant` checks alias and command only, `:1478-1497`; `signAll` copies the requester's host, `:1563`). The grant-covered v1 request now prompts on a legacy card, and its response bytes are today's human-approved bytes.
  - v2 coverage is per principal (E9): a step is covered only by a live grant record of the submitter's own principal whose host fingerprint equals the step's `trusted_host_fingerprint`.
  - `revoke_grant(alias)` clears both sources: it deletes the v1 map entry as today (`:900-904`) and applies Revoke to every live v2 grant record for the alias. **(r5.1)** This is alias-global de-escalation, as v1 revoke is today, and it is the one cross-principal write on the machine plane (§1.5 SA-6.6 carve-out); its response is today's bytes (`status: approved`), which say nothing about whose grant existed. Revoking the newest v2 grant never revives an older one (SA-6.4).
  - `list_grants` shows the v1 map, as today, plus **(r5.1) only the caller's own principal's** live v2 grants (from the peer credential, E17; no peer means no v2 entries), each in today's response shape. r5 listed every principal's v2 grants, whose `grant_id` is that principal's `request_id`, to any group member.
- (r5.2) Frozen v1 behaviour for **v1-created, host-less** grants was OQ3, which the owner ruled (b): v1-created grants are inert for signing. `list_grants` still shows them and `revoke_grant` still clears them.
- `docs/proposed/feature3-grants-reveal-audit-binding.md:86` is corrected in D3.

**E25 — Who receives signatures; the claim and report contracts (rewritten in r5).**
- `approval_claim_v2` locally, or `POST /v2/approvals/{id}/claim` on hosted, is the **only** operation that returns v2 signature material.
  - It starts with the owner-scoped load (§1.5 SA-6.6): another principal's id and a missing id give the same `not_found`.
  - It then compares `SHA-256("sshgate-executor-claim-v1"‖token)` with the frozen hash in constant time, and the `plan_hash`; a mismatch is `invalid_claim`, audited.
  - Its answer is a total function of the record after Clock (r5.3; §1.5 SA-4 Claim): `not_ready`, a terminal approval state, `execution_reported{class}`, `materialization_error{reason}`, `expired`, or the signatures. The first Claim mints (E21); a repeat Claim inside the window returns the same set and adds 1 to `claim_count`.
  - **(r5.1) `not_ready` from the flush gate is short and retried.** Every commit that appends events nudges the flusher as a post-commit effect, on both planes (hosted no longer waits for the worker's 1 s tick). A Claim held back only by the outbox gate waits up to 2 s, outside the store lock, for a flush wake-up and retries once before answering `not_ready`. Every caller retries `not_ready` with bounded backoff (250 ms doubling to 2 s): the A13b coordinator inside its sync budget ((r5.5) until the budget ends or Claim answers `expired`, which after the `approved` it already saw is the window's close and ends the run `expired_unexecuted`, E26), the executor (C2) (r5.3, critique pass 1) until Clock's `window_closed` or the Erase, which Claim answers `expired` ahead of the flush gate (§1.5 SA-4); r5.1's "inside the step's K16 margin" named a pair that does not exist before a successful Claim.
  - **(r5.1) A synchronous request can be claimed only while its caller can be waiting.** For `coordinator=in_process_sync`, Claim answers `expired` once `now ≥ sync_claim_deadline = created_at + 310 s` (the 300 s sync budget plus 10 s of slack for Submit's own latency). (r5.3) `claim_end` is never later than `sync_claim_deadline` (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3.3), so the Claim's own Clock pass commits `window_closed` (or the Erase) and then answers `expired`; r5.1's "writing nothing" is withdrawn. SA-1 rule 3.2's 5 s check applies to repeat Claims too. `coordinator` is still requester-supplied, but the card's synchronous-request line (E26) is now enforced by the signer, so an executor job mislabelled `in_process_sync` cannot execute after a late approval the human was told was inert.
- **The claim-token hash is mandatory where execution applies (r3.1, kept).** Every draft whose kind has `execution_applicable=true` (`command_job`, `transfer`) must carry a well-formed `executor_claim_token_hash` (64 lowercase hex), or freeze refuses it with `invalid_request` on both planes and in the A12 proxy. A `standing_grant` draft carries none. Legacy v1 requests never become drafts and return their signatures on the v1 connection.
- **Report (§1.5 SA-4).** One final report per request, carrying `report_sequence` 1 (spec-body erratum to spec §5 and §6.2: the sequence is fixed at 1, and there are no progress reports). It is accepted whenever the header exists, including after `report_horizon` and after a shed, when the token and `plan_hash` match, the decision is approved, the final slot is empty, (r5.6, critique of the fold) the steps are well formed (exactly `step_count` entries with ordinals 1..`step_count` ascending, E38, in E29's shape: a prefix of `succeeded` and `failed` steps that may end in one abort status, then a tail that is all `not_run` when the prefix ends in an abort status, and may be all `skipped` only when it ends in `failed`; checked before the three values below), `class = approvalplan.AggregateJobClass(steps)` (E29), (r5.6) `executed_ordinals = approvalplan.ExecutedOrdinals(steps)` and `partial = approvalplan.Partial(steps)` (E29), and the executed mask is empty or `claim_count ≥ 1`. So negative reports need no claim, and executed reports do. An exact duplicate (same body digest) is acked; anything else is `invalid_report`, audited. Acceptance erases the signature bytes. The signer's `unreported` view is provisional: a later accepted report replaces it (R3.3 cannot recur, because the final slot is write-once and `unreported` is never stored).
- Get, Wait, List, `list_pending_approvals` and `/v1/poll` never return v2 signatures. No surface or response ever returns `executor_claim_token_hash`.
- In-tree synchronous MCP tools generate an ephemeral in-process claim token. They act as their own kind-specific coordinator and report through `ReportExecution`.
- The executor is used only for `async=true`.

**E26 — Validity and the blocking adapter (K12, K13, §7.1, §11; rewritten in r3, edited in r5).** This is the ratified behaviour. OQ1 (§1.2) is only an optional amendment and is not built.
- Every v2 request, synchronous or async, gets the operator's configured validity: `[approvals] pending_validity` locally, `--approval-validity` on hosted, default 24 h, range 5 min..7 d (K12). The requester never chooses it in v0.2. The draft schema has no validity field at all; the strict decode refuses any unknown field. If OQ1 is ever adopted, it adds the field as an optional, omit-when-absent member, so every existing `draft_hash` and `plan_hash` stays byte-identical.
- The synchronous adapter (`async=false`, A13b) submits with `coordinator=in_process_sync`, then loops Wait (≤55 s per call) for a total sync wait budget of `sigwire.ApprovalWindow` (300 s) from Submit's return. The budget is `Runner.SyncWaitBudget`, injectable for tests.
- (r5.1) The wait ends at the earlier of the 300 s budget and `sync_claim_deadline − 5 s` (E25; the Submit answer carries `created_at`). (r5.3) The budget is a held deadline of §1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4: acquired when Submit returns, nominal 300 s, W = `sync_claim_deadline − 5 s`, ending at the first of its wall and elapsed ends.
- Outcomes:
  - approved within the budget → Claim, run and report as today's output. (r5.1) A Claim answered `not_ready` (the outbox not yet flushed through the decision, normal right after a grant-covered Submit or a tap) is retried with E25's backoff inside the budget; `storage_uncertain` likewise, and (r5.6) hosted `busy` on any call (E21) ((r5.3) `clock_regressed` is withdrawn). (r5.5) The retries end at the first of the budget's end and a Claim answer of `expired` (below). If the budget ends while Claim still answers `not_ready` (a failing audit sink), the call returns the `approval_pending` Denial with the wording "Approval request <id> was approved, but the signer could not release signatures before this call's wait ended (its audit record is not written yet). Nothing ran. Do not resubmit; ask the user to check the signer." The class and action are unchanged (`stop_do_not_retry`), so the Denial set does not grow. (r5.6, critique of the fold) If the budget ends while the last Claim answer was `busy` (hosted contention, E21), the same Denial carries its own sentence instead, because the audit record is not the cause: "Approval request <id> was approved, but the signer was too busy to release signatures before this call's wait ended. Nothing ran. Do not resubmit; ask the user to check the signer.";
  - (r5.6, critique of the fold) **the Submit phase ends without an answer that carries the record** (E21's Submit-phase retry bound, or a resubmit answered `submit_window_closed`): if every attempt was answered `busy`, or a later resubmit was answered `submit_window_closed` (which the signer gives only for an id it does not hold, since an existing id is returned whatever the time, SA-4 Submit), nothing was submitted, and the call returns the `signer_unreachable` Denial (`escalate_to_human`) with "The signer was too busy to accept approval request <id> before its submit window closed. Nothing was submitted and nothing ran. Ask the user to check the signer before retrying." If any attempt's outcome is unknown (`ErrResponseLost` not reconciled by Get, or `storage_uncertain`) and no later answer settled it, the request may exist, and the call returns the v2 verdict-undelivered text as today. `signer_unreachable`'s class-level `Summary` and `HowTo` (`denial.go:96-106`) name only a local socket that is down, which is already untrue of the `record_corrupt` mapping (A13b) and of a hosted signer, so A13b rewords them to "The signer could not be reached or could not serve this request; nothing ran." and "A human checks the signer (`sshgate.status`, then its service or hosted server logs); retry once it is serving." (a named change; class, action and `Retryable` unchanged);
  - (r5.5) the budget-end `not_ready` case above needs a late approval: SA-4 answers Claim `expired` at `claim_end` ahead of the flush gate, and `claim_end` is the first signed step's window end, `decided_at + TTL`. With TTL 60 the budget (300 s from Submit's return, and no later than W = `sync_claim_deadline − 5 s`) ends first only when the decision came after about `created_at + 240 s`. A timely approval with a failing sink meets the next bullet instead, about 60 s after the decision;
  - (r5.5) **approved, then Claim answers `expired`** (the window closed before the signer released the signatures: a failing audit sink, or, after a step back, SA-1 rule 3.2's check on a repeat Claim). Decisions are write-once and the coordinator Claims only after it has seen `approved`, from Submit's answer or from Wait, so this `expired` is never the pending request's expiry. The coordinator stops retrying, records the first step as `expired_unexecuted` and every later step `not_run` (no step has run, because the synchronous run Claims before its first dispatch), runs nothing, and sends the one final report as a negative report (executed mask empty, class `expired_unexecuted`; accepted with `claim_count = 0`, E25), so the execution view becomes `final{expired_unexecuted}`, not `expired_unclaimed`. The call returns the E10 `expired_unexecuted` Denial (`stop_do_not_retry`, not retryable) with this wording: "Approval request <id> was approved, but its signatures expired before the signer released them (its audit record was not written in time). Nothing ran. Do not resubmit automatically: ask the user to check the signer, then submit a fresh request, which needs a new approval." Spec §11's row "Signature expires before first SSH → `expired_unexecuted`, new request/tap required" is this case. The executor already maps a Claim `expired` the same way (SA-5 step 6);
  - `expired` **as a decision**, from Submit's answer, Wait or Get (r5.5: never from a Claim after `approved`), which can happen only when the operator configured a validity no longer than the budget → the existing `approval_timeout` Denial (retry once), which is then truthful;
  - the budget ends while the request is still `pending` → `wait_elapsed`, and the request is not mutated (K12). The tool returns without executing anything: `request_id`, `approval_status=pending`, and the new Denial `approval_pending` (`stop_do_not_retry`, not retryable): "Approval request <id> is still pending until <expires_at>. Nothing ran. This synchronous call has returned, so a later approval will not execute it. Do not resubmit. Ask the user to deny it, or to approve a fresh request while you wait." The in-process claim token dies with the call.
- **A later approval of an abandoned synchronous request** is never claimed, so nothing is minted (E21). (r5.3) Clock closes its window at `claim_end`, no later than `sync_claim_deadline`: the mint slot holds `window_closed`, the execution view shows `expired_unclaimed` (§1.5 SA-6.2) and the record is settled. The card shows it (E49): "approved — not executed: nothing claimed it before its signatures expired". `list_pending_approvals` and `await_approvals` still show the request until then (§1.5 SA-5, C3).
- The decision card of every `in_process_sync` request carries the line "Synchronous request: it runs only if approved while the agent is still waiting (until <sync_claim_deadline>)". The time is the signer's own. (r5.1) The line is now true by construction: Claim refuses an `in_process_sync` record after `sync_claim_deadline` (E25). `coordinator` stays requester-supplied; mislabelling only narrows what can run.
- §11 gains the row: "A blocking caller's wait budget ends → the request stays `pending` (`wait_elapsed`); the call returns `approval_pending`, never executes later, and must not be resubmitted."
- **(r5.5, critique of the fold) Where the quoted wordings go.** A Denial's `Summary` and `HowTo` are class-level static text, built by `newDenial(class)` and pinned per class (`src/mcp/tools/denial.go:44-49`, `denial_shape_test.go`). The quoted sentences above carry a request ID, so each is the call's per-instance message: the tool's returned error text, next to the Denial, as E10's executed ordinals are. Each class's static text must be true of every case the class covers. `expired_unexecuted` covers a K16 failure after earlier steps ran (E10) and an approved Claim that answered `expired` with nothing run, so its `Summary` says a signature window closed before a signed step could run and that `executed_ordinals` say what did run, and its `HowTo` says not to resubmit automatically and that a fresh request needs a new approval. `approval_pending` covers a still-pending request and an approved one the signer did not release in time, so its `Summary` says nothing ran and this call will not run it later, and its `HowTo` says not to resubmit and to ask the user. A13b pins both texts and asserts that each per-instance case returns its own sentence in the error.

**E27 — Idempotency key (§6.1; edited in r5).**
- *Evidence:* the signer assigns some plan fields (principal, `submitted_at`, expiry, transfer ID and legs; `xferID` is minted per call at `daemon.go:1129-1147`), so a retrying client cannot recompute `plan_hash`.
- *Resolution:*
  - Submit is idempotent on `{principal, request_id, draft_hash}`. On hosted the principal is `(client_id, sub_principal)` (E19). The MCP mints `request_id` once per tool call, for synchronous and async calls alike, and every hop carries it unchanged (§1.5 SA-2).
  - `draft_hash = SHA-256("sshgate-approval-draft-v1\0"‖canonical(draft))`. The draft contains only requester-supplied fields, including `executor_claim_token_hash`, `submit_not_after` and the route snapshots. It has no validity field (E26).
  - `plan_hash = SHA-256("sshgate-approval-plan-v1\0"‖canonical(plan))`, computed once at freeze, then stored and returned.
  - The same `draft_hash` returns the stored record, shed or not, whatever the time. A different one is a `conflict`. Because the owner scope is part of the storage key, a conflict can only come from the caller's own record and never says anything about another principal's.
  - The canonical encoding is struct-ordered JSON whose strict decode must re-encode byte-equal (repo convention: `src/policywire/wire.go:350-357`, `src/policy/sign.go:240-247`).

**E28 — Phase-aware SSH (§6.2, §11; edited in r5).**
- *Evidence:*
  - `sess.Run` hides the Start/Wait split (`src/mcp/ssh/client.go:162`).
  - `ExitMissingError` becomes exit −1 with a nil error (`:170-177`).
  - A ctx error after a known completion becomes an error (`:186-188`).
- *Resolution:* add a new `RunPhased` in a new file, `src/mcp/ssh/phased.go`. `Run`, `RunWithStdin` and their fakes stay unchanged. Its outcome classes:
  - **`failed_not_started`:** failures in key load, dial, handshake or `NewSession`, or an exec request the server rejects (`ssh: command failed`); (r5.3, critique pass 1; r5.7, review 8 #1) and, on route `unsigned_read` only, gate exit 77 with the `read jail unavailable` line (E10). `RunPhased` sees no route and returns that case as a known exit 77; jobplan's `StepOutcome` maps it. On every signed route, `signed_reveal` included, the same exit is `failed`, executed (E10).
  - **`indeterminate`:** a `Start` I/O error, `ExitMissingError`, or EOF, transport or ctx errors during Wait.
  - **known:** nil or `*ssh.ExitError`. A known completion wins over a later ctx error.
  - **(r5.4) `guard_refused`:** the pre-start guard (below) refused. No exec request was sent.
- **(r5.4) The pre-start guard.** The signature leaves for the target only at `Session.Start` (`x/crypto@v0.52.0/ssh/session.go:278`). Before that, today's transport loads the key, dials, handshakes and opens a session (`src/mcp/ssh/client.go:89,117,145`), and the dial and handshake alone may take up to `Client.Timeout`, 30 s by default (`:84-87`). So a K16 check made before `RunPhased` is called can pass while the window is spent inside it. `RunPhased` therefore takes a guard, `RunPhased(ctx, target, wireCmd, stdin io.Reader, preStart func() error)`. It performs key load, dial, handshake and `NewSession`, then calls `preStart` immediately before `Session.Start`, with nothing in between. A non-nil return closes the session and the connection, sends no exec request, and returns `PhasedError{Class: guard_refused, Phase: pre_start, Detail: "pre-start guard refused"}`. (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C2-1) **The guard is mandatory for a signed command, and `RunPhased` enforces it.** A nil `preStart` is allowed only for a wire command that carries no signature (an unsigned step inside a job; the `status` probe stays on `Run`, below). When `wireCmd` begins with `SSHGATE_SIG:` and `preStart` is nil, `RunPhased` loads no key, dials nothing and returns `PhasedError{Class: failed_not_started, Phase: pre_dial, Detail: "missing pre-start guard"}`, so a coordinator that forgets the guard fails closed instead of skipping K16. **Every signed coordinator passes the K16 re-check on its held pair (SA-1 rule 3.4) as the guard:** the jobplan runner for every signed step (run and run_batch, sync and executor), `revoke_server`, `update_gate`, and each `transfer` leg. Each transfer leg is judged against its own window from the Claim answer (`decided_at.Unix() + TTL` of that leg). For a step with two Starts (a transfer), the held set is dropped after the last leg's `Start`, never at the first (SA-1 rule 3.4). A RECV-leg guard refusal after the SEND leg ran records the RECV leg as `expired_unexecuted` with zero exec requests to the destination: SEND only reads the source file and writes the sealed envelope to stdout (`src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate/xfer.go:100`, `handleXferSend`), so the source is unchanged and nothing was transferred. Every guard refusal is recorded as `expired_unexecuted`, never as `failed_not_started`. The guard is a pure clock comparison: it performs no I/O and takes no store lock, so the step's marker, if any, is committed before `RunPhased` is called (§1.5 SA-5 step 6).
- Bounded capture follows E56. The executor commits the step marker before `Start` (§1.5 SA-5).
- (r5) r4's `Started` hook is withdrawn: it existed only as the cutpoint for progress reports, and r5 has none (E25).
- **Errors never carry the command (r4, kept).** `golang.org/x/crypto/ssh` `Session.Start` builds `fmt.Errorf("ssh: command %v failed", cmd)` when the server rejects an exec request (`~/go/pkg/mod/golang.org/x/crypto@v0.52.0/ssh/session.go:286-288`). For a signed step `cmd` is the whole wire command, `SSHGATE_SIG:<sig>:<payload> <command>` (`src/sigwire/payload.go:63,89`; built at `run.go:365` and `run_batch.go:221`). Today `run.go:376` and `run_batch.go:274` wrap that error with `%w` into the MCP error, so a rejected exec hands the agent a valid signature, which breaks invariant 3. `RunPhased` therefore never wraps or formats an underlying error. It returns a typed `PhasedError{Class, Phase, Detail}`, where `Detail` comes from a closed set of fixed strings (`exec request rejected`, `dial failed`, `handshake failed`, `new session failed`, `start i/o error`, `channel closed without exit status`, `transport error after start`, `context canceled`, and (r5.4) `pre-start guard refused` and `missing pre-start guard`). The raw error goes only to a debug log, and only after `sanitizeSSHError` has removed every occurrence of the wire command, every `SSHGATE_SIG:` token through the next space, and the payload's base64. `RunPhased(ctx, target, wireCmd, stdin io.Reader, preStart func() error)` takes an optional stdin and (r5.4) an optional pre-start guard (above). After A13b every signed dispatch goes through it: the jobplan runner on both the synchronous path (A13b) and the executor (C2), covering run, run_batch, revoke_server and update_gate (its binary on stdin, today `update_gate.go:185`), and both transfer legs (today `transfer.go:164,173`). The port therefore closes this pre-existing leak on every signed path. `Run` and `RunWithStdin` stay byte-unchanged and remain only for pure-read calls (today's path, A13b) and the `status` probe, which carry no signature; inside a job, reads also go through `RunPhased`.

**E29 — Executor states, step states and batch aggregation (§6.2, §11; rewritten in r5).**
- **Job state is a view.** `JobView(job, epoch)` (r5.3: it judges no deadline; §1.5 SA-5) derives it from the R2 record's slots: `submitted` (no `signer_ack`), (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-8) `acknowledged` (`signer_ack` set, and no marker, result or `outcome`: the signer holds the request, and the job record cannot tell pending from approved, because R2 journals no signer decision), `approved` (a marker or a result exists but the job is neither executing nor final; a first dispatch needs a successful Claim, so a marker proves approval), `executing{k}` (a marker for k from the current epoch with no result), and `final`. r5–r5.4 listed `awaiting` ("the signer's view is pending") and `approved` from the record, which stores neither. **Approval status for an `acknowledged` job comes from the signer, never from the job record:** `exec_get`, `exec_await` and `exec_list` report `execution_status = acknowledged` and no `approval_status`, and the MCP fills `approval_status` from the signer's Get, joined by `request_id` exactly as `list_pending_approvals` joins List (C3). A signer the MCP cannot reach leaves it "unknown (signer unavailable)", and (r5.6, critique of the fold) a Get still answered `busy` after one resend leaves it "unknown (signer busy — retry)" (E21, C3). (r5.1) `JobNext` consults `executing{k}` before choosing any work: a job in flight gets no action at all (§1.5 SA-5 step 3). A final job's class is the step-derived class below, or its `outcome` slot: `denied`, `canceled`, `expired`, `error`, `approval_lost` or `adopted{class}`. (r5.3, OD-3) `error` carries the code of the definitive signer answer that ended the job (§1.5 SA-5). A job that had already executed steps keeps their results, `executed_ordinals` and `partial`. A job file that fails validation is quarantined as `report_auth_lost` (§1.5 SA-8).
- **Step status:** `succeeded`; `failed` (a known non-zero exit); `skipped` (today's stop-policy skip after a known failure, `run_batch.go:254-257,285-297`); `not_run` (never attempted because the run aborted, or because an `outcome` ended the job); and four **abort statuses**, `failed_not_started`, `expired_unexecuted`, `stale_target` and `indeterminate`. Steps after the step that ended the job have no stored result; `JobView` derives them as `not_run` (after an abort status) or `skipped` (after a stop-policy stop). (r5.6, critique of the fold) **Every step of a final job has a status.** A job ended by its `outcome` slot has no ending step, so r5.6's rule above left its unrun steps with no status: a job whose step 1 ran and whose Claim before step 2 answered `record_corrupt` read `[succeeded]` and `partial: false`, against SA-5's "keeps … `partial`". Now, for a final job, `JobView` derives every step with no stored result: `skipped` when a stop-policy stop ended the job, otherwise `not_run` (after an abort status, or when an `outcome` ended it: `error`, `adopted`, `approval_lost` or a decision outcome). So that job reads `[succeeded, not_run]`, `executed_ordinals [1]`, `partial: true`, and a job ended by an outcome before any step ran reads all `not_run`, `partial: false`.
- A run aborts at the first abort status, and every later step is `not_run`. Today a transport error already ends the batch (`run_batch.go:269-274`), and E10 ends it on expiry. (r5.7, critique of the fold) The abort statuses include an `unsigned_read` step the read jail refused (`failed_not_started`, E10). Today that read's 77 is an ordinary non-zero read exit, which under the class default does not stop a mixed batch (`run_batch.go:285-297`), so the approved writes after it run; after this build they are `not_run`. That is a named change (§1.3 item 39): the read did not run, the host's gate needs `gate doctor`, and E29 keeps at most one abort step, the last attempted. A job therefore has at most one abort step, and it is the last step attempted. **The same `JobView` decides this live and in recovery** (R2.1): a stored result with an abort status ends the job whatever the stop policy says, and the job is never resumed.
- **Job class**, from `approvalplan.AggregateJobClass(steps)`, which jobplan, the executor, the sync coordinator and the signer's report check (E25) all use:
  1. the abort step's status, if there is one;
  2. otherwise `failed`, if any step `failed`;
  3. otherwise `succeeded`.
  - Uncertainty and unexecuted approved work therefore always win over an earlier known failure (R1.5): under the class default a failed read in a mixed batch (one that ran and exited non-zero, status `failed`) continues, and `stop_on_error:false` continues past any non-zero exit (`run_batch.go:285-297`), so a later write that lost its connection after invocation must be reported `indeterminate`, not `failed`.
- **Partial execution** travels with the class: `executed_ordinals` (the steps whose status is `succeeded`, `failed` or `indeterminate`, meaning their remote command ran or may have run; never `failed_not_started`, including an exec request rejected inside `Start`) and `partial` (some step executed and some approved step did not). The MCP output, the report (E38) and the cards show both, and the Denial message lists the executed and failed ordinals.
  - **(r5.6, review 7 #3) One partition and one function.** Every step status is in exactly one of two sets. *Executed* (ran or may have run): `succeeded`, `failed`, `indeterminate`. *Not executed* (established as not run): `failed_not_started`, `expired_unexecuted`, `stale_target`, `not_run`, `skipped`. Over the job's full step list (the stored results, plus `JobView`'s derived `not_run` or `skipped` for every step without one, as above; for a report, its one entry per ordinal, E38), `approvalplan.ExecutedOrdinals(steps)` is the ordinals in the executed set, ascending, and `approvalplan.Partial(steps)` is true exactly when both sets are non-empty. An `indeterminate` step counts as executed, the conservative reading for "may have run", so `[failed read, indeterminate write]` as the whole job is `indeterminate` with `executed_ordinals [1, 2]` and `partial: false`; `[failed read, indeterminate write, not_run]` is `indeterminate` with `executed_ordinals [1, 2]` and `partial: true`; a stop-policy stop `[failed, skipped]` is `failed` with `partial: true`. r5–r5.5's A13a fixture expected `partial` for the two-step case, which contradicted this definition. These two functions are the only definition: the jobplan runner's output, `JobView` live and in recovery, the executor's report body, the synchronous coordinator's report and output, the MCP output fields (E20) and the cards all compute them, and the signer's report acceptance requires the report's `executed_ordinals` and `partial` to equal them over the report's steps, else `invalid_report` (E25, §1.5 SA-4 Report). (r5.6, critique of the fold) That equality means the same thing on both sides only because a report must list all `step_count` ordinals (E38) in E29's shape; a report that left out its trailing `not_run` or `skipped` steps is refused, so the signer never stores a `partial` that disagrees with `JobView`.
- `stale_transfer_key` is the mint slot's error reason (E34), surfaced as `materialization_error`.
- Each signature has its own `expires_at`.
- §11 gains a stale_target row and the E26 row.

**E30 — `stop_on_error` (§5, §7.1).**
- *Evidence:* `RunBatchInput.StopOnError` is a three-state `*bool` (`run_batch.go:31,237-241,294`).
- *Resolution:* the plan stores `stop_policy ∈ {class_default_writes_only, any_nonzero, never}` plus a resolved, positive `max_output_bytes`.
  - Async rejects 0.
  - Sync keeps `max_output_bytes: 0` as unlimited (`outputcap.go:54-59`).
- Both values are covered by both hashes.

**E31 — Request IDs and table separation (edited in r5).**
- The v2 grammar is `^a2_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{22}$`: `a2_` plus base64url of 16 random bytes.
- **The MCP mints it, once per tool call, for synchronous and async calls alike** (§1.5 SA-2). The executor keys its job on it and never mints one; the signer and the proxy carry it unchanged.
- This is distinct from v1 `r_…` IDs (`run.go:541-548`; hosted `util.go`).
- v2 records never reach `/v1/poll`, `/v1/audit`, `requests`, `UpdateStatus` or `storetest`.

**E32 — Kinds hosted v2 accepts (K13, §5).**
- `/v2/approvals` accepts `command_job` with routes `unsigned_read`, `signed_write` and `signed_admin`, so hosted update and revoke keep working.
- It rejects `signed_reveal`, `standing_grant`, `transfer` and `register_transfer_key` with typed fail-closed errors, as v1 does today (`hosted.go:146-150,356-384`).
- With `backend=hosted`, the local daemon proxies the v2 kinds (A12).
- The hosted HTTP `WriteTimeout` of 90s (signer-server `main.go:355-356`) fits a 55s wait.

**E33 — Admin steps (§5 `signed_admin`).**
- An admin step carries `trusted_display`, generated by the MCP and never by the agent, and kept apart from the rationale:
  - update: `{staged_basename, staged_version, running_version, size}`;
  - revoke: no `trusted_display`.
- **The signer does not trust `trusted_display`.** The MCP runs as the agent's uid, and any `sshgatesigner`-group process can submit it (`update_gate.go:159` builds the v1 equivalent on the MCP side). So:
  - the new-binary SHA-256 is never taken from the draft. The renderer derives it from the exact signed `SSHGATE_UPDATE <sha>` bytes, as today (`telegram.go:1003-1009`), and a malformed hash renders the existing single-line marker. If a draft still carries a `binary_sha256`, freeze refuses it with `invalid_request` unless it equals the hash in the command bytes;
  - every `trusted_display` field is labelled **"Build metadata (client-supplied, signer-unverified)"** on both surfaces, matching E8c's v1 label.
- The lone-admin-verb rule (`daemon.go:578-597`) is enforced when the plan freezes, on command bytes, by E71's validator.
- Step TTLs come from the originating tool: revoke 60 (`revoke_server.go:34`) and update 300 (`update_gate.go:48`).

**E34 — Transfer freeze (§5; edited in r5).**
- Add `XferRegistry.Snapshot(fp)` returning `{label, box_pub, id_pub, added_at, entry_digest}` (`xferregistry.go`; today only `Lookup` `:117` and `Register` `:142` exist).
- Submit freezes both snapshots and the exact legs into the plan. The legs are already built before notification (`daemon.go:1117-1147`).
- The first Claim re-verifies both digests inside its mint commit (E21). A mismatch writes mint `{error: stale_transfer_key}` and answers `materialization_error`. The legs are never rebuilt from current keys.
- Paths and fingerprints are labelled "requester-supplied, signer-validated", not "signer_trusted".

**E35 — Registry digest (§5).**
- `approvalplan.RouteSnapshot` uses the spec §5 JSON names: `{server_alias, resolved_host, resolved_port, resolved_user, trusted_host_fingerprint, read_only, added_at}`. `registry_entry_digest` is the step field that holds its digest. The digest covers every field, so a tier flip or a re-add counts as drift.
- **The route is requester-supplied.** The signer never reads `servers.json`, which only the MCP and CLI read (`src/mcp/registry`, `src/cli`). The v1 host is taken from the request (`daemon.go:575`). "Trusted" in the spec name means trusted by the MCP's registry, not verified by the signer. The approvalplan doc comment says so, and both surfaces label the route line **"Route (requester's registry; signer-unverified)"**. The signature's host binding is enforced by the target's gate. The label stops an agent from passing a production fingerprint off as "staging". D3 states this in THREAT-MODEL.
- A signed step whose entry has an empty fingerprint is refused before any prompt; the gate would reject it anyway (`src/gate/verify.go:107`).
- The digest only detects drift after approval. `servers.json`, owned by the operator uid, stays the source of trust.

**E36 — Assess contract (§8).**
- Provider input is JSON-encoded. Today commands are joined with `'%d. %s\n'` (`explainer.go:158-162`), so a newline inside a command can forge a list entry.
- The upstream body is read through a 64 KiB LimitReader (today it is 1 MiB, `explainer.go:140`).
- The watchdog is `min(timeout_sec, 5s)`, with a startup warning when `timeout_sec > 5`.
- The persisted result adds `InputDigest` and `GeneratedAt` (K5).
- Legacy records get `alignment: "not_assessed"`, rendered "not assessed (legacy request carries no agent reason)". (r5.1) This applies to local legacy v1 requests, which run one assist attempt before their card (E8b). Hosted v1 rows have no assist, as today: hosted has no explainer now, and adding one would need a column on the frozen `requests` table (§1.3 item 31).

**E37 — "Same snapshot on both surfaces" (§8.1, §13).**
- *Evidence:* no single request is ever reviewed on both surfaces, because `backend.type` is exclusive (signer `main.go:371-376`).
- *Resolution:*
  - At freeze, each plane persists a `ReviewSnapshot` (redacted reason, per-command display segments, spans and byte counts) and the `AssistResult`.
  - The per-request random salt is used only during freeze and then zeroed. It is **never persisted or serialized**: the field is `json:"-"` and is not in the journal, the DB, any view model, Telegram text or the provider body. Rendering needs only the stored segments, so no marker key can later be matched offline against guessed secrets (`MarkerKey` = HMAC(salt, secret)[:4], `src/redact/markers.go:70-78`).
  - Both surfaces render only stored snapshots, and that rendering is byte-stable across a restart.
  - Ledger wording: "Each surface renders only its stored snapshot; both renderers are golden-tested against one shared plan + snapshot + AssistResult fixture."
  - Hosted gains a redactor (`Combined()` plus a per-request salt) and optional assist provider flags. Both land in **A10**, because hosted Submit builds the snapshot at freeze. A11 only renders it.

**E38 — Report payload (§7.4; edited in r5).**
- `ReportExecution` carries one final report with `report_sequence` 1 (E25): per-step `{ordinal, status, exit_code, stdout_bytes, stderr_bytes, truncated}`, the job class, `executed_ordinals`, `partial` and `result_digest`. Its body is a canonical function of the reporter's durable record, so a resend after a lost acknowledgement is an exact duplicate (STATE-MODEL F-1). (r5.1) `result_digest` is computed from the per-step `stdout_sha256`/`stderr_sha256` fields that each step result stores when it commits (§1.5 SA-3 R2), never from the bodies, so removing bodies at 7 d cannot change the bytes of an unacknowledged report that is resent later.
- **(r5.6, critique of the fold) The report lists every step.** The per-step list holds exactly `step_count` entries, ordinals 1..`step_count` in ascending order: E29's full step list. A step with a stored result carries it; a step without one carries `JobView`'s derived status (`not_run` or `skipped`, E29) with no exit code, byte counts or `truncated` (omitted, not zero). The synchronous coordinator builds the same list from its runner's output. So the reporter's `executed_ordinals` and `partial`, computed over the list it sends, are the values `JobView` and the MCP output show, and the signer recomputes them over the same list (E25).
- It never carries output bodies.
- Surfaces label the result "client-reported".

**E39 — Human CLI origin (§5).**
- `rationale_origin` comes from the request kind, never from JSON. Legacy `register_xfer_key` maps to `trusted_human_cli`.
- The program-generated reason is "Transfer-key enrollment requested via sshgate CLI", labelled **"Program-generated reason (sshgate CLI; requester not authenticated as human)"**.
- v0.2 has no v2 register kind. The only caller (`src/cli/cmd/sshgate/xfer.go:45-47,129`) stays on v1.

**E40 — Façade placement (§7.4).**
- The local façade is the package `pkg/signerkit/lifecycle`. The hosted implementation lives in `pkg/signerkit/hosted`.
- `Service` and `Backend` stay unchanged.
- Carve S2 (`service.go:57-64`) stays open.

**E41 — Durability details (edited in r5).**
- Pin `_pragma=synchronous(FULL)` in the sqlitestore DSN (`sqlite.go:192-195`). Today it is FULL only because of the modernc compile default. The DSN already sets `_txlock=immediate`, so every v2 transaction is `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`.
- Local card entries store only challenge hashes. Hosted stores no challenge at all (MAC, E7).

**E42 — Naming.** Frozen hosted-v1 identifiers already say "V2" (`signRequestCmdV2`, `hosted.go:80-95`). New code uses the names approvalplan, approvalwire and `ApprovalPlanV1`. A12 adds a one-line glossary comment above those identifiers.

**E43 — No action needed.** These §5–§8 claims hold at `fb515f9`:
- mixed batches send only writes to the signer (`run_batch.go:160-221`);
- transfer legs are built before notification;
- hosted v1 server-mints its IDs;
- the socket handler receives an `io.ReadWriter`;
- the explainer receives raw input;
- sync output can be unlimited;
- the MCP registers 11 tools;
- the gate allows `exp-ts == 300` (`src/gate/verify.go:90`).

**E44 — Phase restructure (§12).**
- *Evidence:* spec Phase A cannot carry `reason` to the signer without a new wire. The v1 decoders are strict (`daemon.go:522-528,778-783`), `ProtoVersion` is exact-match (`src/sigwire/protocol.go:20`), and K13 forbids extending v1.
- *Resolution:* the v2 carrier, lifecycle core, challenges, renderer and hosted v2 plane move into Phase A, so the synchronous #76 path is complete on both planes when A ends.
  - B is local durability and recovery.
  - C is the executor and async.
  - D is operator queue reliability.
- Local expiry lands in A6 and hosted expiry in A10, which resolves the spec's double claim of B2 and D2.
- The 13-tool docs and manifest land in the same commit as the count-guard change (C3).
- The mapping table is §6.

**E45 — v1 display bounds (§9.1).**
- v1 records use the shared renderer, showing only the fields v1 carries: alias, fingerprint if present, TTL, original-byte SHA-256 and the legacy label.
- On Telegram they go multipart up to the policy bounds (3900-byte parts, ≤40 messages, ≤128 KiB). Beyond those bounds the request terminates as `error`, which replaces today's opaque Send failure (`telegram.go:563-571`).
- The K19 intake bounds stay v2-only.
- Hosted renders every v1 command.

**E46 — §9.1 additions.**
- Add the K16 warning: "Step k is signed and runs after earlier work; its signature may expire before it starts."
- Add the exact K20 label.
- The kind-specific approve labels from `telegram.go:544-555` and `:638` become variants of "Approve all displayed commands".

**E47 — Telegram callback data must be ≤64 bytes (§9.2).**
- Callbacks carry a signer-minted, 12-character base32 card handle instead of the request ID:
  - `v:a:<handle>:<16hex>` (approve), `v:d:…` (deny), `v:x:<snapshot>:<16hex>` (confirm deny-all or cancel);
  - every form is ≤35 bytes.
- The `p:` and `approve:`/`deny:` namespaces stay untouched.

**E48 — Ready-edit challenge rotation (K6, K15; rewritten in r5).**
- When the assist slot fills (ready or unavailable, revision 1) after a card exists, the record's notifier actor appends an `edit` card entry at revision 1 with a fresh challenge allowing `{approve, deny}`, then calls `editMessageText` (§1.5 SA-4, notify step 3). (r5.3) At `assist_deadline` the View reaches revision 1 only once Clock commits the `unavailable` slot (on the tick, or first in any command), and the actor treats that the same way. (r5.1) That snapshot is a pure function of the header (E68), so the edit and any vote bound to it carry the slot's one digest (K8).
- From the moment the edit entry is appended, the revision-0 challenge is no longer the latest entry, so a tap carrying it gets "card updated — tap again" and changes nothing. The new challenge becomes valid only once the edit is confirmed (§1.5 SA-6.1). No vote is possible between those two commits. **(r5.3, OA-5)** Nor before the append: once the assist slot has committed, the revision-0 challenge is refused because its entry's revision is below the View's (SA-6.1), so the interval with no valid challenge starts at the slot's commit, not at the edit entry's append.
- Edits are idempotent ("message is not modified" counts as success). Within the epoch that appended the entry, the record's actor re-runs the edit until it is confirmed, using the raw challenge it still holds in memory. (r5.5, critique of the fold) After a possible send the re-run is driven by §1.5 SA-4 step 3's in-memory retry (1 s doubling to 30 s, while the entry is the latest unretired one, unconfirmed, and the record pending). A known edit failure leads to a replacement card (spec §9.2), sent like a first card.
- **(r5.2) After a restart, an unconfirmed edit is superseded, not replayed.** The entry stores only `chal_hash` (E41), and the callback data `v:a:<handle>:<16hex>` carries the raw challenge, so the edit's bytes cannot be rebuilt once process memory is gone. r5.1's "re-run after a restart" was unimplementable. An edit entry from another epoch with no `confirmed` is therefore handled in one commit: its write-once `retired` field is set, and a fresh `edit` entry for the same card message is appended at the View's revision with a new handle and challenge.
