3. **Every other state is a pure view,** `View(rec, now, epoch, grants)` or (r5.3) `JobView(job, epoch)`, never stored. Get, List, Wait, `list_grants`, `exec_get`, `exec_await`, `exec_list` and the hosted detail GET never write. `grants` is the grant projection as its single writer last left it (3.4). **(r5.3) A view derives no closure from time.** Expiry, assist `unavailable`, a closed claim window and erased signature bytes appear in a view only once they are write-once slots, written by Clock (3.3). `now` feeds only the provisional report horizon (`unreported`, `awaiting_report`) and ages, which close nothing.

   **(r5.3) Time.** r5.1 and r5.2 kept time in a memory-only high-water mark, and four review rounds each found an event it missed (`STATE-REDESIGN-NEEDED.md`, defects D1–D8). r5.3 keeps time in committed facts and in held deadlines instead (§12.8).
   - Every signature carries its own absolute window: `TS = decided_at` and `Exp = decided_at + TTL_k`, both in whole seconds ((r5.3, critique pass 1) `ts := approvedAt.Unix()`, `pkg/signerkit/hosted_sign.go:65`), with `TTL_k ≤ 300 s`. Hosted signs with `SignApproved(cmds, approvedAt)` (`pkg/signerkit/hosted_sign.go:56`).
   - The target's gate refuses a signature once its own clock reaches `Exp` (`src/gate/verify.go:82-84`; exit 65 through `gate.VerifySigned`'s caller, `src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate/main.go:216-225` at `366ba07`, `:213-222` at `4314626`).
   - So a signer-side clock fault moves a signature's window only together with its `decided_at`. The rules below keep the signer side consistent with itself. (r5.3, critique pass 1) No clock reading changes an epoch, interrupts an actor or refuses de-escalation (Revoke, AdminCancel, deny-all); the few refusals that do rest on a clock reading are listed once, in 3.1.
   - **Withdrawn** (D1–D8): r5.1 and r5.2's high-water mark, `t = max(wall, mark)`, `clock_regressed`, the 2 s to 5 min band, the clock break, the mark carried across a reopen, the per-record Startup floor and the executor's K16 floor.
   - Five rules replace them, 3.1 to 3.5. Each names its holders.

   **3.1 Stamps are one wall-clock reading per evaluation.** *Holders: local signer, hosted, executor, MCP sync coordinator.*
   - Each command, read and tick reads the wall clock once, inside its critical section when it has one (under the store lock; on hosted, inside `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`). It uses that `now` for every stamp, Clock pass and guard in that evaluation.
   - The stamps:
     - on R1: `created_at`, a vote's `ts`, `decided_at` (set by a deciding vote, a grant-covered Submit, AdminCancel, deny-all, or a notify-step `error`), `revoked_at` and a burst's `started_at`;
     - on R2: `created_at`, a marker's `at`, `first_delivered_at` and (r5.4) `final_at`;
     - so no stamp exceeds the wall clock at its commit.
   - Clock writes no stamp. An expired decision copies `decided_at = request_expires_at`, and the synthesized assist snapshot copies `generated_at = assist_deadline` (E68).
   - No rule compares two stamps. No validator, renderer or hosted query requires them to be in order: after a step back, a vote's `ts` may come before `created_at`.
   - No clock reading changes an epoch, cancels or joins an actor, yields `uncertain`, or refuses Revoke, AdminCancel or deny-all.
   - (r5.3, critique pass 1) **The refusals that rest on a clock reading are exactly these;** no other command answers a clock error:
     - 3.2's release check: a first Claim's `materialization_error{clock_skew}`, or a repeat Claim's Erase and `expired`;
     - 3.5's submit window: `submit_window_closed`, and `submit_window_ahead` when the value is too far ahead;
     - Clock's committed facts (3.3): a Vote after `request_expires_at` (`vote_refused`), a Claim past `claim_end` (`expired`), a late AssistResult (dropped);
     - OA-5's stale view (SA-6.1): a tap, a Deny included, on a card entry whose revision is below the View's after Clock, until the actor's edit entry is confirmed; on hosted, a vote POST whose MAC-bound revision is below the row's after Clock, until the page is reloaded. Clock's assist fill at `assist_deadline` raises the revision exactly as a provider result does. AdminCancel and deny-all stay challenge-free, so de-escalation always has a path no clock reading refuses.
   - An AdminCancel, deny-all item or Deny on a record Clock has just expired answers "already resolved": the record is already terminal, so nothing is withheld.
   - (r5.3, critique pass 1) **No process-start time gate.** E64's filter for slash commands redelivered after a restart is the Telegram update stream (an `update_id` cut), not a comparison of `Message.Date` with the signer's start time (STATE-MODEL M14).
   - Clock observations are reported only, through `status` (E59):
     - (r5.3, critique pass 1) approved records with an open window whose `decided_at` is more than 5 s ahead of the wall clock, found at Startup (3.2's threshold; r5.2's 5 min was the withdrawn band's), and every `clock_skew` mint slot committed since;
     - wall steps the tick sees, meaning a wall delta that differs from the elapsed delta (3.4) by more than 2 s, reported as the count, the largest and the time of the last one.

   **3.2 No future-dated signature leaves the signer.** *Holders: local signer, hosted.*
   - Signature bytes leave only while `decided_at ≤ now + 5 s`, judged after Clock, in the Claim's own commit. This holds for every Claim, first or repeat.
   - A first Claim that fails this writes mint `{error: clock_skew}` and answers `materialization_error`.
   - A repeat Claim that fails it commits the Erase and answers `expired`.
   - Hosted mints before its Claim transaction (E21) and applies this check inside the transaction. A set that fails is discarded.

   **3.3 Only Clock turns time into state, and every R1 command applies it first.** *Holders: local signer, hosted.*
   - **One window function (OS-2).** For an approved record with `execution_applicable = true`:
     - step k's window ends at `decided_at.Unix() + TTL_k`, which is exactly its gate `Exp`. (r5.3, critique pass 1) The end is in whole seconds because the minted TS is `decided_at` truncated to the second (`pkg/signerkit/hosted_sign.go:65`) and the gate refuses once `nowUnix ≥ Exp` (`src/gate/verify.go:82-84`); a fractional `decided_at` would otherwise put `claim_end` and the holder's W up to 1 s after the gate's `Exp`;
     - (r5.3, critique pass 1) windows exist only for **signed** steps. Spec §5 gives `unsigned_read` a TTL of zero (a shed header still carries it), so a window over every step would make `first_end = decided_at` for any mixed job and close it before its first Claim. `first_end` and `last_end` are the earliest and the latest end over the signed steps. An unsigned step has no window and never bounds a Claim (E74). Freeze refuses a `command_job` with no signed step (`invalid_request`); jobplan never submits one, because an all-read batch runs without approval (`src/mcp/tools/run_batch.go:235`);
     - `last_window_end`, where this plan still uses it, means `last_end`;
     - `claim_end` is `first_end` while `claim_count = 0`, and `last_end` afterwards;
     - for `coordinator = in_process_sync`, `claim_end` is never later than `sync_claim_deadline = created_at + 310 s`;
     - Claim, Clock, SA-6.2, SA-6.5 and SA-9 read `claim_end`, or the slots Clock fills. The report horizon reads `last_end`. A holder's K16 reads step k's own end (3.4).
   - **`Clock(rec, now)`** is pure and lives in `approvalplan`. It fills, in this order, exactly the empty slots whose deadline is at or before `now`:
     1. assist `{unavailable, "assist deadline"}`, the header-derived snapshot of E68, when the slot is empty and `now ≥ assist_deadline`;
     2. decision `{expired, decided_at = request_expires_at, decided_by = clock, epoch, assist_bound}`, where `assist_bound` is the assist slot after step 1, when the slot is empty and `now ≥ request_expires_at`;
     3. for an approved, execution-applicable record with `now ≥ claim_end`: mint `{error: window_closed}` when `claim_count = 0` and the mint slot is empty; otherwise the Erase of any signature bytes still held.

     Clock runs only at the `now` of the evaluation that commits it. It never runs at a stored stamp, a remembered value or any other instant, and that includes Startup and after a reopen.
   - **Clock first.**
     - Every R1 command except Delete begins, after the owner-scoped load (SA-6.6), with `Clock(rec, now)`. It then judges its guards and its answer on the result.
     - `Apply(rec, cmd, now, epoch)` is `cmd` applied to `Clock(rec, now)`. Clock's additions commit with the command's own writes, in its one commit. When neither adds anything, there is no commit.
     - Delete acts only on a settled, fully audited record. (r5.3, critique pass 1) It applies no Clock fill and judges only its own 3.5 ages (SA-9: `submit_not_after + 24 h` and the age limit). It is the one command that skips Clock.
     - The tick applies Clock alone: locally, each second and at each record's `next_due_at`; on hosted, in the worker each second over `DueV2(now)`; at Startup, through `Next` on every record.
     - (r5.3, critique pass 1) **`next_due_at`** is the earliest future instant at which `Next` has time-driven work for the record: `assist_deadline` while the assist slot is empty; `request_expires_at` while the decision slot is empty; `claim_end` while the mint slot is empty **or still holds bytes** (the Erase); `last_end + report_horizon` while a claimed record has no final report; and the Shed and Delete eligibility ages (SA-9; (r5.4) `decided_at + retention` and `max(submit_not_after + 24 h, decided_at + age limit)`, while the record is settled and not yet shed or deleted). A1b's `Next` and A9's column use this one definition. So a claimed, unreported record leaves `live` at `claim_end` on the tick alone, and the hosted worker schedules Shed and Delete as well. **(r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-3) A due pass that commits nothing still advances `next_due_at`.** At `last_end + report_horizon`, and at an age that a guard (settled, fully audited) does not yet let act, `Next` has no slot to fill, so no record commit runs. Locally the tick then recomputes the record's entry in the in-memory due index. On hosted the worker, inside the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, rewrites only the cache columns `class`, `view_status` and `next_due_at` when their values computed at `now` differ from the stored ones. That is a cache write, not a record commit: `record_json` is untouched, nothing is appended, and no decision reads the columns (rule 1). Without it a row whose horizon had passed kept a past `next_due_at` and was re-selected by `DueV2(now)` on every pass until some later commit. **(r5.5) An unflushed outbox is not a `next_due_at` term:** nothing about it is time-driven. SA-7's retry schedule drives it, each second and independently of `DueV2` and the due index, so a failed Flush never waits for the record's next deadline (often `claim_end`), and flush retries never delay Clock work.
     - A Submit that finds a live cap full first applies Clock to the records whose `next_due_at` has passed, before it counts. Each is its own one-record commit; on hosted, each runs in an earlier transaction. This is how Submit already runs Shed and Delete for the history caps (SA-9).
   - **No guard compares a Clock deadline** ((r5.3, critique pass 1) the 3.5 submit window and 3.2's release check are the stated exceptions). Vote, AdminCancel, deny-all items and the notify steps see expiry only as a filled decision slot. AssistResult sees a filled assist slot. Claim sees `window_closed` or erased bytes. So:
     - a Vote after `request_expires_at` commits `expired` and is refused (`vote_refused`) in that one commit;
     - AdminCancel or a Deny on such a record answers "already resolved";
     - a Claim past `claim_end` commits `window_closed` or the Erase and answers `expired`;
     - a late AssistResult is dropped.

     Every time-based refusal is a committed write-once fact before its answer or any effect leaves. An uncertain commit answers `storage_uncertain` and shows nothing (SA-8).
   - **Views read time only from slots.** Status is the decision slot, otherwise `pending`. Assist is the slot, otherwise `preparing` at revision 0. A window is open while the mint slot is empty or still holds bytes. `expired_unclaimed` is the `window_closed` slot. Class `live` follows the same slots and grant-projection membership (SA-6.5).
   - The executor has no Clock. Its only time refusals are K16 (3.4) and the submit window (3.5). `JobView` judges no deadline.

   **3.4 Authority held in memory ends at the first of two clocks, is measured from when it was acquired, and stays ended.** *Holders: local signer (v2 grants); executor and MCP sync coordinator (claimed signature sets and the sync wait budget).*
   - **Two clocks.** The holder uses the wall clock and an elapsed clock E from `internal/elapsed` (§2).
     - On Linux, E is `CLOCK_BOOTTIME`, read with `unix.ClockGettime` from `golang.org/x/sys/unix` (x/sys v0.45.0, already in `go.mod`; `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` at `unix/zerrors_linux.go:736`; `go list -deps` shows `x/sys/unix` in sshgate-mcp, signer-telegram, signer-server and `internal/securestate`, so in every holder's closure). It never steps and it counts suspend.
     - Elsewhere, E is Go's monotonic reading.
     - On Linux, Go's own monotonic reading is `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` (Go 1.27.1 `runtime/sys_linux_amd64.s:270`), which stops during suspend (`time/time.go:54`). It is therefore not used for E there.
     - Every holder reads both clocks through one injectable interface, whose two halves tests move independently. No holder compares a deadline using `time.Now`, `time.Since` or `time.Until` directly. A static test enforces this (A1b, A6, A13a, A13b, C2), because a monotonic reading lost through `Round(0)`, JSON or a time built from Unix seconds is invisible to `NowFunc` tests (§1.4 item 6).
   - **Acquire once.** At acquisition the holder reads both clocks and fixes:
     - `wall_end` W;
     - `elapsed_end = E_acq + max(0, min(nominal, W − wall_acq))`.

     Within one process each deadline is acquired once. A later answer that carries the same window never re-acquires it.
   - **Passed** means `wall ≥ W` or `E ≥ elapsed_end`, after any margin. The holder's single driver records the first finding, and that finding never reverses.
   - **Grant G.**
     - Acquired when the commit that decides G approved in the current epoch is confirmed. Nominal is `duration_seconds`, and W = `decided_at + duration_seconds`.
     - The grant projection's writer removes G at the first finding: on the tick, inside a Submit's grant match, and inside a grant-decided first Claim's liveness check. It never adds G back in that epoch.
     - Liveness (SA-6.4) is projection membership. Reads and `list_grants` show membership; they may trail the tick, but never lead it.
     - No grant is acquired from loaded records, because every grant from an earlier epoch is dead.
     - `grant_expires_at` is still reported as `decided_at + duration_seconds`.
   - **Claimed set, step k.**
     - Acquired once per step in each process: at the first Claim this process sends whose answer carries step k's window (in practice its first successful Claim, whose answer carries every step's window); both clocks are read just before that Claim is sent. (r5.3, critique pass 1) A repeat Claim before a later step (SA-5 step 6) never re-acquires: re-acquiring after a wall step back would compute `elapsed_end` from the lowered wall reading and stretch the elapsed half, which is D4 again. Only a new process acquires afresh (R-4). Nominal is `TTL_k`, and W = `decided_at.Unix() + TTL_k` (3.3), taken from the answer.
     - K16 (E10) passes while `wall + 2 s < W` and `E + 2 s < elapsed_end`.
     - K16 is judged only for the step that `JobNext` (SA-5 step 6) or the sync runner is about to dispatch: the first step with no result, and only when that step is signed. It is checked before the marker and again just before `Start`; (r5.4) the second check is `RunPhased`'s pre-start guard, after key load, dial, handshake and session setup and immediately before `Session.Start` (E28).
     - A failure commits result(k) = `expired_unexecuted` (executor), or ends the sync run with that status.
     - The holder drops the set and its deadlines at the first of: the last signed step's `Start` ((r5.4) for a transfer, the RECV leg's, the last of its two Starts; E28), a K16 failure, the job or run becoming final, or process exit.
   - **Sync wait budget (E26).** Acquired when Submit returns. Nominal is 300 s (`Runner.SyncWaitBudget`), and W = `sync_claim_deadline − 5 s`.
   - (r5.3, critique pass 1) **Timers only wake a driver.** Go's timers, tickers and context deadlines run on the runtime's `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` (Go 1.27.1 `runtime/sys_linux_amd64.s:270`), not on E, and stop during a suspend. So no holder ends authority on a timer: a timer (the K16 re-check, the sync budget's Wait loop, the grant tick) only wakes the driver, which then judges the held (wall, elapsed) pair. Timers that confer no authority use Go's timers as they are: deny-all and cancel snapshots, `WaitMany`, the 65 s `exec_submit` reply, backoffs and the assist watchdog (A4 arms it from the wall `assist_deadline`; a suspend or a step only moves when it fires, and Clock writes `unavailable` at the wall deadline whatever the watchdog does). The static test also flags `time.After`, `time.AfterFunc`, `time.NewTimer`, `time.NewTicker`, `time.Tick`, `context.WithTimeout` and `context.WithDeadline` in holder packages outside named wake-up sites (A1a).

   **3.5 Submit windows and ages are judged at the wall clock, inside the commit that acts on them.** *Holders: local signer, hosted, executor.*
   - A new id is accepted only when `now ≤ submit_not_after ≤ now + 3600 s +` the allowance. The allowance is 5 s on the local signer and the executor, and 300 s on hosted (E73). Otherwise the answer is `submit_window_closed`, or, when the value is too far ahead, (r5.3, critique pass 1) `submit_window_ahead` from the signer (local and hosted). That code is transient: a step back after the caller fixed `submit_not_after` causes it, and the same Submit is accepted once the wall clock re-advances past `submit_not_after − 3600 s −` the allowance, at most the size of the step back later; `JobNext` retries it (SA-5's split). r5.3 answered `invalid_request`, which SA-5 calls definitive, so a resubmit after a 60 s step back ended the job for good. The executor's own `exec_submit` check keeps `invalid_request` for this case, because its caller is the MCP call that fixed the value moments before on the same host. An existing id is returned whatever the time.
   - Ages are judged at `now` inside the commit that removes something: Shed and Delete ((r5.4) counted from `decided_at`, SA-9), the executor's removal of bodies, tokens and job files (SA-9; (r5.4) its ages count from the job's `final_at`), and the report horizon. None of them grants authority, and none is undone when the clock moves back.

   **(r5.3) Residuals, stated only here.** §1.3 item 30, SA-11, SA-12 K12, E73 and Appendix L cite this list.
   - **R-1 Stretch and early end.**
     - *Step back.* A wall step back of X, while the owner runs or while it is stopped, extends by up to X every deadline Clock has not yet passed:
       - pending validity;
       - the assist deadline of a record whose attempt died with its process (a live attempt still ends on its watchdog; (r5.3, critique pass 1) that watchdog runs on Go's `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, not E, which is harmless because it confers no authority, 3.4);
       - an open claim window;
       - hosted challenge `expires_at`;
       - the report horizon, so `unreported` and `awaiting_report` may revert to `executing`;
       - retention ages.
     - *Stamp-derived deadlines.* `request_expires_at`, `assist_deadline`, `sync_claim_deadline` and the windows carry whatever error the clock had at the stamp they come from. That is the same case.
     - *What a step back never does.* Nothing Clock has committed reopens. Held authority (3.4) is never extended. No signature's `Exp` moves, and 3.2 refuses to release a set once the step back exceeds the time since its decision plus 5 s.
     - *Forward step.* A forward step of J ends the same deadlines up to J early, permanently: pending requests expire, and windows close; grants and held sets end by their wall half; retention judged inside the jump is not undone. A jump of 7 d or more can remove undelivered executor bodies; `exec_await` then answers `result_expired` and never re-runs the job. Both directions fail closed.
     - *Downtime.* Time that passed while the owner was down is just one more step. If the clock moved forward, the first Clock applies it; if it moved back, it stretches.
     - *What this gives up.* r5.2's "no stretch for steps under 5 min" is given up.
   - **R-2 Ids no store holds (D8).**
     - These are ids that were never admitted, or that both stores have deleted. The signer deletes a record when it is settled, audited, at least `submit_not_after + 24 h` old, and past its age limit or under count pressure. The executor deletes under SA-9's predicate.
     - If the wall clock later reads at or before such an id's `submit_not_after`, its exact bytes are accepted as a new request. That needs a step back of more than a day after the Delete, or the correction of a forward excursion that drove the Delete.
     - The new request needs a fresh tap, or a live grant of the same principal, which would cover fresh bytes just as well. It gets fresh windows, which the gate judges.
     - Only a same-uid holder of the bytes can do this.
     - E73's "no hop can turn an old request into a new approval" holds while either store holds the id.
     - Closing this would take a durable per-store floor: the largest deleted `submit_not_after`, committed before each Delete (Proposal 1, §12.8). Not taken; it is the owner's call (§11.2).
   - **R-3 Read lag.**
     - Get, List, Wait, cards, `list_grants` and the hosted detail page can show `pending`, `preparing`, an open window or a live grant for up to one tick after the deadline. The lag is longer while the store is unavailable.
     - Nothing acts on that stale view, because every command applies Clock first.
     - There is no restart residual. Views show only committed facts, grants die with their epoch, and held sets die with their process. That replaces r5.2's ≤1 s cross-restart gap.
   - **R-4 Acquisition offset.**
     - A holder whose wall clock is wrong when it acquires a deadline measures K16 or the sync budget from that wrong reading. The measured span is capped at the nominal span of elapsed time.
     - Causes: cross-host skew to hosted, or an executor restarted after a step back smaller than the time since the decision plus 5 s.
     - The gate then refuses what K16 passed, and the step is E10's `failed`, "gate refused (signature)", conservatively counted as executed. A target whose own clock is behind accepts signatures for longer, as it does today.
   - **R-5 Clocks wrong at the source.**
     - *Signer fast by A.* TS and `Exp` are A ahead. 3.2 catches it only if the clock is corrected by more than 5 s before the release. A gate without a future-TS bound accepts the signature until `Exp`, which is A plus TTL of real time. This is §1.4 item 5, pre-existing.
     - *Signer slow.* The gate refuses early, and the step is `failed`.
     - *Every local clock wrong* (a paused or unsynced VM). Signatures carry that clock's TS, and only the gate bounds them.
     - *Outside Linux,* a suspend that coincides with a step back extends held authority by the smaller of the two.
4. **One commit function per store.** `Apply(rec, cmd, now, epoch) → (rec′, answer, events)` is pure and lives in `approvalplan` (stdlib only; E62 closure). (r5.3) It is `cmd` applied to `Clock(rec, now)` (rule 3.3), with `now` the injected clock's one wall reading for the evaluation (rule 3.1). It runs under the store lock: securestate's flock plus the process mutex, or `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. A command's impure inputs (the minter's output, the current xferregistry digests, the deciding grant record, the current registry entry) are read inside the same critical section and passed in, and the store commits once. (r5.1) The one exception is the hosted mint, which is computed before the Claim transaction and passed in; the write-once mint slot decides which set wins (E21). **Effects run only after the commit is confirmed.** Each owner has one reconciler, `Next` on the signer and `JobNext` on the executor. It is the only driver of follow-up work, and it runs after every commit, on every tick (each second, and at each record's `next_due_at`; (r5.3) the tick applies Clock alone, rule 3.3), and at startup. **(r5.1) Work that performs external I/O is single-flight per record or job:** the notify steps run only in the record's notifier actor, Flush only in the one flusher, and `JobNext` only in the job's actor (SA-5); every other trigger only nudges that actor.
5. **Each identity is minted once, at the outermost hop that can retry, and carried inward unchanged.** The owner scope is part of the storage key, so a foreign id and an absent id are the same miss (SA-6.6).

#### SA-2 Identifiers

| Identity | Minted by, when | Carried to | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| v2 `request_id`, `a2_` + base64url(16 random bytes) (E31) | the MCP, once per tool call, sync and async | executor (its job key), local signer, hosted through the stateless proxy | the executor and the signer never mint one |
| Principal | local: `SO_PEERCRED` uid → `local-uid:<uid>` (E17); hosted: `(bearer client_id, sub_principal)` (E19) | part of every storage key | the proxy sets `requester_sub = local-uid:<uid>`; a direct bearer client omits it (empty namespace) |
| `draft_hash`, `plan_hash` | `approvalplan`, one function each (E27) | header | — |
| Claim token, 256 bits | the executor per job (raw value in the job record); the sync coordinator in process | its hash travels inside the draft | E25 |
| `input_hash` | the MCP, over the canonical `exec_submit` input, which (r5.2) includes the caller-fixed `submit_not_after` | executor header | makes `exec_submit` idempotent; (r5.2) a replay can never get a fresh submit window |
| Card handle, local challenge (64 bits) | the record's notifier actor, per card entry | the entry stores the handle and the challenge hash; callback data ≤64 B (E47) | — |
| (r5.1) Record handle | derived, never minted: `base32(SHA-256("sshgate-v2-record-handle-v1\0" ‖ record key))[:16]` (80 bits); the record key is `(principal, request_id)` locally and `(client_id, sub_principal, request_id)` on hosted | every operator surface: cards, `/pending`, `/cancel`, `/renotify`, snapshot rendering, the hosted pending list and `/ui/v2/request/{handle}` routes; hosted stores it as an indexed column | a `request_id` is unique only per principal, so operators never address a v2 record by it alone; a handle or ID matching more than one record is refused as ambiguous (SA-6.6) |
| Hosted challenge | the detail GET: `nonce ‖ expires_at ‖ HMAC(K, …)` (E7) | the browser | K is random per process start; nothing stored |
| Grant id; grant expiry | = the grant request's `request_id`; `decided_at + duration_seconds` | returned by v2 Get and Wait | E9 |
| `event_key` | `(record key, outbox seq)` | audit sink | E23 |
| `report_sequence` | ≡ 1 | report | one final report |
| Epoch | random per store open: local signer, hosted process, executor | recorded in decisions, bursts, card entries and markers | grants and in-flight work are judged against it |

Gone: `job_id`, `attempt_id`, `signature_claim_id`, proxy reservations, executor-minted request ids, the grant install id, and challenge rows.

#### SA-3 Records

**R1 — Approval record** (every v2 kind, including `standing_grant`).
- *Local storage:* one file in the E18 directory, named `r-` + base32(SHA-256(`"sshgate-approval-rec-v1\0"` ‖ principal ‖ `0x00` ‖ request_id)), 0600, versioned canonical JSON, **≤512 KiB encoded (r5.1)**.
- *(r5.1) Bounds are on the encoded file.* securestate requires the stored bytes to equal `json.Marshal` exactly (`internal/securestate/store_linux.go:325-331`) and bounds the encoded body (`:348`), and `json.Marshal` writes `<`, `>`, `&` and controls as 6-byte escapes. A K19-legal 24 KiB command of `<` is stored twice (plan and ReviewSnapshot segments, about 288 KiB encoded), so r5's 256 KiB raw-sized bound refused legal requests. A1a builds the worst-case K19-legal record (every command byte `<`, or every byte inside a minimal secret span, maximal reason and assist, (r5.2) route snapshots and `trusted_display` at their A1a bounds, full vote, card, burst, status and outbox sets) and asserts it encodes within 512 KiB with at least 20% margin; if not, A1a raises the bound, never lowers the K19 limits. Command strings are valid UTF-8 by construction (E27's strict decode must re-encode byte-equal), so encoding is lossless. (r5.5) Locally, each full-payload record also reserves its own whole-life worst case, `R1Reservation`, against the payload budget (SA-9).
- *Hosted storage:* one `approvals_v2` row, primary key `(client_id, sub_principal, request_id)`. Column `record_json` holds the same canonical encoding as the local file. The derived columns `view_status`, `class`, `next_due_at` and (r5.1) `record_handle`, plus the slot columns that admission counts over, are recomputed from the record in the same transaction, and (r5.4) refreshed by the worker when only the report horizon or an age has passed (SA-1 rule 3.3). They are a cache for SQL listing, handle lookup and worker scheduling, never an input to a decision.
- *Single writer:* the store's `Apply`. Commands come from the socket or HTTP handlers, the Telegram callback, the notifier actor, the assist job, the tick or worker, admin commands, and startup.
- *Header* (kept for the record's whole life; ≤1 KiB plus the votes, (r5.2) plus `rationale_redacted`, (r5.6, critique of the fold) plus the decision slot's `decided_by`, which is `ApprovingOperators` joined with commas (`approval_engine.go:273-274`), so up to `required_approvals` operator IDs of at most 128 bytes each (`main.go:630`; their grammar has no character `json.Marshal` escapes), about 33 KiB at N = 256; A1a's worst-case proof covers all three): principal and sub_principal, request_id, kind, `draft_hash`, `plan_hash`, `rationale_origin`, `claim_token_hash`, `coordinator`, `required_approvals` ((r5.4) frozen at Submit from the server's configuration, never from the draft: hosted copies `Config.RequiredApprovals`, as v1 intake does at `handlers.go:237`, `config.go:60-63`, so a later change of `--required-approvals` affects new records only; local is 1, E6; (r5.6) always 1..`MaxRequiredApprovals` = 256: the signer-server refuses to start above 256, A10, and (r5.6, critique of the fold) the freeze writes any server value ≤ 0 as 1, as `Decide` treats it (`approval.go:95-98`); the freeze reads `Server.RequiredApprovals`, which `hosted.New` sets only from a positive `Config.RequiredApprovals` (`config.go:126-128`, default 1 at `server.go:124`) but which a direct `NewServer` caller may leave at 0 or set negative (`server.go:37-40`)), `created_at`, `request_expires_at` (configuration only, E26), `assist_deadline`, `submit_not_after`, per-step TTLs and `step_count`, the mint summary `{none | minted + digest | error + reason}`, `claim_count`, (r5.4) `renotify_gen`, the final-report summary (class, executed mask, partial, result digest, body digest), the decision slot, `audited_through`, and `shed`. **(r5.2)** Also `rationale_redacted`, the snapshot's redacted reason, which is immutable from freeze (spec §8.3, E23). **(r5.1)** Also: the votes ((r5.6) ≤ `VoteCap(rec)`, each `{operator, decision, ts, authn}`, moved from the payload so a shed record still holds who decided and how, E23), and for a `standing_grant` record `grant_alias`, `duration_seconds` and `trusted_host_fingerprint`, so a shed grant still answers Get with `grant_id` and `grant_expires_at` (SA-6.4).
- *Payload* (sheddable): the plan bytes, the ReviewSnapshot (E37), the assist JSON, the signature bytes, the card entries, the bursts, `status_edits`, and the outbox (events not yet audited).
- *Write-once slots:* `assist` {ready + digest | unavailable + reason}; `decision` {approved | denied | canceled | expired | error, `decided_at`, `decided_by`, `terminal_reason`, epoch, (r5.2) `assist_bound {state, revision, digest}`: the assist the View showed at the deciding commit, `not_applicable` for a grant decision (K8, E23)}; `mint` {signatures | error + reason, where (r5.2) reason `window_closed` is Clock's fact at `claim_end` for an unclaimed window ((r5.3) SA-1 rule 3.3)}; `final_report` {class, steps, executed mask, partial, result digest}; `revoked_at` (grants only).
- *Append-only bounded sets:* votes (≤1 per operator, (r5.6) ≤ `VoteCap(rec) = required_approvals + VoteSlack`, `VoteSlack = 64`, so at most 320; r5.1–r5.5 had a flat 64, below the quorum a valid hosted configuration can require); bursts `{epoch, (r5.4) gen, started_at, part_msg_ids, outcome}` (≤8; (r5.4) `gen` is the header's `renotify_gen` when the burst is appended, SA-4 step 1); card entries `{handle, rev, chal_hash, actions, kind first | edit | replacement, epoch, msg_id?, outcome?, confirmed?, retired?}` (≤16; `msg_id`, `outcome`, `confirmed` and `retired` are write-once fields of the entry; (r5.4) `outcome` belongs to the send entries, `first` and `replacement`, and `confirmed` to `edit` entries); `status_edits` (one per display state, ≤12).
- *(r5.1) A full set never overflows silently.* Renotify on a record whose burst set is full is refused with "notification limit reached; cancel and resubmit". A notify step that needs a card entry (edit or replacement) when the card set is full commits decision `{error, terminal_reason: notification_limit}` with best-effort INCOMPLETE edits. A full `status_edits` set skips further status edits, which are best effort. (r5.6, review 7 #2) **The vote set always has room for the quorum.** r5.1–r5.5 refused the 65th vote ("a decision needs far fewer"), but nothing bounded the hosted threshold below 65 (`pkg/signerkit/hosted/config.go:113` checks only N > 0; the roster check accepts any N that enough eligible operators cover, `src/signer-server/cmd/sshgate-signer-server/main.go:845-866`), and votes that cannot count fill the set too: the requester's own vote with self-approval off, and every deny with veto off (`Decide` records but ignores them, `pkg/signerkit/hosted/approval.go:94-133`). So a valid deployment could accept v2 requests that could never be approved. Now, with `need = required_approvals − |ApprovingOperators(EffectivePolicy(rec, flags), votes)|` at the Vote's commit:
  - a vote that **decides** (after it, `Decide` under the effective policy no longer answers `pending`) or **counts** (it adds an approving operator under the effective policy) is always accepted;
  - any other vote is accepted only if, after it, `|votes| + need + 1 ≤ VoteCap(rec)`; otherwise it is refused and audited (`vote_refused{set_full}`), and the operator is told "vote limit reached: this vote cannot change the outcome". Such a vote cannot change the decision under the flags in force, and because it is not stored, the same operator can vote again later, which counts if the flags have changed.
  - *Why it holds.* At freeze `need = N` and `VoteCap − N = 64 ≥ 1`. A counting vote adds one vote and lowers `need` by one; an accepted non-counting vote leaves `|votes| + need + 1 ≤ VoteCap`; a refused vote changes nothing; so `|votes| + need ≤ VoteCap` while the record is pending, and every counting or deciding vote finds a slot (`need ≥ 1`). The `+ 1` absorbs the one vote whose weight a flag change can alter, the requester's: if `--allow-self-approve` is turned off after the requester's approve counted, `need` rises by one and the bound still holds. Deny-veto changes never move `need`.
  - *The threshold is bounded.* `approvalplan.MaxRequiredApprovals = 256`, the policy plane's existing ceiling (`policystore.MaxVotesPerRequest`, `pkg/signerkit/policystore/config.go:32`, enforced at `:72`). The signer-server refuses to start with `--required-approvals` above 256, and `hosted.New` refuses the same (A10); N ≤ 0 keeps today's startup handling, and (r5.6, critique of the fold) the v2 freeze writes it as 1, as `Decide` does. So no v2 record freezes a quorum its vote set cannot hold; a larger N is refused, never lowered (§1.3 item 37). Locally N = 1 with veto on, so every vote decides and the room rule never fires.
- *Monotone counters:* `claim_count`, the outbox sequence, `audited_through`, `noise_dropped`, and (r5.4) `renotify_gen` (0 at freeze; only Renotify raises it, SA-4 step 5; it never exceeds the latest burst's `gen` by more than 1, so the burst bound of 8 bounds it).
- *(r5.5, critique of the fold) The outbox is bounded.* Flush drops the events through `audited_through`, so the outbox holds only events not yet audited. A commit appends a non-noise event only for a write it makes to a write-once slot, to a write-once field of a set entry, to a bounded set, or to `renotify_gen`, at most one event for each, plus at most one for the Erase. Commands whose SA-11 replay row is *exact* or *counter only* append nothing when repeated: a repeat Claim (which adds only to `claim_count`), an acked duplicate Report and a Renotify answered "already pending" add no event. Every refusal or replay that appends anything appends a noise event, folded beyond 32 unflushed (SA-7) and beyond 1 on a shed record (SA-9). So a record's unflushed non-noise events never exceed `OutboxMax`, a constant A1a derives from these slots, fields and set bounds ((r5.6) ≤ `VoteCap(rec)` votes, so `OutboxMax(rec)` is a pure function of the frozen header like `R1Reservation`, ≤8 bursts and so ≤8 raises, ≤16 card entries with their four write-once fields, ≤12 status edits, and the slots). A1a also derives each event kind's worst-case encoded size. `R1Reservation` counts the outbox at `OutboxMax` non-noise events plus 32 noise events and the fold, at those sizes (SA-9). r5.5 cited an outbox bound in SA-3 that SA-3 did not state.

**R2 — Executor job record.** `<configRoot>/executor/j-` + base32(SHA-256(`"sshgate-exec-job-v1\0"` ‖ request_id)), 0600, **(r5.2) ≤ `R2Max` encoded**. Single writer: the singleton executor's commit function, under its flock and process mutex, and (r5.2) only in the process that owns the job directory (SA-1 rule 1).
- *(r5.2) The bound comes from the whole encoded record.* r5.1's bound, `ceil(4/3 × 8 MiB)` + 64 KiB, left 64 KiB for everything except the bodies, the draft included. A K19-legal draft of 24 KiB of `<` encodes to about 144 KiB (`json.Marshal` escapes `<` as `\u003c`, `store_linux.go:360`), so a legal job with maximum output overflowed by more than 80 KiB, and the definite-refusal fallback below would have dropped its bodies. Now `R2Max = ceil(4/3 × 8 MiB) + R2BodyOverhead + R2NonBodyMax`. `R2BodyOverhead` covers base64 padding, quotes and field names for 64 bodies (32 steps × 2 streams). `R2NonBodyMax` is derived in A1a: A1a builds the worst-case non-body record (every draft command byte `<`, `>`, `&` or a control escape; a maximal reason; (r5.2) route snapshots and `trusted_display` at their A1a bounds, since the registry itself bounds nothing (`src/mcp/registry/servers.go`); 32 step results with maximal metadata and both digests; every slot filled; the raw token), measures its encoding, adds at least 20% and rounds up to 64 KiB. A1a asserts that a record with that header and 8 MiB of worst-case bodies encodes within `R2Max`. The admission reservation is per job (SA-9).
- *Header:* request_id (minted by the MCP), `input_hash`, the draft and its `draft_hash`, `submit_not_after` ((r5.2) caller-fixed: the MCP's value from the `exec_submit` input, inside `input_hash`; r5.1 used job creation + 3600 s), `created_at`, the result reservation; the raw claim token (sheddable).
- *Write-once slots:* `signer_ack` (the `plan_hash` the signer returned; `decided_at` and the step expiries arrive with each Claim answer and are not journaled); `outcome`, for a job that ends without a step result (`denied`, `canceled`, `expired`, `error`, `approval_lost`, `adopted{class}`); (r5.3, OD-3) `error` carries a code, and a definitive signer answer can also end a job that has step results (SA-5); `report_result` {acked | refused{code}} ((r5.2) `refused` is committed only on a definitive answer; (r5.3, critique pass 1) that is every code SA-5's one split calls definitive, for example `invalid_report`, `invalid_claim`, `not_found` (the signer no longer holds the header), `record_corrupt`, `invalid_request`, `request_too_large` or `signer_incompatible`; transient answers such as `storage_uncertain`, `lifecycle_unavailable` or a dial failure leave it empty and the report owed, SA-5 step 7; (r5.3) `clock_regressed` is withdrawn); `first_delivered_at`; `bodies_removed` {cause}; **(r5.4) `final_at`**, the wall-clock `now` (SA-1 rule 3.1) of the commit that first makes `JobView` final: the result commit that ends the job (any abort status, a stop-policy stop, the last step, an orphan marker's `indeterminate`, a check failure, or the bodies-dropped retry below) or the `outcome` commit. The commit function sets it in that same commit, so it can never be missing from a final job, even when the executor is killed right after. Later commits (the report, `report_result`, delivery, token or body removal) never touch it, and no age is ever read from `created_at`, a marker's `at` or the file's mtime. SA-9's body-removal and job-file ages count from it.
- *Per step k:* `marker` {epoch, at}; `result` {status, exit, sizes, truncated, `stdout_sha256`, `stderr_sha256`, stdout and stderr bodies under E56's caps}. A step result commits together with its bodies, so a written but unreferenced body can never exist (R1.6's seam). This deliberately departs from Proposal 3's separate body blobs. **(r5.1)** Bodies are `[]byte` (base64 in the canonical JSON), never strings, because output is arbitrary bytes and a JSON string would replace invalid UTF-8 (E56). The two digests stay after the bodies are removed, so the report's `result_digest` never changes (E38, F-1).
- *(r5.1) A definite commit refusal.* With the bounds above ((r5.2) now derived from the whole encoded record), `ErrTooLarge` or a validation refusal cannot happen for valid input, but `JobNext` still answers it: a refused result commit is retried once with the bodies dropped (`bodies_unstorable: true`, sizes and digests kept), so a known exit status is never lost; any other refused commit ends the job with outcome `error` and no dispatch; if that also fails, the executor exits as for an uncertain commit (SA-8).
- *Signatures are never journaled.* A repeat Claim returns the signer's stored set (K11 bullet 2).

**R3 — Hosted v1 `requests` and `approvals`** (frozen, extended). Migration 7 adds `request_expires_at` and `terminal_reason` (E4b). The frozen engine is unchanged: `PrepareVote`, audit, `MarkVoteAudited`, `Decide`, mint, then flip (`approval_engine.go:152-281`). The worker's `ExpireLegacy` and the admin's `CancelLegacy` are pending-only CASes with their own SQL, without `UpdateStatus`'s unaudited-vote barrier (`sqlite.go:352-360`), with the terminal mapping of E4c. A racing `SubmitVote` then loses its flip and discards its own signatures (`approval_engine.go:257-281`); its unaudited vote row stays as evidence. Never pruned (K17, `/v1/audit`).

**Unchanged stores:** the master key, xferregistry, the policy journal, chatStore, the AuditLog (fsync per line, `audit.go:114-126`), and hosted `sessions` and `users`.

**Memory only, by design.** Each item is a projection rebuilt from the records, or something whose loss only refuses: local legacy v1 requests (SA-10) and (r5.4) their resolved tombstones (≤256, E8b); deny-all and cancel snapshots, local and hosted (≤16 per admin, 5 min); the hosted challenge key; the grant projection; (r5.1) the newest-grant mark per `(principal, alias)` (SA-6.4; empty at each new epoch, because every earlier grant is dead); the card-handle index; (r5.1) the record-handle and request-ID indexes (local); (r5.1) the local signer's decoded record cache (B1); the per-principal and admission counters; the due index; waiters; notifier and job actors; the assist pool; the epoch; (r5.3) the held deadlines of SA-1 rule 3.4 (a wall end and an elapsed end per live grant, per held signature set and for the sync budget) and their latches. r5.1's clock high-water mark is withdrawn.

#### SA-4 Commands on R1 (each is exactly one commit)

Every command and every read begins with the owner-scoped load (SA-6.6); (r5.1) operator commands (Vote, AdminCancel, Renotify) and the hosted detail GET resolve the record by its card entry, record handle or full key instead (SA-6.6's operator carve-out). **(r5.3) Every command except Delete then applies `Clock(rec, now)` first and judges its guards on the result, in its one commit (SA-1 rule 3.3).** (r5.3, critique pass 1) No guard below compares a Clock deadline; Submit's window (SA-1 rule 3.5) and Claim's release check (rule 3.2) are the stated exceptions.

- **Submit.** Validation first: E71 `ValidateStepBytes`, the E25 claim-token hash, K2, K19, the E60 TTL table, the E33 update hash and E16 renderability. Then: the same `draft_hash` → return the record, shed or not, whatever the time; another `draft_hash` → `conflict`; a new id → `now ≤ submit_not_after ≤ now + 3600 s` (hosted allows +300 s; (r5.2) the local signer +5 s, E73), then admission (SA-9; (r5.3) a full live cap first runs the admission Clock pass), then freeze. If every signed step is covered by a live, host-bound grant of the submitter's own principal (SA-6.4, E9, E24; E71's byte refusal outermost; (r5.3) the match first drops grants either of whose ends has passed, SA-1 rule 3.4), the freeze commit also writes decision `{approved, decided_at = now, decided_by = grant:<G>, epoch}`. Effects: for a new id only, start the single assist attempt (K8) and nudge the notifier actor.
- **AssistResult.** Written only if the assist slot is still empty after Clock ((r5.3) Clock fills it at `assist_deadline`). A late result is dropped (K6). (r5.6, critique of the fold) On hosted, the assist job resends this command after a busy `BEGIN`, with E25's backoff, until `assist_deadline` (E21); the provider is not called again.
- **Vote.** Guards: the challenge is valid (SA-6.1, including (r5.3) OA-5's revision check); the role is `operator` (E6); step-up passed (hosted, checked by the handler); the decision slot is empty after Clock ((r5.3) a Vote after `request_expires_at` commits Clock's `expired` and its `vote_refused` in one commit); this operator has no vote; (r5.6) the vote room rule of SA-3 (a vote that neither counts nor decides needs `|votes| + need + 1 ≤ VoteCap(rec)` after it, else `vote_refused{set_full}`). Writes: the vote, with `ts = now`; if `Decide(EffectivePolicy(rec, flags), votes)` reaches a result, the decision slot with `decided_at` = the deciding vote's `ts`, `decided_by` = `ApprovingOperators` under that same effective policy (as `approval_engine.go:273` does), and (r5.2) `assist_bound` = the View's assist `{state, revision, digest}` at this commit; the outbox gets the vote and decision events, the decision event carrying that same digest (K8). Every other command that writes the decision slot (grant-covered Submit, AdminCancel, Clock, a notify-step `error`) records `assist_bound` the same way (r5.2). That single commit is K15's CAS. **(r5.1)** A re-send by an operator who already voted changes no state and appends one `vote_replay` noise event, whatever its decision; an authenticated tap or POST that fails a guard (resolved or expired record, stale, retired or undelivered challenge, (r5.6) a vote refused by the room rule) appends one `vote_refused{reason}` noise event. K15: "duplicate challenges/votes are inert and audited" (E61). There are no prepared, audited or counted vote states.
  - **(r5.9, coordinator ruling 2026-10-15, A1b departure 8)** A denial's `decided_by` is the first eligible denier (A1a's validator requires a non-empty value; v1 stored `""`). Approvals keep `ApprovingOperators` joined with commas.
  - **(r5.9, coordinator ruling 2026-10-15, joint review 3) Approval attribution is the quorum.** An approving decision's `decided_by` is the first `required_approvals` eligible approvers in vote order (the operators who made the quorum), joined with commas, never more. A self-approval flag change across a restart can make an earlier vote eligible, so `ApprovingOperators` may name N+1 operators; attribution still records N, which keeps A1a's frozen `decided_by` bound (N operator IDs of at most 128 bytes) unchanged. The votes themselves stay in the header as before.
  - **(r5.4) The effective policy.** Today's engine never trusts the policy it is handed: it overrides `RequiredApprovals` with the request's stored N and `Requester` with the request's stored, authenticated `ClientID` before it calls `Decide` (`approval_engine.go:197-204`), and the policy production supplies carries only the two flags (`main.go:281`, `HumanAPIConfig.ApprovalPolicy`, `human_handlers.go:776`). Passed straight to `Decide`, that policy would default N to 1 (`approval.go:95-98`) and switch the self-approval ban off, because an empty `Requester` matches no vote (`approval.go:113`). So `approvalplan.EffectivePolicy(rec, flags)` builds the policy inside the Vote's own commit, and the Vote command's input type has no field for N or a requester:
    - `RequiredApprovals` = the header's frozen `required_approvals` (SA-3);
    - `Requester` = the record key's authenticated requester: on hosted the bearer `client_id` (E19), on local the principal `local-uid:<uid>`. `sub_principal` never enters it, in either direction (K17: it only narrows the per-client filter). It is `local-uid:<uid>` or empty, never a `users.id`, so it can neither make an operator's vote a self-vote nor stop one being one; a vote by the operator account whose ID equals `client_id` (the machine client is a bootstrapped operator, `main.go:831-845`) is a self-vote whatever the row's `sub_principal`;
    - `DenyVeto` and `AllowSelfApprove` = the server's configured flags (hosted `--deny-veto` and `--allow-self-approve`, `main.go:281`; local: veto on and self-approval off, N = 1, E6), the only values a caller supplies.
- **AdminCancel.** Admin role, `plan_hash` equal to the snapshot item's (or the named record's), View pending after Clock → decision `{canceled}`. (r5.3) On a record Clock has just resolved it answers "already resolved"; no clock condition refuses it.
- **Clock** ((r5.3) rewritten; SA-1 rule 3.3). Pure, in `approvalplan`; applied first by every command except Delete, and alone by the tick, the hosted worker and Startup's `Next`, always at the `now` of the evaluation that commits it. It fills, in this order, exactly the empty slots whose deadline is at or before `now`: (1) assist `{unavailable, "assist deadline"}`, the header-derived snapshot of E68, at `assist_deadline`; (2) decision `{expired, decided_at = request_expires_at, decided_by = clock, epoch, assist_bound}`, with `assist_bound` the assist slot after (1), at `request_expires_at`; (3) at `claim_end`, for an approved, execution-applicable record, mint `{error: window_closed}` when `claim_count = 0` and the mint slot is empty, otherwise the Erase of any signature bytes still held. It writes no stamp, and it is idempotent and never removes or overwrites a slot. r5.2's "runs during a regression and in a clock break" is withdrawn with both.
- **Notify steps.** Only the record's single actor runs them, and it re-reads the record before each Bot API call ((r5.8) and checks the owner's `stopping` latch before each Bot API call and each commit, SA-8). (r5.1) Steps 1 and 2 for one record run inside one `Notifier.Burst` session, which holds `t.policyNotifyMu` from the first part through the card's send and its commit, as the policy precedent holds it across parts and card (`telegram_policy.go:84-150`); no other record's burst or the policy flow can interleave between a record's parts and its card (E14). Store commits inside the session take the store lock after `policyNotifyMu`; no path takes them in the other order, because effects run only after a commit has released the store lock.
  1. Pending, and (r5.4) **a resend is due**: no burst carries `gen = renotify_gen` → append a burst with the current epoch and `gen = renotify_gen`, send the parts, commit their message IDs with outcome `sent`. (r5.4) r5–r5.3 said "no burst since the last `/renotify`", which no stored fact recorded: Renotify only retired card entries, so after a crash during the parts (no card to retire) a confirmed `/renotify` left nothing for `Next` to tell apart from a prohibited automatic resend. `renotify_gen` is that fact. At freeze it is 0 and there is no burst, so the first burst is due; a burst cut by a crash carries the current `gen`, so it is never resent automatically; after Renotify raises the counter, exactly one burst is due, whatever crashes follow before or after its append. **(r5.1) Any send failure observed live** (a known 4xx, a transport error, or E50's 60 s budget ending; (r5.8, review 9 #2) "live" means observed while the owner is not stopping: a call that the owner's stop interrupts is not a failure, commits nothing and triggers no INCOMPLETE edit, SA-8) → burst outcome `error` and decision `{error}` in one commit, then best-effort INCOMPLETE edits (K19).
  2. Pending, the latest burst's outcome is `sent` (r5.1), (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-4/C2-2) that burst carries `gen = renotify_gen` (no resend is due), and no unretired card entry → append a `first` entry when the record has no card entry at all, otherwise a `replacement` (every earlier entry is then retired, by step 3 or Renotify) (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C2-4), at the current assist revision with a new handle and challenge, send the card, commit its `msg_id` (r5.4) and `outcome = sent` together, in one commit. r5–r5.3 committed only `msg_id`, so SA-6.3, which reads `outcome`, would have shown a delivered card as `sending` for ever and as `uncertain` after any restart. **(r5.1)** Any live send failure ((r5.8) live as in step 1) → entry outcome `error` and decision `{error}`, then best-effort INCOMPLETE edits of the parts. The card follows committed parts, so assist can never edit or replace a card that does not exist (R2.3). `uncertain` never comes from a live failure; it is only what recovery derives for a burst or (r5.4) an unretired send entry from another epoch with no outcome (SA-6.3, SA-8), after a crash, an uncertain commit's reopen or (r5.8) an owner stop that interrupted the send.
  3. Pending, and the latest unretired card is delivered at a revision below the View's → append an `edit` entry at the new revision with a new challenge, call `editMessageText`, commit `confirmed`. Edits are idempotent ("message is not modified" counts as success). Within the epoch that appended the entry, the actor re-runs the edit until it is confirmed, from the raw challenge it holds in memory. **(r5.5, critique of the fold) The re-run has a driver.** When `EditCard` reports a possible send (a transport error or a timeout, after which Telegram may or may not have applied the edit), the actor arms an in-memory retry for the record, 1 s doubling to 30 s, and on each expiry re-sends the same edit bytes while the entry is still the record's latest unretired entry, still unconfirmed, and the View is pending. A success ("message is not modified" included) commits `confirmed`; a known failure takes the replacement path below. The timer only nudges the record's actor (SA-1 rule 4), holds no fact, decides nothing and dies with the process ((r5.8) or at the owner's stop latch, SA-8), after which the other-epoch rule below applies. Until the edit is confirmed the card has no valid challenge (E48), so this bounds that gap by Telegram's recovery plus at most 30 s; `/renotify`, AdminCancel and deny-all stay available meanwhile, and a Renotify retires the entry, which stops the timer. r5.5 said only "re-runs", and nothing scheduled the re-run: a timed-out edit left the card with no valid challenge until expiry, a restart or `/renotify`. **(r5.2)** An unconfirmed edit entry from another epoch cannot be replayed, because the entry stores only `chal_hash`, and the callback bytes, which carry the raw challenge, are gone with the old process. So one commit sets its `retired` field and appends a fresh `edit` entry for the same card message, at the View's revision, with a new handle and challenge. Then `editMessageText` is sent with the new bytes and `confirmed` is committed (E48). A known edit failure → retire the card and take step 2 with a `replacement` entry (E48).
  4. A display state from E49 with no `status_edits` entry → one best-effort edit, then append the entry.
  5. `/renotify <handle>` (admin, E64 gate; r5.1 addressing) → (r5.4) in one commit, raise `renotify_gen` by 1 and retire every unretired card entry; step 1 then sends a new burst, and step 2 a card after it (`replacement` when the record already had a card entry, `first` when it had none). The commit is the durable authorization: `Next` resends after a crash at any point after it. A full burst set refuses it (SA-3). (r5.4) It changes nothing, and answers "re-notify already pending", while a resend is already due (no burst carries the current `gen`), so two `/renotify` commands before the new burst is appended authorize one resend, not two. It is refused "a send is in progress — try again" while the latest burst or send entry is from the current epoch with no outcome, because the actor is mid-send and will settle it as `sent` or `error` (K19). It applies only to a record whose View is pending after Clock. (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-4/C2-2) **Steps 1 and 2 never both hold:** step 1 needs a resend due and step 2 needs none, so a Renotify that lands after a burst's outcome commit and before the card's append makes the append's guard false; step 1 sends the `gen = renotify_gen` burst first, and step 2 then sends exactly one card after it. The planner still evaluates the steps in their number order.
- **Claim.** Token or `plan_hash` mismatch → `invalid_claim` (audited). Otherwise the answer is a total function of (r5.3) the record after Clock:
  - (r5.3, critique pass 1) **Precedence:** the two `expired` rows below for a `window_closed` mint slot and for erased bytes are answered ahead of the flush gate, since no signature leaves either way. So a failing audit sink delays a Claim only until `claim_end`, and the executor's `not_ready` retries end there (E25, SA-5).
  - **(r5.9, coordinator ruling 2026-10-15, A1b fix round 1) An accepted final report comes first.** Before the `expired` window rows and the flush gate, a Claim on a record whose final report is accepted answers `execution_reported{class}` (the caller adopts the recorded result), before and after expiry, flushing and shedding. The two `expired` window rows apply only when no report is accepted.
  - **(r5.5) One code, two meanings, told apart by the caller.** The `expired` decision row and the two window rows share the code `expired`. Every coordinator Claims only after it has seen `approved` (from Submit's answer, Wait or Get), and decisions are write-once, so a coordinator's Claim `expired` is always the window's close: the executor records `expired_unexecuted` (SA-5 step 6) and the sync coordinator ends its run `expired_unexecuted` with a negative report (SA-5, E26). Only an `expired` decision seen from Submit, Wait or Get is the pending request's expiry (`approval_timeout`). The wire is unchanged.
  - `not_ready` while pending, or until the outbox is flushed through the decision. (r5.1) Every commit that appends events nudges the flusher as a post-commit effect, and a Claim held back only by the flush gate waits up to 2 s for a flush wake-up, outside the store lock, and is retried once before answering `not_ready`; callers retry `not_ready` with bounded backoff (E25);
  - `denied`, `canceled`, `expired` or `error` for those decisions;
  - `execution_reported{class}` once a final report exists;
  - `expired` when the mint slot holds `{error: window_closed}` (r5.2; (r5.3) Clock's fact at `claim_end`, committed in this Claim's own commit when it is due);
  - `materialization_error{reason}` when the mint slot holds any other error, including a grant-decided record whose grant is no longer live (that Claim writes mint `{error: grant_not_live}`) and (r5.1) a first Claim while `decided_at` is more than 5 s ahead of the wall clock (mint `{error: clock_skew}`, SA-1 rule 3.2);
  - `expired` when a repeat claim finds its signature bytes erased. (r5.3) Clock erases them at `claim_end` in this Claim's own commit when that is due, so "any step window has ended" (first claim) and "the last window has ended" (repeat claim) are now the `window_closed` row above and this row. For `coordinator=in_process_sync`, `claim_end` is never later than `sync_claim_deadline = created_at + 310 s`, so that deadline now commits `window_closed` or the Erase instead of writing nothing (E25, E26). (r5.3) A repeat Claim while `decided_at` is more than 5 s ahead of the wall clock commits the Erase and answers `expired` too (SA-1 rule 3.2);
  - otherwise the signatures. The first Claim mints inside its commit: it re-runs E71 on the stored plan, checks the E34 transfer digests, signs at `decided_at`, writes the mint slot and sets `claim_count` to 1; a repeat Claim adds 1. The bytes leave only after the commit is confirmed, so an uncertain first mint never left the signer and only one signature set ever leaves. (r5.1) Hosted computes the signatures just before the Claim transaction and passes them in; if the slot is already filled, they are discarded and the stored set is returned (E21).
- **Report** (one final report, `report_sequence` 1). Accepted whenever the header exists, including after `report_horizon` and after a shed, if the token and `plan_hash` match, the decision is approved, the final slot is empty, (r5.6, critique of the fold) the steps are exactly one entry per ordinal 1..`step_count`, ascending and in E29's shape (E25, E38; checked first), `class = AggregateJobClass(steps)`, (r5.6) `executed_ordinals = ExecutedOrdinals(steps)` and `partial = Partial(steps)` (E29), and the executed mask is empty or `claim_count ≥ 1`. An exact duplicate (same body digest) is acked. Anything else is `invalid_report`, audited. An accepted report erases the signature bytes.
- **Revoke** (grant records). Writes `revoked_at`; the projection drops the grant. The newest-grant mark is not lowered, so an older grant stays dead (SA-6.4). (r5.1) `revoke_grant(alias)` applies Revoke to every live v2 grant record for the alias, whatever its principal: the one cross-principal write on the machine plane, de-escalation only (SA-6.6).
- **Flush, Erase, Shed** (SA-7, SA-9) are commands like the others; (r5.3) the time-based Erase is Clock's step 3, and an accepted report erases in its own commit. **Delete** removes the record's file or row; it is the store operation of SA-9, and (r5.3) the one command that skips Clock.

#### SA-5 Executor: `exec_submit`, `JobNext`, and the sync coordinator

- **`exec_submit`** carries the MCP-minted `request_id` and (r5.2) the MCP-fixed `submit_not_after` (call time + 3600 s), both inside `input_hash`. The same id with the same `input_hash` returns the existing job; the same id with another `input_hash` is `conflict`; a new id is first held to its window, **(r5.2)** `now ≤ submit_not_after ≤ now + 3600 s + 5 s` (r5.3: at the wall clock, SA-1 rule 3.5; (r5.2) the 5 s is E73's local allowance), else `submit_window_closed` (or `invalid_request` when too far ahead) with no job, no admission and no signer call; (r5.2) then a job whose `R2Reservation` exceeds `R2Max` is refused `request_too_large`, which A1a's draft bounds make unreachable; then admission (≤100 live jobs, and (r5.2) the job's own encoded-record reservation within 256 MiB, SA-9, otherwise `executor_full`), then a fresh `registry.ReadCurrent` (A13a), then the draft (whose `submit_not_after` is the input's, unchanged), then the job commit, then the signer Submit. It answers per E63. (r5.2) So a replay of an old `exec_submit` after both stores have pruned the original is refused at the executor, and the signer would refuse it too (E73, STATE-MODEL OR-1).
- **`JobNext(job)`** is the same function live and in recovery. **(r5.1) It runs single-flight per job:** one per-job actor (a keyed mutex plus a nudge flag, like the signer's notifier actors) executes it; ticks, `WaitMany` wake-ups, `exec_submit` and Startup only set the flag. It evaluates in this order, so that no step is ever chosen for dispatch while evidence says otherwise:
  1. **Final?** A stored result that ends the job (an abort status, a stop-policy stop, or the last step; E29), or an `outcome` → the job is final; go to 7. This covers a check-failure result written without a marker (R3.1).
  2. **Orphan marker.** A marker from another epoch with no result → commit result(k) = `indeterminate`; never re-dispatch (invariant 7). Go to 1.
  3. **(r5.1) In flight.** A marker from the current epoch with no result → the step is running; return without any action. Only the actor that committed the marker records its result.
  4. **No `signer_ack`** → Submit the stored draft again (it is idempotent). `submit_window_closed` → outcome `approval_lost`. (r5.3) Any other definitive answer → outcome `error{code}` (below).
  5. **Signer view.** Pending → Wait. A terminal non-approved decision → `outcome` adopts it. (r5.3) A definitive answer to Get or Wait after `signer_ack` (`not_found`, `record_corrupt`) → outcome `error{code}`.
  6. **Approved.** Let k be the first step with no result. **(r5.2) Claim only when it is needed:** when step k is signed, or when no step of the job has a marker or a result yet (the first dispatch, so that `claim_count ≥ 1` before anything runs). An unsigned step after the first dispatch runs with no Claim, as `run_batch` runs reads today (E74). When a Claim is needed, hold the signatures in memory only. Claim `not_ready` → retry with E25's backoff; `expired` → commit result(k) = `expired_unexecuted`; `materialization_error` → outcome `error`; `execution_reported` → outcome `adopted{class}`; (r5.3) a definitive answer (`invalid_claim`, `not_found`, `record_corrupt`) → outcome `error{code}`. Then, for step k, the checks are a fresh `registry.ReadCurrent` (any read, parse, permission or digest failure refuses, unlike `servers.go:222-263`) and then, **for a signed step only (r5.2)**, K16 ((r5.3) on the held pair of SA-1 rule 3.4 for step k; (r5.3, critique pass 1) acquired once in this process, at the first Claim whose answer carried step k's window, and never re-acquired by this or any repeat Claim: `wall + 2 s < decided_at.Unix() + TTL_k` and `E + 2 s < elapsed_end`; judged only for this step k, the first with no result). A failure commits result(k) = `stale_target` or `expired_unexecuted`. Otherwise commit marker{k, epoch}; **(r5.1)** a Marker answered `already_marked` stops here and never reaches `Start`; for a signed step, re-check K16 immediately before `Start` (a slow marker commit under store contention must not spend the margin; a failure commits result(k) = `expired_unexecuted` beside the marker); run `RunPhased` (E28), (r5.4) whose pre-start guard is that re-check: `RunPhased` calls it after key load, dial, handshake and session setup and immediately before `Session.Start`, so a slow handshake cannot spend the margin either, and a guard refusal sends no exec request and commits result(k) = `expired_unexecuted` beside the marker; commit result(k), or on a definite refusal follow SA-3 R2's rule. (r5.3) The actor drops the held set and its deadlines at the first of: the last signed step's `Start`, a K16 failure, the job becoming final, or process exit (SA-1 rule 3.4). Go to 1.
  7. **Report.** Final, an approved decision was seen, the outcome is not `adopted` or `error`, and `report_result` is empty → Report with a body that is a canonical function of the job record, so a resend is an exact duplicate (STATE-MODEL F-1); (r5.6, critique of the fold) it lists every ordinal 1..`step_count`, with `JobView`'s derived status for each step that has no result (E38). Commit `report_result`: `acked` on an ack; **(r5.2)** `refused{code}` on a definitive refusal ((r5.3, critique pass 1) every code the split below calls definitive, for example `invalid_report`, `invalid_claim`, `not_found`, `record_corrupt` or `signer_incompatible`); nothing on a transient answer, which leaves the report owed and resent later with the same bytes. A job "owes a report" while this step's condition holds; one whose report is settled (`acked` or `refused`), or that never owed one, is "report-settled" for SA-9.
  8. **Delivery.** After an await response has delivered the final result, commit `first_delivered_at`. The await handler itself never writes.
- **(r5.3, OD-3) Definitive and transient signer answers: one split for every answer `JobNext` consumes.** r5.2 split them for Report only, so a job that met `conflict`, `invalid_request`, `request_too_large` or `record_corrupt` on a resubmit, `not_found` or `record_corrupt` on Get or Wait after `signer_ack`, or `invalid_claim`, `not_found` or `record_corrupt` on Claim never became final, owed no report and held a live slot and its reservation for ever.
  - **Transient** (retry with bounded backoff, never ends the job): `storage_uncertain`, (r5.6) `busy` (hosted contention at `BEGIN`: the command was not applied, so the same bytes are resent, E21), `lifecycle_unavailable`, `queue_full` (bounded by the submit window, after which the resubmit answers `submit_window_closed`), Claim's `not_ready` (E25's backoff; bounded by Clock's `window_closed`, which (r5.3, critique pass 1) SA-4's Claim answers ahead of the flush gate), (r5.3, critique pass 1) a resubmit's `submit_window_ahead` (SA-1 rule 3.5; it clears once the wall clock re-advances, at most the size of the step back later), `ErrResponseLost` (reconciled by Get, then the idempotent resubmit) and a dial or transport failure.
  - **Definitive:** every other error code, including `conflict`, `invalid_request`, `reserved_verb`, `request_too_large`, `request_unrenderable`, `invalid_claim`, `not_found`, `record_corrupt` and `signer_incompatible` ((r5.7) a member of A5's closed set, E69).
  - The decisions and Claim statuses already routed in steps 4–6 (`submit_window_closed`, the terminal decisions, `expired`, `materialization_error`, `execution_reported`) keep their rows.
  - A definitive answer commits outcome `error{code}`. The job is final, owes no report (step 7 excludes outcome `error`) and is never dispatched again; steps it already executed keep their results, `executed_ordinals` and `partial` (E29). It then leaves the live cap, and SA-9's deletion predicate can fire once its bodies are gone.
  - (r5.3, critique pass 1) Report uses this same split (step 7): a transient answer leaves the report owed, and every definitive code commits `refused{code}`. r5.3's "Report keeps its own list" left a code on neither list (`invalid_request`, `request_too_large`, `signer_incompatible` from an older signer, E69) owing a report for ever, so SA-9's predicate (3) never held.
- **The sync coordinator** (A13b) keeps r4.1's flow: Submit ((r5.6, critique of the fold) its resends end at E21's Submit-phase retry bound, with E26's Submit-phase row as the ending), Wait for at most 300 s (r5.1: ending no later than `sync_claim_deadline − 5 s`; (r5.3) a held deadline on two clocks, SA-1 rule 3.4), Claim (r5.1: retrying `not_ready` and `storage_uncertain` inside that budget, and (r5.6) `busy` on every call; (r5.3) `clock_regressed` is withdrawn), run (K16 on its held set, SA-1 rule 3.4; (r5.4) the re-check is `RunPhased`'s pre-start guard, E28), one final report. It drops its held set at the first of the last signed step's `Start`, a K16 failure or the run's end. If its token dies with the call, the signer's view later derives `expired_unclaimed` or `unreported`. **(r5.5)** Its Claim retries end at the first of the budget's end and a Claim `expired`. A Claim `expired` after the `approved` it saw is the window's close (SA-4 Claim): the run ends `expired_unexecuted` with nothing run, the one final report is the negative report (empty executed mask, class `expired_unexecuted`), and the call returns the non-retryable `expired_unexecuted` Denial (E26). With a failing sink and a timely decision this comes at `claim_end`, about one TTL after the decision and well before the 300 s budget; the budget-end `approval_pending` wording is only for an approval late enough that its first window outlasts the budget.
- **The agent tools' source of truth** (STATE-MODEL OV-1, F-3): `list_pending_approvals` is the signer's List (sync and async requests alike) joined with the executor's jobs; `await_approvals` reads the job, or the signer's Get for an id the executor does not hold, such as an abandoned synchronous request (C3).

#### SA-6 Views (pure functions)

**SA-6.1 Approval view.** (r5.3) Status: the decision slot if set; otherwise `pending`. Assist: `preparing` (revision 0) while the slot is empty; otherwise the slot (revision 1). Actions: `{deny}` at revision 0, `{approve, deny}` at revision 1. The `unavailable` snapshot Clock writes at `assist_deadline` is a pure function of the header (`generated_at = assist_deadline`, reason `assist deadline`, empty model, prompt-version and input-digest fields), so its digest is fixed by the header (K8, E68). (r5.3) The view derives nothing from `now`: r5's view-derived `expired` and `unavailable` and r5.1's "`now` in every view is `t`" are withdrawn (SA-1 rule 3).
- *Local challenge validity,* all of: (r5.3) the decision slot is empty after Clock (the Vote's own Clock pass); the entry carrying the challenge is the record's latest unretired card entry; **(r5.3, OA-5)** the entry's `rev` equals the assist revision of the record after Clock; its message ID is recorded and equals the callback's (an `edit` entry must also be confirmed, and carries its card's message ID); the challenge hash matches; the action is in the entry's actions; the operator has no vote. The callback answer follows K15. (r5.3, OA-5) The revision check closes a gap r5.2 left open: with the card at revision 0, the assist slot committed (or Clock's `unavailable`, in the Vote's own commit) before the actor appended the edit entry, a tap on the revision-0 card was valid, and the vote bound `assist_bound` and the K8 digest to revision 1, an advisory the operator never saw. K15 binds a challenge to `assist_revision` and `assist_digest`, and hosted's MAC already checks the revision. Now the tap answers "card updated — tap again" (`vote_refused{stale_revision}`), whatever its action. That is a stale-view refusal, which a provider result produces in exactly the same way, not a clock refusal; AdminCancel and deny-all remain the challenge-free de-escalation paths.
- *Hosted challenge validity,* all of: the MAC verifies under the current key; `now < expires_at` (wall clock; R-1); the request's session binding equals the bound one; the plane, record key, `plan_hash`, revision, digest and actions equal the View's (r5.3: after Clock); the action is allowed; the operator has no vote (v1: `SubmitVote` decides duplicates, E7 step 7).

**SA-6.2 Execution view** (approved records with `execution_applicable=true`): `not_executable{reason}` when the mint slot holds an error other than `window_closed`; `final{class}` from the final-report slot; otherwise `expired_unclaimed` (r5.3) if and only if the mint slot holds `{error: window_closed}`, which Clock writes at `claim_end` (`first_end` while `claim_count = 0`; SA-1 rule 3.3); `unreported` when claimed and `now ≥ last_window_end + report_horizon` (provisional: a later report replaces it); `executing` ("approved and claimed; client-reported") when claimed and before the horizon; otherwise `none`. `report_horizon` is `[approvals] report_horizon` locally and `--approval-report-horizon` on hosted, default 24 h, range 1 h..7 d.

**SA-6.3 Notification view** (local; grant-decided and hosted records are `not_applicable`). (r5.4, rewritten by the critique of r5.4, C1-5 and C2-3) It reads the bursts and the **unretired send entries** (`first`, `replacement`) only. `edit` entries never enter it: they complete with `confirmed`, and an unconfirmed one is re-run in its epoch or superseded after it (SA-8). A retired send entry never enters it either. r5.4's first wording read `sending` for a resend due on a terminal record (for ever, since step 1 needs a pending record), read a retired entry as `uncertain`, and read `sent` between a burst's outcome and its card. Now:
- **Pending record** (decision slot empty), first match wins:
  1. no burst → `not_started`;
  2. a notify step 1 or 2 is due (SA-4: a resend is due, or the latest burst carries `gen = renotify_gen` with outcome `sent` and no unretired card entry exists) → `sending`. So E63's wait does not return between a burst and its card (§1.3 item 13);
  3. the latest burst, or the latest unretired send entry, has no outcome → `sending` when it is from the current epoch, `uncertain` when it is from another (r5.1: only a crash or an uncertain store commit produces it, (r5.8) or an owner stop that interrupted the send, SA-8; a live send failure is `error`, SA-4);
  4. otherwise `sent` (the latest unretired send entry's outcome).
- **Terminal record** (no notify step runs any more): `error` when the decision is a notify-step `error` (a burst or send entry with outcome `error`, or `terminal_reason = notification_limit`); otherwise rule 3 as for a pending record (a current-epoch send still in flight reads `sending`, because the actor that started it commits its outcome whatever the decision has become; one from another epoch reads `uncertain`); otherwise the latest unretired send entry's outcome, else the latest burst's; `not_started` with no burst. A Renotify authorization that never went out shows the last settled outcome, never `sending`.
- A delivered card carries `outcome = sent` (SA-4 step 2), so it reads `sent` before and after any restart.

**SA-6.4 Grant liveness (r5.1).** A grant record G is live if and only if all hold: G is approved; G's `decision.epoch` is the current epoch; `revoked_at` is empty; (r5.3) G is still in the grant projection, whose writer removes it at the first finding that either end of its held deadline has passed (`wall ≥ decided_at + duration_seconds`, or E past its elapsed end) and never re-adds it in that epoch (SA-1 rule 3.4; reads show membership and may trail the tick, never lead it); and G is the newest grant for its `(principal, grant_alias)` in this epoch, read from the **newest-grant mark**.
- *The mark* maps `(principal, alias)` to the `request_id` of the grant whose approved decision committed last in the current epoch. Only a commit that decides a grant approved raises it, in commit order (no timestamp tie-break). Revoke, Shed and Delete never lower it. It is empty at Startup and at every reopen, which is safe because every grant from an earlier epoch is dead.
