So revoking the newest grant never revives an older one, and **pruning a superseding grant can never revive the one it superseded**: r5 judged supersession by scanning surviving records for a later grant with the same alias, but the alias lived only in the sheddable plan, so shedding or deleting a revoked or expired superseder made the older grant live again with no tap.
- v2 matching uses only live grant records **of the submitter's own principal**, with E24's host check.
- (r5.2, OQ3 (b)) v1 `sign` consults no grant at all: the `matchGrant` short-circuit (`daemon.go:604-606`) is removed (E8b, E24). A v1 grant in the frozen map is listed and revocable but never spent.
- `revoke_grant` clears both sources (alias-global, SA-6.6); `list_grants` shows the v1 map and the caller's own live v2 grants (E24).

**SA-6.5 Class.** `live`: pending; approved with a signature window still open and (r5.1) no final report yet; or a live grant. (r5.3) "Open" is read from slots: the mint slot is empty or still holds bytes, and Clock closes it at `claim_end`, which is the *first* window end while `claim_count = 0`. So with unequal per-step TTLs (E60) an unclaimed record leaves `live` when its first window ends, not its last (the first-window note of `STATE-REDESIGN-NEEDED.md` §5). A live grant is grant-projection membership (SA-6.4). `awaiting_report`: claimed, no final report, before `last_window_end + report_horizon`. `settled`: everything else. (r5.1) A reported record has had its signatures erased (SA-9), so nothing more can leave for it, and it no longer holds a live slot; this keeps grant-covered synchronous writes from filling the live caps (§1.3 item 28).

**SA-6.6 Owner scope.** Every command and read on the machine plane starts by loading the record under the caller's full owner key; another principal's id and a missing id produce the same `not_found`, byte for byte, with nothing written, audited or computed differently. Only after the owner's record is found are `invalid_claim` and `invalid_report` audited. There is no lazy work to come before the load (R3.4, F-2).
- **(r5.1) Operator carve-out.** Operator surfaces are not principal-scoped, and operators are not principals. An authenticated operator or admin (the local allowed user through the E64 gate or a card entry; a hosted session with its role) addresses a v2 record by its record handle (SA-2) or its full record key, never by a bare `request_id`: Vote resolves through the card entry (local) or the MAC-bound record key (hosted); AdminCancel, Renotify, the hosted detail GET and the snapshot items use the handle or key. A handle, or on Telegram a `request_id`, that matches more than one record is refused as ambiguous and changes nothing.
- **(r5.1) Grant carve-out.** `revoke_grant(alias)` is alias-global de-escalation, exactly as v1 revoke is today (`daemon.go:896-906`): it revokes every live v2 grant record for the alias whatever its principal, and its response bytes reveal nothing about whose grant existed. `list_grants` is principal-scoped for v2 grants. THREAT-MODEL states both (D3).

#### SA-7 Effects and audit: one rule

- Effects run after the commit is confirmed (spec §6.1): a response or callback answer that reports the new state, a Telegram call, a waiter wake-up, signatures leaving the signer, an SSH `Start`, a report, a forward to hosted.
- Every event is appended to the record's outbox in the same commit as its transition. The flusher writes events to the sink with `event_key = (record, seq)`, so a re-run is a recognisable duplicate, then commits `audited_through` (Flush). (r5.1) Every commit that appends events nudges the flusher as a post-commit effect, locally and on hosted; the tick and the hosted worker only retry, (r5.5) on the schedule below. Noise events (`vote_replay`, (r5.1) `vote_refused`, `invalid_claim`, `invalid_report`) beyond 32 unflushed ((r5.5, critique of the fold) beyond 1 on a shed record, SA-9) are folded into `noise_dropped`, which the next flushed event reports.
- **No signature leaves the signer until the outbox is flushed through the decision, and no payload is shed or record deleted until it is flushed completely.** A failing sink therefore delays Claim, so the record may end `expired_unclaimed`; it never changes state.
- **(r5.5) The flush retry schedule.** A record is *unflushed* while `audited_through` is below its outbox's last sequence number. Each owner lists its unflushed records apart from its time-driven due list: locally an in-memory projection that the single writer updates after each confirmed commit and rebuilds at Startup (SA-1 rule 1); on hosted the existing slot column `audited_complete = 0`, under a partial index, read by `UnflushedV2(limit)` (A9). Every second the flusher (local; on hosted, the one flusher goroutine the worker starts) retries every listed record, nearest `next_due_at` first (no `next_due_at` last), at most `FlushBatch` (256) per pass, so the record whose window closes first drains first. The sink is shared, so a pass stops its flush retries at its first sink failure, and the next pass, a second later, retries; that stop is memory only and decides nothing. The post-commit nudge stays the fast path. **(r5.5, critique of the fold) On each owner the flusher is one goroutine and performs every Flush** (SA-1 rule 4): the post-commit nudges and the per-second retry pass both run in it, and on hosted no handler writes to the sink itself. r5.5 had each hosted handler run its own post-commit Flush beside the worker's retries, so two writers could emit one `event_key` at once. So a sink that recovers drains every unflushed outbox within a second per `FlushBatch` records, with no further command on the record, and a dead sink costs one failed write per second. The list is separate from `DueV2` and the due index, so unflushed rows never fill a due pass and never delay Clock. r5–r5.4 listed "a Flush retry" in the worker's `Next` over `DueV2` rows, which `next_due_at` never selected for an outbox: a Flush that failed once waited for the record's next deadline, often `claim_end`, when Clock closed the window first.
- The local sink is the AuditLog. On hosted the committed row is itself the audit fact and stderr is a projection (F-5). Local v1 keeps today's after-response, fail-open audit (SA-10).
- **(r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-1) Local machine-plane refusals with no outbox to carry them.** Today the daemon audits every request, refusals included: `respondError` writes the error line and then `d.audit(…, "error")` on both the written and the failed-write path (`pkg/signerkit/daemon.go:762-771`; the "every request is audited" contract in `handleRequestGrant`'s doc comment, `:774-777`), and `TestSignPath_RejectsTransferVerb` pins the row (`src/signer/daemon_transfer_test.go:329-331`, through `authModeFor`, which fails on a missing row, `daemon_grant_test.go:89-97`). A v2 refusal that stores no record has no outbox. So every local v2 socket request answered with an error code by no commit that appends an event for it (a refused Submit, `reserved_verb`, `invalid_request`, `conflict`, `queue_full`, `submit_window_closed`, `submit_window_ahead`, `request_too_large`, `request_unrenderable`, `not_found`, `record_corrupt`, `lifecycle_unavailable`, `storage_uncertain`), and every refusal in the A12 proxy branch (its own checks and the hosted error codes it passes through, (r5.6) `busy` included), writes exactly one `AuditEvent` exactly as `respondError` does: after the response, fail-open, status `error`, with `Approval` metadata carrying the kind, the caller's `request_id`, the principal and `transition = refused{code}`. The line is a pure function of the caller's own request and the code, so a foreign id's `not_found` and an absent id's are the same line (SA-6.6). It never feeds back into an answer or a record. It is not an outbox event, so it does not gate Claim or Shed. Refusals that commit an event (`invalid_claim`, `invalid_report`, `vote_refused`) stay outbox events and get no second line. Like `respondError`'s audit today, these lines are not capped: each answers one socket request from an `allowed_uids` peer.

#### SA-8 Recovery rule

- **Startup** (local signer, hosted worker, executor): (r5.2) the local owners first take the store-directory ownership flock (SA-1 rule 1; a held store is fatal at process start); list and load every record, validate it, build the projections, mint a new epoch, and run `Next` or `JobNext` on every record ((r5.3) `Next` applies Clock at the wall clock, SA-1 rule 3.3; r5.2's per-record clock floor and the seeded or carried mark are withdrawn). Nothing else runs at startup: no ordered recovery phases, no card reattach, no "signer starting" gate. The Telegram poller and the socket listener start after Startup returns (r5.1, E18). (r5.1) On the local stores, Startup first removes orphan `.tmp-*` files under the exclusive flock (B0), then lists and loads every record inside **one** securestate View transaction (each transaction fsyncs the directory, so per-file transactions would cost 65 536 fsyncs), and the local signer serves later reads from the decoded write-through cache it builds there (B1).
- **Quarantine.** A regular record file that fails validation (decode, schema, version, size or canonical form) is quarantined by its name: every command for that key answers `record_corrupt` (signer) or `report_auth_lost` (executor); it counts against the live caps; it is never dispatched, reported, shed, deleted or overwritten automatically; `status` reports it. (r5.1) A directory entry that cannot be a record at all (a symlink, directory, FIFO or other non-regular file, or a name outside the store's grammar) makes `ListNames` fail, and the store refuses to open: every v2 command answers `lifecycle_unavailable` (executor: every async call answers `executor_unavailable`), `status` names the entry, and the operator removes it. An unexpected object in a 0700 store directory means something outside the signer wrote there, so the whole store fails closed.
- **An uncertain commit is a crash of that store's owner.** r4.1's undone-effect facts (STATE-MODEL M7) are not kept.
  - Signer: poison the store under its operation mutex (as `policy_journal_linux.go:159-167,313-316`), and (r5.8, critique of the fold) set the owner stop's `stopping` latch in that same critical section, before `ErrCommitUncertain` returns to the committing caller, so the caller (an actor among them) already sees the latch; answer `storage_uncertain` to the caller and to every command until the reopen ((r5.8, critique of the fold) at the reopen the poison's `storage_uncertain` takes precedence over the latch's `lifecycle_unavailable`, for commands and reads alike); cancel and join the actors ((r5.8) by the owner stop below, so an interrupted send is not a live failure; (r5.8, critique of the fold) the reopen runs on its own goroutine, the store's reopen loop, started by the poisoning commit, never on the committing caller's goroutine, so an actor whose own commit was uncertain returns and is joined rather than joining itself); discard all lifecycle memory ((r5.3) the reopen carries only the ownership descriptor; r5.2's carried mark is withdrawn); reopen with bounded backoff (1 s doubling to 30 s), mint a new epoch, and run Startup. Every grant dies with the old epoch, which fails closed. Legacy v1 requests are untouched. (r5.2) The reopen goes through the retained ownership descriptor (SA-1 rule 1), so no other process can take the store between the close and the reopen: it is `securestate.OpenDirectory(holderFD, …)`, never `OpenAt`, and it never calls `flock` again. (r5.3) r5.2's clock break, which reused this path, is withdrawn.
  - Executor: exit non-zero. The user unit restarts it on failure (C4), the same way the signer unit restarts today (`scripts/install.sh:182`).
  - Hosted: there is no cache. The operation answers 503 "storage uncertain — retry", and the next command re-reads in a fresh transaction.
- **(r5.8, review 9 #2) An owner's stop is not a failure.** An owner stops on SIGTERM (`Close`), and the local signer also stops its lifecycle at the uncertain-commit reopen above. Cancelling an effect in the middle of a call yields the same transport error that a live failure does, and SA-4 terminalizes live failures (K19). So the stop has its own order and its own classification:
  1. **Intake stops.** The local signer's signal context ends `srv.Listen` and the Telegram poller (signer `main.go:219,321`; A8 starts the poller under that context) before the deferred `svc.Close` (`:288-292`) runs. `Listen` returns only after every connection handler has returned (`socket.go:147`), and each handler's `connCtx` derives from the signal context (`socket.go:200`), so legacy v1 requests have already ended `timeout`, as today, before `Close` runs. (r5.8, critique of the fold) Today `Service.Close` (`service.go:138-144`) returns early when there is no policy journal and otherwise only stops hosted policy recovery and closes the journal; A7 makes it close the attached lifecycle first (A7's `service.go` seam), so the deferred `svc.Close` is what runs the lifecycle's `Close`. The executor stops accepting on its socket the same way and lets in-flight handlers answer (C2 item 6). At the reopen, the poisoned store already refuses every command.
  2. **The latch.** Under the store lock, the owner sets a memory-only `stopping` flag. From then on its commit function refuses every commit: a command still in flight answers `lifecycle_unavailable` (transient, SA-5; the executor answers `executor_unavailable`), and a tap is answered as a `storage_uncertain` tap is (E51). So the last commit is the one that held the lock before the latch. (r5.8, critique of the fold) On the executor, an `exec_submit` whose own job commit landed before the latch is not refused: it answers with that job, as C1's immediate answer does (C2 item 6). Hosted keeps no latch: every hosted command is its own transaction, and `closeOrdinaryWorkers` (A10) cancels and joins the worker, the flusher and the assist pool. (r5.8, critique of the fold) Hosted's cut is therefore per goroutine: HTTP command handlers may commit until the database closes, as v1 handlers do today (`httpSrv.Shutdown`'s 5 s, signer-server `main.go:392-402`, ends before a 55 s `WaitMany` long-poll does, and `PolicyRuntime.Close` goes on to `Server.Close` regardless, `policy_runtime.go:482-490`); the worker, the flusher and the assist commit nothing after `closeOrdinaryWorkers` has joined them; and `Pool.TrySubmit` after `Pool.Close` answers `closed` (A4), which a Submit's assist effect treats as an interruption: it commits no assist slot, never `{unavailable, "assist busy"}`, and Clock fills the slot at `assist_deadline`.
  3. **Cancel and join.** The owner cancels its own effect context, which no caller's or signal context derives, so nothing is cancelled before the latch. That covers the tick, the flusher, the notifier actors with their edit-retry timers, assist jobs and waiters, and on the executor the `JobNext` actors. It then joins them. A Bot API call cannot be cancelled (`tgbotapi` takes no context; the client timeout bounds it, `src/signer/backend/telegram.go:247-263`), so the join waits at most that bound. Every wait the adapter makes (a 429 `retry_after`, E50's budget) selects on the context. (r5.8, critique of the fold) On the executor, `RunPhased` runs under the effect context: its cancellation closes the SSH client at once, as today's watcher does (`src/mcp/ssh/client.go:133-141`), so a step running after `Start` ends at once (the remote command may still run, exactly as after an executor crash, and the step reads `indeterminate` after the restart), and a dial or handshake in progress ends within `Client.Timeout` (30 s, `:84-87,101-113`). The join is therefore bounded by 30 s plus the in-flight signer or report call's own deadline, below systemd's default 90 s stop timeout (C4).
  4. **Interrupted, not failed.** (r5.8, critique of the fold) An effect is interrupted exactly when the owner's `stopping` latch is set at the moment its call returns; the effect reads the latch, never the returned error's type and never a derived context. A per-call deadline, E50's 60 s budget, or the `tgbotapi` client timeout ending while the latch is clear is a live failure, whether or not it is built as a context deadline, and terminalizes as SA-4 says. (The latch is set before the effect context is cancelled, so every cancellation by the stop is seen as an interruption.) An effect that returns, with any result, after the latch is interrupted. It commits nothing (step 2 would refuse it anyway), makes no further external call (no INCOMPLETE edit, no status edit, no edit retry, no assist resend, no SSH dispatch, no report), and is never classified as a live failure. SA-4's live failures (`error` plus INCOMPLETE edits, K19) and E68's committed provider failure are only those observed while the owner is not stopping.
  5. **Recovery is the crash rules, unchanged.** An interrupted effect leaves exactly a crash cut. A burst or an unretired send entry with no outcome reads `uncertain` under the next epoch and is resent only by `/renotify` (spec §6.1, E50); this includes a send that Telegram accepted whose `sent` commit lost to the latch. An unconfirmed edit entry is retired and a fresh one is sent (SA-4 step 3). A display state without its `status_edits` entry is edited once (step 4). An empty assist slot is filled by Clock at `assist_deadline`, and the provider is not called again (K8). An unflushed outbox is flushed; a line the sink took before the cut is a recognisable duplicate by `event_key` (SA-7). On the executor, a marker without a result becomes `indeterminate` and is never re-dispatched, and a job with no marker for its next step resumes by SA-5. Legacy v1 requests end as today (E8b).
  So a stop changes no record beyond what was committed before the latch, decides nothing and edits no card, and a stop during a send is recovered exactly as a crash at that point is. r5.1–r5.7 had no stop rule, so §1.5's live-failure rule overrode B2's "SIGTERM leaves v2 records untouched".
- **Write-ahead states, each completed by the owner's reconciler:**
  - a burst or (r5.4) an **unretired** send entry (`first` or `replacement`) with no outcome: current epoch → in progress; another epoch → `uncertain`, and only `/renotify` resends. (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C2-3) A send entry Renotify has retired, and a burst whose `gen` is below `renotify_gen`, are superseded: they need no driver and the view no longer reads them as in flight (SA-6.3);
  - (r5.4) a pending record on which a resend is due (`renotify_gen` above every burst's `gen`) → notify step 1 sends one burst under that `gen`. Renotify's commit is the authorization, so a cut after it and before the burst's append resends exactly once, even with an empty card set, and a cut after the append is `uncertain` like any other burst;
  - an edit entry not confirmed → from the current epoch, the actor re-runs it from its in-memory challenge (idempotent), (r5.5, critique of the fold) driven by SA-4 step 3's in-memory retry after a possible send; (r5.2) from another epoch, one commit retires it and appends a fresh edit entry, whose edit is then sent (SA-4 step 3, E48);
  - a non-empty outbox → Flush ((r5.5) driven by SA-7's retry schedule each second, not by `next_due_at`);
  - an empty assist slot past its deadline, or an empty decision past `request_expires_at` → Clock (r5.3: at the wall clock);
  - (r5.3) an empty mint slot or held signature bytes past `claim_end` → Clock (`window_closed` or the Erase);
  - a job without `signer_ack` → idempotent resubmit inside the submit window;
  - a marker with no result from another epoch → `indeterminate`; (r5.1) from the current epoch → in flight, completed only by the job's actor that committed it (SA-5 step 3);
  - a final job with `report_result` empty → resend the same bytes until a definitive answer settles it (r5.2, SA-5 step 7);
  - a delivered result without `first_delivered_at` → nothing (retention treats it as undelivered, the conservative side);
  - (r5.4) none for `final_at`: it commits with the transition that makes the job final, so no final job lacks it.
- **Telegram updates redelivered from offset 0** (`telegram.go:339`) become idempotent Vote commands judged by SA-6.1 at processing time: a tap already applied is inert (and audited, r5.1); a tap on a card that is still valid counts once; a tap on a retired or undelivered card is refused (and audited). E64's redelivery cut still drops redelivered slash commands ((r5.3, critique pass 1) an `update_id` cut on the update stream, no longer a `Message.Date` comparison with the signer's start time). (r5.1) The vote's `ts`, and so `decided_at`, is the signer's processing time, because callback queries carry no date; `answerCallbackQuery` on an old query fails ("query is too old") and is best effort, never fatal; a tap processed while the store is `storage_uncertain` is consumed by Telegram, so the operator must tap again (E51).

#### SA-9 Retention and bounds

- **Erase.** Signature bytes are erased (r5.3) by Clock at `claim_end` (the last window end once claimed) or when the final report is accepted; the header keeps the mint digest.
- **(r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-7) R1 ages count from `decided_at`.** Every record Shed or Delete can touch is decided (a pending record is `live`), and `decided_at` is a stamp committed in the commit that decides it (SA-1 rule 3.1; Clock's `expired` copies `request_expires_at`). "Oldest first" orders by it too. No R1 age is read from `created_at`, the file's mtime, the row's update time or any time a view derives (the final-report slot holds no time). r5–r5.4 named no start, so neither `Next` nor A9's `next_due_at` could compute the Shed or Delete instant.
- **Shed** reduces a record to its header in place. Allowed for a settled, fully audited record at retention (local 7 d; hosted `--approval-retention`, 7 d; (r5.4) `now ≥ decided_at + retention`), and under payload pressure, oldest first, also for a fully audited `awaiting_report` record. A shed record still answers idempotent resubmits, Get, List and Wait (`pruned: true`), Claim and Report. (r5.1) The header keeps the decision and the votes, and for a grant its alias, duration and host, so a shed record still says who decided and a shed grant still answers `grant_id` and `grant_expires_at`. This replaces r4.1's tombstones and report stubs.
- **Delete** is allowed only for a settled, fully audited record past `submit_not_after + 24 h`, and either past its age limit ((r5.4) `now ≥ decided_at +` the limit; local 7 d; hosted `--approval-max-age`, (r5.1) default 0 = never) or under count pressure, oldest first (hosted: only when an age limit is set, E23). Submit refuses a new id after its own `submit_not_after`, so a retried old draft can never become a second approval (R1.4) (r5.3) while the record exists; after Delete, SA-1 rule 3's R-2 applies. (r5.1) Neither Shed nor Delete touches the newest-grant mark (SA-6.4).
- **Admission.** (r5.3) When a live cap is full, Submit first applies Clock to the records whose `next_due_at` has passed (each its own one-record commit; on hosted, each in an earlier transaction), so a window that ended under a second ago never holds a live slot (SA-1 rule 3.3). Submit checks the live caps, then the history caps. If a history cap is reached, the handler first runs Shed and Delete commands on eligible records (each its own one-record commit, under the same store lock) and then commits the Submit. If nothing can be reclaimed, Submit answers `queue_full` before any notification. Hosted counts both ceilings inside the Submit transaction, so two Submits cannot both pass.
- **Caps:** r4.1's live caps are kept (≤100 live records per principal and ≤256 live in total locally; the E52 quota on hosted). Local: ≤512 records with a full payload and ≤32 MiB of encoded payload ((r5.5) reserved, below); ≤65 536 records in total; each record ≤512 KiB encoded (r5.1). Hosted: ≤4096 full rows and ≤200 000 rows in total.
- **(r5.5) The local payload budget is a reservation.** r5–r5.4 checked the 32 MiB only at Submit, against what records held then. But an admitted record keeps growing: its assist slot, card entries, bursts, `status_edits`, signature bytes and outbox events are all written after admission, and a live or unaudited record cannot be shed. Near the ceiling those mandatory transitions would either exceed the budget or need a refusal the plan never defined, stranding accepted work (`securestate.WriteFile` bounds only the single file, `internal/securestate/store_linux.go:348`). So:
  - `approvalplan.R1Reservation(rec)` is the encoded size of the record with every set, slot and counter that can still grow at its maximum for that record: assist at `ValidateAssist`'s bound, the vote, card, burst and `status_edits` sets at their SA-3 bounds, the outbox at SA-3's `OutboxMax` non-noise events plus 32 noise events and the fold ((r5.5, critique of the fold) SA-3 now states that bound), the mint slot holding the signature set for its own steps, and the final-report summary. It is a pure function of the frozen header and the immutable payload parts (plan bytes and ReviewSnapshot), which fix the kind, the step count and each step's command length. It is therefore the same at every state of an unshed record, and Startup rebuilds the budget counter from the records (SA-1 rule 1). It is at most the R1 bound, and it covers the whole encoded record, header included.
  - Each full-payload record counts at its `R1Reservation` from its Submit commit until Shed or Delete. Nothing else releases it, so the sum over full records bounds the encoded bytes they can ever hold.
  - **(r5.5, critique of the fold) Shed records and quarantined files.** r5.5 said Shed "leaves only the header", but a shed record still changes: a late Report on a shed `awaiting_report` record fills `final_report` and appends its event (E73), and refused Claims and Reports on it append `invalid_claim` and `invalid_report` noise (SA-6.6). A shed record is settled or `awaiting_report`, so its decision and mint slots are already filled, and Revoke touches only live grants; the late Report's slot is the only one it can still fill. So on a shed record the noise fold is 1 (a second unflushed noise event folds into `noise_dropped`), its outbox holds at most the report event and one noise event, and each shed record stays within `ShedMax`, the header's A1a bound plus those two events at their A1a sizes. Shed records lie outside the payload budget, and together they stay within 65 536 × `ShedMax`, a bound of their own. A quarantined file (SA-8) cannot be decoded, so no `R1Reservation` can be computed for it; it counts as a full record at its encoded size (≤512 KiB) until the operator removes it, so the budget never under-counts bytes on disk.
  - A new id is admitted only if the sum plus its own `R1Reservation` fits 32 MiB. Otherwise the handler first sheds eligible records (SA-9's Shed rule, oldest first: settled and fully audited, or a fully audited `awaiting_report` record under pressure), each its own one-record commit, and if the reservation still does not fit, Submit answers `queue_full` before any notification. Live and unaudited records are never shed to make room, so a budget full of them refuses new work, which fails closed.
  - Every later transition of an admitted record stays inside its own reservation by construction (A1a's bound proof, A1b's property), so no transition of admitted work can push the budget past 32 MiB, and none is refused for lack of room. The 512-full-record and per-record bounds are unchanged.
  - A1a records `R1Reservation` for a one-step, 200-byte write with a 500-byte reason, and asserts that 256 such live records reserve at most half the budget; if they do not, A1a raises the budget (and B1's cache estimate with it), never the live caps or the K19 limits.
  - Hosted keeps no byte budget: its ceilings count rows, and each row is bounded by the R1 bound that A1a proves for the worst case, so it needs no reservation. The executor already reserves per job (below).
- **Executor** (R2): ≤100 live jobs. (r5.2) Each job reserves its worst-case **encoded record**: its own encoded non-body part, measured at admission from its draft, plus the base64 of its maximum output (≤8 MiB raw) and the per-step body overhead (SA-3 R2). All of it counts against a 256 MiB budget. **(r5.3, OR-5) The reservation lifecycle:** a live job counts at its reservation; a final job counts at `R2FinalCharge(job)`, its actual encoded size plus the encoded maximum of its still-empty post-final slots (`report_result` with its longest code, `first_delivered_at`, `bodies_removed` with its longest cause), a pure function of the record in the shape of `R1Reservation` ((r5.5, critique of the fold) r5.5 counted the encoded size alone, so those slots, which commit after the job is final and must not be refused, grew past what the budget counted). `R2FinalCharge` is at most the job's reservation, which covers every slot filled, and it never rises after finality, because every later commit fills a slot already charged at its maximum or removes bytes; body removal releases the bodies' share (E75). Bodies are removed 7 d after the job ends ((r5.4) at `final_at + 7 d`, judged at the wall clock in the removing commit, SA-1 rule 3.5); under pressure only when delivered at least 24 h before, never while undelivered (r4.1's E75 rule). (r5.4) r5–r5.3 stored no completion time, so after a restart the job's end could be reconstructed only from `created_at`, a marker's `at` (both too early for a long-running job) or the file's mtime (moved by every later report and delivery commit). (r5.2) The raw token is removed once the job is final, its report is settled or was never owed (SA-5 step 7), and the bodies are gone.
  - **(r5.2) Whole-job deletion has one predicate**, and both the age path and the count path use it. A job file may be deleted only when all of these hold: (1) the job is final; (2) its bodies are removed (`bodies_removed` set) or it never had any; (3) it is report-settled, meaning `report_result` is `acked` or `refused{code}`, or the job never owed a report; (4) `now ≥ submit_not_after + 24 h`. Then it is deleted 30 d after the job ends ((r5.4) `now ≥ final_at + 30 d`), or earlier under count pressure, oldest first ((r5.4) by `final_at`). r5.1's age and count paths checked only (1) and (4). So count pressure could delete an undelivered result before its 7 d, and either path could delete the token of a report whose acknowledgement was lost. With (2), an undelivered body under 7 d old protects its job file. With (3), a lost ack keeps the job and its token until a resend settles it.
  - A report that will never settle cannot hold a job forever. Each answer is either definitive, which settles it as `refused{code}` (including `not_found` once the signer has deleted the header, §1.3 item 17), or transient, which means the signer is unreachable or retrying. An executor whose signer is unreachable cannot run new work either.
  - When only protected jobs fill the 10 000 files, admission answers `executor_full`, which fails closed.
  - At most 10 000 job files. Await on a body-less job answers `result_evicted` or `result_expired` with the class.

#### SA-10 Frozen v1, as separate small types

- **Local v1:** an in-memory `LegacyRequest`. (r5.2, OQ3 (b)) v1 `sign` matches no grant, so every v1 approval is a `LegacyRequest` (or `d.Backend` outside production, E8e). It keeps today's `sync.Once` first-of {tap, deadline} semantics (`telegram.go:595-606,774-795`); it renders through the shared renderer with K3's label and a `v:` challenge, (r5.1) after one assist attempt with alignment `not_assessed`, as today's explainer runs before the send (E8b); it is never journaled; the v1 response and its audit stay today's, after the response and fail-open (`daemon.go:674-743`); (r5.4) after resolution it stays as a bounded memory-only tombstone, and every tap that does not resolve it writes one fail-open `legacy_vote_replay` or `legacy_vote_refused` audit line (E8b, K15); r4.1 §1.3 item 20 is withdrawn. The production path never reaches the direct `Backend` methods (E8e).
- **Hosted v1:** R3, with `handleVote` running `withSession`, the id and resolution checks, role, (r5.1) the MAC check, then step-up, and then the unchanged `SubmitVote`, whose same-decision resume is untouched, with the HTTP-layer duplicate audit and lost-race 409 of E7 step 7. (r5.8) Every authenticated POST it refuses on a resolved row, at the challenge check or as a duplicate writes one fail-open inert line (E7 steps 3, 5 and 7; K15).

#### SA-11 Property and model harness, and the explicit rows

- **Harness (A1b; grafted from Proposal 2).** `approvalplan/statetest` (stdlib only) generates records, commands, clocks and epochs, and checks: replay is deterministic; (r5.4) every command meets its own **replay row** below (r5–r5.3 said every command is idempotent, giving the same record and answer when applied twice, up to noise events and except Marker; Claim contradicts that by design, because a repeat Claim adds 1 to `claim_count`); write-once slots are never overwritten and counters never decrease; views never write; for every crash prefix, with the last commit landed or not, recovery under a new epoch never re-performs a send or a dispatch and leaves no write-ahead state without its driver; exactly one signature set ever leaves; foreign and absent ids answer identically. **(r5.1) Added:** under any interleaving of ticks, waiter nudges and Startup with a live step, a marked step is dispatched exactly once (single-flight); no Shed, Delete or Revoke ever makes a dead grant live; (r5.3) Clock's assist snapshot is a pure function of the header; (r5.1's clock property is replaced by r5.3's, below); every duplicate or refused vote appends exactly one noise event (until the fold). **(r5.2) Added** (its clock-break, K16-floor and Startup-floor properties are replaced by r5.3's, below): for every crash prefix around an edit, a challenge is valid only on the latest unretired entry, and an interrupted edit from another epoch is superseded, never replayed; `JobNext` never Claims for an unsigned step after the first dispatch and never refuses one on a signature window; a job file is never deleted while its bodies are protected or its report is owed; an `exec_submit` whose input window has passed never creates a job. **(r5.4) Added:** for every crash prefix, the bursts of a record carry distinct `gen` values, every one at most `renotify_gen`, and a `gen` above 0 appears only after a committed Renotify raised the counter to it, so no burst is sent without its authorization and none is lost after it; a send entry with a `msg_id` has `outcome = sent`, and the notification view never reads `sending` or `uncertain` from an `edit` entry; `final_at` is set exactly when `JobView` is final, in the commit that makes it so, and never changes; a Vote's decision equals `Decide(EffectivePolicy(rec, flags), votes)` whatever policy values the caller tries to supply, and `sub_principal` never changes it. **(r5.5) Added:** for every record reachable from a frozen record by any command sequence, its encoded size is at most `R1Reservation` of that frozen record, and `R1Reservation` computed at any unshed state equals the value at freeze; for every crash prefix and every pattern of sink failures that ends with a working sink, every unflushed outbox drains within ⌈N / `FlushBatch`⌉ + 1 retry passes after the sink recovers, where N is the number of unflushed records at that moment, the records nearest their `next_due_at` first, with no further command (the retry list is a driver independent of `next_due_at`; (r5.5, critique of the fold) r5.5 said "two passes", which holds only for N ≤ `FlushBatch`, and hosted allows 4096 full rows; the generator covers N > `FlushBatch`); for every pattern of edit results in which possible sends end in a success, with no further command, an in-epoch edit entry is confirmed by the actor's retry (SA-4 step 3); a shed record never exceeds `ShedMax` (SA-9). **(r5.8) Added:** an owner stop at any point (any effect in flight, either result returned after the latch) commits nothing after its latch, makes no external call after it, and leaves a store that the next Startup treats exactly as a crash at the latch (SA-8). (r5.8, critique of the fold) The property is per owner: the local signer and the executor are checked against their latch; hosted, which has no latch, is checked at its cut (SA-8 step 2): the worker, the flusher and the assist commit nothing after `closeOrdinaryWorkers` has joined them, and no interrupted assist commits a slot. **(r5.3) Time properties**, holding under arbitrary wall steps in process, across a reopen and across a restart, with independent wall and elapsed fakes plus a suspend fake (SA-1 rule 3):
  1. no stamp exceeds the wall clock at its commit, and no bytes leave while `decided_at > now + 5 s`;
  2. Apply is the command applied after Clock; Clock is idempotent, never removes or overwrites a slot, and writes only deadlines ≤ its `now`;
  3. a view shows a time fact only after its commit; every reported refusal **on an existing record** was committed in its own answer's commit. (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-9) The one uncommitted exception is SA-1 rule 3.5's refusal of a **new id** (`submit_window_closed`, `submit_window_ahead`, and the executor's `exec_submit` window check), which stores nothing by design; the harness checks it separately: no record or job, no event, no effect, and a later acceptance of the same bytes is R-2's case;
  4. a request shown expired never takes a vote, and a window shown closed never releases;
  5. an ended grant is never live again in its epoch, and no grant outlives either of its ends;
  6. a K16 failure is committed and never dispatched, and is judged only for the first step with no result, when signed (D3);
  7. no clock reading changes an epoch, cancels an actor, yields `uncertain`, or refuses Revoke, AdminCancel or deny-all; (r5.3, critique pass 1) a Deny tap is refused on a clock reading only as OA-5's stale view (Clock's assist fill has raised the View's revision above its card entry's), and only until the actor's edit is confirmed; a Deny on a challenge still current after Clock is never refused by a clock reading; the harness checks the Deny half only on challenges still current after Clock, and C-46 pins the stale case;
  8. across a restart or reopen, landed or not, nothing committed is contradicted, and Clock writes nothing due after that wall clock (D1);
  9. with a gate fake and a correct gate clock, no step executes at gate time ≥ `Exp`.

  **(r5.4) Replay rows.** "Applied twice" means the same command applied again right after its first application, with no other command between, at the same `now` or a later one at which no further Clock deadline has fallen due. A due Clock fact commits on the first application only, so the second commits none (X3). Each command has exactly one row, except Renotify, which has one per pre-state (r5.4, critique of r5.4):
  - **Exact: same record, same answer.** Submit with the same draft; AssistResult; Clock; Flush; Erase; Shed; Delete; Revoke; Report as an exact duplicate (acked); each notify step (its guard is false the second time); Renotify's exact sub-case, from a state in which a resend is already due ("re-notify already pending" both times, SA-4 step 5); and on the executor, `exec_submit` with the same `input_hash`, and the result, outcome, `report_result` and `first_delivered_at` commits (the `final_at` a result or outcome commit set is not set again).
  - **Noise only: the record changes by one noise event (until the fold), same answer.** Vote of either decision (`vote_replay`, answered with the stored outcome); every refused tap or POST (`vote_refused`); `invalid_claim`; `invalid_report`. (r5.4) The legacy v1 table follows the same row with one fail-open audit line instead of an outbox event (E8b); (r5.8) so does hosted v1, with E7's inert line, (r5.8, critique of the fold) but with no fold: at most one line per POST, written only for an operator-role session (E7 step 3), and uncapped, like SA-7's local refusal lines, because each answers one authenticated HTTP request and a v1 row has no outbox to fold into.
  - **Counter only: Claim.** A repeat Claim that returns signatures returns the byte-identical set, never calls the minter again, leaves the mint slot, its digest and every other slot and set unchanged, and adds exactly 1 to `claim_count`, the only change. A repeat Claim with any other answer (`not_ready`, `expired`, a terminal state, `materialization_error`, `execution_reported`) changes nothing and gives the same answer.
  - **Same record, the second answer says so.** AdminCancel and each deny-all item ("already resolved" after "canceled"); Marker (`already_marked`, and no `Start`); (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-6/C2-5) Renotify from a state with no resend due (the first raises `renotify_gen` and retires the cards; the second changes nothing and answers "re-notify already pending"). Rows are therefore per (command, pre-state) where a command has two, and Renotify is the only such command.
  - Two Renotify commands separated by the burst append they authorized are two authorizations, not a replay; a redelivered `/renotify` is dropped before it becomes a command (E64's `update_id` cut).

  Plus the static time-read test (SA-1 rule 3.4), and (r5.3) OA-5's revision check (a challenge on an entry below the View's revision after Clock never yields a vote) and OD-3's split (every definitive answer `JobNext` consumes makes the job final). The residuals R-1 to R-5 of SA-1 rule 3 are the only exceptions. A model of signer, executor, Telegram and gate fakes drives random schedules through these checks. A6, A10, B1, C1 and C2 run the same properties against their real stores. (r5.8, critique of the fold) Which owner runs which property: the R1 properties, crash-prefix recovery through Startup and the signer's stop property run in A6 (memory store, through A6's own `startup.go`), B1 (file store) and A10 (hosted, with the stop property at hosted's cut); the R2 store-level properties (`exec_submit` idempotency, the input window never creating a job, reservation bounds) run in C1 with results applied through A1b's pure transitions; and every property that needs `JobNext`, C2's retention or the executor's latch (single-flight with a live step, `JobNext`'s Claim rules, OD-3's split, crash-prefix recovery of jobs, a job file never deleted while protected, `final_at`, and the executor's stop property) runs in C2. C1 has no `JobNext` and no latch, so it runs none of those.
- **Explicit, tested rows** (STATE-MODEL §5.1). (r5.3, critique pass 1) 21 of its 42 obligations stay as single-site rules: OA-1 (Decide over votes, (r5.4) under the record's effective policy), OA-4 (one rule per regime), OA-5 (challenge validity, (r5.3) including the card entry's revision), OA-7 (grant liveness), OA-8 and OT-3 (E71 at Submit and at mint), OS-2 (one window function), OS-3 (erasure plus E28's sanitizer), OX-2 (`JobNext` plus `AggregateJobClass`), OX-3 (report acceptance), ON-1 and ON-2 (the notify steps), OR-1 (the delete predicate and the submit window), OR-5 (admission per store), OD-1 (effects after commit; uncertain = crash), OD-2 (the outbox rule), (r5.3) OD-3 (one definitive/transient split, SA-5), OC-1 (time closures are committed facts or latched held deadlines, SA-1 rule 3), OT-1 (`ReadCurrent`), OT-2 (E34 at mint), OV-1 (the agent-tool join). The rest hold by construction or disappear with their record. §6.2 rows C-28 to C-33 and (r5.3, critique pass 1) C-35, C-39, C-46 and C-47, and (r5.4) C-49 to C-54, and (r5.5) C-58 to C-60, and (r5.6) C-61 to C-63, test the new ones.

#### SA-12 K-ruling conformance and spec-body errata

- **K1–K20 are implemented as ratified, and no amendment is a default.**
  - K6, K8: one assist attempt, an immutable slot, and the digest in the decision event, (r5.2) and in the decision slot's `assist_bound`, which survives Shed (E23).
  - K10: the executor owns its job journal and claims; approval wakes it into Claim and SSH.
  - K11: all four bullets hold. Bullet 2 resumes through a repeat Claim of the signer's stored set; bullet 4 holds because a result commits atomically with its bodies.
  - K12: (r5.3) v2 `expired` is "a sweeper/CAS terminal state", as ratified: Clock writes it, on the tick or first in the command that would otherwise act on the record (SA-1 rule 3.3); `wait_elapsed` never writes; local v1 keeps today's behaviour; hosted v1 gets a 24 h TTL. (r5.1) "Signature TTL begins only at approval and remains at most five minutes": `decided_at` is the wall clock at the deciding commit, never a high-water value, and no signature is minted with a TS more than 5 s ahead of the wall clock (SA-1 rule 3). (r5.3) An expiry is shown only once it is a committed slot, so it stays a fact across any clock event, reopen or restart; r5.2's mark and break sentences are withdrawn. A step back stretches only what Clock has not yet written (SA-1 rule 3, R-1).
  - K14: per-record 0600, versioned, bounded JSON under securestate hygiene, written by fsync and atomic rename.
  - K3: legacy v1 requests carry the protocol-generated label and no rationale-alignment assessment; (r5.1) local legacy cards keep the AI advisory summary through one assist attempt before the card (E8b, spec §9.1).
  - K15: a random challenge, one vote per operator, the threshold crossed in one commit, duplicates inert and (r5.1) every one audited (E61), (r5.4) local legacy v1 taps included (E8b). (r5.4) The threshold and the requester are the record's own (SA-4 Vote's effective policy), as hosted v1 binds them today.
  - K16: per-step local checks, with no extra round trip; (r5.3) judged on the holder's (wall, elapsed) pair for the step about to be dispatched (SA-1 rule 3.4).
  - K17: `sub_principal` only narrows the per-client filter.
  - K18: admin-only cancel and a confirmed snapshot, with the admin role checked per item.
  - K19: the card is sent only after the parts commit; (r5.1) any send failure observed live, on a part or on the card, means `error` with INCOMPLETE edits; `uncertain` is only a crash cut (SA-4, E50); (r5.8) a send that an owner's stop interrupts is such a cut, never a live failure (SA-8).
- **Spec-body errata (not K):** spec §6.1's "minting claim at the threshold" becomes "mint at the first Claim, TS = `decided_at`, one durable set" (E21). Spec §5 and §6.2: `report_sequence` is fixed at 1 (E25), and `delivery_count`/`last_delivered_at` become `first_delivered_at` (SA-3 R2).

## 2. Package map and dependency rules

| Package (new unless noted) | Role | May import | Must never be imported by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `src/approvalplan` (+ `statetest`) | Contract and pure state core (§1.5): plan/draft types, validators, bounds, IDs and storage-key derivations, canonical hashes, the R1 and R2 record schemas, AssistResult schema, `AuditMetadata` (E62), `ValidateStepBytes` (E71), `AggregateJobClass` (E29), and the pure functions `View`, `Apply`, `Next`, `Class`, `Decide`, (r5.4) `EffectivePolicy`, `GrantLive`, `JobView`, `JobNext`, the Claim answer and the notify-step planner. `statetest` holds the generators, properties and model of §1.5 SA-11 | stdlib only (reserved prefixes are mirrored constants, drift-tested in A7) | gate |
| `src/redact` (adds `spans.go`) | Union-span API | existing | — (gate bytes unchanged) |
| `src/approvalreview` | Surface-neutral review snapshot, Telegram part packer, hosted view model | approvalplan, redact, policyreview (EscapeBytes) | gate, MCP |
| `src/approvalwire` | Local socket frames for v2 kinds and hosted `/v2/approvals` JSON | approvalplan | gate |
| `pkg/signerkit/assist` | Assessor interface, fail-closed provider input, watchdog runner, OpenAI-compatible provider | approvalplan, redact, net/http | gate, MCP |
| `internal/securestate` (adds `ListNames`, `Remove`; B0) | Hardened 0700/0600 directory store | existing | — |
| (r5.3) `internal/elapsed` (A1a) | The elapsed clock E of §1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4 (`CLOCK_BOOTTIME` through `unix.ClockGettime` on Linux, Go's monotonic reading elsewhere); the one injectable two-clock interface every holder reads; a fake whose wall and elapsed halves tests move independently, with a suspend step; and the static time-read test helper | stdlib, `golang.org/x/sys/unix` (already in every importer's closure) | `src/approvalplan`, which stays stdlib only and takes both readings as arguments. (r5.3, critique pass 1) The test-support package `internal/elapsed/elapsedtest` (the `Fake` and the static time-read helper) is imported only by `_test.go` files, including approvalplan's |
| `pkg/signerkit/lifecycle` (+ `lifecycletest`) | Local façade: the single-writer engine over `approvalplan.Apply`, projections, reconciler, outbox flusher, notifier actors, `LegacyRequest`, memory store (A6), file store (B1), Startup (A6, store-agnostic; (r5.8) moved from B2 to B1, (r5.8, critique of the fold) then to A6, with B1 adding the file store's side); defines its own `AuditWriter`/`Minter`/`Notifier`/`LegacyNotifier`/`Freezer`/`SnapshotBuilder`/`XferDigests` interfaces (E62) | approvalplan, assist (interface), `internal/securestate`, (r5.3) `internal/elapsed` | MCP, executor. **It must never import `pkg/signerkit`, `hosted`, `sqlitestore`, sqlite or webauthn** (`verify-lifecycle-closure`) |
| `pkg/signerkit/hosted` (adds `approvals_v2_*.go`) | Hosted façade over the same `Apply`, machine and human v2 routes, MAC challenges, ordinary worker | existing + lifecycle (test suite), approvalplan, approvalreview, assist, (r5.3) `internal/elapsed` (timers only) | local signer |
| `pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore` (adds migration 7, `approvals_v2.go`) | `approvals_v2` rows, v1 expiry columns, `ExpireLegacy`/`CancelLegacy` | existing | local signer |
| `src/mcp/sign` (adds `approval_client.go`) | MCP client for v2 kinds | approvalwire, lineframe | — |
| `src/mcp/registry` (adds `ReadCurrent`) | Fail-closed current-entry read (A13a) | existing | — |
| `src/mcp/jobplan` | Draft builder (classify, RouteSnapshot, TTL table, stop policy, bounds) and step runner (K16, stale_target via `ReadCurrent`, phased SSH, bounded capture) | approvalplan, classify, mcp/registry, mcp/ssh, (r5.3) `internal/elapsed` | signer |
| `src/mcp/ssh` (adds `phased.go`) | `RunPhased` | existing | — |
| `src/executorwire`, `src/executor`, `src/executor/cmd/sshgate-executor` | Singleton executor (Phase C): R2 job store and `JobNext` driver | approvalplan, approvalwire, jobplan, mcp/sign, mcp/registry, `internal/securestate`, (r5.3) `internal/elapsed` | signer, MCP (the MCP imports only executorwire through `src/mcp/execclient`) |

`pkg/signerkit` gains imports of `approvalplan`, `approvalwire` and `lifecycle`. (r5.3) `src/mcp/tools` (the A13b sync coordinator) also imports `internal/elapsed`; it adds no module to the MCP closure. `pkg/signerkit/hosted_v2_client.go` must not import `hosted` (cycle).

New Makefile gates (`verify-gate-closure`, `verify-mcp-closure` and `verify-lifecycle-closure` in A1a, `verify-executor-closure` in C4), all added to `preflight` (`Makefile:204`) and `.PHONY`:
- `verify-gate-closure`: `go list -deps ./src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate` must not match `approvalplan|approvalwire|approvalreview|/pkg/signerkit|/src/executor|/src/mcp/`.
- `verify-mcp-closure`: the deps of `./src/mcp/cmd/sshgate-mcp` must not match `/pkg/signerkit|modernc.org/sqlite|go-webauthn|pquerna/otp`. Today the MCP imports no `pkg/signerkit` at all, which I verified with `go list -deps`.
- `verify-lifecycle-closure`: a no-op until `pkg/signerkit/lifecycle` exists. From then on, `go list -deps ./pkg/signerkit/lifecycle/...` must not match `/pkg/signerkit$|/pkg/signerkit/(hosted|sqlitestore)|modernc.org/sqlite|go-webauthn` (E62).
- `verify-executor-closure`: the same forbidden set as the MCP gate, applied to `./src/executor/cmd/sshgate-executor`.

## 3. Build phases

Each sub-phase lists: **Goal / Depends / Owns** (the only files it may write) **/ Content / Tests (+ §13 ledger IDs, see §6) / Must not touch (+ named bumps) / Accept / Risk**. Every state behaviour below is §1.5's; where a sub-phase's wording and §1.5 differ, §1.5 wins and the sub-phase is corrected.

### A0 — Re-ground and baseline (orchestrator only; no code)

- **Goal:** start from a known base.
- **Steps:**
  1. Resolve §0.2.
  2. Create the integration branch.
  3. Re-grep every symbol cited in §1 and §3, and record any moved lines in `release-2026-10-02/A0-regrounding.md`.
  4. Record GATEHASH and the `go list -deps` output of gate, mcp, signer-telegram and signer-server in `release-2026-10-02/baseline/`.
  5. Update the spec header per E1. The spec is untracked, so this is a local-workspace edit.
  6. Re-check invariant 10 against #22's shipped plan identity.
- **Accept:** STD at the base. Pre-existing failures are recorded, not fixed.
- **Risk:** #22 may touch `src/redact` or `src/classify`, which would shift the A2 baseline.

### A1a — `approvalplan` contract types (spec A.1) — SERIAL ROOT

- **Goal:** every type, validator, hash and record schema the rest of the build depends on.
- **Owns:** `src/approvalplan/**` except the A1b files, (r5.3) `internal/elapsed/**`, plus `Makefile` (three closure targets added to `preflight` and `.PHONY`: gate, mcp, lifecycle; E62).
- **Content:**
  - `plan.go`: `ApprovalDraft` and `ApprovalPlanV1`, a discriminated union of `CommandJob{Steps, StopPolicy, MaxOutputBytes}`, `StandingGrant{Alias, Scope, Commands, DurationSeconds, TrustedHostFingerprint, RegistryEntryDigest}`, `Transfer{…E34}` and `RegisterTransferKey`.
  - `Step{Ordinal, Route, Target RouteSnapshot, RegistryEntryDigest, ExactCommand, CommandSHA256, SignatureTTLSeconds, TrustedDisplay}`. `Route` is the `unsigned_read|signed_write|signed_reveal|signed_admin` enum. `RouteSnapshot` uses the spec §5 JSON names `{server_alias, resolved_host, resolved_port, resolved_user, trusted_host_fingerprint, read_only, added_at}`, with a doc comment that "trusted" means trusted by the requester's registry, not verified by the signer (E35). The plan also carries `ExecutorClaimTokenHash` (mandatory for execution-applicable kinds, E25), `RationaleOrigin`, `SubmitNotAfter` (E73) and `Coordinator` (`in_process_sync | executor`, display-only, E26). There is no validity field in the draft (E26, §1.2). These names are frozen with the A1a goldens.
  - `record.go` (r5): the versioned canonical R1 and R2 schemas of §1.5 SA-3 (header, write-once slots, bounded sets, counters, sheddable payload), their size bounds, and the storage-key derivations (`RecordKey(principal, request_id)`, `JobKey(request_id)`). (r5.1) Also `RecordHandle(record key)` (SA-2); the bounds are on encoded bytes (R1 ≤512 KiB; R2 ≤ `R2Max`, which (r5.2) is `ceil(4/3 × 8 MiB) + R2BodyOverhead + R2NonBodyMax`, with both constants derived here from the worst-case encoding, SA-3 R2); R2 bodies are `[]byte`; the R1 header carries the votes and the grant fields. (r5.2) The R1 header also carries `rationale_redacted`; the decision slot carries `assist_bound {state, revision, digest}`; the mint slot's error reasons include `window_closed`; and R2's `report_result` refusal codes are the closed set of SA-3. `R2Reservation(draft, max_output_bytes)` returns a job's own encoded worst case for admission (SA-9). (r5.5) `R1Reservation(rec)` returns an R1 record's whole-life encoded worst case for local payload admission, from its frozen header and immutable payload only (SA-9). (r5.3, OD-3) R2's `outcome` `error` carries the code of the definitive answer that ended the job.
  - `audit.go`: `AuditMetadata` (E23's fields) and the outbox event type.
  - `rationale.go`: K2 validation. Trim, then require 1..500 bytes, valid UTF-8 and a single line. Reject C0, DEL, C1, U+061C, U+200E/F, U+202A–U+202E and U+2066–U+2069. Also holds the exact K3/K4/E8c/E39 labels and `LegacyRationaleText`.
  - `bounds.go`: K19 limits of 32 steps, 4096 bytes per step and 24576 bytes total. Validity 300..604800 with default 86400, always operator-configured, never requester-chosen (E26). The E73 submit window (≤3600 s, plus 300 s hosted skew and (r5.2) 5 s local skew), (r5.2) the route-snapshot bounds that the strict draft decode enforces on every step (`server_alias` 1..64 bytes, `resolved_host` 1..253, `resolved_user` 1..64, each printable ASCII without space, `"`, `\`, `<`, `>` or `&`; `trusted_host_fingerprint` in `xferwire.ValidFingerprint`'s form; `added_at` canonical RFC 3339 UTC; `resolved_port` 1..65535; `trusted_display`'s `staged_basename` ≤255 bytes and each version ≤128 bytes, valid UTF-8 without controls; outside them → `invalid_request` naming the field, and jobplan, A13a, applies the same check before any Submit), `report_horizon` (default 24 h, range 1 h..7 d), the E60 route TTL table with `MaxStepTTL(route, command)` (used by both the MCP draft builder and signer freeze), and §1.5 SA-9's caps. (r5.6) `MaxRequiredApprovals = 256`, `VoteSlack = 64` and `VoteCap(rec) = required_approvals + VoteSlack` (§1.5 SA-3); a record whose `required_approvals` is outside 1..256 is refused at encode and decode. Await takes 1..100 IDs and waits ≤55 s.
  - `ids.go`: E31 and E47 grammars and generators, and the `requester_sub` grammar (E19).
  - `canon.go`: strict canonical JSON; `DraftHash`, `PlanHash`, `ClaimTokenHash`, `InputHash`, `ChallengeHash`, `LegacyHostedPlanHash`, the hosted challenge MAC input encoding (E7); `RouteSnapshot.Digest` (E35), which (r5.1) normalizes `added_at` to UTC before hashing, so the same registry entry read in two time zones digests the same.
  - `assist.go`: the `AssistResult` schema, `ValidateAssist` (§8.2 limits, closed enums plus `not_assessed`, control/bidi rejection, ≤8 KiB canonical size) and `AssistDigest`.
  - `verbs.go`: `ValidateStepBytes(route, command)` and the standing-grant entry check (E71), with the mirrored `SSHGATE_` and `SSHGATE_XFER_` prefix constants.
  - `aggregate.go`: `AggregateJobClass(steps)` and the partial-execution facts (E29): (r5.6) the executed/not-executed partition of step statuses, `ExecutedOrdinals(steps)` and `Partial(steps)`, the only definition every surface and the report check use.
  - **(r5.3) `internal/elapsed`** (about 30 lines plus a test): `Clock` with `Wall() time.Time` and `Elapsed() time.Duration`; the real clock reads `unix.ClockGettime(unix.CLOCK_BOOTTIME, …)` on Linux and Go's monotonic reading elsewhere (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4); (r5.3, critique pass 1) test support lives in `internal/elapsed/elapsedtest`, which only `_test.go` files import, so `src/approvalplan` stays stdlib only (§2): `Fake` with independent `StepWall(d)` (either sign), `Advance(d)` (both halves) and `Suspend(d)` (wall and the Linux E advance, Go's monotonic reading does not); and `AssertNoDirectTimeReads(t, files, allow…)`, a `go/ast` scan over an explicit file list that fails on `time.Now`, `time.Since` and `time.Until`, and on `time.After`, `time.AfterFunc`, `time.NewTimer`, `time.NewTicker`, `time.Tick`, `context.WithTimeout` and `context.WithDeadline` (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4), outside the named adapter file and the named wake-up sites. `statetest` (stdlib only) takes its two readings as arguments and keeps its own pure two-reading fake.
- **Tests:** table tests per validator; golden canonical bytes and hashes for about 10 fixture plans and for R1 and R2 records in `testdata/`; strict round-trip; fuzz seeds for `ValidateRationale`, the canonical decode and the ID grammar. Also:
  - `ValidateStepBytes`: `SSHGATE_XFER_SEND …` refused on all four routes; `SSHGATE_UPDATE <hex>` and `SSHGATE_REVOKE` refused on every route except `signed_admin`; `signed_admin` with `rm -rf /`, `SSHGATE_UPDATE nothex`, `SSHGATE_REVOKE extra` or `SSHGATE_FOO` refused; a fuzz seed set;
  - `AggregateJobClass`: an exhaustive table over step-status sequences, including [failed read, indeterminate write] → `indeterminate`, [failed, expired_unexecuted, not_run] → `expired_unexecuted` with `partial`, [failed, succeeded] under `never` → `failed`, and [succeeded, failed_not_started, not_run] → `failed_not_started` with `partial`;
  - **(r5.6) `ExecutedOrdinals` and `Partial` (review 7 #3):** every status is in exactly one set of the partition; the same exhaustive table carries both values: [failed read, indeterminate write] → `[1, 2]`, `partial: false`; [failed read, indeterminate write, not_run] → `[1, 2]`, `partial: true`; [indeterminate] → `[1]`, `false`; [succeeded, succeeded] → `false`; [failed, skipped, skipped] → `[1]`, `true`; [failed_not_started, not_run] → `[]`, `false`; [expired_unexecuted] → `[]`, `false`; a property over generated sequences: `Partial` is true exactly when both sets are non-empty, and `ExecutedOrdinals` is ascending and never contains a not-executed status;
  - strict decode refuses any unknown draft field (so a requester-chosen validity is refused); `submit_not_after` bounds; a `command_job` or `transfer` draft without a well-formed `executor_claim_token_hash` is refused, and a `standing_grant` draft carrying one is refused;
  - record schemas: an R1 or R2 record over its size bound is refused at encode; a shed R1 record keeps exactly the header fields of §1.5 SA-3; two principals with the same `request_id` get different record keys and different record handles;
  - **(r5.1) encoded bounds:** the worst-case K19-legal R1 record (every command byte `<`, `>`, `&` or a control byte; separately, every byte inside a minimal-length secret span with the longest rule-ID list; maximal reason and assist; full vote, card, burst, status and outbox sets ((r5.6) the vote set at `VoteCap` for `required_approvals = 256`, 320 votes whose operator IDs are 128 bytes of the bootstrap grammar, `main.go:630`, with their outbox events; (r5.6, critique of the fold) the decision slot filled with `decided_by` naming 256 such operators, comma-joined, and the decision outbox event carrying the same list; if the R1 bound then lacks 20% margin, A1a raises the bound, as SA-3 provides, never lowers `MaxRequiredApprovals` or the K19 limits); (r5.2) `rationale_redacted` at its maximum, and every route snapshot and `trusted_display` at its bound) encodes within 512 KiB with at least 20% margin; an R2 record whose steps hold 8 MiB of bodies made of invalid UTF-8 and `<` bytes encodes within its bound and round-trips byte-exactly (bodies are `[]byte`);
  - **(r5.5) `R1Reservation` (review 6 #2):** for the worst-case K19-legal record and for each fixture plan, the record grown from its frozen form to every set and slot bound it can reach (assist at its bound, full card, burst, `status_edits`, vote and outbox sets, the noise fold, the signature set for its steps, a final-report summary) encodes within `R1Reservation` of the frozen form, and `R1Reservation` is at most 512 KiB; `R1Reservation` computed from the frozen form and from the grown form are equal; it is recorded for a one-step, 200-byte write with a 500-byte reason, and 256 such records reserve at most 16 MiB (otherwise A1a raises the budget, SA-9); (r5.5, critique of the fold) A1a derives SA-3's `OutboxMax` and each event kind's worst-case encoded size, which `R1Reservation` uses, and `ShedMax` (the header bound plus one report event and one noise event; (r5.6, critique of the fold) the header measured with 320 votes, `decided_by` at 256 IDs of 128 bytes and `rationale_redacted` at its maximum, never the nominal 1 KiB), and asserts that a shed record grown by a late Report and any sequence of refused Claims and Reports stays within `ShedMax`;
  - **(r5.2) the whole R2 record, as review 4 asked:** a 32-step draft holding 24 KiB of command text that is all `<` (about 144 KiB encoded), a maximal reason of `<`, route snapshots and `trusted_display` at their bounds (r5.2), and 8 MiB of output split across all 64 bodies, made of invalid UTF-8 and `<` bytes, encodes within `R2Max`; the non-body part of that record is at most `R2NonBodyMax / 1.2`; the same record round-trips byte-exactly; `R2Reservation` for that draft is at least the record's encoded size; a record over `R2Max` is refused at encode; a regression case pins r5.1's bound (`ceil(4/3 × 8 MiB)` + 64 KiB) as too small for this record, so the derivation cannot quietly regress;
  - **(r5.2) shed keeps the audit binding:** a shed R1 record keeps `rationale_redacted` and the decision slot's `assist_bound`, byte-equal to the values before the shed;
  - **(r5.3) `internal/elapsed`:** on Linux the real `Elapsed` is non-decreasing across 10⁵ reads and agrees with `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` read directly within 1 ms; `Fake.StepWall(−1 h)` leaves `Elapsed` unchanged and `Suspend(8 h)` advances both; `AssertNoDirectTimeReads` fails on a fixture file calling `time.Now`, and (r5.3, critique pass 1) on one calling `context.WithTimeout` outside a named wake-up site, and passes on the adapter; `GOOS=darwin go build ./internal/elapsed` succeeds;
  - **(r5.2) route-snapshot bounds:** a 65-byte alias, a 254-byte host, a 65-byte user, a `<` or a space in any of them, a malformed fingerprint, a non-UTC `added_at`, port 0, or an over-long `trusted_display` field is refused `invalid_request` by the strict decode; each field at its bound is accepted.
- **Ledger:** R1 at the contract level, D-7 bounds, C-15, C-19 (contract), (r5.1) C-38 (contract; r5.2 whole R2 record), (r5.2) C-45 (contract), (r5.3, critique pass 1) C-35 (the `internal/elapsed` clock and the static time-read helper), (r5.5) C-59 (the reservation bound).
- **Must not touch:** anything else.
- **Accept:** STD; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/approvalplan/... ./internal/elapsed/...`; `make verify-gate-closure verify-mcp-closure verify-lifecycle-closure`.
- **Risk:** the canonical encodings are stored identities, so any later change needs a version bump. Freeze them here.

### A1b — `approvalplan` pure state core and its harness (r5)

- **Goal:** §1.5 as pure, stdlib-only code that every store calls, with the property and model harness every store reuses.
- **Depends:** A1a.
- **Owns:** `src/approvalplan/{view,apply,next,class,decide,grant,claim,notify,jobview,jobnext}.go`, (r5.3) `clock.go` and `held.go`, and their tests, and `src/approvalplan/statetest/**`.
- **Content:**
  - `View`, `Class` and `GrantLive` (§1.5 SA-6, with the newest-grant mark as an explicit input, r5.1); (r5.3) the time core of SA-1 rule 3: in `clock.go`, `Clock(rec, now)` with the one window function (`first_end`, `last_end`, `claim_end`) and its fill order, Clock-first `Apply`, `Next`'s `next_due_at` ((r5.3, critique pass 1) SA-1 rule 3.3's one definition: `assist_deadline` and `request_expires_at` while their slots are empty, `claim_end` while the mint slot is empty or still holds bytes, the report horizon, and the Shed and Delete eligibility ages), and the 5 s release check for first and repeat Claims; in `held.go`, the pure `Held`, `Acquire` and `Passed` over a (wall, elapsed) pair, which the holders call with readings from `internal/elapsed`. r5.1 and r5.2's clock helper (the mark, the regression refusal, the break planner, the Startup floor, the carried mark and the K16 floor) is deleted from the design before it is built.
  - `Apply` for every R1 command of §1.5 SA-4, with its guards, its write-once slot discipline, its outbox events and its answers. Impure inputs (minter output, xfer digests, the deciding grant, `ReadCurrent` results) are explicit arguments.
  - The Claim answer function; the report acceptance rule; `Decide(policy, votes)` for v2 quorum and veto, a stdlib port of the hosted `Decide` (`approval.go:94`) that A10 property-tests against the original; (r5.4) and `EffectivePolicy(rec, flags)` (SA-4 Vote), which takes N from the header's `required_approvals` and the requester from the record key, never from its caller. The Vote command's input carries only the flags `{DenyVeto, AllowSelfApprove}`. (r5.6) The Vote guard includes SA-3's room rule, through the pure `VoteRoom(rec, flags, vote)` (counts, decides, or the `|votes| + need + 1 ≤ VoteCap(rec)` check).
  - `Next(rec, now, epoch)`: the signer reconciler's work list (Flush, Clock, Erase, Shed, Delete eligibility, notify steps and terminal edits due, wake-ups, `next_due_at`). (r5.5) `next_due_at` has no outbox term; the pure `Unflushed(rec)` predicate (`audited_through` below the outbox's last sequence) defines SA-7's retry list, which the local flusher and the hosted worker drive.
  - The notify-step planner: which of §1.5 SA-4's notify steps is next for a record, given its sets and the View.
  - `JobView` and `JobNext` (§1.5 SA-5) as a pure planner that returns the next action (resubmit, wait, claim, check, marker, report, deliver, done, or (r5.1) none while a step is in flight) for the executor to perform; (r5.2) it plans a Claim only before a signed step or the first dispatch, and K16 only for signed steps; (r5.3) K16 only for the first step with no result, on a `Held` pair passed in; and SA-5's definitive/transient split (OD-3); the executor's retention planner applies SA-9's job-file deletion predicate; and the `exec_submit` admission function refuses a new id outside its input window.
  - `statetest`: generators for records, commands, clocks and epochs; the properties of §1.5 SA-11; a model of signer, executor, Telegram and gate fakes with crash injection after every commit, landed or not.
- **Tests:**
  - every command's guards and writes, one table row each; every pair of a command and a state that is not a row is refused;
  - the properties of §1.5 SA-11 over the pure core, including the crash-prefix model (no second burst or card send, no second dispatch of a marked step, exactly one signature set leaves, every write-ahead state has its driver);
  - Claim totality: every reachable combination of decision, mint slot, final report, `claim_count`, window position and outbox flush gets exactly one answer, and the answer is the one §1.5 SA-4 lists;
  - report acceptance: a negative report with `claim_count = 0` accepted; an executed report without a claim refused; a class that disagrees with `AggregateJobClass` refused; (r5.6) a report of `[failed, indeterminate]` claiming `partial: true`, and one whose `executed_ordinals` omit the `indeterminate` step, each refused `invalid_report`, while the same report with `[1, 2]` and `partial: false` is accepted; (r5.6, critique of the fold) for a 3-step record, a report listing only 2 steps (`[failed, indeterminate]` with `partial: false`, and separately `[failed]` with class `failed`), one listing ordinals `[1, 3, 2]`, one with a duplicate ordinal, `[not_run, succeeded, succeeded]`, `[indeterminate, succeeded, not_run]` and `[succeeded, skipped, failed]` are each refused `invalid_report` before the class check, while `[failed, indeterminate, not_run]` (`[1, 2]`, `partial: true`), the stop-policy `[succeeded, failed, skipped]` (`[1, 2]`, `partial: true`), the negative `[expired_unexecuted, not_run, not_run]` (empty mask, `claim_count = 0`) and `[stale_target, not_run, not_run]` are each accepted; an exact duplicate acked; a different body refused; a report after `report_horizon` and after a shed accepted, and the View then shows `final`, never `unreported` (R3.3);
  - `JobNext` precedence: each of the four abort statuses × the three stop policies × last or not-last step, with and without an orphan marker, never yields a dispatch after final evidence; a check-failure result written without a marker yields no dispatch (R3.1); an orphan marker from another epoch yields `indeterminate` before any dispatch choice;
  - grant liveness: approved, current epoch, unrevoked, unexpired and not superseded; revoking the newest of two grants for an alias leaves both dead; a new epoch kills every grant; (r5.1) G2 (24 h) and G1 (1 h) requested for one alias, G1 approved after G2 → G2 dead; G1 then revoked or expired, then shed or deleted → G2 still dead; a grant of principal A never covers principal B's Submit;
  - owner scope: for every read and command, a foreign key and a missing key give byte-identical answers;
  - views never write: a property over every read path;
  - **(r5.1) single-flight:** `JobNext` over a job with a current-epoch marker and no result yields no action; a Marker on an occupied slot answers `already_marked` and the planner never yields `Start` after it;
  - **(r5.3) clock (SA-11 time properties 1–9), with independent wall and elapsed fakes and a suspend fake:** no stamp exceeds the wall clock at its commit; a `decided_at` 1 h ahead of the wall clock at the first Claim gives `materialization_error{clock_skew}`, and at a repeat Claim the Erase and `expired` (a step back larger than the time since the decision plus 5 s); steps back of 10 s and of 1 h change no epoch, cancel no actor and refuse no Revoke, AdminCancel or deny-all, and a Claim for a record decided after the step proceeds; (r5.3, critique pass 1) the only clock refusals are SA-1 rule 3.1's list: a record decided at D, a 10 s step back at D + 1 s and a first Claim at D + 2 s → `materialization_error{clock_skew}`, committed (D > now + 5 s), while the same first Claim sent instead at D + 6 s returns signatures; Clock is idempotent, never overwrites a slot, and writes only deadlines ≤ its `now`; **Clock order after downtime:** a record past both `assist_deadline` and `request_expires_at` gets assist `unavailable` and then `expired` with `assist_bound` = that `unavailable` slot, in one Clock pass; **a vote at `request_expires_at + 1 s` with the tick paused** commits `expired` and `vote_refused` in one commit and is answered refused; **X3:** an idempotent re-Submit of an existing id, and a Claim answering `not_ready`, each commit a due Clock fact exactly once and nothing when nothing is due; **admission Clock pass (X5):** a principal at its live cap with one window ended 0.5 s ago, tick paused → Submit first commits that record's `window_closed`, then admits;
  - **(r5.1) assist digest:** (r5.3) Clock's `unavailable` snapshot is a pure function of the header: Clock run at two different `now` values past the deadline writes byte-identical slots with equal digests;
  - **(r5.1) duplicates:** a same-decision re-send, an opposite re-send and a tap on a resolved record each append exactly one noise event and change nothing else;
  - **(r5.1) K19:** a live transport error on a part, or on the card, gives decision `error`; only a burst or (r5.4) an unretired send entry (`first`/`replacement`) from another epoch with no outcome is `uncertain`;
  - **(r5.1) synchronous claim deadline:** an `in_process_sync` record approved late answers Claim `expired` after `sync_claim_deadline`; (r5.3) that Claim's own Clock pass commits mint `{error: window_closed}` (`claim_end` is capped at `sync_claim_deadline`), and a claimed sync record's bytes are erased there;
  - **(r5.3) review 4's grant case, against the pair:** grant G approved at 12:00 for 1 h; at 13:01 the wall clock steps back to 12:54 → the tick removes G (E passed), a grant-covered Submit at 12:55 gets no `grant:` decision (it waits for a vote), and G never re-enters the projection in that epoch; the same with the tick paused, where the Submit's own grant match latches G dead; **S3:** a 1 h grant, then `Suspend(8 h)` together with `StepWall(−8 h)` → G is dead (E counts the suspend); **S4 in both directions**, (r5.3, critique pass 1) against a signer fake that keeps returning the held set to every repeat Claim, so the pair and not the signer decides: steps `[signed w1 running 90 s, signed w2 TTL 60]` with a 7 min step back during w1 → w2 `expired_unexecuted`, zero dispatches, and the repeat Claim before w2 does not re-acquire (a re-acquire would pass K16); steps `[signed w1 running 10 s, signed w2 TTL 60]` with a 7 min forward step during w1 → w2 `expired_unexecuted` by its wall half alone (E is at 10 s); **S1 (D1):** a record created at 14:00 on a fast clock, the clock corrected to 13:00, approved at 13:10 with TTL 60, a restart at 13:10:30 → no `window_closed` and no Erase is written, and a Claim at 13:10:40 returns signatures; **S5 (D3):** `[signed done, unsigned r2, signed w3]` with w3's window ended → r2 runs, then w3 meets K16 and gets `expired_unexecuted`; r2 is never left without a result; **a repeat Claim after a step back larger than the time since the decision plus 5 s** commits the Erase and answers `expired`; **R-2 pinned:** a denied record deleted, then a 25 h step back and the exact bytes resubmitted → accepted as a new request that waits for a vote (never a `grant:` decision without a live grant); **the static time-read test** over `src/approvalplan` passes. r5.2's "Startup floor with stamps 1 h ahead", break and K16-floor tests are deleted;
  - **(r5.3, OA-5) revision binding (X1):** a card entry at revision 0; the assist slot commits (and, separately, `assist_deadline` passes with the tick paused, so the Vote's own Clock pass fills `unavailable`) before the actor appends the edit entry; a Deny tap and an Approve tap on the revision-0 challenge → `vote_refused{stale_revision}`, no vote, answer "card updated — tap again"; after the edit entry at revision 1 is confirmed, a tap on its challenge counts and `assist_bound` has revision 1; (r5.3, critique pass 1) this is the one clock-caused refusal of a Deny that SA-11 item 7 allows, and a Deny on a challenge still current after Clock is never refused;
  - **(r5.3, OD-3) definitive answers:** `JobNext` over each definitive code at each consuming step (`conflict`, `invalid_request`, `request_too_large`, `record_corrupt` on resubmit; `not_found`, `record_corrupt` on Get or Wait after `signer_ack`; `invalid_claim`, `not_found`, `record_corrupt` on Claim) → outcome `error{code}`, final, no report owed, deletable under SA-9's predicate once bodies are gone; a code after an executed step keeps that step's result and `partial` ((r5.6, critique of the fold) `JobView` over 3 steps, step 1 `succeeded`, then outcome `error{record_corrupt}` → steps `[succeeded, not_run, not_run]`, `executed_ordinals [1]`, `partial: true`; outcome `adopted{failed}` or `approval_lost` before any step ran → all `not_run`, `executed_ordinals []`, `partial: false`; a stop-policy stop → `skipped` tail; an abort → `not_run` tail); each transient answer (`storage_uncertain`, (r5.6) `busy`, `lifecycle_unavailable`, `queue_full`, `not_ready`, a dial failure) → a retry with the job unchanged;
  - **(r5.3) first window (SA-6.2, SA-6.5):** steps with TTL 30 and 60, approved and unclaimed → at `decided_at + 30` Clock writes `window_closed`, the class leaves `live`, the execution view is `expired_unclaimed`, and Claim answers `expired`; once claimed, the Erase comes at `decided_at + 60`;
