### A8 — Telegram notifier reconciler (K6, K15, K19, K20, E7, E14, E22, E45–E51)

- **Depends:** A3, A4, A6 and A7 (A8 constructs the production lifecycle on A7's `AttachApprovals` seam and `legacyApprove`).
- **Owns:** `src/signer/backend/**`, and `src/signer/cmd/sshgate-signer-telegram/main.go` (**first writer**). That covers the `[approvals]` TOML section, with `pending_validity` (24 h, range 5 m..7 d), `allowed_uids`, `journal_root` (default `filepath.Dir(Paths.Key)/approvals`, E18; read from B1 on, and written by `doInitFlow`), `retention` (7 d) and `report_horizon` (24 h, range 1 h..7 d); lifecycle construction with the Telegram notifier; starting the poller only after the lifecycle's Startup returns; and the assist-provider and redaction wiring. There is no switch that disables the lifecycle or routes v1 around it (E8e).
- **(r5.1) Startup order in `main.go`.** Today `tb.Run` starts the poller inside `buildTelegramBackend` (`main.go:606`), before `signerkit.New` (`:275`) and `srv.Listen` (`:321`). A8 moves `tb.Run` out of `buildTelegramBackend` and fixes the order: build the backend (no polling); `signerkit.New`; build the lifecycle with `svc.ApprovalMinter()` and the Telegram notifier; `svc.AttachApprovals(lc)`; `lc.Startup()`; `tb.Run`; `srv.Listen`. A wiring test asserts no callback is processed and no socket connection accepted before Startup returns.
- **Content:**
  - `telegram_v2.go` implements the `Notifier` I/O steps; the lifecycle's actor sequences them by §1.5 SA-4, and `telegram_v2.go` never decides card state on its own:
    - (r5.1) `Burst(ctx, fn)` takes `t.policyNotifyMu` and holds it while `fn` runs the record's `SendParts`, its commit, `SendCard` and that commit (E14), so neither another record's burst nor the policy flow interleaves between a record's parts and its card;
    - `SendParts` pre-renders every part, then sends them, with partial sends and 429 handled per E50, and returns each part's outcome and message ID; (r5.8, review 9 #2) the 429 wait and the budget select on `ctx`, so a stop ends the wait at once and returns the context error; (r5.8, critique of the fold) the actor classifies by the owner's latch alone (§1.5 SA-8 step 4), never by that error, and the budget ending is returned as the budget failure, which the actor maps to `error`, however the budget is built; (r5.1) any failure is reported as a failure, never as a possible send, because the actor maps every live failure to `error` (K19);
    - `SendCard` sends a decision card rendered from the view and revision the actor passes: revision 0 while `preparing` shows Deny only, revision 1 shows Approve and Deny;
    - `EditCard` (treating "message is not modified" as success), `EditStatus` and `MarkIncomplete` perform the single Bot API call the actor asks for and report a known failure, a possible send, or success.
  - `telegram_v2_legacy.go` implements `LegacyNotifier`: the legacy card through the shared renderer, multipart within E45's bounds, its AI advisory section once the request's assist attempt is terminal (r5.1, E8b), and its terminal edit.
  - `telegram_v2_callback.go`: parses `v:` before the legacy parse, applies E51's checks, resolves the handle through the card-handle index (or the legacy table and (r5.4) its tombstones), calls `lifecycle.Vote` or resolves the `LegacyRequest`, and answers only after the commit. (r5.4) A tap that does not resolve a `LegacyRequest`, and a tap whose handle resolves to nothing at all (a v2 record that was deleted, or a card from before a restart), writes the one fail-open audit line of E8b and answers with today's one string, "expired or already resolved" ((r5.5, critique of the fold): r5.4 also listed "expired" and "already resolved", which today's handler never sends; E8b). A `storage_uncertain` answer becomes "not recorded yet — tap again"; (r5.3) r5.1's `clock_regressed` text is withdrawn; `vote_refused{stale_revision}` (OA-5) becomes "card updated — tap again". `answerCallbackQuery` errors are logged and ignored (best effort).
  - `telegram_v2_commands.go`: the E64 command gate (sender, private chat, not forwarded, and (r5.3, critique pass 1) not redelivered, judged by E64's `update_id` cut, not by `Message.Date`). A8 ships the gate with no commands registered yet.
  - `telegram.go`:
    - in `handleCallback`, after the allowed-user check and next to the `p:` branch (`:470-473`), route `v:` to `telegram_v2_callback.go`;
    - in `dispatch` (`:404-420`), add one hook to the E64 command gate;
    - apply the K7 gate inside `runExplainer`.
  - Linked-chat check (E64): when the chat linked through `chatStore` is not the allowed user's private DM, log a startup WARN and refuse v2 notification (decision `error`). Legacy v1 cards are still delivered to the linked chat, with E64's tap check. No request uses the direct `Backend` path in production (E8e).
  - The `[backend.telegram.explainer]` block (`main.go:136-143`) maps to `assist.OpenAI`, with an E36 warning when `timeout_sec > 5`.
- **Tests (fake Bot API, following the `telegram_test.go` harness):**
  - parts arrive before the card; there is no Approve until assist is terminal; an edit failure leads to a replacement card; a partial failure gives `error` plus INCOMPLETE edits; 429 retry; (r5.1) a transport error (connection reset) on part 2, and separately on the card, gives `error` plus INCOMPLETE edits, never `uncertain`; two records submitted together never interleave parts and cards (E14);
  - a wrong user, chat or message ID is refused; the command gate refuses a wrong user, a group chat, a forwarded message and (r5.3, critique pass 1) a slash command in the drained batches (E64's `update_id` cut), all with no state change, and replies only to the linked DM; (r5.3, critique pass 1) with a test command registered on the gate, a signer started with its wall clock 10 min fast whose clock is then corrected accepts a later command, and one started 10 min slow drops a command sent before the restart;
  - a group-linked chat refuses v2 notification, while a legacy v1 card is still delivered there with a challenge; the allowed user's tap on it is accepted and another member's is refused;
  - **`main.go` wiring:** the constructed daemon always has a lifecycle and the Telegram notifier, for every config, including one that still sets r2's `enabled = false` or `legacy_v1_via_lifecycle = false` (the TOML decode is not strict, `main.go:337`, so the config struct simply has no such fields);
  - **no direct path in production (E8e):** with the daemon built the way `main.go` builds it, v1 `sign`, `request_grant`, `transfer` and `register_xfer_key` requests produce only `v:` callback data, never `approve:`/`deny:`, and each card carries the K3 legacy label and a challenge;
  - **audit sink failing on a v2 tap (R3.2):** the vote commits and the tap is answered with the decision; no signature leaves until the sink recovers and the outbox flushes;
  - the actor's first Bot API call follows the burst-append commit (E63);
  - **notification and assist coordination against the fake Bot API (R2.3):** (1) the provider answers immediately → one burst, then one card already carrying Approve, and no `editMessageText`; (r5.4) the card's entry holds `msg_id` and `outcome = sent`, and Get shows notification `sent`; (2) the fake answers part 2 with 429 `retry_after=6` while the 5 s assist deadline passes → no card I/O before part N is accepted, then one card at revision 1 with Approve; (3) part 3 returns 400 while assist completes → no card, no replacement, INCOMPLETE edits on parts 1–2, and the decision is `error`; (4) assist completes while the card's send is in flight → exactly one `editMessageText`, and a tap on the preparing challenge after the edit entry exists gets "card updated"; (4b) (r5.5, critique of the fold) `editMessageText` times out once as assist completes and then works → with no other command the edit is confirmed within 2 s by the actor's retry, a tap before that answers "card updated — tap again", and an Approve tap after it decides; (5) (r5.3, critique pass 1) OA-5 before the edit entry: the assist slot commits, and separately the 5 s `assist_deadline` passes with the tick paused, before the actor appends the edit entry → a Deny tap and an Approve tap on the revision-0 card each answer "card updated — tap again" with no vote; after the edit is confirmed a tap counts;
  - **local legacy with the production adapter (R2.6):** with the daemon built as `main.go` builds it, a v1 `sign` card tapped Approve one second before `ApprovalWindow` approves and signs, exactly as today; with the deadline first, the v1 client receives `timeout`, the card is edited to expired, and a later tap is answered "expired or already resolved" ((r5.5, critique of the fold)) with no signature and (r5.4) one `legacy_vote_refused{expired}` line in the AuditLog; (r5.4) **duplicate approve, opposite replay and post-timeout taps against the fake Bot API (review 5 #6):** each is answered with today's string, "expired or already resolved", changes neither the v1 response bytes nor the resolution, and adds exactly one AuditLog line; (r5.5, critique of the fold) the tap that loses a `sync.Once` race gets the same answer and no second footer edit; a tap on a handle the signer does not know (after a restart) adds one `unknown_card` line;
  - a replay is inert; the legacy card shows the K3 label and the E8c labels; forging strings in the reason, assist fields and legacy reason render neutralized (E65);
  - goleak stays clean, with the actors and assist jobs joined; the output equals A3's shared fixture;
  - the terminal edits for denied, expired and approved, and the signer-derived edits `expired_unclaimed` (an abandoned synchronous request approved late, E26), `not_executable` (including `grant_not_live` and (r5.1) `clock_skew`) and `unreported` (E49), each sent at most once per display state per epoch (r5.1 wording: a crash between the edit and its append repeats it once, E49);
  - (r5.1) every card and `/pending` line shows the record handle; a duplicate tap and a redelivered tap each produce one audit event (E61);
  - an `in_process_sync` card carries the synchronous-request line (E26);
  - the policy explainer test stays green;
  - (r5.8, review 9 #2) the fake Bot API answers part 2 with 429 `retry_after=30`, and the burst's context is cancelled during the wait: `SendParts` returns the context error within 100 ms and no later part is sent; the same 429 with no cancellation sends part 2 after the wait; (r5.8, critique of the fold) the fake answers part 2 with 429 `retry_after=61` and nothing stops the owner → the 60 s budget ends, `SendParts` returns the budget failure (not a context error), and through the A6 actor the decision is `error` with INCOMPLETE edits on part 1, never an interruption.
- **Named change:** the nil-rules explainer tests in `telegram_test.go` (E15).
- **Ledger:** R3 (Telegram), R5 (Telegram), R4 (local legacy, production), D-1, D-2, D-7, C-13 (only signer-polled callbacks can vote), C-16 (Telegram), C-21 (send path), C-24, C-25, (r5.1) C-37 (Telegram), (r5.3, critique pass 1) C-46 (Telegram), (r5.4) C-53 (Telegram) and C-54 (production), (r5.8) C-67 (adapter).
- **Must not touch:** `telegram_policy*.go` and its tests, `formatApprovalMessage` and the tests pinning it (`reveal_format_test.go`, `telegram_update_test.go`, `telegram_transfer_format_test.go`, `telegram_secretredact_test.go`, `telegram_extra_test.go`, `telegram_transfer_test.go`), except the K7-named change.
- **Accept:** STD; FROZEN-LOCAL; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/signer/...`.
- **Risk:** fake-API tests only. Never point a test at the operator's bot token or chat.

### A9 — Hosted migration 7 and v2 row store (E4b, E4c, E6, E41, E52, E53, E75)

- **Depends:** A1a, and (r5.2) A1b: its acceptance derives the slot columns with `approvalplan`'s View and Class and runs a property over A1b's `statetest` generator (review 4 #9).
- **Owns:** `pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/**` (including new `migration7.go` and `approvals_v2.go`), and `pkg/signerkit/store/store.go` (optional `Request.ExpiresAt`/`TerminalReason`, plus optional extension interfaces following the `ListPendingPage` precedent at `human_handlers.go:648`). It also owns **one line** in `src/signer-server/cmd/sshgate-signer-server/main_test.go:349`, routed by the orchestrator because A10 owns that package later: `{id: "wrong-role", role: "auditor"}` becomes `role: "admin"`, which keeps the non-operator meaning that `TestValidateApprovalRosterPreventsUnreachableFreshPolicy` relies on.
- **Schema (migration 7, `approval_lifecycle_v2`; r5):**
  - `requests`: `+request_expires_at INTEGER`; `+terminal_reason TEXT CHECK (terminal_reason IS NULL OR terminal_reason IN ('expired','canceled'))`; the backfill of E4b.
  - `approvals_v2`: primary key `(client_id, sub_principal, request_id)`; `record_json` (the canonical R1 record of §1.5 SA-3, ≤512 KiB encoded, r5.1); the slot columns admission and listing need (`decision_status`, `decided_at`, (r5.3, critique pass 1) `mint_state` (`none | bytes | erased | error`, with the error reason, `window_closed` included), `last_window_end` (≡ `last_end`; used only for `awaiting_report` and `unreported`), `claim_count`, `final_class`, `audited_complete`, `shed`, `payload_bytes`, `submit_not_after`, `created_at`, `request_expires_at`), each written from the record in the same transaction; and the cache columns `view_status`, `class`, `next_due_at` and (r5.1) `record_handle` (SA-2). (r5.3) `next_due_at` comes from `approvalplan.Next` ((r5.3, critique pass 1) SA-1 rule 3.3's one definition, which includes `claim_end` while the mint slot still holds bytes, and the Shed and Delete ages; r5.3's "over the empty slots" never scheduled a claimed row's Erase). `view_status` and `class` are exact for every slot fact, because views read time only from slots (SA-1 rule 3.3). (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-3) The two states that read `now`, `unreported` and the step from `awaiting_report` to `settled` at `last_window_end + report_horizon` (SA-6.2, SA-6.5), are brought up to date by the worker's cache-only refresh within one pass of falling due (SA-1 rule 3.3; R-3's read lag).
  - Indexes: `(client_id, sub_principal, created_at)` for listing; `next_due_at` for the worker; a partial index on full rows (`WHERE shed = 0`) and an index over deletion candidates by `submit_not_after`, for the two ceilings; (r5.1) a non-unique index on `record_handle` (a lookup returning more than one row is refused as ambiguous) and one on `decided_at` for `/ui/audit`'s `v2_entries`; (r5.5) a partial index `ON approvals_v2(next_due_at) WHERE audited_complete = 0` for the worker's flush retry list (§1.5 SA-7). `audited_complete` is 1 exactly when `approvalplan.Unflushed(rec)` is false.
  - **No** `approval_votes_v2`, `vote_challenges` or `deny_all_snapshots` table (r5: votes live in the record, challenges are MACs, snapshots are memory-only).
  - The users role triggers (E6). Migration 7 first runs `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (…)` with the exact migration-2 DDL (`migrations.go:85-91`), so the role pre-count and the `CREATE TRIGGER … ON users` work on a ledger-repair DB that recorded migrations 1–2 without a users table. `TestMigration3_PublishesOnlyHistoricalTerminalVotes` (`operators_test.go:513-565`) is such a fixture, and it stays unchanged. This matches the ledger's documented repair stance (`validateMigrationLedger` doc comment, `migrations.go:375-378`).
- **Code:**
  - the DSN pins `synchronous(FULL)`;
  - v1 Insert writes `request_expires_at`, and its quota counts v2 `live` rows by the slot-column predicate (E52);
  - `ExpireLegacy` and `CancelLegacy`: pending-only CASes with their own SQL and no unaudited-vote barrier (E4c);
  - the v2 primitives, each used inside a caller's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction: `GetV2(tx, key)`, `PutV2(tx, key, record, columns)`, `DeleteV2(tx, key)`, `CountV2(tx)` (full rows and all rows); (r5.1) and the plain WAL reads used outside commands and outside any transaction (never `BeginTx`, which under `_txlock=immediate` takes the write lock): `ReadV2(key)`, `ByHandleV2(handle)`, `RecentDecidedV2(limit)`, and (r5.5, critique of the fold) `ListV2(client, sub, cursor, limit)`, `DueV2(now, limit)` and (r5.5) `UnflushedV2(limit)` (rows with `audited_complete = 0`, nearest `next_due_at` first, nulls last; SA-7). r5.5 listed those three among the in-transaction primitives although none takes a `tx`; on the `:memory:` store a query made outside `tx` while a transaction is open waits for the one pooled connection for ever. The worker and the flusher read a list first and then open one transaction per row, which re-reads the row with `GetV2(tx)`; the flusher writes to the sink between the two, outside any transaction (A10). Inside a transaction every read goes through `tx`, because the `:memory:` store has one connection (`sqlite.go:205-209`).
- **Named bump:** `policy_migration_test.go:168` changes 7 to 8 (E53).
- **Tests:**
  - upgrading a v6 fixture DB preserves rows and backfills; re-applying is idempotent;
  - the role triggers work, and a pre-existing bad role gives the named failure; a ledger-repair DB with no users table opens and gains the table and triggers;
  - `PRAGMA synchronous` returns 2;
  - mixed v1/v2 quotas;
  - **`ExpireLegacy` and `CancelLegacy` succeed on a pending v1 row that holds an unaudited vote**, leave the vote row in place, and a later `UpdateStatus` by a racing `SubmitVote` affects no row; (r5.1) each returns whether it changed the row and the operators and decisions of the vote rows left, which A10 and D2 put in their audit line;
  - `record_json` round-trips byte-exactly; the slot columns written by `PutV2` equal those derived from the record by `approvalplan` (a property over A1b's generator); (r5.3, critique pass 1) the E52 `live` predicate over `decision_status`, `mint_state` and `final_class` equals `approvalplan.Class` on every generated record; (r5.5) `audited_complete = 0` exactly when `approvalplan.Unflushed` holds, and `UnflushedV2` returns exactly those rows in that order, using the partial index (`EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`);
  - two concurrent `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` Submits at a ceiling of N admit at most N rows.
- **Ledger:** C-12 (hosted), D-6 (store), C-22 (schema), C-26 (hosted store), (r5.5) C-58 (store).
- **Must not touch:** the bodies of migrations 1–6, `verifyPolicySchema`, the policy tables and triggers, `storetest`.
- **Accept:** STD; FROZEN-HOSTED; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/... ./pkg/signerkit/store/... ./pkg/signerkit/storetest/... ./pkg/signerkit/hosted/... ./src/signer-server/...` (hosted and signer-server create users through the new triggers); independent review (migration).
- **Risk:** live hosted DBs, because the migration only rolls forward. Release-note it (§1.3).

### A10 — Hosted v2 machine plane, engine, redactor, assist and worker (E3, E4, E19, E21, E25, E32, E37, E52, E63, E67, E68, E71, E75)

- **Depends:** A1b, A3 (hosted Submit persists the `ReviewSnapshot` at freeze, E37), A4 (assist), A5, A6 (the `lifecycletest` suite) and A9.
- **Owns:**
  - `pkg/signerkit/hosted/server.go` (routing and worker fields);
  - new `approvals_v2_engine.go`, `approvals_v2_handlers.go` and `approvals_v2_worker.go`;
  - `config.go`, including the hosted redactor (`Combined()` plus a per-request salt) and the optional assist-provider fields (E37); (r5.6) `New` returns `ErrInvalidRequiredApprovals` for `RequiredApprovals > approvalplan.MaxRequiredApprovals` in every mode (today `:113` refuses only N ≤ 0, and only with `Auth.RPID` set), because every v2 row freezes it (§1.5 SA-3);
  - `policy_runtime.go`, **only `Server.Close` (`:212-227`)**: an explicit exception. Add an idempotent `closeOrdinaryWorkers()` call that cancels and joins the LIFECYCLE worker and the assist pool; (r5.8) an assist call it interrupts commits no assist slot (§1.5 SA-8), and a worker transaction in flight commits or rolls back as a unit, as today. (r5.8, critique of the fold) Hosted has no latch, so its interruption test is the pool's own closed flag, set by `Pool.Close` before it cancels: an assist effect whose call returns once the pool is closed commits nothing, and a `TrySubmit` after it answers `closed` (A4). HTTP handlers still running after `closeOrdinaryWorkers` (a `WaitMany` long-poll outlives `httpSrv.Shutdown`'s 5 s) may commit until `Database.Close`, as v1 handlers do today (§1.5 SA-8 step 2). It runs on every `Close`, including when the policy plane was never attached. The policy-worker cancel/join code is otherwise unchanged, and `PolicyRuntime.Close` (`:463-503`) already calls `Server.Close` before `Database.Close`;
  - `handlers.go`, where v1 intake sets the expiry and nothing else changes;
  - `src/signer-server/cmd/sshgate-signer-server/main.go` (**sole writer**): (r5.6) `--required-approvals` above `approvalplan.MaxRequiredApprovals` (256) refuses startup in both modes, naming the limit, before any store or plane is built (today `:213` checks only N > 0, and only with `--ui`; that check is unchanged); flags `--approval-validity` (24 h), `--approval-retention` (7 d, shed), `--approval-max-age` ((r5.1) default 0 = never delete, E23), `--approval-report-horizon` (24 h, range 1 h..7 d), `--approval-max-full-rows` (4096), `--approval-max-rows` (200 000), `--legacy-v1-pending-ttl` (24 h), `--bootstrap-admin`, `--assist-endpoint`, `--assist-model`, `--assist-key-file` (0600, using the `loadAPIKey` pattern at `main.go:782`) and `--assist-timeout`; ordinary-plane `RedactSalt`/`Combined()`; `StartOrdinaryWorkers` in both modes. In non-policy mode `main.go` today only defers `database.Close()` (`:340`) and never calls `srv.Close()`, so add `defer srv.Close()` after it (deferred calls run LIFO), which closes the server before the DB in both modes;
  - `src/signer-server/install/{sshgate-signer-server.service,deploy.sh}`;
  - `src/signer-server/README.md`.
- **Engine (r5):**
  - every v2 operation is one transaction: the owner-scoped `GetV2` on `(client_id, sub_principal, request_id)` (E19), `approvalplan.Apply`, `PutV2` with the record and its columns, `COMMIT`; the effects (response, waiter wake-up, assist start) only after `COMMIT` returns;
  - **uncertain commits (§1.5 SA-8):** an error from `COMMIT`, or from the driver after the statement was sent, answers 503 "storage uncertain — retry" and runs no effect. The engine keeps no record cache, so the next command re-reads;
  - Submit applies E71's reserved-verb check and E25's mandatory claim-token hash first, then idempotency, then, for a new id only, the submit window, admission (the E52 quota, then both ceilings counted inside the transaction, after any reclamation the handler ran in earlier transactions), and freeze with the E60 TTL maximum, the E33 hash check and E16's `request_unrenderable`; each stored row is at most the R1 bound, 512 KiB encoded (`request_too_large`; r5.1: no K19-legal request reaches it, A1a);
  - the Vote command per §1.5 SA-4, with quorum and veto from `approvalplan.Decide` (r5.4) under `approvalplan.EffectivePolicy(rec, flags)`, built inside the Vote's transaction from the row's frozen `required_approvals` and its key's `client_id`; the handler passes only `Cfg.ApprovalPolicy`'s two flags (`main.go:281`), and the engine's Vote input has no field for N or a requester; an eligible v2 voter is `role='operator'` (E6);
  - (r5.4) Submit's freeze writes `required_approvals` from `Config.RequiredApprovals`, as v1 intake does (`handlers.go:237`);
  - Claim (r5.1): the signatures are minted outside the transaction and the Claim transaction CASes the mint slot (E21); a `BEGIN` that fails with `SQLITE_BUSY` is a retryable 503 "busy", never "storage uncertain"; Report per §1.5 SA-4;
  - (r5.6) **`busy` on every v2 machine route (E21):** one helper classifies the error (exported inside the package, so A11 and D2 reuse it); Submit, Claim and Report answer the `busy` envelope for a busy `BEGIN`, and Get, List and Wait for a plain read ended by `SQLITE_BUSY`; the worker and the flusher skip a busy row until the next pass; (r5.6, critique of the fold) the assist job resends its `AssistResult` commit after a busy `BEGIN` with E25's backoff until `assist_deadline`, without a second provider call. The human-plane routes are built by A11 (the v2 vote POST) and D2 (cancel and deny-all), and each applies E21's human-plane rule there; r5.6 assigned them to A10, which owns none of those handlers;
  - (r5.6, critique of the fold) Submit's freeze writes `required_approvals` as 1 when `Server.RequiredApprovals ≤ 0` (SA-3);
  - (r5.1) every committed command that appended events nudges the hosted flusher as a post-commit effect, so Claim does not wait for the worker's tick (SA-7); (r5.5, critique of the fold) the flusher is one goroutine, started with the worker and joined by `closeOrdinaryWorkers()`, and it performs every hosted Flush, nudged or retried (SA-1 rule 4: Flush only in the one flusher). For each row it reads the unflushed events with a plain `ReadV2`, writes them to the stderr sink outside any transaction ((r5.8, critique of the fold) each through `Call` as an `AuditCall` with `Lifecycle = "approval-<transition>"` and `Approval`, never through `Verdict`, E23; a test flushes a v2 approve, a `vote_replay` and a `vote_refused` and finds three kind-`lifecycle` lines and no `verdict` line), then commits Flush in its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (`GetV2(tx)`, `audited_through` raised to the last sequence written, `PutV2`). r5.5 had each handler run Flush itself beside the worker's retries; (r5.3) every v2 transaction applies Clock first (SA-1 rule 3.3); reads apply nothing and never write;
  - **the worker** is the hosted reconciler: at startup and every second it runs `approvalplan.Next` over `DueV2` rows (Clock, Erase, Shed, and Delete only when `--approval-max-age` is set), (r5.5) then has the flusher retry the rows `UnflushedV2(256)` lists, nearest `next_due_at` first, stopping at the first sink failure ((r5.5, critique of the fold) `DueV2` and `UnflushedV2` are plain reads made outside any transaction, A9, and each listed row is then re-read with `GetV2(tx)` and committed in its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`) (§1.5 SA-7; r5–r5.4 put the Flush retry inside the `DueV2` pass, which never selected a row for its outbox), and `ExpireLegacy` over overdue v1 rows, writing one audit line per expired v1 row (E4c, r5.1). It owns a bounded, joined assist pool that never queues (E68). (r5.1) At the row ceiling with Delete off, it logs once per hour that `--approval-max-rows` needs raising or `--approval-max-age` setting. (r5.3) The worker has no clock break: it applies Clock at the wall clock over `DueV2(now)`, which also writes mint `{error: window_closed}` and the Erase at `claim_end`, and it reports wall steps it sees (E59).
- **Tests:**
  - **(r5.7, review 8 #3) Scope.** A10 builds no human route, so its tests drive votes only through the engine's Vote entry point, with the verified-challenge input A11's handler passes after its MAC and step-up checks (the voter, the decision and the challenge's revision and digest, SA-4 Vote). No A10 test calls `/ui/v2/...`, the vote POST or `/ui/audit`; every assertion that needs them is in A11. r5.3–r5.6 wrote several of the cases below with approve POSTs and `v2_entries`, which exist only after A11;
  - `lifecycletest` runs against the hosted implementation;
  - an E3 routing regression with policy attached, while every policy route and dirty-path test stays unchanged; (r5.7, critique of the fold) with and without the policy plane, bare `/v2`, `/v2/`, `/v2/other`, `/v2/approvals/a/b`, `/v2/approvals//claim` and an empty id each answer 404 with a body byte-equal to the `not_found` envelope golden (E3), the same bytes as an unknown id;
  - **owner scope over HTTP (R3.4, E19):** two `sub_principal`s of one client, and a direct bearer client with none, get byte-identical `not_found` for each other's ids on Get, Wait, List, Claim and Report, for full and shed rows; the same `request_id` under two `sub_principal`s is two rows;
  - legacy TTL: `/v1/poll` returns `error`, and the row is `error` with `terminal_reason=expired`, **including a row that holds an unaudited vote**; `/v1/poll` and `/v1/audit` never see v2 rows;
  - N concurrent final approvals (engine Votes) produce one decision, and the first Claim's signature TS equals `decided_at`;
  - a restart with a record in `preparing`: (r5.7, critique of the fold) with `--assist-timeout` 5 s (so `assist_deadline = frozen_at + 5 s`, E68) and the worker's 1 s tick, the row's View reads assist revision 1 by `assist_deadline` + 1 s, or 1 s after the restart when the restart comes later, with no second provider call (r5.7 said "within 5 s of freeze", which Clock cannot meet: it commits `unavailable` only at a tick or command at or after the deadline, SA-6 R-3) ((r5.7, review 8 #3) the detail GET's Approve-enabled challenge is A11's);
  - TTL 61 on `signed_write` is refused; (r5.3; r5.7: engine, not HTTP) an engine Vote after expiry with the worker paused commits `expired` and its `vote_refused` in one transaction and answers refused `expired` (the POST's 409 is A11's order-of-checks test);
  - goleak shows no goroutine survives `Server.Close`, in **both** policy and non-policy modes (v21 R69); the policy worker count is unchanged;
  - (r5.8, review 9 #2) `Server.Close` while an assist provider call is in flight, the call then failing with the context error: no assist slot is committed and no resend is made; after a restart, Clock writes `{unavailable, "assist deadline"}` by `assist_deadline` + 1 s, or 1 s after the restart when the restart comes later, and the provider is not called again (K8). The same provider error with no `Close` commits its own `unavailable` slot, as E68 says; (r5.8, critique of the fold) **a Submit after the cut:** with a v2 `WaitMany` long-poll holding `httpSrv.Shutdown` past its 5 s, a v2 Submit whose handler commits after `closeOrdinaryWorkers` has joined the assist pool → the record is committed `pending`, `TrySubmit` answers `closed`, no assist slot is committed (never `{unavailable, "assist busy"}`), and after a restart Clock fills the slot at `assist_deadline` and the provider is never called for it;
  - **reserved verbs (E71) over HTTP:** `TestHostedV2Submit_RejectsTransferVerb` (an XFER step under each route → 400 `reserved_verb`, no row, no audit of a signed event); a mislabelled route and a non-sole `signed_admin` step are refused; a stored plan corrupted to carry an XFER step is refused at the first Claim;
  - **retention and ceilings (R1.4, R2.4, R2.7):** a shed denied record answers a same-tuple resubmit with `denied` and `pruned: true`, and a different draft with `conflict`; a claimed record whose command is still running 20 minutes after its window is shed under full-row pressure, its report is accepted over HTTP, and after `--approval-report-horizon` a report is still accepted while the row exists; with `--approval-max-age` set (r5.1: Delete is off by default), a sustained-load run of 20 000 submits and decisions on a fake clock keeps live rows within quota and both ceilings, and deletes only rows past `submit_not_after + 24 h`; with injected ceilings (`--approval-max-full-rows=8`, `--approval-max-rows=20`): reclaimable rows are reclaimed and the Submit succeeds; protected rows only → `queue_full`, no row written, nothing protected deleted; the clock past the horizon admits the next Submit; 16 concurrent Submits at the ceiling admit exactly as many rows as fit;
  - the report matrix over HTTP with the worker running;
  - **uncertain commits (R2.2):** a store wrapper that commits and then returns an error, applied in turn to Submit, Vote, Claim (the mint), Report, Clock and Flush. Each answers 503 with no dependent effect: no signatures, no waiter wake, no success. Concurrent Get and Wait callers see only committed state. A retry of the same request then returns the landed outcome (an idempotent Submit, an inert re-vote with the stored decision, the same signature set, an acked report), and exactly one signature set ever leaves the signer. The wrapper that fails before the commit gives the same 503, and the retry applies the command for the first time;
  - the audit sink failing: votes and decisions commit; Claim answers `not_ready` until the worker's Flush succeeds; no row is shed or deleted meanwhile;
  - **(r5.5) flush retry with no further mutation (review 6 #1):** the stderr sink fails exactly one write, the post-commit Flush of an approve committed through the engine's Vote ((r5.7) the approve-POST form is A11's), and works afterwards; no command touches the row after the decision; the worker runs. Within two passes `audited_complete` is 1, the decision line is on the sink, and an executor-style Claim loop (E25's backoff from `decided_at + 1 s`) over HTTP gets signatures before `claim_end` (TTL 60). A counting `DueV2` wrapper shows the row was not selected by `DueV2` before `claim_end`; a counting `UnflushedV2` wrapper shows it was selected until it drained and never after. The same with the sink failing until `decided_at + 50 s`. With 300 unflushed rows and a dead sink, another row's `window_closed` still commits within one pass of its `claim_end`, and each pass makes one failed sink write;
  - **(r5.1) mint outside the transaction:** a custody signer that blocks for 10 s holds no SQLite lock (a concurrent Submit commits meanwhile); two concurrent first Claims both mint, exactly one set is stored and both callers receive that stored set; a `BEGIN` busy timeout answers 503 "busy" and the retry succeeds;
  - **(r5.6) contention (review 7 #1):** on a file-backed store in `t.TempDir()` (the `:memory:` store has one connection, `sqlite.go:205-209`, so it cannot show contention), a second connection holds `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for 6 s, past the 5 s busy timeout, across each of a Submit, a Claim and a Report in turn: each answers HTTP 503 with the `busy` envelope byte-equal to the golden, the row is unchanged (`record_json` byte-equal, no event appended, `claim_count` unchanged, no signature in the response, and a Claim's out-of-transaction mint discarded), and the same request bytes sent again after the lock is released succeed exactly once (one record, one signature set, one accepted report); a `SQLITE_BUSY` injected at `COMMIT` by the store wrapper still answers `storage_uncertain`; a worker pass under the held lock commits nothing, logs contention, and the next pass applies the due Clock fact; (r5.6, critique of the fold; corrected in r5.7, review 8 #4) the assist commit under contention, in two cases. **Early busy, retried in time:** `--assist-timeout` 5 s, a provider that returns a ready result 0.5 s after freeze, and a store wrapper that fails that `AssistResult` commit's `BEGIN` once, at once, with an injected `SQLITE_BUSY` (E21's detection): the commit is resent after E25's first 250 ms backoff and lands before `assist_deadline`, the provider is called once, and the row reaches revision 1 with that result. **Lock held past the deadline:** a second connection takes `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` before the provider's result and holds it 6 s; because `assist_deadline ≤ frozen_at + 5 s` (E68), no resend can commit in time, so after the release the slot reads Clock's `{unavailable, "assist deadline"}` (revision 1, E68's header snapshot), the provider result is dropped, and the provider is called once; an `AssistResult` attempt still waiting in `BEGIN` at the release applies Clock first and adds nothing. r5.6 expected the result to land in this case, which E68's deadline rules out; a `NewServer` with `RequiredApprovals` 0 and, separately, −1 freezes `required_approvals = 1` and the record encodes (`AttachHuman` refuses such a server, `server.go:221-223`, so this is the machine-only shape) (the human-plane vote POST under the lock is A11's test, cancel and deny-all D2's);
  - **(r5.7, review 8 #4) reads during writer contention:** on the same file-backed store, which `Open` puts in WAL mode (`pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/sqlite.go:104,187,193-194`), with a second connection holding `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for 6 s, Get and List each answer 200 from committed state within 1 s, because a WAL writer does not block readers. (r5.7, critique of the fold) Wait is tested on two records, because a Waiter is woken only after a confirmed commit (A6) and none can land under the lock: a Wait on a record decided before the lock was taken returns its committed decision within 1 s, and a Wait with a 1 s bound on a pending record returns 200 with the unchanged pending summary at that bound (within 2 s). Read-side `busy` is tested by injection: a store wrapper that returns an injected `SQLITE_BUSY` from each one's plain read answers the 503 `busy` envelope, byte-equal to the golden;
  - **(r5.6) threshold bound (review 7 #2):** `hosted.New` with `RequiredApprovals: 257` → `ErrInvalidRequiredApprovals`, with or without `Auth.RPID`; the signer-server binary with `--required-approvals 257` exits non-zero naming the 256 limit, with and without `--ui`, before opening the database; with `--required-approvals 256` and 256 eligible operators it starts, and v1 rows behave as today for every N ≤ 256;
  - **(r5.1) flush on the synchronous path:** with the worker's tick paused, an approve committed through the engine's Vote, followed at once by a Claim over HTTP, returns signatures (the flusher's post-commit nudge plus the 2 s wait), never a lasting `not_ready`;
  - **(r5.5, critique of the fold) one hosted flusher:** with a counting sink, 50 concurrent engine Votes (approves on 50 rows) while the retry pass runs never write one `event_key` twice while the sink works; a sink write stalled 5 s holds no SQLite lock (a concurrent Submit commits meanwhile); on the `:memory:` store the worker pass and the flusher pass each complete under a 1 s test deadline;
  - **(r5.1) retention defaults:** with `--approval-max-age` unset, no v2 row is ever deleted; at an injected `--approval-max-rows=20` with only settled rows, Submit answers `queue_full` and the worker logs the ceiling; shedding still runs and a shed row keeps its decision and votes (E23);
  - **(r5.3) clock:** wall steps back of 10 s and of 1 h refuse neither Submit nor an engine Vote ((r5.7) the vote POST is A11's), and a Claim for a row decided after the step proceeds ((r5.3, critique pass 1) A1b's 10 s `clock_skew` case is repeated against the row store); (r5.3, critique pass 1) with the worker running and no command, a claimed, unreported row leaves `live` at `claim_end` (its Erase is due through `next_due_at`), and Shed and Delete run at their ages; the E52 predicate over the slot columns equals `approvalplan.Class` on A1b's generator, with Clock run and not run; with the worker paused, an engine Vote after expiry commits `expired` (refused `expired`) and a Claim past `claim_end` commits `window_closed` (`expired`), and a 7 min step back afterwards changes neither; a row decided while the clock ran 1 h fast gives `materialization_error{clock_skew}` at its first Claim, and a pre-minted set is discarded when Clock or the 5 s check refuses inside the transaction (E21);
  - **(r5.2) advisory binding survives Shed (review 4 #8):** with the stderr sink replaced by one that discards everything, a row is approved at assist revision 1 (ready), Flushed, then Shed at `--approval-retention`, and the signer-server restarts. The row's header then still yields `rationale_redacted`, `decided_by`, the votes and `assist_bound {state, revision, digest}`, and they equal the decision event that was flushed before the Shed ((r5.7, review 8 #3) `/ui/audit`'s `v2_entries` showing them is A11's). The same holds for a decision at revision 0 (Deny only) and for one bound to the synthesized `unavailable` snapshot;
  - `approvalplan.Decide` agrees with the hosted `Decide` (`approval.go:94`) over a generated corpus of policies and votes; (r5.4) and the v2 engine's decision agrees with **`SubmitVote`'s**, not just `Decide`'s: over a generated corpus of stored N, requester `client_id`, `sub_principal`, flags (including a lying `Requester` and a zero `RequiredApprovals` in the handler's policy struct) and votes, the v2 Vote's decision equals `Decide` under the effective policy `approval_engine.go:197-204` builds for the same row; the admission predicate agrees with `approvalplan.Class`;
  - **(r5.4) effective policy at the engine (review 5 #1; the v2 port of `TestEngine_SelfApproveOnVsOff`, `approval_engine_test.go:224-260`):** with `--required-approvals 2`, a v2 row approved by one operator stays pending and is approved by the second, and the first Claim then returns signatures; the same row submitted with N = 2, after a server restart with `--required-approvals 1`, still needs two approvals (N is frozen); with `--allow-self-approve=false`, an approve by the operator account whose ID is the bearer `client_id` leaves the row pending, for `sub_principal` empty and for `local-uid:1000` alike, while another operator's approve decides it; with `--allow-self-approve=true` the same vote decides it; with `--deny-veto` on, a requester's own deny does not veto when self-approval is off, and another operator's deny does; with the handler's policy struct deliberately carrying `Requester: "someone-else"` and `RequiredApprovals: 0`, every outcome above is unchanged; the cache columns equal the View computed at the `now` of every commit and every worker pass. **(r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-3)** A claimed, unreported row passes `last_end + report_horizon` with the worker running: within one pass `class` reads `settled`, `view_status` `unreported` and `next_due_at` the next future term (the Shed age) or null, `record_json` is byte-unchanged, no event is appended, and the next pass does not select the row (a counting `DueV2` wrapper).
- **Ledger:** L2, L3, L6 (engine), L7 (hosted), R4 (hosted legacy), (r5.4) C-49 (engine), C-57, (r5.5) C-58 (hosted), (r5.6) C-61 (hosted machine plane, worker and assist), C-62 (engine and startup), C-2 (hosted), C-12 (hosted), L4, C-15, C-16, C-18, C-20, C-23 (hosted), C-26 (hosted), C-28 (HTTP), (r5.1) C-35 (hosted), (r5.2) C-39 (hosted), C-45 (hosted), (r5.8) C-67 (hosted assist).

The first page carries both lists, and later pages follow each list's own token. (r5.1) Every `v2_pending` item carries its `record_handle` and its `sub_principal`, and links to `/ui/v2/request/{handle}`.

A handle that matches no row is 404 and more than one row 409 "ambiguous handle".

  - **Session context (E7):** `withSession` also stores `{session ID, ExpiresAt}` in the request context under an unexported key; `next`'s signature is unchanged. Logout is unchanged (no challenge rows exist).
  - **MAC challenges (E7):** the key is generated at `Server` construction and never persisted; the detail GET issues `vote_challenge` for both planes, with `allowed_actions={deny}` while a v2 record's assist is preparing; it writes nothing; `expires_at` never exceeds the session's `ExpiresAt`.
  - The vote POST runs E7's order of checks in `handleVote` ((r5.1) the challenge check now before step-up, and the v1 duplicate and lost-race handling of E7 step 7; (r5.8, review 9 #1) and the v1 inert lines of steps 3, 5 and 7, through A6's inert-verdict encoding). The `role='operator'` check is at the HTTP layer for v1 rows and in the v2 engine (A10) for v2 records.
  - **(r5.6, critique of the fold) Contention on the v2 vote POST (E21, E7):** A11 builds the per-step `busy` mapping with A10's classifier: steps 3 and 5 → 503 "busy — retry" before step-up; a `SQLITE_BUSY` from step 6's `ConsumeTOTPStep` → 503 "busy — retry with a fresh code" (found with `errors.As` through `StepUp`'s error), never 401; step 7 → 503, "busy — retry with a fresh code" when step 6 consumed a step, otherwise "busy — retry". v1 rows keep today's answers. `request.js` shows a 503 as "the signer is busy — tap again" and, for the fresh-code message, clears the code field and asks for the next code.
  - `request.js`: `terminal()` (`:10-11`) learns the v2 statuses and treats a v1 `terminal_reason` as terminal; it renders the reason, assist, expiry, states and the K20 label as text nodes only; votes post the challenge. (r5.1) It reads `?id=` for v1 and `?h=` for v2 and builds the matching route; `pending.js` links v2 items by handle.
- **Tests:**
  - `TestUIPending_RenderIntegrityFields` (`human_handlers_test.go:748`) is extended;
  - a planted secret is absent from all UI JSON; the salt and the MAC key never appear in any response;
  - a missing challenge returns 400; a stale revision returns 409 "view changed"; a MAC altered in any bound field returns 409;
  - **the order of checks (r5.1):** unknown 404, resolved 409 ((r5.3) after a Clock pass in its own transaction, so a v2 row past `request_expires_at` with the worker paused is committed `expired` and answered 409), non-operator 403, then the challenge checks (missing 400, stale 409), then step-up 401, then the vote; a stale challenge with a valid TOTP consumes no TOTP step, so the operator can reload and vote at once;
  - **replays and duplicates (E61, r5.1):** v2: the same challenge posted twice by one operator → the second is inert and audited (`vote_replay`); the opposite decision → inert and audited (`vote_replay`); a POST on a resolved record → 409 and audited (`vote_refused`); (r5.4, E7 step 5) a POST whose challenge carries a revision below the row's after Clock, one whose MAC has expired, and one bound to another session each answer 409 "view changed", append exactly one `vote_refused{stale_challenge | expired_challenge | session_mismatch}` event in their own transaction, and consume no TOTP step; with the flusher held, 40 such POSTs on one row leave 32 unflushed events and `noise_dropped = 8` (SA-7's fold). v1: an opposite POST with a valid challenge → 409 "operator already voted" and one `vote_duplicate` audit line; a same-decision POST after an audited vote → today's 200 answer and one `vote_duplicate` audit line; a same-decision POST after `SubmitVote`'s audit failed (500) resumes to the flip, as today, with no extra line;
  - **(r5.8, review 9 #1) v1 replays on resolved rows and stale v1 challenges (K15), kept apart from the pending N-of-M duplicates above:** with a recording sink and `--required-approvals 1`: (a) operator A approves → 200 `flipped: true` and one ordinary `verdict` line; A then approves again with a freshly fetched challenge → today's 409 "request already resolved" bytes and exactly one `verdict_inert` line, `vote_duplicate`, decision approve; (b) the same, with A's second POST a deny → 409 and one `vote_duplicate` line, decision deny; (c) operator B posts on the approved row → 409 and one `vote_refused{resolved}` line; (d) after expiry: a pending row past `request_expires_at` with the worker paused, posted by A → 409, the row expired with its E4c expiry line, and one `vote_refused{expired}` line; a second POST gives one more `vote_refused{expired}`; with `--required-approvals 2`, an operator whose approve was audited before the expiry and who posts after it gets `vote_duplicate`; (e) after cancel: a row canceled through A9's `CancelLegacy` → 409 and one `vote_refused{canceled}` line; (f) ((r5.8, critique of the fold) under E7 step 5's v1 mapping) an expired challenge on a pending v1 row → today's 409 "view changed" and one `vote_refused{expired_challenge}` line; a cross-session challenge, one minted before a server restart, and a garbled one → 409 and one `vote_refused{stale_challenge}` line each; exactly one line per POST and no TOTP step consumed; (g) a fake store that resolves the row between step 3 and `SubmitVote` (`ErrAlreadyResolved`) → 409 and one `vote_refused{resolved}` line; (r5.8, critique of the fold) (g') the same race under `--required-approvals 2` for an operator whose approve was already audited → exactly one `vote_duplicate` line for that POST, not a second line; (g'') an admin-role session posting on a resolved row → today's 409 and no line; (h) ((r5.8, critique of the fold) `SubmitVote`'s own vote audit is fail-closed, `approval_engine.go:178-190`, so the sink fails only after each case's setup and only for verdicts carrying `Approval`) every answer above is byte-identical and the row unchanged. In every case the refused POST adds no `verdict` line, the row and its vote rows are byte-unchanged, and `git diff` of `approval_engine.go` and `approval_engine_test.go` is empty;
  - **(r5.1) a v1 vote that loses to `ExpireLegacy` or `CancelLegacy`** (fake store that runs the CAS between `PrepareVote` and the flip) → 409 "request already resolved", the minted signatures discarded, and the expiry or cancel audit line names the vote row left;
  - **(r5.1) two principals, one `request_id`:** two `sub_principal`s submit the same `request_id`; `/ui/pending` shows two `v2_pending` items with different handles; each detail GET, vote, (D2) cancel and the snapshot act on exactly one record; `/ui/request/a2_…` is 404;

  - **session binding (R1.9):** a challenge fetched in session A and posted from session B of the same operator → 409 "view changed", inert; after logout and a fresh login the old challenge → 409; an expired session → 401; **a signer-server restart → the old challenge gives 409** and a reloaded one works;
  - a v1 row overdue at POST time → 409 "request already resolved" and is expired, even when it holds an unaudited vote;
  - a non-operator (`admin`) cannot vote;
  - (r5.4) over HTTP, with `--required-approvals 2` and self-approval off: a v2 approve POST by the operator whose ID is the bearer `client_id` leaves the row pending, and two other operators' POSTs approve it (C-49);
  - (r5.6) over HTTP, the vote room boundary (C-62): with `--required-approvals 3` (`VoteCap` 67), deny-veto off, self-approval off and 67 eligible operators seeded: 63 deny POSTs are accepted; a 64th deny and the requester's own approve each answer 409 "vote limit reached: this vote cannot change the outcome", append one `vote_refused{set_full}` and store no vote; three other operators' approve POSTs are then all accepted and the third approves the row (66 votes stored); the same sequence with `--deny-veto` on is denied by the first deny;
  - (r5.6, critique of the fold; moved from A10) **vote POST under contention (C-61):** on a file-backed store in `t.TempDir()`, a second connection holds `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for 6 s: (a) step-up off: a v2 approve POST answers 503 "busy — retry", the row is byte-unchanged with no event appended, and the same challenge posted after the release counts; (b) `--require-step-up`: the POST answers 503 "busy — retry" at step 3, the operator's `totp_replay.last_step` is unchanged, and the same challenge and the same code then count; (c) a store wrapper that answers `SQLITE_BUSY` only to `ConsumeTOTPStep` → 503 "busy — retry with a fresh code", never 401; (d) a wrapper that lets step 6 consume and then fails step 7's `BEGIN` with `SQLITE_BUSY` → 503 "busy — retry with a fresh code", no vote stored, the same code then refused 401 and the next code counts; (e) a v1 row under (c) keeps today's 401; (f) (r5.7, review 8 #4) under the held lock the detail GET, a plain WAL read, answers 200 with a challenge; a JS test renders both 503 messages as text nodes;
  - **(r5.7, review 8 #3; moved from A10) the hosted flush and assist machinery through the vote POST and the UI:** (r5.7, critique of the fold) labelled (g)–(l), because (a)–(f) already name the contention cases above: (g) the stderr sink fails exactly one write, the approve POST's post-commit Flush, and works afterwards, with the worker running and no later command: within two passes `audited_complete` is 1 and an executor-style Claim loop over HTTP gets signatures before `claim_end` (TTL 60); (h) with the worker's tick paused, an approve POST followed at once by a Claim returns signatures; (i) with a counting sink, 50 concurrent approve POSTs on 50 rows never write one `event_key` twice; (j) a restart with a record in `preparing`, `--assist-timeout` 5 s and the worker's 1 s tick: by `assist_deadline` + 1 s, or 1 s after the restart when the restart comes later, the detail GET issues a challenge whose `allowed_actions` include approve, with no second provider call (r5.7, critique of the fold); (k) wall steps back of 10 s and 1 h refuse no vote POST; (l) A10's advisory-binding case (discarding sink, Flush, Shed, restart) for revision 1, revision 0 and the synthesized `unavailable` snapshot: `/ui/audit`'s `v2_entries` shows `rationale_redacted`, `decided_by`, the votes and `assist_bound`, equal to the flushed decision event;
