### A12 — Local daemon's hosted proxy: a stateless forwarder (E5, E19, E32, E42, E69)

- **Depends:** A7, A8 (`main.go`: A8 is the first writer), A10, A11 (the end-to-end test approves through the challenge-bearing human vote route).
- **Owns:** new `pkg/signerkit/hosted_v2_client.go` (an HTTP implementation of the machine-side `Lifecycle` operations, speaking `approvalwire` JSON; it must not import `hosted`, E62); the `backend=hosted` branch in `pkg/signerkit/daemon_approvals.go`; signer `main.go` (**second writer**: `buildHostedBackend` wiring); a comment-only glossary line in `hosted.go` (E42); new external test `tests/hostedproxy/` (or `package signerkit_test`), because an internal `signerkit` test cannot import `hosted`.
- **Content (r5):** every v2 kind is forwarded with `requester_sub = local-uid:<peer uid>` (E19). The proxy keeps **no state**: no index, no reservation, no journal (E66 is deleted). E71's reserved-verb check and E25's mandatory claim-token hash run before anything is forwarded. A hosted non-envelope 404 on any `/v2/approvals` route is answered with the local `signer_incompatible` frame, byte-exact per E69, plus one `refused{signer_incompatible}` audit row ((r5.7, review 8 #2): r5–r5.6 named the code but A5 had no frame for it). A hosted 503 "storage uncertain" passes through as `storage_uncertain`, and (r5.6) a hosted 503 `busy` envelope as `busy`, never as `lifecycle_unavailable` or a transport error (E21). Submit returns at hosted commit (E63). Hosted errors never collapse to `timeout` (E5).
- **Tests:**
  - an in-process `hosted.Server` (A10) behind the local daemon socket, driven by the MCP approval client, end to end;
  - **owner scope through the proxy (R1.2, R3.4):** uid B cannot Get, Wait, List, Claim or Report uid A's request; the `not_found` responses are byte-identical to a missing id's, for full and shed hosted rows (peer uid injected through the test peer hook); uids A and B submitting the same `request_id` concurrently, in both orders under `-race`, get two independent records, and neither can see the other's; after a proxy restart nothing changes, because there is nothing local to lose;
  - a lost Submit response is reconciled by A's own idempotent resubmit or Get;
  - a `command_job` draft without `executor_claim_token_hash` is refused before anything is forwarded; an E71 reserved verb is refused before any hosted call (the fake hosted server sees zero requests); (r5.4, SA-7) each of these proxy refusals, and a hosted `conflict`, `queue_full` and `not_found` passed through, writes exactly one local audit row with transition `refused{code}`;
  - a hosted server without `/v2/approvals` gives `signer_incompatible`; a hosted 503 storage-uncertain reaches the caller as `storage_uncertain`;
  - **(r5.7, review 8 #2) older hosted server → current proxy → MCP client:** two fixtures of `366ba07`'s answers to every v2 route (`POST /v2/approvals`, `GET /v2/approvals/{id}`, `GET /v2/approvals?limit=&cursor=`, `POST /v2/approvals/wait`, `/claim`, `/report`), captured by the orchestrator at A0's base and committed as testdata: the policy plane attached (its dispatch answers JSON 404 `{"error":"not found"}` for every non-policy `/v2/` path, `pkg/signerkit/hosted/server.go:250-252`, `policy_runtime.go:79-93`) and not attached (the mux's plain-text 404). An `httptest` server replays them behind the current daemon in `backend=hosted`, driven by the A5 MCP approval client: each of the six kinds receives the `signer_incompatible` frame byte-equal to A5's golden and returns `ErrSignerIncompatible`; the proxy writes exactly one `refused{signer_incompatible}` audit row per call; a v2-capable server's typed `not_found` envelope still reaches the client as `not_found`; (r5.7, critique of the fold) so does a current server's grammar 404 (an empty id, an id carrying an escaped `/`, and a v1 `r_…` id, each on Get, Claim and Report, and `await_approvals`-style Gets of such ids): each reaches the client as `not_found`, never `signer_incompatible`, and the proxy writes `refused{not_found}`, never `refused{signer_incompatible}`; A13b runs the same fixtures through the MCP tools;
  - **(r5.6) contention through the proxy (review 7 #1):** the in-process `hosted.Server` on a file-backed store, a second connection holding `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` past the busy timeout once during each of Submit, Claim and Report, driven by the A5 MCP approval client through the local daemon socket, (r5.6, critique of the fold) with the test resending by hand, because neither coordinator exists yet (A13b follows A12, and C2 is in Phase C): each busy answer reaches the client as `ErrBusy` (`busy` in the local frame); the test resends the same Submit, Claim and Report values after the release, and a recording `http.RoundTripper` under the proxy shows each resent request body byte-identical to the first (the Submit draft and `request_id`, the Claim's token and `plan_hash`, the canonical report body); there is then one record, one signature set and one accepted report; the proxy writes exactly one fail-open local audit row with `transition = refused{busy}` for each `busy` it passes through, as SA-7 requires of every passed-through code, and none for the resends that succeed. A13b and C2 build the same harness in their own test files (an in-process `hosted.Server` on a file-backed store behind the daemon socket, the lock-holding connection and the recording transport, all from public constructors, as test-only imports) to drive their coordinators through it; this test is its reference;
  - v2 reveal, grant and transfer fail closed;
  - **(r5.1) hosted-mode v1 is today's path (E8e):** with the daemon built as `main.go` builds `backend=hosted`, v1 `sign`, `request_grant`, `transfer` and `register_xfer_key` go through `HostedServerBackend` and their request and response bytes equal today's (a recorded fixture), and no `LegacyRequest` is ever created; (r5.2, OQ3 (b)) hosted mode never holds a v1 grant, because `HostedServerBackend.RequestGrant` fails closed before any HTTP call (`hosted.go:356-366`), so `request_grant` stores nothing; the test asserts that refusal, as today, and that a later v1 `sign` on the same alias is forwarded to `HostedServerBackend`. OQ3 itself is covered by A7's backend-independent tests;
  - `hosted_test.go`, `hosted_extra_test.go` and `hosted_transfer_test.go` stay unchanged and green.
- **Ledger:** L7 (proxy), C-15 (proxy), C-17, C-28 (proxy), (r5.4) C-55 (proxy), (r5.6) C-61 (proxy), (r5.7) C-65 (proxy).
- **Must not touch:** the `HostedServerBackend` v1 methods (`hosted.go:129-384`), `wire_frozen_test.go`.
- **Accept:** STD; FROZEN-LOCAL; FROZEN-HOSTED.

### A13a — `jobplan`, phase-aware SSH and `registry.ReadCurrent` (E10, E28, E29, E30, E33, E35, E56, E60, E71, E74)

- **Depends:** A1a, and (r5.2) A1b: the runner leaves finality to `approvalplan.JobView`, which A1b supplies (review 4 #9).
- **Owns:** `src/mcp/jobplan/**`, `src/mcp/ssh/phased.go` and its test, and in `src/mcp/registry/servers.go` the additive `ReadCurrent` and `Snapshot` helpers.
- **Content:**
  - The draft builder: classify every step and assign routes ((r5.7, critique of the fold) route `signed_reveal` only for the one step of a single-command `run(reveal:true)` draft: `RunBatchInput` has no reveal field and a `run_batch` draft never has a `signed_reveal` step, because bulk reveal is forbidden, `run_batch.go:50-55`, `run_batch_reveal_test.go` `TestRunBatch_NeverReveals`); full ordered batch; TTL table; stop policy; bounds; refuse an empty fingerprint; trusted display for admin steps; E71's `ValidateStepBytes` as an early courtesy check; `submit_not_after` and `coordinator` (E73, E26). It takes the `request_id`, and (r5.2) for an executor job the `submit_not_after`, from its caller (the MCP fixes both, §1.5 SA-2, SA-5); it never computes a window of its own for async.
  - **(r5) `registry.ReadCurrent(alias) (RouteSnapshot, digest, error)`** reads and validates the current `servers.json` on every call: the same insecure-mode refusal, decode and legacy-wrapper detection as `Load` (`servers.go:97-196`), then the alias lookup. It never consults or updates the cache. Any stat, read, permission, parse or lookup failure is an error, which the caller treats as a refused dispatch (`stale_target`), never as "unchanged" (R3.6). (r5.1) It opens the file once and validates and reads through that descriptor (`Load` stats and reads by path twice, `:98`, `:115`); an absent file or an empty body is "alias not found", an error (where `Load` treats them as an empty registry, `:98-107`, `:118-124`). An I/O failure (`EMFILE`, `EINTR`, `EACCES`) keeps the `stale_target` class but carries the distinct fixed detail "registry unreadable", so the operator is not told the target drifted. An unreadable registry ends the step at once rather than waiting, because waiting would spend the step's signature window. `Get`, `List` and `reloadIfChanged` keep their behaviour (`servers.go:222-263`) unchanged for every other caller. (r5.5) That behaviour is last-known only for a file that cannot be loaded: a read failure keeps the cache (`:241-247`), and a malformed or insecure file fails `Load`, which then leaves the map as it was (`:111-113`, `:126-128`); a mode-only change leaves the bytes' hash unchanged, so nothing reloads (`:250-253`). An absent file empties the registry (`Load`, `:98-107`, reached from `:230-238`), and a valid changed file is reloaded (`:255-264`).
  - The step runner, behind a `PhasedRunner` interface: per-step K16 check on **signed** steps only (r5.2; an unsigned step has no signature window, E10, E74); (r5.3) on the held (wall, elapsed) pair of §1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4, read through `internal/elapsed` and acquired once per step in the process, at the first Claim whose answer carries that step's window, never at a repeat Claim (r5.3, critique pass 1), for the step about to be dispatched only, with the held set dropped at the last signed step's `Start`, a K16 failure or the run's end; (r5.4) the check before `BeforeStep`, and the re-check passed to `RunPhased` as its pre-start guard (E28) for every signed step, so it runs after the dial, handshake and session setup and immediately before `Session.Start`; a `guard_refused` answer becomes `expired_unexecuted`; the stale_target check immediately before dispatch, comparing `ReadCurrent`'s digest with the frozen one; bounded capture in async mode and today's semantics in sync mode; the E29 step-status model, abort rule and `AggregateJobClass`, with `executed_ordinals` and `partial`.
  - **(r5.7, review 8 #1; inputs corrected in r5.7, critique of the fold) `StepOutcome(kind, earlierExecuted, phased) → (status, annotation, remediation)`**, the one mapping from a `RunPhased` result to E29's step status, used by the runner for every step on both paths and by the transfer coordinator for each leg (A13b). `kind` is the step kind: `unsigned_read`, `signed_write`, `signed_reveal`, `admin_revoke`, `admin_update`, `xfer_send` or `xfer_recv`. It is the route, refined for `signed_admin` by the verb in the step's command bytes (which `ValidateStepBytes` already limits to `SSHGATE_REVOKE` and `SSHGATE_UPDATE <hash>`) and given by the transfer coordinator for a leg; it is a function input only, never stored or sent. `earlierExecuted` is whether any earlier step of the job is in E29's executed set. **Status** depends only on the route: a known non-zero exit after dispatch is `failed` (executed) on every route, except that exit 77 with the `read jail unavailable` line is `failed_not_started` on `unsigned_read` alone (E10); stderr never changes a signed step's status. **Annotation and remediation** depend on the kind: `signed_write` and `signed_reveal` get E10's "gate refused (signature)" for exit 65 and for 77 without the line, with retry advice only when `earlierExecuted` is false, and for 77 with the line the `read_jail_unavailable` remediation with no retry advice; `admin_revoke`, `admin_update`, `xfer_send` and `xfer_recv` reproduce today's texts (`revoke_server.go:116-121`; `update_gate.go:190-215`, where 65 is a deterministic binary-check refusal, "Do NOT re-run", 70 a failed binary replace and 77 a denied update; `xferSendExitNote` and `xferRecvExitNote`, `transfer.go:208-237`), never carry retry advice and never read the jail line, which the gate cannot print for them (it dispatches those verbs before classify, `src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate/main.go:232-254`).
  - A `StepJournal` hook interface, `BeforeStep(k)` (called after the checks, before `Start`; the executor commits the marker there) and `AfterStep(k, result)` (the executor commits the result there; a check failure calls `AfterStep` with no `BeforeStep`). The synchronous path passes a no-op. Whether the job is final is decided by `approvalplan.JobView`, not by the runner (§1.5 SA-5).
  - `RunPhased` with the typed, command-free `PhasedError` (E28) and (r5.4) the optional `preStart` guard, called after `NewSession` and immediately before `Session.Start`. The runner never formats an underlying SSH error into any output.
- **Tests:**
  - the stop-policy matrix matches today's `run_batch` behaviour exactly (`run_batch.go:237-297`), (r5.7, critique of the fold) except, by name, an `unsigned_read` step the read jail refused: it is `failed_not_started` and ends the run, where today its 77 continues under the class default (E29, §1.3 item 39);
  - K16 gives `expired_unexecuted` followed by `not_run`; (r5.2) an unsigned read after a signed step whose window has closed is dispatched (no K16 refusal), and a later signed step is still refused by K16; (r5.3) with the `internal/elapsed` fake: a 7 min step back during a running signed step still refuses the next signed step whose window has really ended (S4); a forward step past W refuses it by the wall half; `Suspend(8 h)` with `StepWall(−8 h)` refuses it; the static time-read test over `src/mcp/jobplan` passes;
  - stale_target; an empty fingerprint is refused;
  - **`ReadCurrent` fails closed (R3.6):** after a successful draft build, the registry file is made malformed, unreadable (mode 0000), insecure (group-writable), absent, or changed for that alias; each `ReadCurrent` returns an error or a different digest, and the runner refuses the step as `stale_target` with zero dispatches. (r5.5, review 6 #5) `Get` keeps today's behaviour in each scenario, asserted separately: malformed, unreadable and insecure → `Get` returns the last-known entry, unchanged; absent → `Get` returns not-found, because `Load` empties the registry (`servers.go:98-107`); a valid file changed for that alias → `Get` returns the new entry (the reload at `servers.go:255-264`). Each case is first run against the code at A0's base to confirm it is today's behaviour, not a new one;
  - **(r5.4) the pre-start guard (review 5 #4):** against an in-process `x/crypto/ssh` server that counts exec requests and whose handshake, and separately whose session-channel open, a test hook delays, with the `internal/elapsed` fake: K16 passes before `BeforeStep`; during the delay the wall half is moved past W − 2 s → `RunPhased` returns `guard_refused`, the server records **zero** exec requests, the step is `expired_unexecuted` and later steps `not_run`; the same with only the elapsed half moved past `elapsed_end − 2 s`; with neither moved, the guard passes and exactly one exec request arrives; for an unsigned step no guard runs and a delayed handshake still dispatches; a guard error string never carries the command;
  - (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C2-1) `RunPhased` with an `SSHGATE_SIG:` wire command and a nil guard returns `failed_not_started` with `Detail` "missing pre-start guard", and the in-process server sees no connection; with an unsigned wire command and a nil guard it runs;
  - phased SSH against an in-process `x/crypto/ssh` server: a rejected exec gives `failed_not_started`, a channel closed without exit-status gives `indeterminate`, exit 3 is known, and cancel after exit is still known;
  - **aggregation (E29):** a mixed batch under the class default whose earlier read exits non-zero and whose later write's channel closes after `Start` → job `indeterminate`, steps `[failed, indeterminate]`, (r5.6, review 7 #3) `executed_ordinals [1, 2]` and `partial: false` (both steps ran or may have run; r5–r5.5 expected `partial` here, against E29's definition); the same batch with a third, signed step → steps `[failed, indeterminate, not_run]`, job `indeterminate`, `executed_ordinals [1, 2]`, `partial: true`, and no `BeforeStep` for step 3; in every case below the runner's `executed_ordinals` and `partial` equal `approvalplan.ExecutedOrdinals` and `Partial` over its step list; the same earlier failure followed by a later signed step whose window has expired → `expired_unexecuted`, later steps `not_run`, `partial`; `stop_on_error:false` with an earlier failed write and a later dial failure → `failed_not_started`, `partial`; an explicit `stop_on_error:true` stop after a failure → `failed`, later steps `skipped`, (r5.6) `partial: true`;
  - **(r5.7, review 8 #1; keyed on the step kind in r5.7, critique of the fold) `StepOutcome` table:** every step kind (`unsigned_read`, `signed_write`, `signed_reveal`, `admin_revoke`, `admin_update`, `xfer_send`, `xfer_recv`) × `earlierExecuted` (false, true) × {exit 0, exit 3, exit 65, exit 70, exit 77 without the line, exit 77 with the line, exit 3 with the line, each `PhasedError` class} has one row. Exit 77 with the line is `failed_not_started`, not executed, only for `unsigned_read`; on every signed kind it is `failed`, executed. "gate refused (signature)" with retry advice appears only for `signed_write` and `signed_reveal` with `earlierExecuted` false; the `read_jail_unavailable` remediation only for `unsigned_read`, `signed_write` and `signed_reveal`. The `admin_update` rows equal `update_gate.go:190-215`'s texts (65 the binary-check refusal with no retry, 70 the failed replace, 77 the denied update) for both values of `earlierExecuted`, the `admin_revoke` rows equal `revoke_server.go`'s, and the `xfer_send` and `xfer_recv` rows equal `xferSendExitNote` and `xferRecvExitNote`; none of these four kinds carries retry advice or the read-jail remediation, with or without the line. Through the in-process SSH server: a `signed_reveal` step whose command prints `gate: read jail unavailable: jail probe failed` to stderr and exits 77 → `[failed]`, `executed_ordinals [1]`, `partial: false`; `[unsigned_read printing the line and exiting 77, signed_write]` → `[failed_not_started, not_run]`, `executed_ordinals []`, `partial: false`, and the server records no exec request for step 2 ((r5.7, critique of the fold) the named change of §1.3 item 39);
  - `BeforeStep` runs strictly before `Start` and `AfterStep` strictly after completion, and a `BeforeStep` error prevents dispatch; for each abort status × each stop policy × last or not-last step, the runner never calls `BeforeStep` for a later step after an abort;
  - **signatures never leak through SSH errors:** an in-process SSH server rejects the exec request for a signed wire command (`SSHGATE_SIG:<sig>:<payload> touch /x`). `RunPhased` returns `failed_not_started` with `Detail` "exec request rejected". Neither `PhasedError.Error()`, the runner's step result, nor any captured log line contains `SSHGATE_SIG:`, the signature base64, the payload base64 or the command text. The same holds for a handshake failure, a `Start` I/O error and a transport error after `Start`. A fuzz seed over random command bytes asserts that no error string contains the command.
- **Ledger:** C-3, C-5, C-7, C-9, C-19 (runner), C-27, C-30 ((r5.5) with `Get`'s three behaviours), (r5.2) C-44 (runner), (r5.3) C-35 (runner), (r5.4) C-52 (runner), (r5.6) C-63 (runner), (r5.7) C-64 (runner).
- **Must not touch:** `ssh/client.go` `Run`/`RunWithStdin`, `known_hosts.go`, and the behaviour of `registry` `Get`/`List`/`Load`.
- **Accept:** STD; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/mcp/jobplan/... ./src/mcp/ssh/... ./src/mcp/registry/...`.

### A13b — MCP synchronous migration to v2 (K3, K12, K13; E10–E12, E25–E27)

- **Depends:** A5, A7, A13a, A12 (merge gate: hosted-backed users get no interim break).
- **Owns:** `src/mcp/tools/**`, `src/mcp/server.go`, `src/mcp/*_test.go`, `src/mcp/cmd/sshgate-mcp/main.go`, `tests/integration/**`, `commands/{run,run_batch,revoke}.md`, the reason edits in `skills/**`, `AGENTS.md` (reason, exit-65 per E10, v2 verdict-undelivered, (r5.7, critique of the fold) and the read-jail line at `:101-102`), the reason edits in `README.md`, and the reason column in `docs/design.md`.
- **Content:**
  - `Runner` gains `Approvals ApprovalClient`, a new interface. `SignClient` stays for `revoke_grant` and `list_grants`.
  - `run` and `run_batch`:
    - classify;
    - pure reads take today's path;
    - otherwise validate the reason and bounds, mint the `request_id` once for this call (§1.5 SA-2), then build the draft with an ephemeral claim token;
    - Submit with `coordinator=in_process_sync` and `submit_not_after = now + 120 s` (E73), reconciling `ErrResponseLost` with `Get` (and resubmitting the same tuple, same `request_id`, only inside the window);
    - **`storage_uncertain` (§1.5 SA-8)**, (r5.6) **`busy`** (E21: hosted contention, the command not applied), and (r5.1) a Claim's `not_ready` (E25; (r5.3) `clock_regressed` is withdrawn), from any signer call are retried with the same request (Submit tuple, claim token, report `seq` and body) with bounded backoff inside the sync wait budget, (r5.6, critique of the fold) and for Submit, which comes before that budget exists, inside E21's Submit-phase retry bound (the draft's `submit_not_after`, or 125 s of E). It is never mapped to a refusal or a Denial while its bound lasts. When the Submit-phase bound ends, or a resubmit answers `submit_window_closed`, E26's Submit-phase row applies: every attempt `busy` (or the window closed for an id the signer does not hold) → nothing was submitted, the `signer_unreachable` Denial with the "too busy to accept" sentence; an attempt whose outcome is still unknown → the v2 verdict-undelivered text (the request may exist). A later step returns `approval_pending` (with E26's `not_ready` or `busy` sentence) or, after Claim, reports what ran;
    - Wait loop for the sync budget `Runner.SyncWaitBudget` (300 s, E26), (r5.1) ending no later than `sync_claim_deadline − 5 s` from the Submit answer; (r5.3) the budget is a held deadline on the `internal/elapsed` pair (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4), and K16 uses the pair through jobplan's runner; on budget end with the request still pending, return the `approval_pending` Denial and execute nothing;
    - Claim, run via `jobplan`, Report (best effort; a negative report when nothing ran, E25). (r5.5) The coordinator Claims only after it has seen `approved`, so a Claim `expired` is the window's close, never the request's expiry: it ends the `not_ready` retries, records the first step `expired_unexecuted` and the rest `not_run`, sends the negative report and returns the non-retryable `expired_unexecuted` Denial with E26's approved-but-expired wording. `approval_timeout` comes only from an `expired` decision seen in Submit's answer, Wait or Get (§1.5 SA-4 Claim, SA-5). The synchronous coordinator sends one final report with `report_sequence` 1 whose body is a canonical function of its results (§1.5 SA-4), and every signed dispatch goes through `RunPhased` (E28), so no SSH error carries a signature into the MCP output. Before each signed step the runner calls `registry.ReadCurrent` (A13a), so a sync batch fails closed on an unreadable or changed registry too.
  - `request_grant`, `revoke_server`, `update_gate` and `transfer` gain `reason` and use their v2 payloads. Each acts as its own coordinator. `request_grant` and `revoke_server` now get Denial remediation. **(r5.5, critique of the fold)** Every in-process coordinator that Claims (the `run`/`run_batch` runner, `revoke_server`, `update_gate` and both `transfer` legs) applies the r5.5 rule above: a Claim `expired` after the `approved` it saw ends the call `expired_unexecuted` with nothing run, sends the one negative report and returns the non-retryable `expired_unexecuted` Denial, never `approval_timeout`. `revoke_server` signs with TTL 60 (`revoke_server.go:34`), so a timely approval with a failing sink meets this case exactly as `run` does; today it wraps every sign error (`revoke_server.go:56,105`), and the timeout mapping is the retryable `approval_timeout` (`run.go:495`, `denial.go:85-95`). For `update_gate` and `transfer` (TTL 300) the budget normally ends first, which gives the approved-but-unreleased `approval_pending`; the same rule covers the case where the window closes first.
  - (r5.7, review 8 #1) `read_jail_unavailable`'s class-level `Summary` is reworded to be true on every route, because a signed step now carries it while counted as executed (E10): "The gate reported that it could not confirm or set up the host's kernel read jail. An unsigned read refused this way did not run; on an approved (signed) command the line cannot prove that, so the command is counted as possibly run (see executed_ordinals)." A named change; class, action, `Retryable` and `HowTo` are unchanged. (r5.7, critique of the fold) The single unsigned `run` read keeps today's path and Denial class (`runRead`, `run.go:282-305`: `err == nil`, the Denial attached); only its `Summary` text changes, as this named change. Every signed `run` changes, a plain approved write as well as a reveal: today `runWrite` returns "…so nothing ran" for any signed command whose 77 carries the line (`run.go:402-404`); after A13b the step is `failed` and executed, and the error says the gate reported its read jail unavailable, the command may have run, it must not be retried, and a human runs `gate doctor` on the host (§1.3 item 38). The "did not run" reading applies only to an `unsigned_read` step, which the gate cannot run as a write. `AGENTS.md:101-102` is rewritten to match: an unsigned read refused this way did not run; an approved (signed or revealed) command that reports it counts as possibly run, so do not retry it, and a human runs `gate doctor` on the host. §4.1 lists the edit.
  - `denial.go` gains the 9 classes (E11), including `approval_pending`. (r5) `record_corrupt` from the signer maps to the existing `signer_unreachable` Denial (`escalate_to_human`). (r5.6, critique of the fold) So does a Submit phase that ends with nothing submitted (E26); `signer_unreachable`'s class-level `Summary` and `HowTo` are reworded per E26 to be true of all its cases (a named change; class, action and `Retryable` unchanged).
  - Input jsonschema text and `serverInstructions` follow E12.
- **Named changes (K3, K9, E11):**
  - `denial_shape_test.go` moves from 11 to 20 classes ((r5.3) #22 phase 1, `366ba07`, added `read_jail_unavailable`; r5.2 said 10 to 19);
  - (r5.7) `read_jail_unavailable`'s `Summary` text (above);
  - `transfer_test.go:241-257` adds `reason` to its allow-list;
  - `run_reveal_test.go:128-153` is rewritten because the reason now travels in the plan;
  - every write literal in tests gains `Reason`;
  - `tests/integration/helpers_test.go` (`:422+`) starts a daemon with a lifecycle and a scripted notifier.
- **Parity table (part of the spec for this port; committed as a comment table in `src/mcp/tools/parity_v2_test.go`).** Every write path moves from `Runner.Sign`/`TransferClient` to the new `ApprovalClient`. The 28 test files under `src/mcp` that wire `Sign:` were checked. Construction-only uses (`ping`, `list_servers`, `status*`, `list_grants`, `revoke_grant`, `outputcap` reads) keep `Sign` and are unchanged. Every test that drives a write and asserts on the fake signer moves to a `fakeApprovals` with the equivalent assertion:

  | Test (file:line) | Old assertion (v1 fake) | New assertion (fake `ApprovalClient`) |
  |---|---|---|
  | `run_reveal_test.go:128-153` | `sign.gotCmds[0].Reveal/Reason` | draft step route is `signed_reveal`/`signed_write`; plan `reason` equals the input; Claim returns the signature the runner uses |
  | `run_batch_reveal_test.go` `TestRunBatch_NeverReveals` | `RunBatchInput` has no reveal field; every bulk `CmdReq.Reveal` is false (no bulk reveal) | (r5.7, critique of the fold) ported, not dropped: `RunBatchInput` has no reveal field (asserted over its struct fields), and no step of any `run_batch` draft has route `signed_reveal`; per-step routes in one draft; reads stay `unsigned_read` at their ordinals |
  | `run_test.go`, `run_batch_test.go`, `run_batch_guards_test.go` | `gotCmds` count/order/TTL 60/`Host` | draft steps in order; `signature_ttl_seconds=60`; `trusted_host_fingerprint` from the registry; stop policy (E30) |
  | `run_signer_errors_test.go:223` (exit 65; (r5.3, critique pass 1) `:194` at `4314626`) and `format_internal_test.go:108-111` | "exit 65 … retry once" note | E10: first executed step → retry note; a later step → `failed` "gate refused (signature)" with no retry advice |
  | (r5.3, critique pass 1; r5.7) `run_signer_errors_test.go:191-221` `TestRun_GateReadJailDeny77` (#22 phase 1, `366ba07`) | read-jail exit 77 → `read_jail_unavailable` Denial, "nothing ran", for the reveal and the unsigned read alike | (r5.7, review 8 #1; corrected in r5.7, critique of the fold) the unsigned read: today's path, `err == nil`, exit 77 and a `read_jail_unavailable` Denial whose `Summary` is the reworded text; inside a mixed `run_batch` (an `unsigned_read` step before a signed step) an `unsigned_read` step with the line is `failed_not_started`, not executed. The reveal case: the step is `failed`, `executed_ordinals [1]`, the Denial is still `read_jail_unavailable` with `Retryable: false`, and the error contains neither "nothing ran" nor "did not run". The plain-77 case (no line) is unchanged |
  | `run_verdict_unknown_test.go` | v1 verdict-undelivered Denial | `ErrResponseLost` → `Get` reconcile; still unknown → the v2 verdict-undelivered text |
  | `run_error_path_denial_test.go`, `run_missing_key_test.go`, `run_readonly_test.go` | Denial before/after `Sign` | the same Denial before Submit; the fake sees zero calls for reads and Tier-1 |
  | `update_gate_test.go` (request-shape cases) | `CmdReq.Reason` "Build:" line, TTL 300 | `trusted_display` metadata (no `binary_sha256`); `signed_admin` TTL 300; hash only in the command bytes (E33) |
  | `update_gate_test.go:322-362` (exit 65 on update) | binary-check refusal, no "retry" advice | unchanged wording; it now arrives through the v2 step runner, from `StepOutcome`'s `admin_update` rows (A13a), never E10's retry advice although the update is always the job's first executed step (r5.7, critique of the fold) |
  | `revoke_server_test.go` | `Sign` with TTL 60, revoke verb | `signed_admin` step, TTL 60, `reason` required |
  | `request_grant_test.go` | `RequestGrant` fields | v2 `standing_grant` payload with `trusted_host_fingerprint` and `registry_entry_digest`; `reason` required |
  | `transfer_test.go:230-257` | `h.gotReq.SrcFP/DestFP/TTLSec` | the draft's transfer fields; TTL 300; `reason` in the allow-list |
  | (r4) `transfer_test.go`, `update_gate_test.go`, `revoke_server_test.go` SSH fakes | fake `SSH.Run` / `SSHStdin.RunWithStdin` for the signed legs | a fake `PhasedRunner` (E28: every signed dispatch, including the update stream and both transfer legs, goes through `RunPhased`); same assertions on wire command, stdin bytes and exit handling, (r5.7, critique of the fold) the exit texts coming from `StepOutcome`'s `admin_revoke`, `admin_update`, `xfer_send` and `xfer_recv` rows (`TestUpdateGate_GateRefusalExit70FsReplace` and `TestTransfer_SendGateDenyNoRecv` included) |
  | `livelog_hook_test.go`, `livelog_redact_test.go` | live-log lines around `Sign` | the same lines around Submit/Claim; reveal output still blanked |
  | `server_test.go`, `server_alltools_test.go` | tool wiring with `Sign` | wiring adds `Approvals`; the tool count stays 11 in A13b |
  | `sign/client*_test.go` | v1 client | **unchanged** (the v1 client stays) |

- **Integration tests that change (orchestrator runs them at the end of Phase A):**
  - `e2e_test.go:80` `WriteDeniedByStubBackend` → `WriteDeniedByScriptedNotifier`. Today `startSigner` (`helpers_test.go:428-444`) uses `StubBackend`, which with no lifecycle would answer `lifecycle_unavailable`. The helper wires a lifecycle with a scripted deny notifier, and the assertion stays `errors.Is(err, signpkg.ErrDenied)` through the v2 denial mapping.
  - `phase2_test.go` (`startSignerTelegram`, `:297-330`; deny `:673`; timeout `:881-914`) drives the real `TelegramBackend` with `fakeTG`. Its taps move from `approve:`/`deny:` to `v:a`/`v:d` callbacks carrying the card handle and challenge. The timeout case configures `pending_validity = 5m`, advances the lifecycle's injected clock past it instead of relying on a 500 ms `reqTimeout`, and asserts `approval_timeout` (E26). A new sibling case keeps the default 24 h validity, shortens `Runner.SyncWaitBudget`, and asserts `approval_pending`, a still-pending card, and no execution after a later approve tap.
  - `phase3_signed_write_test.go:41`, `phase4_test.go:220` and `phase5_update_test.go:138` use `startSignerAutoApprove` (`phase4_test.go:182-194`). That helper gains a lifecycle with an auto-approve notifier.
  - `phase5_update_test.go:270` (`TamperedStreamRefused`) calls `signClient.Sign` directly and stays on v1, unchanged.
  - `phase3_test.go` (provisioning, `noopSign`) is unchanged.
- **Tests:**
  - for each tool, `reason_required` fires before any signer call (the fake client sees zero calls);

  - bounds;
  - reads stay reason-free and prompt-free;
  - lost submit reconciles; the `request_id` is minted once per call, and every retry (lost response, `storage_uncertain`, (r5.6) `busy`) carries the same id and draft bytes;
  - a fake `ApprovalClient` that answers `storage_uncertain` once on Submit, Claim and Report in turn: each call is retried with identical bytes, the run completes exactly once, and no Denial is returned; (r5.6) the same with `ErrBusy` once on each of Submit, Wait, Claim and Report, for `run`, `run_batch`, `revoke_server`, `update_gate` and `transfer`; with `ErrBusy` on every Claim until the budget ends, the call returns budget-end `approval_pending` with E26's `busy` sentence (never the audit-record sentence, never `signer_unreachable` or a Denial built from `busy`); (r5.6, critique of the fold) **Submit-phase bound:** with `ErrBusy` on every Submit and the `internal/elapsed` fake, the coordinator stops resending at the draft's `submit_not_after` (and, separately with the wall clock stepped back 7 min, at 125 s of E), never calls Wait or Claim, and returns the `signer_unreachable` Denial with the "too busy to accept" sentence and zero runs; with `ErrResponseLost` on the first Submit, Get then `busy` until the bound → the v2 verdict-undelivered text; with `ErrResponseLost`, then a Get answering `not_found`, then a resubmit answering `submit_window_closed` → the "too busy to accept" sentence; the reworded `signer_unreachable` text is pinned; **(moved from A12) the coordinator through the hosted proxy (C-61):** on A12's harness (an in-process `hosted.Server` on a file-backed store behind the local daemon socket, test-only imports), a second connection holds `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` past the busy timeout once during each of Submit, Claim and Report of a sync `run` and of a sync `run_batch`; the recording transport shows each resent body byte-identical to the first; each call completes exactly once with one record, one signature set and one accepted report, and no Denial; (r5.3) r5.1's `clock_regressed` retries and their test are deleted;
  - **(r5.3) the sync budget on two clocks:** with the `internal/elapsed` fake, a 7 min wall step back during Wait does not extend the 300 s budget (E ends it), a forward step past `sync_claim_deadline − 5 s` ends it by the wall half, and either end returns `approval_pending` with nothing run; a 7 min step back during a long step 1 of a sync `run_batch` still refuses step 2 if its window has really ended (D5); the static time-read test over the coordinator's files passes ((r5.3, critique pass 1) an explicit file list; `provision.go:409` and `status.go:168-170`, which hold no deadline, are outside it);
  - **(r5.1) `not_ready` on the synchronous path:** against the real A6 memory lifecycle with its flusher delayed 1 s, a grant-covered sync write and a human-approved sync write each complete exactly once with no Denial; with the flusher delayed 6 s, the coordinator retries `not_ready` inside the budget and still completes; (r5.5) the stopped-flusher case is split by when the approval lands, below;
  - **(r5.5) approved, then the window closes before release (review 6 #3):** against the real A6 memory lifecycle, the production `Runner.SyncWaitBudget` (300 s), a `signed_write` with TTL 60, the `internal/elapsed` fake and the flusher stopped. (a) **Timely approval:** approved at `created_at + 10 s` → the coordinator's Claim answers `not_ready` until `claim_end = decided_at + 60 s`, then `expired`; the call returns within 5 s of `claim_end` (one 2 s backoff step plus the Claim's own 2 s flush wait), about 70 s after Submit and not at the 300 s budget, with the `expired_unexecuted` Denial (`stop_do_not_retry`, `Retryable: false`) in E26's approved-but-expired wording, `execution_status = expired_unexecuted`, no executed ordinals and no `partial`; the fake `PhasedRunner` records zero runs; the signer accepted one negative report with an empty executed mask and `claim_count = 0`, and its execution view is `final{expired_unexecuted}`; restarting the flusher afterwards drains the outbox and runs nothing. The same with a grant-covered write (approved at Submit). (b) **Late approval:** approved at `created_at + 270 s` → the budget ends first, with Claim still `not_ready`, and the call returns the `approval_pending` Denial in its approved-but-unreleased wording (E26); a later flush never runs it, and Claim answers `expired` at `claim_end` (no later than `sync_claim_deadline`). (c) **Expiry as a decision:** the 5-minute-validity case below still gives `approval_timeout` from Wait's `expired`, and the fake records no Claim. A fake `ApprovalClient` that answers Claim `expired` after Wait answered `approved` never yields `approval_timeout`, for `run`, `run_batch`, `revoke_server`, `update_gate` and `transfer` alike; (d) (r5.5, critique of the fold) **`revoke_server`:** case (a) repeated with its `signed_admin` step (TTL 60), approved at `created_at + 10 s` with the flusher stopped → it returns within 5 s of `claim_end` with the `expired_unexecuted` Denial, the fake `PhasedRunner` records zero exec requests, the registry entry is unchanged, and the signer accepted one negative report; `update_gate` approved at `created_at + 10 s` (TTL 300) under the same conditions returns budget-end `approval_pending` with zero exec requests;

A revealed read (`cat /etc/app.env`) gives the same, because the MCP cannot tell the two apart, and so does a plain signed `run` write (§1.3 item 38). `run_batch` cannot carry a reveal (`RunBatchInput` has no reveal field, A13a), so its case uses `signed_write`: a sync `run_batch` of `[systemctl restart app, systemctl restart web]` whose first step prints the line and exits 77 → step 1 `failed`, step 2 `skipped` by the class default's stop (never run), `executed_ordinals [1]`, `partial: true`, the same Denial and error assertions, and the report accepted. A mixed `run_batch` `[unsigned_read printing the line and exiting 77, signed_write]` → `[failed_not_started, not_run]`, `executed_ordinals []`, no exec request for step 2, the `read_jail_unavailable` Denial (§1.3 item 39); an all-read batch keeps today's path. The single unsigned `run` read returns `err == nil`, exit 77 and the Denial with the reworded `Summary`;
  - `unsupported kind` gives `signer_incompatible`; (r5.7, review 8 #2) so does the A12 proxy's `signer_incompatible` frame, end to end: an older hosted server (A12's `366ba07` fixtures) → the current A12 proxy → a sync `run` write and a sync `run_batch` → the `signer_incompatible` Denial (`escalate_to_human`), no retry, no Wait or Claim, zero SSH dispatches;
  - with a configured 5-minute validity, sync expiry gives `approval_timeout`; with the default validity, the end of the sync wait budget gives `approval_pending`, the record stays `pending`, and a later approval is never executed by the returned call (the fake records zero runs), ending with the view `expired_unclaimed`;
  - the draft the MCP builds has no validity field, and the record's `request_expires_at` always equals `submitted_at` plus the configured validity;
  - an E71 reserved verb in `run` or `run_batch` is refused before any signer call;
  - K16 in a batch; (r5.4) through a fake `PhasedRunner` that honours the guard, a signed sync step whose handshake outlasts its window ends `expired_unexecuted` with zero exec requests (C-52); (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C2-1) the same for `update_gate` and `revoke_server` (zero exec requests, nothing on stdin); for `transfer`, a destination handshake that outlasts the RECV leg's window after the SEND leg ran gives the RECV leg `expired_unexecuted`, zero exec requests to the destination, no envelope on any stdin, and an output that says nothing was transferred; each of the four coordinators passes a non-nil guard for every signed `RunPhased` call (a fake `PhasedRunner` that fails the test on a nil guard with an `SSHGATE_SIG:` command); E29 aggregation surfaces as `execution_status` with `executed_ordinals` and `partial` ((r5.6) a sync `run_batch` of `[failing read, write whose channel closes after Start]` returns `executed_ordinals [1, 2]` and `partial: false`, and its report is accepted; with a third step, `partial: true`, (r5.6, critique of the fold) and its report lists all three ordinals, `[failed, indeterminate, not_run]`, and is accepted by the real A6 lifecycle; a `stop_on_error:true` batch stopped after a failure reports `[succeeded, failed, skipped]` with `partial: true` and is accepted);
  - stale_target;
  - hosted-backed end to end through A12.

**Phase A end:** PHASE-END, plus `make test-integration`, plus independent review on the full A diff. Recorded in WORKLOG.

### B0 — `securestate` additions: `ListNames`, `Remove` and (r5.1) `RemoveOrphanTemps` (r5; may run during Phase A)

- **Goal:** the only new security-primitive code r5 needs. One file per record requires listing and deleting files inside a pinned securestate directory; today a transaction has only `ReadFile`, `ReadCanonicalJSON`, `WriteFile` and `WriteCanonicalJSON` (`internal/securestate/store_linux.go:241-370`).
- **Depends:** A0 only. It touches nothing another lane owns, so it can run in its own lane during Phase A; it must merge before B1 and C1.
- **Owns:** `internal/securestate/**`, additive only.
- **Content:**
  - `(*Transaction).ListNames() ([]string, error)`: (r5.1) lists through a **fresh** descriptor, `openat(dirFD, ".", O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_CLOEXEC)`, closed before returning, because `getdents` on the pinned `dirFD` would share its file offset with every other `*at` call (never a path); skips `.` and `..` (which `validateDataName` would otherwise reject, `store_linux.go:542-557`), the lock file and `.tmp-*` names; validates every other name with `validateDataName` and its type with an `fstatat(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` regular-file check; and returns the names sorted. A name that fails validation, or an entry that is not a regular file, is an error (fail closed); the caller decides (§1.5 SA-8: the whole store refuses to open; content-invalid regular files are quarantined by name).
  - `(*Transaction).Remove(name string) error`: write transactions only; `validateDataName`; (r5.1) `statPath` validation that the entry is a regular 0600 single-link file of the expected uid (`:504-516`), the same check `WriteFile` makes before it replaces; `unlinkat(dirFD, name, 0)`; then fsync the directory. A missing name is `ErrNotFound`. A failed directory fsync after a successful unlink is `ErrCommitUncertain`, exactly like a post-rename failure in `atomicReplace` (`:370-438`); the next transaction's directory fsync (`:231`) or a reopen's (`:153`) confirms it.
  - **(r5.1)** `(*Transaction).RemoveOrphanTemps() (int, error)`: write transactions only; unlinks every `.tmp-*` regular file and fsyncs the directory once. Under the exclusive flock no other transaction is running, so every such file is the orphan of a process that crashed between creating its temp file and renaming it (`atomicReplace` cleans up only on an in-process error, `:376-391`). The lifecycle and the executor call it at Startup; `status` reports the count.
  - Test seams follow the existing unexported ones (`beforeTransactionSync`, `beforeRename`, `afterRename`): `afterUnlink`.
  - **(r5.1) Linux only.** The package has only `store_linux.go` and its test, so there is no non-Linux behaviour to reuse; r5's "the darwin build gets the same signatures" is withdrawn. Every importer is build-tagged: `filestore_linux.go` (B1) and the executor's Linux files (C1); the `!linux` builds use the memory store (E18) and the executor stub.
- **Tests:** listing skips `.`, `..`, the lock and temp files and returns every data file; a listing interleaved with `ReadFile` and `WriteFile` calls in the same transaction is complete and correct (the fresh descriptor); a symlink, a directory, a FIFO or an unsafe name in the directory makes `ListNames` fail; `Remove` of a regular file unlinks it durably; `Remove` refuses the lock name, `.tmp-*` names, `..`, names with `/`, a symlink, a hard-linked file and a file with mode 0644; `Remove` inside a read transaction is refused; an injected directory-fsync failure after unlink returns `ErrCommitUncertain`; `RemoveOrphanTemps` removes temp files left by a subprocess killed between create and rename, and leaves data files alone; two processes holding the store see consistent listings under the flock; every existing securestate test stays unchanged and green; `GOOS=darwin go build` of every importer still succeeds.
- **Ledger:** C-23 (primitive), C-33.
- **Must not touch:** the behaviour of any existing securestate method; `policy_journal_linux.go`.
- **Accept:** STD; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./internal/securestate/... ./pkg/signerkit/...` (the policy journal is its existing user); **its own independent review**, separate from any phase review. If the reviewer rejects the methods, B1 and C1 fall back to a fixed set of shard files holding the same records, written with the existing `WriteCanonicalJSON` (no new primitive), and §1.5's rules are unchanged.
- **Risk:** a filesystem primitive in a security-critical store. Keep it to these three methods (r5.1: `RemoveOrphanTemps` added).

### B1 — Local per-record store (K14, E18, E54, E73, E75)

- **Depends:** Phase A, B0.
- **Owns:** new `pkg/signerkit/lifecycle/filestore_linux.go` and `filestore_other.go` (the `!linux` memory store with a warning), (r5.8, review 9 #3) the file store's side of Startup ((r5.8, critique of the fold) `startup.go` itself is A6's, because A6's gate already restarts over the memory store; r5.8 had moved it here from B2), and signer `main.go` (**third writer**: `journal_root` default `filepath.Dir(Paths.Key)/approvals`, E18; `retention`; `report_horizon`; the `doInitFlow` template line).
- **Content:**
  - **(r5.2) ownership first (SA-1 rule 1):** before Startup loads anything, the store opens `journal_root` once, `mkdirat` (as `securestate.OpenAt` does, then fsync the parent), then `openat(…, O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC)`, and takes `flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on that descriptor. It keeps the descriptor for the life of the process, and every open, at Startup and at each reopen, is `securestate.OpenDirectory(holderFD, uid, true)`. At process start, `EWOULDBLOCK` makes the signer exit non-zero, logging "approval store <dir> is held by another signer process", before `srv.Listen`; (r5.2) any other `flock` error is fatal too, with the local-filesystem log line of SA-1 rule 1. A reopen never calls `flock`: it reuses the held descriptor. A uid or mode mismatch is refused by `OpenDirectory`'s existing checks;
  - one securestate file per R1 record, named by `approvalplan.RecordKey` (§1.5 SA-3); `Commit` is one `WriteCanonicalJSON`; Delete is `Remove`; listing is `ListNames`;
  - versioned schema 1; validate on every read and write; sizes checked before allocation, on the encoded bytes (r5.1);
  - **(r5.8, review 9 #3) Startup, the one function used at process start and at every reopen after an uncertain commit,** is A6's store-agnostic `startup.go` ((r5.8, critique of the fold) list, load and validate every record, build the projections, the `R1Reservation` counter and the unflushed list, mint a new epoch, and run `Next` on every record). B1 supplies the file store under it: the ownership flock, `RemoveOrphanTemps`, the single View transaction behind `List`, the decoded cache, quarantine by file, and the reopen loop that calls it. `main.go` starts the poller after Startup returns (A8). r5–r5.7 put Startup in B2, which depends on B1, while B1's reopen and its tests already needed it; r5.8 moved it to B1, while A6's own gate already needed it;
  - **(r5.1) Startup and reads.** Startup runs `RemoveOrphanTemps` in one Update, then `ListNames` and every `ReadCanonicalJSON` inside **one** View transaction (each securestate transaction fsyncs the directory and takes the store mutex, `store_linux.go:194-233`, so one transaction per file would cost 65 536 directory fsyncs). It builds a decoded, write-through record cache: every later read is served from it, every commit updates it only after the commit is confirmed, and an uncertain commit discards it with the rest of the lifecycle memory (§1.5 SA-8). Reads therefore never take the securestate lock and never serialize with writers. Worst case the cache holds at most 32 MiB of full records plus 65 536 × `ShedMax` of shed records ((r5.5, critique of the fold) a shed record can still gain a late report and one noise event, §1.5 SA-9); quarantined files are not decoded, and they count in the budget at their encoded size;
  - (r5.1) a `ListNames` failure (a non-regular entry or an unknown name) makes the store refuse to open: v2 kinds answer `lifecycle_unavailable`, `status` names the entry, and the reopen loop retries with its backoff until the operator removes it (§1.5 SA-8);
  - **quarantine (§1.5 SA-8):** a file that fails validation is listed as quarantined by name, answers `record_corrupt` for its key, counts against the live caps, and is never overwritten or deleted automatically;
  - **an uncertain commit is a crash of the owner (§1.5 SA-8, R2.2):** on `ErrCommitUncertain` the store poisons itself under its operation mutex exactly as `policy_journal_linux.go:313-316` does; every command answers `storage_uncertain`; the lifecycle cancels and joins its actors ((r5.8) by §1.5 SA-8's owner stop, so a send an actor was making is interrupted, not failed, and makes no INCOMPLETE edit), discards its memory and projections, reopens ((r5.2) `securestate.OpenDirectory(holderFD, uid, true)`, never `OpenAt`, which would resolve the path by name again (`store_linux.go:83-110`), plus a first transaction, whose directory fsync under the flock is the durability confirmation, `store_linux.go:221-233`) with backoff from 1 s doubling to 30 s, mints a new epoch and runs Startup ((r5.8) A6's `startup.go`, above; r5–r5.7 said "B2's code path"), (r5.3) carrying nothing but the ownership descriptor; (r5.8, critique of the fold) the poison and the stop latch are set in one critical section before `ErrCommitUncertain` returns, the reopen runs on the reopen loop's own goroutine, never the committing caller's, and until the reopen the poison's `storage_uncertain` is the answer to every command and read, never the latch's `lifecycle_unavailable` (§1.5 SA-8);
  - two test seams on the lifecycle store, mirroring the policy journal: `afterStoreUpdate` (the replacement is visible but the acknowledgement is lost) and `beforeStoreUpdate` (`ErrCommitUncertain` before the callback runs, the not-installed case of securestate's pre-transaction directory fsync);
  - caps, Shed and Delete per §1.5 SA-9, on the tick and before a Submit that needs room; signature erasure persisted; (r5.5) the payload budget counts each full record at `approvalplan.R1Reservation`, rebuilt at Startup from the loaded records, and the flusher's unflushed list is rebuilt there too (§1.5 SA-7).
- **Tests:**
  - `lifecycletest`, including §1.5 SA-11's properties and its crash-prefix model, runs against the file store;
  - fault injection at every command's commit, with the write landed (`afterStoreUpdate`) and not landed (`beforeStoreUpdate`): the caller gets `storage_uncertain` and no dependent effect happens (no callback success, no Bot API call, no signatures, no report ack); 8 concurrent Get, Wait, Claim and List callers during the poisoned window all get `storage_uncertain` and never a cached answer; after the reopen the new epoch is in force, every v2 grant is dead, and a retry of the same command reaches the landed outcome or applies it for the first time; exactly one signature set ever leaves;
  - a burst or (r5.4) send entry appended before an uncertain commit, whose outcome never committed, is `uncertain` after the reopen and is never resent automatically; (r5.8, review 9 #2) the poison lands while part 2 is in flight and, separately, while an edit is in flight, and the held call then returns a transport error: no INCOMPLETE edit and no further Bot API call follows, and after the reopen the burst is `uncertain` and the edit is superseded once; (r5.8, critique of the fold) **the uncertain commit is the actor's own:** the actor's `sent` commit for part 2 is uncertain (`afterStoreUpdate`, and separately `beforeStoreUpdate`) → the actor returns without joining itself (no deadlock; the test runs under a 10 s timeout and goleak), makes no further Bot API call, and the reopen completes; 8 concurrent Claim, Vote and Get callers during that window all get `storage_uncertain`, never `lifecycle_unavailable`; after the reopen the burst, which has no outcome in either case, is `uncertain`, and nothing is resent automatically; (r5.2) an unconfirmed edit entry from before the reopen is retired, and a fresh edit entry is appended in the same commit and then sent (it is never replayed, because its raw challenge is gone);
  - symlinks, insecure modes, oversize files and unknown names are refused; a corrupt record file is quarantined and its key answers `record_corrupt`;
  - **(r5.5) payload reservation on files (review 6 #2):** A6's reservation case against the file store with real files, plus a restart in the middle (after approval and before Claim): Startup rebuilds the same budget counter and the same unflushed list, no record file ever exceeds its `R1Reservation`, the sum of the full records' file sizes never exceeds the injected budget at any commit, and no commit of admitted work is refused; with the default 32 MiB budget, 256 live one-step writes from three principals are admitted and the 257th is refused by the total live cap, not the budget; **(r5.5, critique of the fold)** a shed `awaiting_report` record, with the writer failing, receives 40 invalid Claims and Reports and then its late Report: its file holds the report event and one noise event, never exceeds `ShedMax`, and `noise_dropped` counts the rest; a quarantined 400 KiB file lowers the free budget by its size until the operator removes it, and Startup rebuilds the same counter;
  - retention: Shed and Delete never touch an unaudited, live or `awaiting_report`-for-delete record; a claimed, running record shed under pressure still accepts its report after a restart; a deleted denial's id, resubmitted after its `submit_not_after`, gets `submit_window_closed`;
  - **stress:** an hour of simulated grant-covered writes at 1/s (fake clock, real fsync on tmpfs and on disk) never returns `queue_full`, stays within every cap, and reports p99 commit latency against E54's 250 ms gate; (r5.1) **burst:** 150 grant-covered writes submitted within 60 s, each claimed and reported within 2 s, never return `queue_full` (reported records leave the live class, §1.5 SA-6.5); 150 left unclaimed return `queue_full` from the 101st for that principal, before any notification, and admit again once (r5.3) Clock closes their windows, with the tick paused too (the admission Clock pass, SA-9);
  - **startup load:** Startup over 65 536 record files (512 full, the rest shed) is measured on disk; if it exceeds 10 s, B1 lowers the total-record cap rather than changing the store shape. (r5.1) The 10 s gate applies on local SSD with a warm cache, the reference class for the operator's signer host; a cold-cache run (a fresh copy of the store) and, where available, a rotating disk are measured and recorded but do not gate;
  - **(r5.1) reads never touch disk after Startup:** a store wrapper counts securestate transactions; Get, List and Wait add none; 8 concurrent readers during a 512 KiB commit never wait on the securestate lock;
  - **(r5.1) orphan temps and listing failures:** a `.tmp-*` file left by a killed subprocess is removed at Startup; a FIFO placed in the store directory makes the store refuse to open with `lifecycle_unavailable` and a `status` line naming it, and removing it lets the next reopen succeed;
  - (r5.8, review 9 #3) Startup at process start and Startup after the reopen are one function: a test drives both over the same file store and compares the projections, the budget counter, the unflushed list and the effects `Next` starts (a pending record with no burst gets exactly one; one with an `uncertain` burst gets none), and shows the epoch changes each time;
  - `--dev` puts the store under the dev key's directory; a reload equals the pre-crash state;
  - **(r5.2) two processes, one journal (review 4 #3):** two signer subprocesses with different `Paths.Socket` (so the socket singleton does not stop the second) and one `journal_root`. The second exits non-zero naming the directory, and the first's records, votes and mint slots are unchanged after a run of Votes, Claims and AdminCancels. The same holds when the second reaches the directory through a symlinked parent, and through a bind mount where available. A direct test drives two `filestore` instances in two processes against one directory and shows the second never loads a cache. The first's uncertain-commit reopen keeps ownership: while it reopens, a third process trying to start still exits. **(r5.2) The reopen opens the held inode:** during the reopen backoff, `journal_root` is renamed and a fresh 0700 directory is put at its path; the reopened store's directory has the held descriptor's device and inode, never the new directory's, and serves the original records. **(r5.2) Not a local filesystem:** a `flock` seam returning `EBADF` (as NFS emulation does) makes the signer exit non-zero with the local-filesystem log line, before `srv.Listen`. **(r5.3) An uncertain Clock-first commit:** a Get shows a request `pending`; a Vote after its `request_expires_at` makes a commit (Clock's `expired` plus `vote_refused`) that is uncertain, once not landed (`beforeStoreUpdate`) and once landed (`afterStoreUpdate`); the voter sees `storage_uncertain`. After the reopen, with the wall clock 3 min behind: not landed → the record is `pending` and a re-tap is judged afresh (R-1); landed → it is `expired` and a re-tap is refused. Nothing shown before the cut is contradicted.
- **Ledger:** C-12 (local), C-18, C-23 (local), C-26 (local), C-32 (durable), (r5.1) C-38 (local), (r5.2) C-41 (local), (r5.3, critique pass 1) C-39 (file store; its uncertain Clock commit test), (r5.5) C-59 (file store), (r5.8) C-67 (reopen).
- **Must not touch:** `policy_journal_linux.go`; `internal/securestate` beyond B0.
- **Accept:** STD; FROZEN-LOCAL; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./pkg/signerkit/lifecycle/... ./internal/securestate/...`.

### B2 — Restart matrix, shutdown and admin re-notify (K11, E18, E63; spec §6.1 notification) ((r5.8) was "Startup = Next, …"; Startup moved to B1)

- **Depends:** B1.
- **Owns:** the shutdown handling in `daemon_approvals.go`, and `src/signer/backend/telegram_v2*.go` (`/renotify <id>` registered behind the E64 gate). (r5.8, review 9 #3) `startup.go` moved to B1, whose uncertain-commit reopen runs it ((r5.8, critique of the fold) and then to A6); B2 adds no startup code. (r5.8, critique of the fold) B2 edits neither `service.go` (A7 makes `Service.Close` close the lifecycle) nor `main.go`.
- **Content:**
  - **Startup** is A6's `startup.go` over B1's file store ((r5.8, review 9 #3) moved to B1, (r5.8, critique of the fold) then to A6; its content is unchanged). B2 proves it end to end, with the Telegram notifier and `/renotify`, through the restart matrix below; `main.go` starts the poller after Startup returns (A8).
  - SIGTERM leaves v2 records untouched, cancels and joins the actors, and ends legacy requests as today. (r5.8, review 9 #2) It is §1.5 SA-8's owner stop: the signal context stops the poller and `srv.Listen`, which returns only after every handler has returned, so legacy v1 requests have already ended `timeout`, as today (`socket.go:147,200`); then the deferred `svc.Close` (`main.go:288-292`) runs the lifecycle's `Close` (A6: the latch, then cancel and join) through A7's `Service.Close` hunk ((r5.8, critique of the fold) today's `Service.Close`, `service.go:138-144`, touches no lifecycle and returns at once without a policy journal; r5.8 wrongly said the shutdown handling, including that `Close`, was in `daemon_approvals.go` and that legacy requests end after it). `main.go`'s existing order (`:219,288-292,321`, with A8's poller under the same context) then gives the stop, so B2 asserts it and edits no `main.go` line. "Untouched" means unchanged after the last commit before the latch, and no v2 card is edited.
  - `/renotify <handle>` is admin-only, passes the E64 gate (sender, private chat, not forwarded, not redelivered by (r5.3, critique pass 1) the `update_id` cut), and applies the Renotify command (§1.5 SA-4 notify step 5): (r5.4) one commit raises `renotify_gen` and retires every card entry, so every old challenge stops being valid, and a new burst follows, driven by `Next` from that committed counter, so a crash between the command and the burst cannot lose it. It is the only way to resend an `uncertain` notification. (r5.1) It takes the record handle; a `request_id` works only when it matches exactly one record, otherwise "ambiguous id — use the handle from /pending" (E64). A full burst set refuses it.
- **Tests:** the restart matrix:
  - **no reattach:** a pending record with a delivered card stays votable after a restart through that same card, and no new card is sent;
  - **(r5.2) an interrupted edit, at both cuts review 4 named:** (a) a crash after the edit entry's commit and before `editMessageText` is called; (b) a crash after the fake Bot API has accepted the edit (the card now shows the interrupted entry's buttons) and before `confirmed` commits. After the restart, in both cases, one commit retires the interrupted entry and appends a fresh edit entry, and exactly one `editMessageText` follows, carrying the fresh handle and challenge. The fake records no edit with the interrupted entry's bytes. Taps on the revision-0 challenge and on the interrupted challenge (in case (b) these are the buttons on screen) answer "card updated — tap again" and change nothing; the fresh challenge counts once it is confirmed. Repeated crashes at cut (a) use one card entry each, and the 16th ends the request `error{notification_limit}` with INCOMPLETE edits;
  - a burst appended in the old epoch with no outcome → `uncertain` after the restart, never auto-resent; `/renotify` retires the cards and sends one new burst and card; the old card's taps are then refused;
  - **(r5.4) the cut after `/renotify` commits and before the new burst is appended, with an empty card set (review 5 #2):** a burst is cut by a kill during its parts (no card was ever sent); restart → `uncertain`, nothing sent; `/renotify` commits (`renotify_gen = 1`); the process is killed at a hook after that commit and before the burst's append (`afterStoreUpdate` landed, and separately `beforeStoreUpdate` not landed, on the Renotify commit); restart → landed: exactly one new burst under `gen = 1` and then one card (`first`, since no card entry ever existed; r5.4, critique of r5.4 C2-4), recorded by the fake Bot API; not landed: nothing sent, still `uncertain`, and a re-sent `/renotify` works; a kill after the gen-1 burst's append → `uncertain` again after the next restart, with no automatic third burst;
  - **(r5.4) a delivered card stays `sent` across a restart (review 5 #5):** a record whose card was delivered (`msg_id` and `outcome = sent` committed) shows notification `sent` before the restart and after it, never `uncertain`; a record with an unconfirmed edit entry from before the restart also shows `sent`, while the edit is superseded (C-42);
  - a crash right after the Submit commit → exactly one burst after the restart (R1.8, C-21);
  - a crash after a card was sent but before its message ID committed → `uncertain`; that card's taps are refused (no recorded message ID); `/renotify` resolves it, (r5.4) with the new burst's parts first and then exactly one `replacement` card; (r5.4, critique of r5.4 C1-4) the same with a delivered card (`outcome = sent`) instead: `/renotify` retires it, and the fake Bot API records the new parts and then exactly one `replacement` card, in that order; (r5.1) by contrast, a transport error on the same send while the signer keeps running → `error` with INCOMPLETE edits, and `/renotify` is not needed;
  - (r5.1) two principals' records with the same `request_id`: `/renotify <request_id>` is refused as ambiguous and changes nothing; `/renotify <handle>` acts on exactly one;
  - (r5.3) a restart with the clock 1 h behind (it moved back while the signer was down): nothing new is written and nothing committed is contradicted; Startup reports the future-dated stamps in `status`; commands are accepted at once; a record decided at the future time gives `materialization_error{clock_skew}` at its first Claim, and an already-claimed one answers a repeat Claim `expired` with its bytes erased in that commit (SA-1 rule 3.2); r5.2's floor test is deleted;
  - (r5.3) a restart with the clock 1 h ahead: Clock commits every due fact (`expired`, `unavailable`, `window_closed`, erased bytes) before anything is served, and a second restart with the clock corrected shows the same facts; **S1 against the file store (D1):** a record created at 14:00 on a fast clock, the clock corrected to 13:00, the record approved at 13:10 with a 60 s window, a restart at 13:10:30 → no `window_closed` and no Erase is written, the window stays open, and a Claim at 13:10:40 returns signatures;
  - `/renotify` from a wrong user, a group chat, a forwarded message or a redelivered (pre-start) update does nothing and is audited;
  - (r5.3, critique pass 1) **E64 without a clock:** the signer starts with its wall clock 10 min fast, NTP then corrects it, and a later `/renotify` on an `uncertain` record is accepted; a `/renotify` sent before a restart and redelivered from offset 0 is dropped, and the record's burst count is unchanged;
  - a restart while assist is `preparing` → (r5.3) Clock commits `unavailable` within a tick of the deadline, the card is sent or edited at revision 1, and the provider is called zero more times (K8);
  - **Telegram redelivery from offset 0:** a tap applied before the restart is redelivered and is inert; a tap that arrived during the downtime on a still-valid card counts once; a tap on a retired card is refused;
  - a vote committed and then a crash before the callback answer → the re-tap is inert and answered with the stored decision;
  - SIGTERM while pending and idle → unchanged after the restart; grants are dead after any restart;
  - **(r5.8, review 9 #2) SIGTERM during a send, a card, an edit and a status edit:** a signer subprocess with the file store and the fake Bot API, which holds the chosen call until the process has received SIGTERM and then (i) fails it with a connection reset or, separately, (ii) applies it and drops the response: (a) during part 2 of 3; (b) during the card send; (c) during the `editMessageText` of an assist edit; (d) during a terminal status edit; (e) ((r5.8, critique of the fold) no call is held) the fake answers part 2 with 429 `retry_after=30` and SIGTERM arrives during that wait. In every case the process exits 0 within the client-timeout bound, the record file equals its bytes after the last commit before the latch, and the fake records no INCOMPLETE edit and no call after the held one (in (e), no call after the 429). (r5.8, critique of the fold) The decision slot is unchanged: empty in (a), (b), (c) and (e), and in (d) the terminal decision committed before the stop, with no `status_edits` entry appended for the display state being edited. (r5.8, critique of the fold) **A stop, not a death:** in (a), the fake releases part 2 with success 2 s after SIGTERM; the process exits only after that release (at least 2 s after SIGTERM, which a process that skipped `Close` would not wait for), commits nothing after the latch, and the restart shows the same `uncertain` burst. After a restart: in (a), (b) and (e) the notification is `uncertain`, the request is pending, nothing is resent automatically, a tap on the card the fake delivered in (b)(ii) is refused, and `/renotify` sends one burst and one card (`replacement` when a card entry exists, `first` otherwise), after which an Approve tap decides; in (c) one commit retires the interrupted edit entry and one fresh `editMessageText` follows; in (d) the status edit is sent once. A connection reset on the same call with no SIGTERM still gives `error` with INCOMPLETE edits (the contrast, as in A8).
- **Ledger:** C-1, C-2 (recovery), C-16 (restart), C-21, C-24 (restart), (r5.1) C-35 (restart), C-37 (Telegram), (r5.2) C-39 (restart), C-42, (r5.3, critique pass 1) C-46 (the stale-card taps after a restart), (r5.4) C-50 and C-53 (restart), (r5.8) C-67 (SIGTERM).
