**Phase B end:** PHASE-END and independent review.

### C1 — Executor core (K10, E17, E55, E56, E75; §1.5 SA-3 R2)

- **Depends:** Phase B (and B0).
- **Owns:** `src/executorwire/**` (lands first, as its own commit), `src/executor/**`, `src/executor/cmd/sshgate-executor/**`.
- **Content:**
  - Kinds `exec_submit`, `exec_get`, `exec_await` and `exec_list`, at protocol version 1, with goldens. `exec_submit` carries the MCP-minted `request_id` and the input whose canonical hash is `input_hash` (§1.5 SA-2). (r5.2) The input includes the MCP-fixed `submit_not_after`, which is required, so `input_hash` covers it.
  - Linux:
    - a singleton flock; (r5.2) and, separately, the ownership flock on the job store directory `<configRoot>/executor` (SA-1 rule 1, the same code shape as B1). The singleton lock lives in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, so it does not stop two executors with different runtime directories and one config root. The ownership flock does. A held job store makes the executor exit non-zero, naming the directory; (r5.2) any other `flock` error is fatal with the same local-filesystem log line as B1;
    - a socket at `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/sshgate/executor.sock` (directory 0700, socket 0600) that requires `SO_PEERCRED` uid == the executor's own uid;
    - the job store: one securestate file per job under `<configRoot>/executor`, named by `approvalplan.JobKey`, holding the R2 record; `RemoveOrphanTemps` then `ListNames` at startup (r5.1), `Remove` for deletion; quarantine of invalid files as `report_auth_lost`; (r5.1) bodies stored as `[]byte` and every bound and reservation on encoded bytes (§1.5 SA-3 R2);
    - a random epoch per process start;
    - `exec_submit` idempotency, (r5.2) the input window (a new id after its `submit_not_after` → `submit_window_closed`; more than 3605 s ahead → `invalid_request`, (r5.2) the 5 s being E73's local allowance; both before admission; (r5.2) `R2Reservation` over `R2Max` → `request_too_large`) and admission (§1.5 SA-5): ≤100 live jobs, the per-job worst-case reservation, (r5.2) `approvalplan.R2Reservation` of its own draft and output cap, within the 256 MiB budget, otherwise `executor_full`; (r5.8, review 9 #3) then a fresh `registry.ReadCurrent` (A13a), the draft built with `jobplan` (A13a; the MCP's `request_id`, `coordinator=executor` and the input's `submit_not_after`, unchanged), and the job commit, after which C1's `exec_submit` answers with the committed job (`submitted`) at once. C1 thus owns `exec_submit` up to and including the job commit, and the idempotency above is a property of that commit. The signer Submit after the commit, E63's answer timing and `JobNext` are C2's. r5–r5.7 gave the draft and the commit to C2 while C1's idempotency tests needed them;
    - a 256-bit claim token generated per job;
    - **an uncertain commit exits the process non-zero** (§1.5 SA-8); a seam lets tests trigger it in a subprocess;
    - `exec_get`, `exec_await` and `exec_list` read `JobView` and never write; (r5.4, E29) for an `acknowledged` job they report no `approval_status`, which the MCP joins from the signer.
  - Other OSes get a stub that fails with "unsupported platform".
- **Tests** ((r5.8, review 9 #3) at the store and protocol boundary only: C1 has no signer client and no `JobNext`, so an assertion that needs either is C2's; a step result is committed by applying `approvalplan`'s pure result transition (A1b) through C1's store, never by running a step): a second instance fails the singleton lock; a foreign uid is refused, simulated through an injectable peer check; the store's hygiene tests; reservation accounting never lets admitted worst cases exceed the budget, and (r5.3, critique pass 1) counts a final job at its encoded size and releases the bodies' share at body removal (OR-5) ((r5.8) with the final-result and body-removal transitions applied directly); **`exec_submit` idempotency (R3.7):** the same id and input from a second connection (a second MCP session) returns the same job, the same id with another input is `conflict`, and ((r5.8) in place of "the fake signer sees one Submit", now C2's) a commit-counting store wrapper shows exactly one job file and one job commit; a corrupt job file is quarantined, its id answers `report_auth_lost`, and an `exec_submit` with that id never overwrites it; an injected uncertain commit makes the subprocess exit non-zero, and a restart reloads the job files alone ((r5.8) a landed commit's job answers `exec_get` with its committed state and a not-landed one is absent; `JobNext`'s recovery is C2's); reads never change any job file (a store wrapper counts commits); **(r5.1)** a job whose committed step results hold 8 MiB of output made of invalid UTF-8 and `<`, `>`, `&` bytes commits within the R2 bound and `exec_await` returns the bytes exactly; (r5.2) the same with a draft of 24 KiB of `<` command text and a maximal `<` reason (review 4 #5: maximum escaped draft and maximum output together) commits with its bodies ((r5.8) the store accepts it with no `ErrTooLarge`; that `JobNext` never takes the bodies-dropped fallback is C2's); the reservation for a 32-step job is the encoded worst case of the whole record and is at least what that job's file reaches; **(r5.2) the input window:** an `exec_submit` whose `submit_not_after` has passed is refused `submit_window_closed` and creates no job file; an existing job is returned whatever the time; two inputs differing only in `submit_not_after` are `conflict`; (r5.2) a `submit_not_after` 2 s beyond `now + 3600 s` (the MCP read its clock just before a small step back) is accepted, and 6 s beyond is `invalid_request`; a draft whose reservation is forced over `R2Max` through a test-only bound gets `request_too_large` and no job file; **(r5.2) two executors, one job store (review 4 #3):** two executor subprocesses with different `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and one `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`; the second exits non-zero naming `<configRoot>/executor`, and the first's jobs are unchanged; a `.tmp-*` file left by a killed subprocess is removed at startup. ((r5.8) Moved to C2: "the fake signer sees one Submit", the three "no signer call" halves, and the result commit refused definitively and retried without bodies.)
- **Ledger:** L12, C-11, C-12 (executor), C-23 (executor), C-31, (r5.1) C-38 (executor), (r5.2) C-40 (executor), C-41 (executor), (r5.3, critique pass 1) C-48 (accounting).
- **Accept:** STD; `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/executorwire/... ./src/executor/...`.

### C2 — Executor job engine: `JobNext` (K10, K11, §6.2, E25, E26, E29, E56, E63, E73–E75)

- **Depends:** C1.
- **Owns:** `src/executor/**`.
- **Content:**
  1. (r5.8, review 9 #3) After C1's `exec_submit` has committed the job (its window check, admission, a fresh `registry.ReadCurrent`, and the `jobplan` draft with the MCP's `request_id`, `coordinator=executor` and (r5.2) the input's `submit_not_after`, unchanged; r5.1 used `now + 3600 s`), C2 Submits the draft to the signer, as SA-5 orders it, and nudges the job's `JobNext`, whose step 4 resubmits while there is no `signer_ack`. r5–r5.7 had C2 build the draft and commit the job, which C1's own tests needed.
  2. **K10 return (E63):** `exec_submit` answers the MCP once the signer record is committed and its notification view has left `not_started`/`sending`, polling Get every 500 ms for at most 65 s; at that bound it answers `sending` explicitly.
  3. A waiter calls `WaitMany` in batches of ≤100 IDs and ≤55 s, and nudges `JobNext` on each change; (r5.1) a nudge only sets the job actor's flag.
  4. **`JobNext`** drives every job per §1.5 SA-5, live and at startup alike, (r5.1) in one actor per job: finality first; an orphan marker to `indeterminate`; (r5.1) a current-epoch marker without a result means in flight, so no action; resubmit or `approval_lost`; wait or adopt; Claim (signatures in memory only; (r5.2) only before a signed step or the first dispatch), the step checks (`ReadCurrent`, and K16 for a signed step), marker ((r5.1) `already_marked` never reaches `Start`), (r5.1) K16 again immediately before `Start` for a signed step, (r5.3) both on the held (wall, elapsed) pair of §1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4 and only for the step about to be dispatched, the held set dropped at its drop point, `RunPhased` with bounded capture ((r5.4) its pre-start guard is the K16 re-check, E28), result ((r5.4) with `final_at` in the commit that makes the job final, §1.5 SA-3 R2); the one final report with a canonical body, (r5.2) settled by an ack or a definitive refusal and resent with the same bytes after a transient answer; `first_delivered_at` after a delivery. A Claim answered `not_ready` is retried with E25's backoff. (r5.3, OD-3) Every signer answer is classified by §1.5 SA-5's one definitive/transient split: transient answers, `storage_uncertain` among them, are retried with identical bytes and bounded backoff and never end the job; a definitive answer ends it with outcome `error{code}` and no report.
  5. Retention per §1.5 SA-9 (bodies, token, and (r5.2) the job file only under SA-9's deletion predicate, on both the 30-day path and the count-pressure path), (r5.4) with every age counted from `final_at`. (r5.3, OR-5) Budget accounting follows the reservation lifecycle: a live job at its reservation, a final job at `R2FinalCharge` ((r5.5, critique of the fold) its encoded size plus its still-empty post-final slots at their maximum, §1.5 SA-9), and body removal releases the bodies' share.
  6. **(r5.8, review 9 #2) The executor's owner stop (§1.5 SA-8):** SIGTERM stops the socket (no new connection is accepted; handlers in flight answer), sets the `stopping` latch in the job store's commit function (later commits refused), then cancels the executor's own effect context and joins the `JobNext` actors and the handlers. (r5.8, critique of the fold) A call in flight answers `executor_unavailable` only when the latch refused that call's own commit (or, for a read, when it cannot be served). An `exec_submit` whose own job commit landed before the latch answers with that job, its `request_id` and committed `JobView` (`submitted` or `acknowledged`), at once, ending E63's poll, exactly as C1's immediate answer does; the MCP then awaits it, and the job runs after the restart (SA-5). r5.8 answered it `executor_unavailable`, an `escalate_to_human` Denial (E11) that invites a resubmit under a new id while the committed job still runs, which under a live grant runs the write twice. `RunPhased` runs under the effect context, so the cancel closes the SSH client (§1.5 SA-8 step 3) and the join is bounded by `Client.Timeout` plus one signer call's deadline. A Claim, `RunPhased`, Report or Submit that returns after the latch commits nothing and is never classified as a step result or an outcome; the next start recovers it by SA-5 (an orphan marker becomes `indeterminate`).
- **Validation:** async rejects `max_output_bytes=0`.
- **Tests:**
  - **R3.1's exact cut:** step 1's check fails as `stale_target` and its result is committed; the process is killed before anything else; the registry entry is restored; the restart inside the signature window performs **zero** SSH dispatches, keeps `stale_target`, and reports it (negative, empty mask);
  - **abort precedence at restart (R2.1):** for each abort status as the last stored result, under `stop_on_error:false`, the class default and an explicit `true`, with a next step that would pass its checks, the restart ends the job with that class, later steps `not_run`, and the fake SSH server sees no dispatch of the next step;
  - **an orphan marker** from the previous epoch becomes `indeterminate` and is never re-dispatched; an uncertain marker commit (the process exits) gives the same result after the restart;
  - **(r5.1) single-flight during a live step (R2.1, invariant 7, K11):** ticks and `WaitMany` nudges arrive every 10 ms, and a second `exec_submit` of the same id arrives, while a 5 s `RunPhased` runs; the fake SSH server sees exactly one dispatch of that step and the job has one result for it; a Marker command applied twice answers `already_marked` the second time and no `Start` follows;
  - **(r5.1) K16 after a slow marker commit:** an injected 3 s delay in the marker commit on a step whose window then has under 2 s left → result `expired_unexecuted` beside the marker, zero dispatches, later steps `not_run`;
  - **(r5.4) K16 after a slow handshake (review 5 #4):** the marker commits quickly, then the in-process SSH server delays the handshake (and, separately, the session open) while the `internal/elapsed` fake moves the wall half past W − 2 s (and, separately, only the elapsed half past `elapsed_end − 2 s`) → result `expired_unexecuted` beside the marker, the server records zero exec requests, later steps `not_run`, the report carries `expired_unexecuted`, and a restart re-dispatches nothing (the marker has its result);
  - **(r5.4) completion time (review 5 #3):** (a) a long-running job: one step runs 8 d on the fake clock; nothing is removed at `created_at + 7 d` or at the marker's `at + 7 d`; `final_at` is the result commit's `now`, bodies are removed at `final_at + 7 d` and the file's age path opens at `final_at + 30 d`; (b) finalization then an immediate kill: the executor is killed right after the result commit that ends the job; after the restart `final_at` is present and equals that commit's `now`, and body removal happens at `final_at + 7 d`, not at restart + 7 d; (c) later writes: the report's `report_result`, `first_delivered_at` and the token removal, made at `final_at + 1 h`, + 2 d and + 3 d, leave `final_at` unchanged, and touching the file's mtime moves no age; (d) a job ended by an outcome (`denied`, `approval_lost`, `error{code}`) gets `final_at` in the outcome's commit;
  - crash injection at each cut: before Submit; after Submit with the response lost; after approval before Claim; after a marker before `Start`; after `Start` before the result; after result k before marker k+1 (inside the window the restart's repeat Claim returns the same set and runs step k+1 exactly once; after the last window Claim answers `expired`, step k+1 becomes `expired_unexecuted`, later steps `not_run`, `partial`; steps 1..k are never re-run); after the final result before the report; after the report before `report_result` (the resend is an exact duplicate and is acked, F-1);
  - a resubmit after a lost response inside the window is idempotent; after the window the job ends `approval_lost` (`submit_window_closed`) and the fake signer sees no second card;
  - a lost Submit response followed by a denial: the job adopts `denied`, also when the signer record has been shed;
  - Claim answers against the real A6 memory lifecycle (a test-only import, so `verify-executor-closure` on the binary is unaffected): `expired` → `expired_unexecuted` with an accepted negative report; `materialization_error{grant_not_live}` after a revoke → outcome `error`, no dispatch, no report; `execution_reported` → `adopted`;
  - **(r5.8, review 9 #3; moved from C1) the signer side of `exec_submit`:** the same id and input from two connections → the fake signer sees exactly one Submit and holds one record; an `exec_submit` refused `submit_window_closed`, `invalid_request` or `request_too_large` makes no signer call (the fake signer's socket records no connection); a result commit refused definitively (an injected `ErrTooLarge`) is retried without bodies and keeps the exit status; a job with the maximum escaped draft and maximum output commits its results through `JobNext` with their bodies, never through the bodies-dropped fallback;
  - **(r5.8, review 9 #2) SIGTERM is not a step failure:** the executor subprocess receives SIGTERM (a) after a step's marker, while `RunPhased` is still dialing, before `Start`; (b) while a step runs after `Start`; (c) during a Claim `not_ready` backoff; (d) while the final report is in flight; (r5.8, critique of the fold) (e) while an `exec_submit` is in E63's poll after its job commit and its signer Submit. In each case no result, outcome or `report_result` is committed after the latch, and the fake SSH server sees no further exec request; the process exits within 5 s of SIGTERM in (b), (c), (d) and (e), and within `Client.Timeout` + 5 s in (a). In (e) the `exec_submit` caller gets the job (`request_id`, `acknowledged`), not `executor_unavailable`; through C3's client (in C5) the MCP output does not invite a resubmit; and after the restart the job runs exactly once with one signer record. After a restart, (a) and (b) give `indeterminate` (the marker has no result and is never re-dispatched), (c) resumes with its first successful Claim ((r5.8, critique of the fold) `not_ready` comes only before the first mint, so the restart's Claim mints and `claim_count` becomes 1; r5.8 said "a repeat Claim") and runs the step exactly once, and (d) resends a byte-identical report, which is acked;
  - `storage_uncertain` (r5.6) and `busy` from Submit, Get, Wait, Claim and Report are retried with identical bytes and never end the job (a job whose Claim answers `busy` until past `claim_end` then gets `expired` and ends `expired_unexecuted`, never `error{busy}`); (r5.6, critique of the fold; moved from A12) **the executor through the hosted proxy (C-61):** on A12's harness (an in-process `hosted.Server` on a file-backed store behind the local daemon socket, test-only imports, so `verify-executor-closure` on the binary is unaffected), a second connection holds `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` past the busy timeout once during each of the job's Submit, Claim and Report; the recording transport shows each resent body byte-identical to the first; the job runs exactly once, with one record, one signature set and one accepted report, and ends with its step class, never `error{busy}`;
  - a mixed batch whose earlier read fails and whose later write loses its connection after `Start` ends `indeterminate` end to end, and the report carries that class, (r5.6) `executed_ordinals [1, 2]` and `partial: false`, and is accepted by the real A6 lifecycle; with a third step, `partial: true`, (r5.6, critique of the fold) and the report lists `[failed, indeterminate, not_run]` and is accepted; a `stop_on_error:true` job stopped after a failure reports `[succeeded, failed, skipped]` (`partial: true`) and is accepted; the executor killed after step 2's result and restarted derives the same `executed_ordinals` and `partial` through `JobView`, and the resent report is byte-identical;
  - **`ReadCurrent` after approval (R3.6):** a malformed, unreadable, insecure or changed registry before a step → `stale_target` and zero dispatch;
  - **(r5.7, review 8 #1; driven at the executor's own surface in r5.7, critique of the fold) a signed reveal that prints the jail line, async:** `exec_submit` is called directly through C1's `executorwire` client (C2 needs no MCP code; C3 builds the async tool path and `await_approvals`) with a single-command `reveal:true` input whose command is a write, so its one draft step is `signed_reveal`; the in-process SSH server answers exit 77 with `gate: read jail unavailable: …` on stderr → the stored result is `failed`, `exec_await` shows `executed_ordinals [1]` and the `read_jail_unavailable` remediation with no retry advice, the report's executed mask holds 1 and the real A6 lifecycle accepts it; the executor killed after that result and restarted derives the same through `JobView`, re-dispatches nothing and resends a byte-identical report; a multi-command input whose first step is an `unsigned_read` printing the line, followed by a write, ends the job `failed_not_started`, not executed, later steps `not_run`. The `run(async:true, reveal:true)` → `await_approvals` form is C3's (fake executor) and C5's (real executor);
  - **(r5.2) unsigned tail after the last signed window (review 4 #7):** `[signed write that runs 90 s, unsigned read]` with the write's TTL 60: the read runs after the write's window has closed, with no Claim for it, the job ends `succeeded` with both steps executed, and the report (claim_count 1) is accepted. The same holds with the executor killed after the write's result commit and restarted after the window: the read runs with no Claim and nothing is re-run. `[signed write (90 s), unsigned read, signed write]`: the read runs, the second write gets Claim `expired` and becomes `expired_unexecuted`, the class is `expired_unexecuted` with `partial`. `[unsigned read, signed write]` whose grant was revoked before the first Claim: no step runs and the outcome is `error`;
  - **(r5.3) the executor's K16 on two clocks (replaces r5.2's clock-break test):** **S4:** a job holds a Claim answer for `[signed write that runs 90 s, signed write TTL 60]`; the wall clock steps back 7 min during the first write → the second gets `expired_unexecuted` with zero dispatches (E ends it), later steps `not_run`, and the report carries that class; (r5.3, critique pass 1) the signer fake keeps returning the held set to the repeat Claim before the second write, so K16 rather than the signer decides, and the repeat Claim does not re-acquire the pair; with a first write that runs 10 s and a 7 min forward step during it, the second write gets `expired_unexecuted` by the wall half alone; **suspend:** `Suspend(8 h)` with `StepWall(−8 h)` between the steps gives the same; **restart after a step back:** the executor is killed after the first write's result, the wall clock steps back by more than the time since the decision plus 5 s, and on restart the repeat Claim answers `expired` (the signer's 3.2 check commits the Erase) and the second write becomes `expired_unexecuted`; **S5:** `[signed write done, unsigned read, signed write]` with the third step's window ended → the read runs, then the third step fails K16; the held set is dropped at the last signed step's `Start` (no set is held after it); the static time-read test over `src/executor` passes;
  - **(r5.3, OD-3) definitive answers end the job:** a fake signer answering `conflict` to a resubmit, `not_found` to Wait after `signer_ack`, `record_corrupt` to Get, and `invalid_claim` to Claim, each in its own job → outcome `error{code}`, no report sent, the job leaves the live count and its reservation, and it is deleted at 30 d or under count pressure once its bodies are gone; `record_corrupt` on the Claim before step 2 of a job whose step 1 ran → outcome `error{record_corrupt}`, step 1's result kept, `partial` ((r5.6, critique of the fold) `exec_await` shows `[succeeded, not_run]` for a 2-step job, `executed_ordinals [1]`, `partial: true`, the same after a restart); each transient answer leaves the job unchanged and retried; (r5.3, critique pass 1) a resubmit answered `submit_window_ahead` after a 60 s step back is retried and accepted once the wall clock re-advances, and the job is never ended `error`;
  - **(r5.3, OR-5) reservation lifecycle:** 300 single-step jobs run one after another, each reserving about 2.7 MiB and producing 1 KiB → all admitted; the budget in use equals the live job's reservation plus the finished jobs' `R2FinalCharge` ((r5.5, critique of the fold)); a finished job's share drops to its encoded size without bodies, plus its post-final allowance, when its bodies are removed (the record keeps each step's metadata and both digests, SA-3 R2); the `report_result`, `first_delivered_at` and `bodies_removed` commits of 100 finished jobs never raise the budget in use and none is refused;
  - **(r5.2) replay after both stores pruned (review 4 #2):** a job runs under a live v2 grant and is reported. On a fake clock the signer record is deleted (past `submit_not_after + 24 h` and its age) and the job file is deleted (30 d). The original `exec_submit` bytes are then sent again, same `request_id` and `input_hash`, with a live grant for the alias still in force. The executor answers `submit_window_closed`, creates no job and makes no signer call; the fake SSH server sees no second dispatch; the signer sees no Submit. A hand-built Submit of the old draft straight to the signer is refused `submit_window_closed` too;
  - **(r5.2) job-file deletion predicate (review 4 #6):** with an injected ceiling of 4 job files: (a) four final jobs with undelivered bodies under 7 d old → no deletion, and the next admission answers `executor_full`; (b) four final jobs whose report acknowledgements are lost (the fake signer acks but the response is dropped) → no deletion and the tokens kept; the resend is an exact duplicate and is acked, after which each job becomes deletable once its bodies are gone; (c) a report answered `invalid_report`, and separately `not_found` (the signer deleted the header), and (r5.3, critique pass 1) separately `signer_incompatible` and `invalid_request`, → `report_result = refused{code}`, the token dropped, and the job deletable once its bodies are gone; (d) a report answered `storage_uncertain` or with a dial failure → still owed, the job kept, and `status` shows the owed count; (e) at 30 d a job whose report is still owed is kept;
  - **storage (E75):** 500 async jobs with maximum outputs never exceed the 256 MiB budget; pressure never removes an undelivered body; admission refuses `executor_full` when only undelivered results fill the budget; an evicted job's await returns `result_evicted` with its class and never re-executes; bodies are removed 7 d after the job ended ((r5.4) `final_at + 7 d`) whether or not delivered, and await then returns `result_expired`; a job file is never deleted before `submit_not_after + 24 h`;
  - await writes nothing; `first_delivered_at` is committed by `JobNext` after the response;
  - **no signature in any executor artifact** ((r5.5) review 6 #4: r5–r5.4 also forbade command text in the job file, which R2's header must hold, since the draft with its exact commands is what recovery resubmits, SA-3 R2): a signed step whose exec request the in-process server rejects leaves no signature material (`SSHGATE_SIG:`, the signature base64 or the signed-payload base64) anywhere in the job file, the `exec_await` response, the executor log or the report, and no job file ever contains signature bytes; **command text** appears in none of the diagnostic fields: the `exec_await` error, each step result's status detail and error fields, the `PhasedError` text, the executor log and the report body; separately, the job file's stored draft decodes to the submitted draft byte-exactly, its `draft_hash` recomputes to the header's, and each step's `ExactCommand` equals the input command; the test uses a command string that occurs nowhere else in the fixture, so a match outside the draft is unambiguous;
  - `exec_submit` returns `sent` once the fake notifier sends, and `sending` after 65 s when the notifier stalls;
  - N jobs resolving out of order; repeated await; an output-flood attack; stale_target; K16; the write volume of a worst-case job (32 steps, 8 MiB) is measured and recorded;
  - **(r5.1) contention:** 100 concurrent worst-case jobs on local disk: record the bytes written and the p99 marker-commit latency. If the p99 marker commit exceeds 1 s, C2 switches to writing each step's bodies as their own file **before** the result commit that names them by digest (an unreferenced body file is harmless and is removed through `ListNames` at startup; R1.6's seam came from the reverse order). That change stays inside R2 and does not change `JobNext`;
  - **(r5.1) burst:** 150 single-step grant-covered async writes within 60 s: jobs are admitted up to the live cap and the encoded reservation budget ((r5.2) each single-step job reserves its own encoded header plus the base64 of 2 MiB, about 2.7 MiB, so roughly 90–95 jobs; the measured figure is recorded), the rest get `executor_full` until earlier jobs finish, none waits on a card, every admitted job runs exactly once, and the numbers are recorded for §1.3 item 28;
  - **(r5.1) the report body is stable:** a final report whose acknowledgement is lost is resent after the bodies have been removed at 7 d, and it is byte-identical and acked (`result_digest` comes from the stored per-step digests, E38).
- **Ledger:** (r5.3) C-47, C-48, (r5.4) C-51, C-52 (executor), (r5.6) C-61 (executor), C-63 (executor), (r5.7) C-64 (executor), (r5.8) C-67 (executor), C-38 (the half moved from C1; C-31 is listed below), L1, L3, L11, L12, L13, C-3, C-4, C-6, C-7, C-10, C-11, C-18 (executor), C-19, C-20, C-23 (executor), C-27 (executor), C-30, C-31, C-32 (executor), (r5.1) C-34, C-35 (executor), (r5.2) C-40, C-43, C-44, (r5.3, critique pass 1) C-39 (executor: a closure the signer committed is never contradicted across an executor restart).
- **Risk:** each commit rewrites the whole job file, and each step makes two (the marker and the result), so a worst-case job rewrites roughly 64 × its file size, which with base64 bodies grows toward `R2Max`, about 11 MiB (r5.1 correction of r5's "32 × 8 MiB"; r5.2 bound). That is the price of committing bodies atomically with their result (§1.5 SA-3 R2). securestate has one store-wide mutex and flock (`store_linux.go:194-215`), so a large rewrite delays every other job's marker commit; the K16 re-check after the marker makes that fail closed, and the contention measurement above decides whether the body-file variant is needed.

### C3 — MCP async + the 13-tool surface (K9, E20, E57, E75; §1.5 SA-5)

- **Depends:** C1 (executorwire); its integration tests need C2, so ((r5.8, critique of the fold) §5's merge-only edge) C3 starts after C1 and merges only after C2.
- **Owns:**
  - `src/mcp/execclient/**`;
  - `src/mcp/tools/**`: the async branch, new `await_approvals.go` and `list_pending_approvals.go`, the executor probe in `status.go`, the `executor_unavailable` Denial (also used for `executor_full`, E11);
  - `src/mcp/server.go`: 2 consts, 2 `AddTool` calls, handlers, `serverInstructions`;
  - `src/mcp/cmd/sshgate-mcp/main.go`: `SSHGATE_EXECUTOR_SOCK`, dialed lazily;
  - `src/mcp/*_test.go`, `packaging/mcpb/**`, and the docs listed in §4.1.
- **Named changes (K9, E11):**
  - `TestServe_RegistersExactlyAgentTools` (`server_test.go:262-316`) moves from 11 to 13, and still asserts there is no `add_server`;
  - `denial_shape_test.go` moves (r5.3, critique pass 1) from 20 to 21 (A13b ends at 20, counting #22's `read_jail_unavailable`);
  - `server_alltools_test.go`'s `connectAllTools` adds the two tools (fix its "eight" comment).
- **Content:**
  - `async=true`: the MCP mints the `request_id`, (r5.2) fixes `submit_not_after = now + 3600 s` in the input, and computes `input_hash` over it (§1.5 SA-2), then calls `exec_submit`; on `ErrResponseLost` it retries with the same id and input (the same window, never a fresh one), which the executor answers with the existing job (R3.7). If the MCP process itself dies first, an agent retry is a new request (§1.3 item 25). (r5.1) `serverInstructions` and the AGENTS.md 13-tool sweep therefore say: "After an MCP restart, or when an async call's reply was lost, call `list_pending_approvals` before resubmitting; under a live standing grant a resubmitted write runs again without a tap";
  - `execclient` uses a per-call deadline of at least 75 s for `exec_submit` (E63's 65 s plus margin), and the await deadline plus 10 s for `exec_await`;
  - `execclient` verifies the **server's** peer uid after connecting (`SO_PEERCRED` on Linux). It must equal the MCP's own uid, and the socket's parent directory must be owned by that uid with mode 0700. Otherwise it refuses with `executor_unavailable` before sending anything, so a socket pointed elsewhere never receives commands or reasons and cannot return forged results;
  - **`list_pending_approvals`** (STATE-MODEL OV-1, F-3): the signer's List for this principal (sync and async requests alike), joined with `exec_list` by `request_id`; an executor that is down leaves the execution column "unknown (executor unavailable)", and the list still shows every signer record; (r5.6, critique of the fold) a signer List answered `busy` (`ErrBusy`, E21) or `storage_uncertain` is resent once after 250 ms; if it is still not answered, the tool returns the executor's jobs with the approval column "unknown (signer busy — retry)" and a top-level note that the signer's list could not be read, never a tool error;
  - **`await_approvals`**: per id, the executor's job if it holds one (`exec_await`; (r5.4, E29) for an `acknowledged` job the `approval_status` comes from the signer's Get for the same id, joined as `list_pending_approvals` joins), otherwise the signer's Get, which covers an abandoned synchronous request (`approval_pending`, E26). (r5.6, critique of the fold) A Get answered `busy` or `storage_uncertain` is resent with E25's backoff inside the await deadline; one still unanswered at the deadline gives that item, or that job's `approval_status`, "unknown (signer busy — retry)", never a tool error. It never signs, claims or executes; unknown or foreign IDs get a per-item error that leaks nothing;
  - reads ignore `async`;
  - **async reveal follows K9 as ratified:** `run(async:true, reveal:true)` is accepted; the executor runs the `signed_reveal` step, stores its raw output in the 0600 job file under the E56/E75 caps and retention, and `await_approvals` returns it repeatably (§7.2). OQ2's rejection is only an optional amendment (§1.2) and is not built;
  - the live log writes each terminal async job once per MCP process (best effort), and blanks reveal output exactly as the synchronous path does (`src/mcp/server.go:354`);
  - await validates 1..100 IDs and a wait of ≤55 s.
- **Tests:**
  - `smoke-fresh-install.sh` still passes with no executor; the executor being absent gives a Denial;
  - an impostor socket (a listener whose peer uid differs, simulated through an injectable peer check) or a world-writable socket directory is refused before any byte is sent;
  - the output schema validates; await returns the stored result and never executes;
  - a dropped `exec_submit` reply is retried with the same `request_id` and yields one job and one card (R3.7); (r5.2) the retried input is byte-identical, `submit_not_after` included, and a retry after that window gets `submit_window_closed`;
  - `list_pending_approvals` shows an abandoned synchronous request and every async job; `await_approvals` on the synchronous id returns the signer's view, later `expired_unclaimed`;
  - **async reveal:** end to end with a fake executor, await returns the raw output; the live log blanks it; with the real executor (C5), the job file is mode 0600 and E75 retention removes the body;
  - `executor_full` maps to the `executor_unavailable` Denial with the "collect finished results" remedy;
  - (r5.7, critique of the fold) (moved from C2) `run(async:true, reveal:true)` of a write, against a fake executor whose `exec_await` answers C2's stored result for a step that printed the jail line (`failed`, `executed_ordinals [1]`, `read_jail_unavailable`): `await_approvals` shows `failed`, `executed_ordinals [1]` and the `read_jail_unavailable` remediation with no retry advice, and never says the command did not run;
  - await of an evicted job returns `result_evicted` with its class, and of an age-expired job `result_expired`;
  - (r5.6, critique of the fold) a fake signer client that answers `ErrBusy` to List once → the list is complete after the resend; to every List → the executor's jobs with "unknown (signer busy — retry)" and the note, no tool error; to Get for an `acknowledged` job and for an id the executor does not hold, once → the real status, and until the await deadline → "unknown (signer busy — retry)" per item, with the other items answered.
- **Ledger:** L1, L4 (await), L5, L7, L11, L13, R2 (async reads), C-31, (r5.2) C-40 (MCP), (r5.6, critique of the fold) C-61 (read tools), (r5.7, critique of the fold) C-64 (async MCP).
- **Accept:** STD; FROZEN-MCP (with the named bumps); `flock $LOCK go test -race -count=1 ./src/mcp/...`; `bash scripts/smoke-fresh-install.sh`.

### C4 — Executor packaging (E57)

- **Depends:** C1. It runs in parallel with C2 and C3.
- **Owns:**
  - `Makefile`: `build`, `install-local` (`:151-162`), the `verify-versions` loop (`:307-332`), `.PHONY`, and `verify-executor-closure` added to `preflight`;
  - new `packaging/systemd/sshgate-executor.service`, a user unit with `ExecStart=` holding an absolute path rendered at install time, `Restart=on-failure` (load-bearing in r5: an uncertain commit exits the executor, §1.5 SA-8), `UMask=0077`, `NoNewPrivileges=yes` and `WantedBy=default.target`; (r5.8, critique of the fold) no `TimeoutStopSec` below systemd's default 90 s, which exceeds the owner stop's join bound (`Client.Timeout`, 30 s, plus one signer call's deadline, §1.5 SA-8 step 3), so a stop is not turned into a SIGKILL; (r5.3, critique pass 1) if the unit gains a `SystemCallFilter`, it keeps `@default` (which holds `clock_gettime`; `internal/elapsed` calls it as a raw syscall, x/sys `zsyscall_linux.go:585-586`, so the filter applies to it);
  - new `scripts/install-executor.sh` and `scripts/uninstall-executor.sh`. Both run as the user, refuse root, and support `--dry-run`. Enable/start runs only when the operator invokes the script;
  - `tests/integration/setup_test.go:41-42,64-65` adds both scripts to the lint lists;
  - `.github/workflows/release.yml`: a linux-only executor build;
  - `INSTALL.md`, `docs/install-step-by-step.md`, `commands/setup.md`, `docs/E2E-TEST-STRATEGY.md`.
- **Verify without touching live units:**
  - `bash -n` and shellcheck;
  - render the unit into the scratchpad;
  - `systemd-analyze --user verify <scratch copy>` where available;
  - (r5.3, critique pass 1) `systemd-analyze syscall-filter` confirms `clock_gettime` is allowed by any filter the unit sets;
  - `make verify-versions` covers `sshgate-executor --version`;
  - **never run `systemctl --user enable` or `start` on the operator's box.**
- **Accept:** STD; `make verify-versions verify-executor-closure`.

### C5 — Async integration and e2e (orchestrator)

- **Owns:** `tests/integration/async_*_test.go` (tagged).
- **Content:**
  - a real executor process, plus a daemon with a lifecycle and scripted notifier, plus the Docker SSH target;
  - every process started gets a temporary `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (E70), so it never touches the operator's executor socket, lock or journal;
  - an async run returns promptly;
  - approval leads to execution;
  - await returns the stored result;
  - killing the executor mid-SSH leads to `indeterminate`;
  - killing the executor right after a step's result commit (a fault hook in the real binary, enabled only by a test build tag) returns the known result after restart and resumes or finalizes per `JobNext`; killing it right after a check-failure result commit dispatches nothing after restart (R3.1); an injected uncertain commit exits the process and the restarted executor recovers from its files alone (E74, §1.5 SA-8);
  - an async reveal against the Docker target: await returns the raw output, the job file is 0600, and the MCP live log is blanked;
  - (r5.7, critique of the fold) an async `run(reveal:true)` on the Docker target whose command writes a marker file, prints `gate: read jail unavailable: test` to stderr and exits 77: `await_approvals` shows `failed`, `executed_ordinals [1]` and `read_jail_unavailable` with no retry advice, the marker file exists, and the job is never re-run;
  - two MCP processes share one executor; an `exec_submit` whose reply is dropped is retried by the same MCP with the same `request_id` and yields one job (R3.7);
  - (r5.1) while a signed step that appends one line to a counter file and then sleeps 5 s runs on the Docker target, both MCPs await the job in a tight loop and the executor's tick runs; the counter file holds exactly one line (C-34);
  - (r5.8, critique of the fold) SIGTERM to the executor while an async `run` waits in E63's poll after its job commit: the MCP's answer names the job and does not invite a resubmit, `await_approvals` after the restart shows it, and the counter-file step runs exactly once (C-67).
- **Accept:** `flock $LOCK make e2e` (serial; owns port 2222).

**Phase C end:** PHASE-END, plus `make e2e`, plus independent review.

### D1 — Telegram queue operations (K18, §9.2, E47, E49)

- **D1a (lifecycle lane, serial first):**
  - deny-all and cancel snapshots **in memory** (§1.5 SA-3; ≤16 per admin, 5 min): an immutable item list plus a random confirmation challenge. (r5.1) Items are `{record key, plan_hash}` for v2 records (a `request_id` alone is unique only per principal, SA-6.6) and `{legacy request handle, plan_hash}` for legacy v1 requests, and the snapshot renders each item with its record handle. Confirm applies each item as its own idempotent AdminCancel command, with the admin check per item and the `plan_hash` binding; an item no longer pending or whose `plan_hash` differs is skipped and reported. A restart discards the snapshot (§1.3 item 24); items already canceled stay canceled;
  - the execution-report display states of E49 for the notifier actor's `status_edits` (§1.5 SA-4 notify step 4).
- **D1b (Telegram lane; L-display is the only writer of `telegram.go` `dispatch` and `telegram_v2_commands.go`):**
  - every command below is registered behind the E64 gate (sender is the allowed user; private chat whose ID is the allowed user; not forwarded; not redelivered), and every reply goes only to the linked DM;
  - `/pending`: pending v2 records and legacy requests, ≤20, oldest first, with record handles (r5.1), hashes and reason excerpts, neutralized (E65);
  - `/deny_all`: renders the snapshot, then a `v:x:<snapshot>:<chal>` confirmation, then each item → `canceled`. Later arrivals are excluded;
  - `/cancel <handle>`: the same machinery with a single-item snapshot; (r5.1) a `request_id` is accepted only when it matches exactly one record, otherwise "ambiguous id — use the handle from /pending" (E64);
  - execution-derived edits labelled "client-reported" (E49).
- **Tests:** the snapshot excludes later arrivals; a stale snapshot fails closed; a confirmation after a signer restart is answered "snapshot expired — send /deny_all again" and cancels nothing; replays are inert; every E49 row has a golden; a record that a vote decided between snapshot and confirmation is skipped and reported; **each of `/pending`, `/deny_all`, `/cancel` sent from a wrong user, a group chat, a forwarded message or a pre-start (redelivered) update does nothing, leaks nothing, and is audited.** (r5.1) Two principals' records with the same `request_id`: `/pending` lists two items with different handles; `/cancel <request_id>` is refused as ambiguous; `/cancel <handle>` and a `/deny_all` confirmation each cancel exactly the named records.
- **Ledger:** D-5, D-6 (local), C-10 (labels), (r5.1) C-37 (Telegram).

### D2 — Hosted admin and queue UI (K18, §9.3)

- **Depends:** Phase C. It runs in parallel with D1.
- **Owns:** `pkg/signerkit/hosted/**`, including `pkg/signerkit/hosted/refapp/**` (r5.1 path fix).
- **Content:**
  - `POST /ui/request/{id}/cancel` (v1 rows) and (r5.1) `POST /ui/v2/request/{handle}/cancel` (v2 records): admin-only. A v1 row goes through `CancelLegacy` (`denied`, `terminal_reason=canceled`; E4c, no wait for an unaudited vote), with its audit line (r5.1); a v2 record goes through AdminCancel (`canceled`);
  - `POST /ui/deny-all/snapshot` and `POST /ui/deny-all/{snapshot}/confirm`, with snapshots **in memory** (≤16 per admin, 5 min, lost on restart), a per-item admin check and `plan_hash` binding; (r5.1) v2 items carry the full record key, never a bare `request_id`;
  - v2 pending cursor pagination (≤50);
  - execution views rendered and labelled "client-reported";
  - the admin UI;
  - (r5.6, critique of the fold) **contention (E21), with A10's classifier:** a v2 cancel whose AdminCancel `BEGIN` is busy answers 503 "busy — retry" and changes nothing; the snapshot POST whose plain read is busy answers 503 and creates no snapshot; the confirm runs each item's AdminCancel in its own transaction, reports a busy item as "not canceled (busy)" beside the skipped items, still runs the rest, and is answered 200 with the per-item list; the snapshot is discarded at confirm as before, so the admin takes a new snapshot for the items left. v1 cancel (`CancelLegacy`) keeps today's error handling.
- **Tests:** a non-admin is refused; an operator's ordinary deny still obeys veto; (r5.8, review 9 #1) after an admin cancels a v1 row through the route, an operator's vote POST gets today's 409 and one `vote_refused{canceled}` inert line, and the row is unchanged; later arrivals are excluded; cancel of a v2 record that a vote decided first answers "already resolved"; cancel of a v1 row holding an unaudited vote succeeds (`denied`, `terminal_reason=canceled`, no mint), and a racing `SubmitVote` discards its signatures; an expired snapshot refuses confirmation; a confirmation after a signer-server restart gets 409 "snapshot expired"; (r5.1) two `sub_principal`s with the same `request_id`: each cancel and each snapshot item acts on exactly one row; (r5.6, critique of the fold) on a file-backed store with a second connection holding `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for 6 s: a v2 cancel answers 503 "busy — retry" with the row byte-unchanged, and succeeds when resent after the release; (r5.7, review 8 #4) a snapshot POST under that held lock answers 200 and creates its snapshot, because its read is a plain WAL read that a writer does not block; separately, a store wrapper that returns an injected `SQLITE_BUSY` from the snapshot's plain read answers 503 "busy — retry" and no snapshot exists; a confirm of three items whose second item's transaction meets the held lock (a store wrapper that takes the lock only for that item) answers 200 with items 1 and 3 canceled and item 2 "not canceled (busy)", and a second confirm of the same snapshot answers 409 "snapshot expired"; JS tests, including the 503 and the per-item "not canceled (busy)" text.
- **Ledger:** D-5, D-6 (hosted), C-12, C-16 (cancel), (r5.1) C-37 (hosted), (r5.6, critique of the fold) C-61 (cancel and deny-all), (r5.8) C-66 (after cancel).

### D3 — Retention, metrics, docs and final review

- **Content:**
  - confirm the retention and storage policies of §1.5 SA-9 and E75 end to end (the mechanisms land in A6, A10, B1, C1 and C2), and document `report_horizon`, `--approval-report-horizon`, `--approval-retention`, `--approval-max-age`, `--approval-max-full-rows` and `--approval-max-rows`;
  - queue metrics per E59 (including the outbox backlog, quarantines and storage use, and (r5.1) orphan temp files removed, future-dated stamps found at Startup, (r5.3) wall steps seen by the tick and a store refused for an unexpected directory entry), through the `status` tool and hosted log lines;
  - docs:
    - `docs/THREAT-MODEL.md` (additions; today it says nothing about grants): invariant 3 is an interface property; results are client-reported; K20; route facts and admin build metadata on approval cards are requester-supplied and signer-unverified (E33, E35); reserved verbs are checked on command bytes at every intake and at mint (E71); the grant host binding (E24), grants as records that die with the store epoch, and (r5.2) that v1 `sign` spends no grant at all (OQ3 ruled (b)), which closes the pre-existing cross-host gap for v1-created grants in this release and leaves it only in older signers; `sub_principal` is a partition inside one hosted client, not an authentication boundary (E19); async reveal output is stored in the executor's job file (C3, OQ2); signature material never appears in SSH or MCP errors, job files, reports or logs (E28, §1.5 SA-3); uncertain storage commits restart the store's owner and fail closed (§1.5 SA-8); (r5.1) v2 grants cover only their own principal and never a v1 request, `revoke_grant` is alias-global de-escalation and `list_grants` is principal-scoped for v2 (§1.5 SA-6.6); operators address v2 records by handle; signer stamps are wall-clock and the gate has no future-TS bound (§1.4 item 5); (r5.3) time-based closures are committed write-once facts, so no clock event reopens what was shown; grants and held signature sets end at the first of a wall-clock and an elapsed (`CLOCK_BOOTTIME`) deadline, so neither a step back nor a suspend extends them; the gate's missing future-TS bound and its proposed fix (§1.4 item 5); the v1 grant suspend note (§1.4 item 6); after a step back of more than a day an exact replay of a request both stores deleted is a new request that still needs a tap or a live grant (SA-1 rule 3, R-2); one process owns each local store directory for its whole life;
    - `docs/design.md`, `docs/TESTING.md` and `docs/ROADMAP.md` status;
    - the `docs/proposed/feature3-…md:86` correction;
    - the K7 provider-disclosure text in the setup docs;
    - (r5.8, critique of the fold) the integrator note for embedders of `signerkit` (E23): a `Verdict` with `Approval.replay` set is not a resolution, and a `Call` whose `Lifecycle` starts with `approval-` is an approval-lifecycle event, not custody; a custom `AuditSink` that counts verdicts must check `Approval`;
    - the CHANGELOG (including that note).
  - The final triple-lens review: correctness, spec conformance and security. Then independent review.
- **Accept:** PHASE-END and `make e2e`.

## 4. Cross-cutting

### 4.1 Docs and tool surface

**The reason sweep (A13b):**
- `AGENTS.md`, `:20-43` tool bullets, plus new guidance for `reason`, the E10 replacement for exit-65 advice, and v2 reconcile (replacing the `:59-61` "Verdict undelivered" text for v2); (r5.7, critique of the fold) the read-jail line at `:101-102` (A13b content);
- `README.md`, around `:143`;
- `commands/run.md:27`, `commands/run_batch.md:37`, `commands/revoke.md:29`;
- `skills/debugging-remote-servers/SKILL.md:64-113`, `skills/software-installation/SKILL.md:164`, `skills/linux-server-debugging/SKILL.md:186`, `skills/server-setup/SKILL.md:125`;
- `src/mcp/server.go:130-140` (`serverInstructions`);
- the tool jsonschema descriptions.

**The 13-tool sweep (C3, in the same commit as the guard bump):**
- `AGENTS.md:18`, which becomes "exactly thirteen tools", and its tool list;
- `README.md:143` and `:215`;
- `CHANGELOG.md:22` (Unreleased);
- `docs/design.md:203-218`;
- `docs/TESTING.md:441-456`;
- `docs/ROADMAP.md:205-208`;
- `docs/FUTURE.md:61`, `:81` and `:134`;
- `skills/debugging-remote-servers/SKILL.md:9` and `skills/server-setup/SKILL.md:17`;
- `packaging/mcpb/manifest.json:43-56`, which gets 13 entries and keeps `tools_generated:false`;
- `packaging/mcpb/README.md:15`.

**Environment-variable docs:** `SSHGATE_EXECUTOR_SOCK` goes everywhere `SSHGATE_SIGNER_SOCK` is listed: `.mcp.json`, `gemini-extension.json`, `server.json` `environmentVariables` (never add a line starting with `"version"`; `verify-dist` counts exactly two), the mcpb manifest and launcher comment, `docs/install-generic-mcp.md` and the README. Each place says async needs the Linux executor service.

No test reads these docs, so the PHASE-END reviewer greps for `eleven|11 tool` and gets an empty result outside `archive/`.

### 4.2 Packaging `sshgate-executor`

- It is a user-level systemd unit. The repo has no precedent for one; every existing unit is a system unit.
- It is installed by the user, never by `install.sh`, which runs as root.
- The executor needs `sshgatesigner` group membership to dial the signer socket. The docs must tell the user to log out and in, or restart `user@`, after the group add, and to run `loginctl enable-linger` so the executor survives logout.
- The MCPB, Smithery and darwin installs have no executor. On those, async returns `executor_unavailable` and the docs say so.
- The release is Linux-only for the executor.

### 4.3 Safety, restated for every lane

- Never restart, stop or reinstall the operator's live signer or any live unit, and never use the operator's bot token or chat in tests.
- Nothing is pushed, tagged or released.
- Every push needs the owner's word, a fresh a PII audit of the repository with its report reviewed, and `make preflight`.
- `dist/gate` is never regenerated in this build (§1.4).
- The plan, reviews and verdict files stay in `local-workspace/release-2026-10-02/`, which is gitignored. WORKLOG entries are publishable summaries.

### 4.4 Review cadence

- Phase ends run PHASE-END, including independent review.
- High-stakes sub-phases get their own independent review pass as well:
  - A1a, A1b and A5 together (schema, state core and wire), which the reviewer checks rule by rule against §1.5;
  - **B0 alone** (r5: the only new security-primitive code, `securestate.ListNames`/`Remove`, plus r5.1's `RemoveOrphanTemps`);
  - A6 (the single-writer engine, the outbox gate and the notifier actor);
  - A9 (migration);
  - A10 and A11 together (the hosted trust boundary, including MAC challenges and `sub_principal`);
  - A7 (the peer-credential and grant boundary);
  - A2 and A3 together (E16 display fidelity, the r2 blocker);
  - C2 (`JobNext` and the executor's crash semantics).
- r4.1's separate passes for A12 (the ownership index) and for A6b with A9 (the two-phase vote store) are gone with those mechanisms; A12 is reviewed inside the Phase A end review.
- **Critique pass 1 of r5 against the code, then an independent review of the r5 contract, come before A1a** (§11).
- After a third fix round on the same subsystem, stop, write down how it actually works, and redesign. r5 is that redesign for the durable state; a fourth round of state findings means returning to `STATE-MODEL-76-65.md`, not adding rows. (r5.3) The time model was redesigned under this rule (§12.8); a finding against it returns to `STATE-REDESIGN-NEEDED.md`'s constraints C-a to C-g, not to new rows.

## 5. Parallelism

**Dependency DAG (r5: an explicit edge list; it is authoritative, and every edge is drawn).** A sub-phase starts only when every predecessor is merged into the integration branch. (r5.8, critique of the fold) The one exception is an edge marked **merge-only**: it gates the merge (and the acceptance run before it), not the start.

```
A0   → A1a, A2, B0
A1a  → A1b, A3, A4, A5, A9, A13a
A2   → A3, A4
A1b  → A6, A9, A10, A13a   (r5.2: A9 and A13a added, review 4 #9)
A4   → A6, A8, A10, A11
A3   → A8, A10, A11
A5   → A7, A10, A12, A13b
A6   → A7, A8, A10
A9   → A10
A7   → A8, A12, A13b
A10  → A11, A12
A11  → A12  (the proxy's end-to-end test votes through the challenge route)
A8   → A12  (main.go: A8 is the first writer, A12 the second)
A13a → A13b
A12  → A13b (merge gate: hosted-backed users get no interim break)
A13b → (Phase A end)
(Phase A end), B0 → B1 → B2 → (Phase B end)
(Phase B end) → C1 → C2, C3, C4
C2   → C3 (merge-only: C3's integration tests need C2; (r5.8, critique of the fold) C3 starts after C1 and merges, with its integration acceptance, only after C2), C5
C3   → C5
(Phase C end) → D1a → D1b
(Phase C end) → D2
D1b, D2 → D3
```

Layered view under the lane schedule (r5.2): {A0} → {A1a, A2, B0} → {A1b, A4, A5} → {A3, A6, A9, A13a} → {A7, A10} → {A8, A11} → {A12} → {A13b}. The critical path is A1a → A1b → A6 → A7 → A8 → A12 → A13b, with A1b → A9 → A10 → A11 → A12 alongside; the two r5.2 edges add no length to it (A9 and A13a sit beside A6, and A10 already waited for A1b). Splitting A1 lets A3, A4, A5, A9 and A13a start as soon as the types freeze, while A1b builds the state core; merging A6a and A6b removes one serial step.

**Lanes in Phase A.** One writer per file. A lane can start a sub-phase once its dependencies are merged into the integration branch.

| Lane | Sub-phases in order | Owns |
|---|---|---|
| L-core | A1a → A1b → A6 → A7 → A12 | `src/approvalplan/**`, (r5.3) `internal/elapsed/**`, `pkg/signerkit/lifecycle/**`, `pkg/signerkit/{audit,audit_sink,socket,daemon,daemon_approvals,peercred_*,xferregistry,service,hosted_v2_client}.go`, (r5.2) `src/signer/daemon_grant_test.go` and `src/signer/daemon_authmode_deny_test.go` (A7's named OQ3 changes), and comment-only lines in `pkg/signerkit/{backend.go,daemon_internal_test.go}` |
| L-display | A2 → A4 → A3 → A8 | `src/redact/spans*`, `src/approvalreview/**`, `pkg/signerkit/assist/**`, `src/signer/backend/**` |
| L-hosted | A9 → A10 → A11 | `pkg/signerkit/{sqlitestore,store}/**`, `pkg/signerkit/hosted/**` (in `policy_runtime.go`, only `Server.Close`), `src/signer-server/**` |
| L-mcp | A5 → A13a → A13b | `src/approvalwire/**`, `src/mcp/**`, `tests/integration/**`, the reason docs |
| L-store | B0 | `internal/securestate/**` |

- **Serial points:** A0, then A1a. A2 and B0 may run alongside A1a.
- **Shared files, merged in a fixed order:**
  - `src/signer/cmd/sshgate-signer-telegram/main.go`: A8 → A12 → B1;
  - `src/signer-server/cmd/sshgate-signer-server/main.go`: A10 only. `main_test.go:349` gets one line from A9, routed by the orchestrator;
  - `src/signer/backend/telegram.go` `dispatch` and `telegram_v2_commands.go`: A8 → B2 → D1b, all L-display;
  - `Makefile`: A1a, then C4 only.
  - A lane that needs a file another lane owns asks the orchestrator. It never edits that file itself.
- **Merge order for A:** A1a, A2, B0 (whenever ready), A1b, A5, A4, A3, A9, A6, A13a, A7, A10, A8, A11, A12, A13b. Every sub-phase appears after all of its predecessors in the edge list ((r5.2) rechecked with `A1b → A9` and `A1b → A13a`: A1b precedes both). The orchestrator runs STD after every merge.
- **B is serial** (B1 then B2), because B2 touches both the lifecycle and Telegram (one writer, L-core then L-display).
- **C:**
  - C1 lands `executorwire` first;
  - C2 (executor lane) runs in parallel with C3 (L-mcp) and C4 (packaging lane); (r5.8, critique of the fold) C3 merges after C2 (the merge-only edge);
  - C5 is orchestrator-only.
- **D:**
  - D1a (L-core) then D1b (L-display);
  - D2 (L-hosted) runs in parallel with D1;
  - D3 is serial and last.
- **Tests:** every lane takes `$LOCK`. Integration and e2e are orchestrator-only, serial, and own port 2222.

**Sub-phase count (r5):** 28 — A0, A1a, A1b, A2–A5, A6, A7–A12, A13a, A13b (16 in Phase A); B0, B1, B2 (3); C1–C5 (5); D1a, D1b, D2, D3 (4).
