### 6.2 §13 acceptance ledger → closing sub-phase

**Rationale and assist**

- **R1** — Missing, blank, oversized or control-bearing reason fails before prompting. A1a, A13b (MCP), A7/A10 (signer re-validation).
- **R2** — Pure reads stay reason-free and prompt-free. A13b, C3.
- **R3** — All approval kinds show the unverified reason. A8, A11, A13b.
- **R4** — Frozen v1 stays byte-compatible, and legacy timeout behaviour is tested. A7 (FROZEN-LOCAL + legacy tests), A8 (production legacy), A10 (legacy TTL), A12.
- **R5** — The same persisted snapshot renders on both surfaces (reworded per E37). A3 fixture, A8, A11.
- **R6** — Assist checks alignment and concerns and summarizes the batch. A4.
- **R7** — Outage, malformed output or prompt injection can neither block nor approve. A4, A6 (watchdog gating), A8.
- **R8** — Only redacted input reaches the provider; no policy means skip; docs disclose. A4, A8, A10 (hosted wiring), A11, D3 (docs).
- **R9** — Assist cannot change bytes, quorum or signing. A6.

**Async lifecycle**

- **L1** — N async writes return promptly and resolve out of order. C2, C3.
- **L2** — Nothing pending or terminal-non-approved can mint. A1b, A6, A10.
- **L3** — The approval timestamp governs the signature timestamp, and execution starts immediately. A6, A10, C2.
- **L4** — Poll timeout is distinct from expiry. A6, A10, C3.
- **L5** — Duplicates are idempotent. A1b, A6, A8, A11, C1, C3.
- **L6** — Hosted N-of-M takes one challenge per vote and has a separate terminal CAS; (r5.4) N and the requester are the row's own (C-49). A10, A11.
- **L7** — Client A cannot see client B. A6, A7, A10, A12 (through the proxy's `sub_principal`, E19), C1, C3.
- **L8** — The local identity comes from peer credentials. A7.
- **L9** — Lost submit reconciles; a conflicting plan is a conflict. A6, A13b, C1, C2.
- **L10** — Blocking run and run_batch stay compatible. A13b, A12.
- **L11** — Repeated await never re-executes. C2, C3.
- **L12** — One singleton executor and atomic claim across MCP sessions. C1, C2, C5.
- **L13** — Unlimited async output is rejected and quotas hold under attack. C2, C3.

**Display and batch integrity**

- **D-1** — Every step shows its redacted display, spans, route, fingerprint, TTL, hash and reason. A3, A8, A11.
- **D-2** — Approve-all is bound to the immutable displayed list. A6, A8, A11.
- **D-3** — Control, bidi and non-ASCII content cannot forge the card, in commands, the reason or assist text (E65); an unrenderable v2 plan is refused (E16). A2, A3, A8, A11.
- **D-4** — A stale or mutated view fails closed. A6, A11.
- **D-5** — Deny-all is snapshot-bound. D1, D2.
- **D-6** — Admin cancel and deny-all are role-enforced, and ordinary deny obeys veto. A9, D1, D2.
- **D-7** — Oversized requests fail before notification, and multipart Approve comes only after every part. A1a, A6, A8.

**Crash and security matrix**

- **C-1** — Pending restart, `uncertain` handling and re-notify. (r5.4) A1b (model), B2.
- **C-2** — One durable signature set. A1b (model), A6, A10, B1, B2.
- **C-3** — An expired signature before SSH gives `expired_unexecuted`. A13a, A13b, C2.
- **C-4** — A mid-SSH restart gives `indeterminate`. C2.
- **C-5** — Pre-invocation and post-invocation SSH failures are distinguished. A13a, C2.
- **C-6** — A post-result restart returns the stored result. C2.
- **C-7** — Registry drift gives `stale_target`. A13a, A13b, C2.
- **C-8** — The transfer freeze, and `stale_transfer_key`. A7.
- **C-9** — 60/300 TTLs plus the K16 warning. A1a, A3, A13a.
- **C-10** — Reports are monotonic and client-reported. A6, C2, D1, D2.
- **C-11** — Claim-token properties. A6, C1, C2.
- **C-12** — Limits, expiry and pruning. A6, A9, A10, B1, C1, C2, D2, D3.
- **C-13** — No harness, C3 or yolo path can manufacture a verdict. A8 (only signer-polled callbacks; E51 checks), A11 (session + MAC challenge), E13 (no machine vote kind), plus the D3 review.
- **C-14** — No SQLite in the local signer. Every sub-phase, through STD.

**Plan-added acceptance rows.** They are not spec §13 rows, but they close the same way and PHASE-END checks them. C-15 to C-22 came from independent review 1, C-23 to C-27 from review 2, C-28 to C-33 from review 3 and the r5 redesign, C-34 to C-38 from r5's critique pass 1 (r5.1), and C-39 to C-45 from independent review 4 (r5.2), and C-46 to C-48 from the r5.3 time redesign, and C-49 to C-54 from independent review 5 (r5.4), and C-55 to C-57 from the critique of r5.4, C-58 to C-60 from independent review 6 (r5.5), C-61 to C-63 from independent review 7 (r5.6), and C-64 and C-65 from independent review 8 (r5.7), and C-66 and C-67 from independent review 9 (r5.8); r5 restates each in its terms.

- **C-15** — Reserved verbs are refused on command bytes at every v2 intake and re-checked at the first Claim; a grant never covers one (E71). A1a, A6, A7, A10, A12.
- **C-16 (r5)** — A v2 vote is one commit: never lost, doubled or blocked by an interrupted audit; expiry and cancel race it by commit order under one lock; hosted v1's same-decision resume survives, and v1 expiry and cancel never wait for an unaudited vote (E4c, E7, E72). A6, A8, A10, A11, B2, D2.
- **C-17 (r5)** — Hosted rows are keyed on `(client_id, sub_principal, request_id)`; through the stateless proxy another uid's request is the same miss as a missing one, concurrently and after a restart (E19, A12). A10, A12.
- **C-18** — Retention never removes live work and never lets an old id become a new approval (r5.3, critique pass 1) while either store holds the id (after both delete it, §1.5 SA-1 rule 3's R-2); a claimed command running past its window stays reportable while its header exists; every store stays bounded under sustained load (§1.5 SA-9, E73, E75). A6, A9, A10, B1, C1, C2.
- **C-19** — Batch aggregation preserves uncertainty and partial execution; final evidence is never followed by a dispatch, live or after a restart (E29, E74, §1.5 SA-5). A1a, A1b, A13a, C2, C5.
- **C-20 (r5)** — One final report per request: negative reports need no claim, executed reports do, exact duplicates are acked, late reports are accepted while the header exists, and `unreported` is never stored (E25, §1.5 SA-4). A1b, A6, A7, A10, C2.
- **C-21** — An accepted request whose notification never started gets exactly one burst and card, after the Submit effect, the tick or a restart (E63). A6, A8, B2.
- **C-22** — Hosted vote challenges are session-bound MACs; logout, relogin, session expiry and a server restart invalidate them (E7). A9, A11.
- **C-23 (r5)** — An uncertain commit is a crash of the store's owner: no dependent effect runs, the store fails closed until it is reopened under a new epoch, retries reach the landed outcome, and concurrent readers never see cached state (§1.5 SA-8). B0, B1, A10, C1, C2.
- **C-24** — Assist completion never races the review burst: no card is edited or replaced before it exists, the card renders the View's revision, card I/O for a record is serialized, and a challenge is valid only on a delivered or confirmed entry (§1.5 SA-4, SA-6.1). A6, A8, B2.
- **C-25 (r5)** — Local legacy v1 behaves exactly as today: the first of a tap and the deadline wins once, and a caller that received `timeout` can never be approved (E8b, K12); (r5.4) the taps that do not resolve it are audited (C-54) without changing any answer. A6, A7, A8.
- **C-26** — Every v2 store has a hard live bound and a hard history ceiling, enforced atomically at admission, with reclamation of unprotected history and `queue_full` when only protected history remains (§1.5 SA-9). A6, A9, A10, B1.
- **C-27** — Signature material never appears in any SSH error, MCP error, job file, report or log, including when the server rejects an exec request (E28). (r5.5) Command text never appears in an executor or SSH diagnostic surface: the `exec_await` error, each step result's status detail and error fields, the `PhasedError` text, the sanitized SSH debug log (E28), the executor's own log and the report body, while the job file's journalled draft keeps the exact commands byte-exact for recovery (SA-3 R2). (r5.5, critique of the fold) Two closed logs carry command text by contract and keep it: the MCP live log (`src/mcp/livelog/livelog.go:13-16,38-39`, the whole command by design; A13b keeps its lines) and the signer AuditLog (`AuditEvent.Commands`, `pkg/signerkit/audit.go:39,64-66`). r5.5's "a log line" contradicted both. Signature material stays banned from both, as from everything else. A13a, A13b, C2.
- **C-28 (r5)** — Owner scope first: on every read and command, over every transport, for full and shed records, another principal's id and a missing id answer byte-identically, and nothing state-dependent happens before the owner check (§1.5 SA-6.6; R3.4, F-2). A1b, A6, A7, A10, A12.
- **C-29 (r5)** — Grants are records: the grant id and expiry come from the durable record; revoke, supersession, expiry ((r5.3) by either clock, §1.5 SA-1 rule 3.4) and a new epoch end a grant and stop grant-approved requests not yet claimed; revoking the newest grant never revives an older one; v1 `revoke_grant` and `list_grants` cover both sources, (r5.1) the list only for the caller's own v2 grants (E9, E24; R3.5, F-4; C-36). A1b, A6, A7.
- **C-30 (r5)** — Dispatch requires a fresh, validated read of the current registry entry; any read, parse, permission or digest failure refuses the step (`stale_target`) while `Get` keeps today's behaviour ((r5.5) last-known for a malformed, unreadable or insecure file; not-found for an absent file; the new entry for a valid changed file; A13a; R3.6). A13a, A13b, C2.
- **C-31 (r5)** — `exec_submit` is idempotent on the MCP-minted `request_id` and `input_hash`: a lost reply retried from the same or another MCP session yields one job and one approval; the same id with other input is `conflict` (§1.5 SA-5; R3.7); (r5.2) and a replay never gets a fresh submit window (C-40). C1, (r5.8: the one signer Submit) C2, C3, C5.
- **C-32 (r5)** — The state core's properties hold for every store: replay is deterministic, (r5.4) every command meets its replay row (exact, noise only, Claim's `claim_count` only, or same record with an answer that says so; Renotify's row depends on whether a resend was already due), write-once slots are never overwritten, views never write, and no crash prefix leads to a second send or dispatch; (r5.3) Apply is the command applied after Clock, and Clock is idempotent, never removes or overwrites a slot and writes only deadlines ≤ its `now` (§1.5 SA-11). A1b, A6, A10, B1, C2.
- **C-33 (r5)** — `securestate.ListNames` and `Remove` fail closed on unexpected entries and report an uncertain unlink as `ErrCommitUncertain`; (r5.1) the listing uses a fresh descriptor, `Remove` validates the file first, and orphan temp files are removed at store open (B0).
- **C-34 (r5.1)** — A step is dispatched at most once, live and after a restart: `JobNext` is single-flight per job, a current-epoch marker without a result means in flight, and `already_marked` never reaches `Start` (§1.5 SA-5). A1b, C2, C5.
- **C-35 (r5.1; rewritten in r5.3)** — Clock errors fail closed and never refuse de-escalation (§1.5 SA-11 time properties 1, 2, 5, 6, 7 and 9; (r5.3, critique pass 1) the only clock-based refusals are SA-1 rule 3.1's list): no stamp exceeds the wall clock at its commit; no signature bytes leave while `decided_at > now + 5 s`, at a first or a repeat Claim; no grant, held signature set or sync budget outlives either of its ends, and an ended grant is never live again in its epoch, under steps back, forward steps and a suspend fake (review 4's grant case, S3); a K16 failure is committed and never dispatched, and is judged only for the first step with no result, when it is signed (S4, S5); no clock reading changes an epoch, cancels an actor, yields `uncertain`, or refuses Revoke, AdminCancel or deny-all, and (r5.3, critique pass 1) it refuses a Deny tap only as OA-5's stale view (C-46); E64's command gate reads no clock; with a correct gate clock no step executes at gate time ≥ `Exp`; lifecycle, approvalplan, jobplan's runner, the sync coordinator and the executor read time only through the injected clock, and end no authority on a Go timer (static test) (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3); (r5.3, critique pass 1) windows exist only for signed steps and end at whole seconds, and a held pair is acquired once per step per process. A1a, A1b, A6, A10, A13a, A13b, B2, C2.
- **C-36 (r5.1)** — Grants: Shed, Delete and Revoke never revive a superseded grant; a v2 grant covers only its own principal and never a v1 request; `list_grants` never shows another principal's v2 grant (§1.5 SA-6.4, E24); (r5.2, OQ3 (b)) no grant of either kind ever auto-signs a v1 `sign`, including the cross-host request, and a prompted v1 response is byte-identical to today's human-approved one. A1b, A6, A7.
- **C-37 (r5.1)** — Operator surfaces address v2 records by record handle: two principals with the same `request_id` are two items, each action reaches exactly one record, and an ambiguous id is refused (§1.5 SA-2, SA-6.6). A8, A11, B2, D1, D2.
- **C-38 (r5.1)** — Record bounds hold on the encoded bytes: the worst-case K19-legal R1 record fits its bound, and executor bodies of arbitrary bytes round-trip exactly within theirs (§1.5 SA-3); (r5.2) the R2 bound comes from the whole encoded record, so maximum escaped draft content and maximum output together commit with their bodies, and each job's reservation covers its own file. A1a, B1, C1, C2.
- **C-39 (r5.2; rewritten in r5.3)** — Time closures are committed facts (§1.5 SA-11 time properties 3, 4 and 8; STATE-MODEL OC-1): a view shows expiry, assist `unavailable`, a closed window or erased bytes only after Clock commits it; every time-based refusal (a Vote after expiry, a Claim past `claim_end`) is committed in its own answer's commit; a request shown expired never takes a vote and a window shown closed never releases; across a restart or reopen, landed or not, nothing committed is contradicted and Clock writes nothing due after that wall clock (S1, D1); with unequal TTLs an unclaimed record closes at its first window end; a live cap full of just-ended windows admits after the admission Clock pass (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3.3, SA-6). A1b, A6, A10, B1, B2, C2.
- **C-40 (r5.2)** — `exec_submit`'s submit window is fixed by the MCP inside `input_hash`; a replay after both the job file and the signer record have been pruned is refused `submit_window_closed` and never runs, even under a live grant (§1.5 SA-5, E73, STATE-MODEL OR-1), (r5.3) absent a step back of more than a day after both stores deleted the id (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3, R-2; A1b pins the residual). A1b, C1, C2, C3.
- **C-41 (r5.2)** — One process owns each local store directory for its whole life: a second signer with another socket and the same `journal_root`, or a second executor with another runtime directory and the same config root, refuses to start and never loads a cache; ownership survives an uncertain-commit reopen (§1.5 SA-1 rule 1). B1, C1.
- **C-42 (r5.2)** — An interrupted edit is superseded, never replayed: after a crash before the API call, and after Telegram accepted the edit but before `confirmed` committed, one commit retires the entry and appends a fresh one, one edit is sent, and only the confirmed fresh challenge counts (§1.5 SA-4 step 3, E48). A1b, B2.
- **C-43 (r5.2)** — A job file is deleted only when the job is final, its bodies are gone or it never had any, its report is settled or never owed, and `submit_not_after + 24 h` has passed; undelivered bodies and owed reports survive count pressure and the 30-day age; definitive refusals settle a report (§1.5 SA-9, SA-5 step 7). A1b, C2.
- **C-44 (r5.2)** — Signature windows bind only signed steps: an unsigned step after the last signed window runs without a Claim, live and after a restart between steps, while a later signed step is still refused (§1.5 SA-5 step 6, E74, K16). A1b, A13a, C2.
- **C-45 (r5.2)** — The advisory binding is durable on every plane: after Flush, Shed and restart, with stderr discarded, a hosted row still yields the redacted reason and the assist state, revision and digest bound to its decision (§1.5 SA-3, E23, K8, spec §8.3). A1a, A6, A10, A11.
- **C-46 (r5.3, OA-5)** — A local challenge is valid only while its card entry's revision equals the assist revision of the record after Clock: a tap on a revision-0 card after the assist slot commits, or after Clock writes `unavailable` in the Vote's own commit, is refused "card updated — tap again" (`vote_refused{stale_revision}`) whatever its action, and no vote binds an `assist_bound` that its card did not show (§1.5 SA-6.1, K15, K8). A1b, A6, A8, B2.
- **C-47 (r5.3, OD-3)** — Every signer answer `JobNext` consumes falls on one side of one definitive/transient split: a definitive answer on resubmit, Get, Wait or Claim ends the job with outcome `error{code}` and no report, frees its live slot and reservation, and lets SA-9's predicate delete it; a transient answer is retried and never ends the job (§1.5 SA-5). A1b, C2.
- **C-48 (r5.3, OR-5)** — The executor budget counts a live job at its reservation and a final job at its encoded size, and body removal releases the bodies' share, so finished jobs with small outputs never fill it (§1.5 SA-9, E75). C1, C2.
- **C-49 (r5.4)** — A v2 vote is decided under the record's effective policy: the threshold is the `required_approvals` frozen at Submit and the requester is the record key's authenticated identity (`client_id` on hosted), never a caller-supplied value; `sub_principal` never changes it; with self-approval off the requester's own approve or deny counts for nothing; the v2 decision equals `SubmitVote`'s for the same row, votes and flags (§1.5 SA-4 Vote, K15, K17). A1b, A10, A11.
- **C-50 (r5.4)** — Re-notify is a committed authorization: a confirmed `/renotify` sends exactly one new burst and card even when a crash follows before the burst's append and no card exists, a burst cut after its append is `uncertain` and never resent automatically, and a repeated `/renotify` before the resend authorizes nothing more (§1.5 SA-4 steps 1 and 5, SA-8). A1b, A6, B2.
- **C-51 (r5.4)** — Executor retention counts from a write-once `final_at` committed with the transition that ends the job: a long-running job keeps its bodies 7 d after it ends, a restart right after finalization keeps the original deadline, and later report, delivery and token writes never move it (§1.5 SA-3 R2, SA-9). A1b, C2.
- **C-52 (r5.4)** — K16 is re-checked inside `RunPhased`, after the dial, handshake and session setup and immediately before `Session.Start`: a handshake or session open that outlasts either half of the held deadline sends zero exec requests and ends the step `expired_unexecuted` (E10, E28, §1.5 SA-5 step 6). (r5.4, critique of r5.4) `RunPhased` refuses a signed wire command with no guard, and every signed coordinator (jobplan, `revoke_server`, `update_gate`, both `transfer` legs) passes one. A13a, A13b, C2.
- **C-53 (r5.4)** — A delivered card commits `msg_id` with `outcome = sent`, so the notification view reads `sent` before and after a restart; only bursts and send entries (`first`, `replacement`) can read `sending` or `uncertain`, never edit entries (§1.5 SA-4 step 2, SA-6.3). A1b, A6, A8, B2.
- **C-54 (r5.4)** — Every authenticated tap on a local legacy v1 card that does not resolve the request (a duplicate approve, an opposite replay, a tap after the deadline, a stale challenge, an unknown handle) is inert and writes one bounded, fail-open audit line, while the tap's answer, the v1 response bytes and the first-of semantics stay today's (K15, E8b, E61). A6, A7, A8.
- **C-55 (r5.4, critique of r5.4)** — Every local v2 machine-plane request refused with no record commit to carry the event (a refused Submit, `reserved_verb` included, and `not_found` among others), and every refusal in the hosted proxy branch, writes exactly one fail-open audit row with transition `refused{code}`, identical for a foreign and an absent id, as `respondError` audits every refusal today (§1.5 SA-7, E71, SA-6.6). A7, A12.
- **C-56 (r5.4, critique of r5.4)** — A hosted v2 vote POST refused at the challenge check (stale revision, expired MAC, another session) appends exactly one `vote_refused` noise event in its own transaction, consumes no TOTP step and answers 409 "view changed" (E7 step 5, E61, K15). A11.
- **C-57 (r5.4, critique of r5.4)** — A hosted row whose report horizon or age falls due with no slot to fill has its cache columns (`class`, `view_status`, `next_due_at`) refreshed by the worker within one pass, with `record_json` unchanged, and is not re-selected on every pass (§1.5 SA-1 rule 3.3, A9). A10.
- **C-58 (r5.5)** — An unflushed outbox has its own retry driver: each owner retries every unflushed record each second, independently of `next_due_at` and `DueV2`, so after a single failed Flush and a recovered sink, with no further command, the outbox drains and a Claim gets signatures before the window closes; a dead sink never delays Clock work (§1.5 SA-7, SA-1 rule 3.3, SA-8). A1b, A6, A9, A10, (r5.7) A11 (through the approve POST).
- **C-59 (r5.5)** — The local payload budget is a reservation: each full-payload record counts at `R1Reservation`, its whole-life encoded worst case, from Submit until Shed or Delete, so admitted records completing assist, notification, approval, Claim and audit transitions never exceed the budget and are never refused for it, and the counter is rebuilt identically at Startup (§1.5 SA-9, E54, E75). A1a, A1b, A6, B1.
- **C-60 (r5.5)** — An approved request whose Claim window closes before release is `expired_unexecuted`, not `approval_timeout`: every synchronous coordinator ((r5.5, critique of the fold) `run`, `run_batch`, `revoke_server`, `update_gate` and `transfer`), which Claims only after seeing `approved`, ends the run `expired_unexecuted` with nothing run, sends a negative report and returns the non-retryable `expired_unexecuted` Denial, about one TTL after a timely decision rather than at the budget; `approval_timeout` comes only from an `expired` decision (§1.5 SA-4 Claim, SA-5, E26). A13b.
- **C-61 (r5.6)** — Hosted contention is a transient, wire-defined answer: a `SQLITE_BUSY` at `BEGIN` (or on a plain read) answers HTTP 503 with the closed-set `busy` envelope and applies nothing; the client types it `ErrBusy`, the proxy passes it through, and the synchronous coordinator and the executor resend the same request, claim and report bytes until it clears, so contention during Submit, Claim or Report never ends a job, never yields a Denial built from `busy`, and never leaves more than one record, signature set or accepted report (E21, §1.5 SA-5). (r5.6, critique of the fold) The synchronous Submit phase ends at its own retry bound with "nothing was submitted" when every answer was `busy` (E26); the human plane answers 503 at each transaction without spending a TOTP step it has not consumed, and says when a fresh code is needed (E7); a deny-all confirm reports a busy item per item; the hosted assist commit is resent until `assist_deadline`; the MCP's read tools show "unknown (signer busy — retry)", never a tool error. (r5.7, review 8 #4) A plain read during a writer's lock still answers (WAL), and a busy assist commit lands only while `assist_deadline` allows. A5, A10 (machine plane, worker, assist), A11 (vote POST), A12 (proxy, client-level), A13b (sync coordinator through the proxy, Submit-phase bound), C2 (executor through the proxy), C3 (read tools), D2 (cancel and deny-all).
- **C-62 (r5.6)** — The vote set always has room for the quorum: each v2 record holds up to `required_approvals + 64` votes; a vote that counts or decides is never refused for room, whatever earlier non-counting votes (self-votes with self-approval off, denies with veto off) and flag changes came before it; a vote that cannot change the outcome is refused `set_full` only when it would leave no room for the approvals still needed plus one; and a threshold above 256 is refused at signer-server startup and in `hosted.New`, never lowered (§1.5 SA-3, SA-4 Vote, K15). A1a, A1b, A10, A11.
- **C-63 (r5.6)** — `partial` and `executed_ordinals` have one definition: `approvalplan.ExecutedOrdinals` and `Partial` over the full step list (r5.6, critique of the fold: every step of a final job has a status, `JobView` deriving `not_run` for the unrun steps of a job its `outcome` ended, and a report must list every ordinal 1..`step_count` in E29's shape or be refused), with `indeterminate` counted as executed; `[failed, indeterminate]` is not partial and `[failed, indeterminate, not_run]` is; the runner, `JobView` after a restart, the executor's and the sync coordinator's reports, the MCP output and the signer's report acceptance (which refuses a disagreeing report as `invalid_report`) agree (E29, E25, §1.5 SA-4 Report). A1a, A1b, A13a, A13b, C2.
- **C-64 (r5.7, review 8 #1)** — Gate stderr is never execution evidence on a signed route: a post-dispatch exit 77 carrying the `read jail unavailable` line is `failed_not_started`, not executed, only on `unsigned_read`; on every signed route, `signed_reveal` included, it is `failed` and in `executed_ordinals`, with the `read_jail_unavailable` remediation and no retry advice, so a revealed write that prints the line is reported as run, on the sync and async paths alike, and its report is accepted (E10, E28, E29; `jobplan.StepOutcome`). (r5.7, critique of the fold) `StepOutcome` is keyed on the step kind, so `update_gate`, `revoke_server` and the transfer legs keep today's exit texts with no retry advice, and no `run_batch` draft has a `signed_reveal` step. A13a, A13b, C2, C3, C5.
- **C-65 (r5.7, review 8 #2)** — Version skew has a wire answer end to end: in front of a hosted server without `/v2/approvals`, the current proxy answers every v2 kind with A5's closed-set `signer_incompatible` frame (fixed bytes) and one `refused{signer_incompatible}` audit row; the MCP client types it `ErrSignerIncompatible`, as it does `unsupported kind` and a direct non-envelope 404, and the MCP returns the `signer_incompatible` Denial with nothing run; a typed `not_found` stays `not_found` (E13, E69). A5, A12, A13b.
- **C-66 (r5.8, review 9 #1)** — Every hosted v1 vote POST by an operator-role session that is refused (r5.8, critique of the fold: only these refusals; a 401, a 400 for a missing challenge, a 403 and a step-up 401 stay unaudited, as today) on a resolved row (an N=1 approve followed by a second approve or a deny, a POST after expiry or cancel), at the challenge MAC check, as a duplicate at step 7, or after a lost `ErrAlreadyResolved` race, is inert and audited at most once (K15): it writes one fail-open line, `vote_duplicate` or `vote_refused{reason}`, that the sinks encode as inert and no reader can take for a resolution; the answer bytes are today's and the engine is untouched (E7, E23, E61). A6 (encoding), A11, D2.
- **C-67 (r5.8, review 9 #2)** — An owner's stop is not a failure: SIGTERM, and the local signer's uncertain-commit reopen, stop intake, set a memory-only latch that refuses every later commit, then cancel and join the effects. A Telegram part, card, edit or status edit, a 429 wait, an assist call, a Flush, an SSH step or a report that the stop interrupts commits nothing, sends no INCOMPLETE edit and makes no further external call, and the next start recovers it by the crash rules (`uncertain` and `/renotify`, a superseding edit, Clock's assist fill, an orphan marker's `indeterminate`). The same failure observed with no stop still terminalizes per K19 (§1.5 SA-8). (r5.8, critique of the fold) "With no stop" is judged by the latch alone, so a send budget or per-call deadline ending is still a live failure; the signer's deferred `Service.Close` is what closes the lifecycle; an executor `exec_submit` whose job commit landed answers with that job, never `executor_unavailable`; and on hosted, which has no latch, a Submit after the cut finds the assist pool `closed` and commits no slot. A4, A6, A7, A8, A10, B1, B2, C2, C5.

> **§7–§9 are the r2–r4.1 dispositions, kept verbatim as history.** They cite r4.1's mechanisms and line numbers (E66, E72's S1–S4 vote protocol, tombstones, report stubs, the SC-n rows), which r5 withdrew. Nothing in §7–§9 binds where it disagrees with §1.5; §10.3 says where r5 resolves each review finding.

|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **BLOCKER:** `ok=false` hides the whole command, and PEM always gives `ok=false` | **Accepted** | PEM lives only in the Writer (`writer.go:170-201,208-285`), not in `rawMatches`. Hiding unmatched bytes contradicts §9.1 and exceeds K20. → E16 rewritten: explicit PEM spans; `request_unrenderable` for v2; a count-unavailable `RedactString` render for v1; never hide unmatched bytes. A2 and A3 tests; new Denial class (A13b moves to 18, C3 to 19). |
| 2 | Hosted proxy collapses local uids into one principal | **Accepted** | `withAuth` principal only. → E66 ownership index (A12, journaled in B1), isolation test. The alternative, single-uid-only, is rejected: it would break the default empty `allowed_uids`. |
| 3 | `preparing` never recovers; a saturated pool withholds Approve | **Accepted** | → E68 (deadline from freeze, never queue), A4 pool, A6b, A10, B2. Merged with critique 1 #11. |
| 4 | v1 `matchGrant` honours v2 grants on any host; OQ3 default should be (b) | **Fix accepted; default flip rejected** | Fix → E24 (merged with critique 1 #8). Flipping OQ3 to (b) would change frozen v1 behaviour for host-less v1 grants, which is the owner's call. With E24 the residual covers only v1-created grants, and no in-tree client creates one after A13b. OQ3 stays, with recommendation (b). |
| 5 | Slash commands lack sender, chat and redelivery checks | **Accepted** | → E64 (adds non-forwarded, `Date ≥ process start`, reply only to the DM); B2 and D1b tests. Merged with critique 1 #13. |
| 6 | `trusted_display` and route facts shown as trusted | **Partly accepted** | Accepted: the signer derives the update hash, refuses a mismatch, and labels both as client-supplied / requester's registry, signer-unverified (E33, E35, A3, D3). Rejected: renaming `trusted_host_fingerprint`. The ratified spec §5 name is kept, with a documented meaning and an honest display label. |
| 7 | Signer does not enforce the route-TTL table | **Accepted** | `daemon.go:570-573` caps only at `MaxSigValidity`. → E60, A1 `MaxStepTTL`, A6a and A10 tests. |
| 8 | Expiry depends on the sweeper | **Accepted** | → E67, E7 step 2, A6a, A6b, A10 tests. |
| 9 | Claim and report preconditions; signature zeroing | **Accepted** | → E25 claim and report matrix, E54, C2 journal zeroing, A6a tests. Merged with critique 1 #10. |
| 10 | Persisted salt enables offline confirmation of secrets | **Accepted (stronger fix)** | `markers.go:70-78`. The salt is never persisted or serialized (E37); provider input carries no marker keys (E15); A3 and A4 tests. |
| 11 | Reason and advisory text can forge the card | **Accepted** | Merged with critique 1 #14 → E65. |
| 12 | `execclient` does not verify the server's uid | **Accepted** | → C3 peer-uid and directory-owner check, plus test. |

## 8. independent review 1 disposition (r3, 2026-10-03)

an independent reviewer, X-High, reviewed r2 and returned NO-GO with 11 numbered findings and 2 smaller corrections (review note 1-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)). Every finding was re-checked against the code at `fb515f9` and the ratified design before it was folded in. None was rejected.

| # | Finding | Disposition | Evidence checked / where folded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | P1: the v2 signing path does not port the reserved-command guards | **Accepted** | `daemon.go:548-561` rejects `SSHGATE_XFER_*` before grant matching; `matchGrant` refuses every `SSHGATE_` command outermost (`:1456-1470`, `isAdminVerb` `:1510`); both read command bytes, while the v2 `route` is requester-supplied (E35). Hosted v1 has no such check, so hosted v2 needs its own. → new E71 (`ValidateStepBytes` at the local freeze, the proxy and the hosted freeze, before grant matching; byte-level grant refusal; mint-time recheck; prefix drift test); E9, E33; A1, A6a, A6b, A7, A10, A12, A13a tests (v2 ports of `TestSignPath_RejectsTransferVerb`, `TestGrant_AdminVerbNeverAutoSigned`, `TestSignRequest_AdminVerbMustBeSoleCommand`); ledger C-15. |
| 2 | P1: the hosted-proxy ownership index needs an atomic submission protocol | **Accepted** | `withAuth` injects one `MachineClientID` for every request (`hosted/server.go:310-325`), so hosted cannot separate local uids. r2 never forbade overwriting an owner. → E66 rewritten: immutable owners; `reserved`→`accepted`; conflict cleanup removes only the caller's own reservation; reconcile against hosted `draft_hash` before any use; index loss handled because the draft hash covers a secret-derived claim-token hash that no surface returns (E25); bounds (E75). A5 adds `draft_hash` to responses; A12 and B1 tests cover concurrent conflicting submits followed by every read operation, lost responses, and restart; ledger C-17. |
| 3 | P1: challenge consumption and vote recovery can strand approvals | **Accepted** | `PrepareVote`'s same-decision duplicate resumes audit and finalization (`sqlitestore/operators.go:354-392`); unaudited votes block every flip (`sqlite.go:352-360`); r2's E7 step 3 answered 409 to any repeat vote and no worker finished an interrupted vote. → new E72 (S1 consume-and-prepare in one transaction, S2 audit with `event_key`, S3 decide at the vote's `ts`, S4 mint; recovery at every cut by same-challenge resume, worker, tick and startup; decision catch-up; expiry and cancel run S2–S3 inline and never discard a timely vote; v1 rows keep the frozen engine with the resume restored and worker audit completion only). E7 step 3, E4c, E21, E23, E61, E67; A6b, A8, A9, A10, A11 (HTTP-level audit-failure and crash tests), B2, D1a, D2; ledger C-16. |
| 4 | P1: pruning can erase outstanding execution and resurrect denied submissions | **Accepted** | r2 E54 pruned any terminal audited record; r2 C2 resubmitted after not-found. → new E73 (`Settled`/`Prunable` across approval, materialization, execution and votes; tombstones that answer resubmits and reads; a submit window inside the draft hash so an old ID can never be recreated). E54 rewritten (caps count live records); E74 (no resubmit after the window); A1, A6a, A9, A10, B1, C2 tests, including pressure while a claim is outstanding and a lost Submit followed by a denial; ledger C-18. |
| 5 | P1: batch aggregation hides an indeterminate execution | **Accepted** | Under the class default a failed read in a mixed batch continues, and explicit `stop_on_error:false` continues past any failure (`run_batch.go:285-297`); a transport error ends the batch (`:274`). → E29 rewritten (four abort statuses, at most one per job and always the last attempted; abort class wins over earlier `failed`; `executed_ordinals` and `partial`); `AggregateJobClass` in A1, also used by the signer's report check (E25); A1, A13a, A13b, C2 tests for an earlier known failure followed by a later indeterminate step and by a signature expiry; ledger C-19. |
| 6 | P2: recovery overlooks the durable-result / nonterminal-index crash window | **Accepted** | r2 C2 wrote the result file before the index, but recovery turned every `executing` into `indeterminate`, against K11 and C-6. → new E74 (per-step `executing{step, attempt_id}` markers, step-granular result file with digest and `final` flag, recovery that publishes a valid final file's class, never re-dispatching); A13a `StepJournal` hooks; C1, C2 crash injection exactly between the final result file and the index publication; C5 fault hook; ledger C-19. |
| 7 | P2: approved-but-unexecuted failures cannot satisfy the report preconditions | **Accepted** | r2 E25 required `claim_count ≥ 1` and ready signatures for every report, so an executor whose claim returned `expired` (no signatures), or a materialization error, could never report. → E25 rewritten: negative reports need no claim; executed and `executing` reports still need a claim and materialized signatures; signer-derived `expired_unclaimed`/`not_executable`; Claim's ordered answers, including `execution_reported` after zeroing and `pruned`. A5, A6a, A10, C2 tests; E49 rows; ledger C-20. |
| 8 | P2: restart recovery omits accepted notifications that never started | **Accepted** | E63 returns at freeze with `not_started`; r2 B2 handled only `sending` and `sent`. → E63 (durable `not_started → sending` CAS before external I/O; tick and startup resend of `not_started`), E18 step 5, A6a, A8, B2 tests at both cutpoints; ledger C-21. |
| 9 | P2: hosted vote challenges omit the session binding | **Accepted** | Spec §5 ("operator/session-bound") and §9.3 require it; `withSession` passes only `sess.UserID` (`human_handlers.go:129`). → E7 (hashed `session_binding`; session ID carried in the request context without changing `next`'s signature; logout deletes the session's challenges; Telegram binds to the recorded card); A9 schema; A11 tests for reuse from another session of the same operator, logout and relogin, and session expiry; ledger C-22. |
| 10 | P2: storage bounds are incomplete beyond the local approval snapshot | **Accepted** | r2 bounded only `approvals.json`; 8 MiB per job does not bound the executor total. → new E75 (one bound and cleanup lifecycle per store: local journal and tombstones, hosted v2 rows, votes, challenges, deny-all snapshots, proxy index, executor results with admission reservation and delivered-only eviction), assigned to A6a, A9, A10, A11, A12, B1, C1, C2 and D2, with sustained-load tests in B1, A10 and C2. Hosted v1 `requests` rows stay unpruned: pre-existing, and frozen `/v1/audit` exposes all rows under K17. E56, E59; ledger C-12, C-18. |
| 11 | P1: unanswered owner questions silently amend ratified behaviour | **Accepted** | K1–K20 were ratified unchanged, yet r2 built OQ1 (`SyncAdapterValiditySeconds=300`) and OQ2 (reject async reveal) as defaults. The direct legacy Telegram path (E8e/E64, plus the `legacy_v1_via_lifecycle` switch) emits request-ID callbacks (`telegram.go:558-559`) with no K15 challenge and no K3 label. → §1.2 reworked into optional amendments that are never built by default; E26 rewritten to the ratified 24 h validity with a non-mutating `wait_elapsed` (`approval_pending`, a new Denial class, so 19 classes in A13b and 20 in C3); C3 builds async reveal per K9 and §10.3; E8e and E64 remove the direct path from production (v1 group-linked cards stay deliverable through the lifecycle); A7 drops both switches; OQ3 keeps its non-amending build (a). §1.3 items 10–15. |
| — | A9's schema omits `assist_deadline` | **Accepted** | The A9 `approvals_v2` column list had no `assist_deadline`, which A10 persists at freeze and the worker reads (E68). → A9 schema. |
| — | A6a needs an A4 dependency | **Accepted** | A6a runs assist jobs under `assist_deadline`, and the §2 package map lets `lifecycle` import the `assist` interface, but r2 listed A6a as depending on A1 only. → A6a depends on A1 and A4; §5 is now an explicit edge list with every edge drawn (including r2's A3 → A10 and A11 → A12); L-display runs A2 → A4 → A3 → A8; merge order A1, A2, A5, A4, A3, …. |

**Found while applying the binding owner rule (not raised by the review).** K10 says `async: true` asks the executor "to durably submit/notify and then returns", but r2's E63 returned at freeze, before the card was sent. → E63 and C2: `exec_submit` waits, at most 65 s, for the notification outcome, while the signer's own Submit still returns at freeze. §1.3 item 13.

**Line-cite fix.** r2's E7 said the second approve at `human_handlers_test.go:575` gets its 409 from step 3; it comes from step 2 (the row is already resolved, `human_handlers_test.go:574-582`).

### 8.1 Independent check of r3 (r3.1, 2026-10-03)

Each review finding was re-checked against r3's text, the ratified K-rulings and the code at `fb515f9`. All 11 findings and both smaller corrections are addressed by r3. These r3.1 edits close what r3 left open or contradictory:

| # | Gap in r3 | r3.1 change |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | A foreign `reserved` entry whose hosted response was lost, after a proxy restart, made the true owner's own resubmit return `conflict`, and the tick reconciled only `not_found` reservations. Over-bound behaviour and "remove under pressure" were undefined. | E66: a Submit meeting a stale foreign reservation reconciles it first; the tick reconciles every reservation older than 30 s; accepted entries become removable only past the E73 horizon and once settled; `queue_full` before forwarding when full. E75 row; A12 tests. |
| 2 | E66's index-loss argument relies on the draft hash covering a secret claim-token hash, but no rule made that hash mandatory. | E25: `executor_claim_token_hash` is mandatory on execution-applicable v2 drafts on both planes and in the proxy. A1, A6a, A10, A12 tests. |
| 3 | E7 step 3 resumes on any existing same-decision vote, while E72 described resume only with the **same** challenge, leaving a re-send after a challenge rotation undefined. | E72 and A6b: resume is keyed on the existing `(request_id, operator)` vote, whatever challenge is carried. A6b and A11 tests. |
| 3 | E67 promised "a tap made in time is never discarded" on both planes, but E72 forbids the worker to finalize a v1 row, and E7 step 2 left an overdue v1 row with a prepared vote undefined. D2 applied S2–S3 to v1 rows, which nothing can do. | E67, E72 and E7 step 2 state the v1 rule: expiry and cancel audit the vote (S2 only), then CAS; an overdue v1 row gets 409 and is never resumed. D2 content and tests. |
| 3 | E7 step 3 needs to see a prepared v1 vote, but the frozen `ListVotes` hides prepared votes. | A9 adds the `GetOperatorVote` extension interface. |
| 9 | E7's fixed order omitted the existing step-up, so its position relative to the resume and to challenge consumption was undefined. | E7 step 4a: step-up for every POST, resumes included, before consumption. A11 test. |
| 4 | A6a listed the submit-window check before idempotency, contradicting E73 ("an existing record or tombstone is returned whatever the time"); A10's order was likewise ambiguous. | A6a and A10: validate, then idempotency, then the submit window for new IDs only. A6a test. |
| 6 | E74 ended a job interrupted between steps with step k+1 `failed_not_started` ("executor restarted between steps"). That is neither outcome K11 ratifies for an approved, undispatched step ("resume only while the exact stored signatures remain valid; otherwise `expired_unexecuted`"), and it stretches §6.2's `failed_not_started`. | E74 and C2: resume at step k+1 under the K16 and stale_target checks; never re-dispatch a step marked `executing`. C2 crash-cut expectations. |
| 7 | A negative report was accepted after an executed or `executing` report, so a record could move from "ran" back to "nothing ran". | E25: such a report is `invalid_report`. A6a test. |
| 10 | Local `/deny_all` and `/cancel` snapshots (D1a) had no bound or cleanup. | E75 row. |
| — | A9's `vote_challenges` column list lacked `request_id`, which its own partial unique index uses. | A9 schema. |
| — | E63's async return can take 65 s, but no client deadline was set for `exec_submit`. | C3: `execclient` deadline ≥ 75 s. |

Owner questions are unchanged: OQ1 and OQ2 (optional amendments, not built) and OQ3 (frozen v1; build (a)).

## 9. independent review 2 disposition (r4, 2026-10-03)

an independent reviewer, X-High, reviewed r3.1 and returned NO-GO with 8 numbered findings and 2 precision fixes (review note 2-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)). Every finding was checked against the code at `fb515f9` and the ratified design before it was folded in. None was rejected. Findings 1–7 are recovery and state-contract gaps of the kind the previous round also found one at a time, so r4 folds them into **§1.5, one durable state contract**, rather than patching each erratum separately. The errata now point at §1.5.

| # | Finding | Disposition | Evidence checked / where folded |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | P1: recovery can continue a batch after an abort | **Accepted** | r3.1's E74 checked only "last step" and the stop policy for a non-final result file, so an `indeterminate` step persisted under `stop_on_error:false` resumed at k+1. Today a transport error ends the batch unconditionally (`src/mcp/tools/run_batch.go:269-274`), and E29 already said a run aborts at the first abort status. → §1.5 SC-4 recovery (b), with the abort status checked first, for all four statuses; the runner now writes the final file directly when a step ends the job (SC-4 step 4; A13a's `AfterStep(…, final)`); E29, E74; A1 precedence table; A13a and C2 tests covering every abort status × every stop policy, with the fake SSH server seeing no dispatch of step k+1. |
| 2 | P1: `ErrCommitUncertain` cannot mean the transition was refused | **Accepted** | `internal/securestate/store_linux.go:40-44` says the replacement may already be installed. `withLock` confirms directory durability before any later transaction (`:219-233`). The policy journal poisons itself and requires a reopen (`pkg/signerkit/policy_journal_linux.go:159-167,313-316`). → §1.5 SC-0.5: never infer rollback, never run dependent effects, poison and reopen and reload for securestate stores, re-read through the `state_version` CAS on hosted, undone-effect facts for work the process provably did not start, SC-6 recovery after reconciliation. The rule is applied per record in SC-2.V (vote), SC-3 (reservation), SC-4 (marker, result, index) and SC-5 (sending, receipt). E18 and B1 are corrected. B1, A10 and C2 run post-rename and commit-error tests with concurrent readers and retries, both with the replacement installed and without it. Ledger C-23. |
| 3 | P1: assist completion can race the initial multipart notification | **Accepted** | r3.1's A6a launched notification and assist independently, and A8's assist completion edited the card or sent a replacement, though nothing guaranteed a card existed yet. → §1.5 SC-5: a per-record notifier actor is the only code that performs card I/O. Assist completion only commits SC-1.S and nudges it. Before any card exists, nothing is edited or replaced, and the card is later rendered from the persisted revision. Challenges are minted inactive and activated in the receipt commit (SC-2.C). E48, E63, E68 and E18 are updated, and A6b's `Notifier` exposes only I/O steps. A6b and A8 test immediate assist completion, a burst lasting over 5 s with the deadline firing, a partial-send failure during completion, and completion while the card send is in flight; B2 tests the card cutpoints. Ledger C-24. |
| 4 | P2: settlement permits pruning work that is still executing | **Accepted** | r3's E73 settled an approved record at its last signature's `expires_at + report_grace`. Signature expiry bounds invocation, not duration (`src/mcp/ssh/client.go:28-35`). → §1.5 SC-1.R adds the `awaiting_report` class (claimed, window ended, no final report), which never counts against the live caps and under pressure is compacted into a **report stub** holding the authenticated report state. SC-1.X adds signer-derived `unreported` at `last_window_end + report_horizon` (default 24 h, configurable), replacing `report_grace`. E54, E66, E73 and E75 are updated; A9 adds the stub columns. A6a, A10 and B1 test a command still running 20 minutes past its window under pressure, with its report accepted against the stub. Ledger C-18. |
| 5 | P2: the report transition contract is contradictory | **Accepted** | r3.1's E25 required a nonterminal execution state, yet accepted negative reports after terminal signer-derived settlement. And a progress report sent at E74's pre-`RunPhased` marker would have blocked a genuine `failed_not_started`. → §1.5 SC-1.X: one matrix keyed on the current state and the report kind. It gives an exact definition of `executed_mask`: steps with status `succeeded`, `failed` or `indeterminate`; never `failed_not_started`, including an exec request rejected inside `Start`. **The progress cutpoint** is after the new `RunPhased` `Started` hook (E28), sent off the dispatch path by a sequenced per-job reporter. The matrix also gives exact-duplicate acks, one-time client detail on signer-derived states, and the closed-report answers. E25, E28 and E29 are updated. The matrix is exercised through the local socket with the tick running (A7) and over HTTP with the worker running (A10), plus A6a and C2. Ledger C-20. |
| 6 | P2: local legacy timeout conflicts with prepared-vote recovery | **Accepted** | K12: frozen local-socket v1 "cannot later become approved" after its terminal `timeout`. The current backend resolves the request once, at its timer or the caller's ctx end (`src/signer/backend/telegram.go:599-606,774-783`). r3.1's E67 and E72 explicitly gave local legacy records the prepared-vote block. → §1.5 SC-1.L: the legacy deadline is a CAS that a prepared vote does not block; the vote is audited as uncounted and S3 never runs; the v1 response is derived from the committed terminal state. E8b, E67 and E72 are corrected. A6b (engine), A7 (library composition) and **A8 with the production lifecycle adapter**: an audit failure across the deadline gives v1 `timeout`, `expired`, and no signature. Ledger C-25. |
| 7 | P2: the hosted history ceiling has no enforcing operation | **Accepted** | r3's E75 tombstoned rows "when settled plus tombstoned rows exceed 200 000"; tombstoning does not lower that sum, and A10's 20 000-request test never reached the ceiling. → §1.5 SC-1.R: two hard hosted ceilings (full rows and all rows, both flags), checked inside the Submit transaction (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`) after in-transaction reclamation (tombstone, compact, delete closed tombstones outside the horizon), then `queue_full` when only protected history remains. A row-size bound at freeze. The same admission rule locally, plus a live-bytes cap (r3's 256 live × 256 KiB exceeded its own 32 MiB file cap). A9 primitives; A10 tests with injected small ceilings, covering reclaimable and protected history and concurrent Submits; A6a's local equivalent. Ledger C-26. |
| 8 | P2: A7's acceptance contract depends on A8 | **Accepted** | A7 asserted that the production daemon always has a lifecycle **and notifier**, but the notifier is A8's. → A7 no longer writes signer `main.go`. A8 becomes its first writer, owning the `[approvals]` section, lifecycle construction with the Telegram notifier, and the moved wiring test. The writer order is A8 → A12 → B1. The DAG edge A7 → A8 is now a full edge. E8e records the interim state between the two merges (today's production behaviour, never released). |
| P1 | Executor seven-day expiry vs pressure eviction | **Accepted** | E75 kept results "never beyond 7 d", which removes undelivered results too, while C2's test said "undelivered results are never evicted". → E75 row and §1.5 SC-4 retention: pressure eviction never removes an undelivered result; age expiry removes any result at 7 d; the job tombstone records the cause (`evicted` or `expired`) and await answers `result_evicted` or `result_expired`. C1, C2 and C3 tests reworded and extended; §1.2 OQ2 text aligned. |
| P2 | Sanitized phased-SSH errors | **Accepted, widened** | `x/crypto/ssh` `Session.Start` returns `ssh: command <cmd> failed` (`~/go/pkg/mod/golang.org/x/crypto@v0.52.0/ssh/session.go:286-288`), and the signed wire command is `SSHGATE_SIG:<sig>:<payload> <cmd>` (`src/sigwire/payload.go:63,89`). It is wrapped today into MCP errors at `run.go:376` and `run_batch.go:274`, a pre-existing leak. → E28: `RunPhased` returns only typed, command-free errors, and raw errors reach a debug log only after sanitizing. Every signed dispatch (run, run_batch, revoke_server, update_gate with stdin, both transfer legs) goes through `RunPhased` after A13b. A13a, A13b and C2 tests cover MCP errors, result files, reports and logs. §1.3 item 19; ledger C-27. |

**The binding owner rule, re-applied (not a review finding).** §1.2 is reworked so the build carries no amendment hook at all. r3 had reserved a `requested_validity_seconds` draft field for OQ1; r4 removes it (E26). A requester-chosen validity is still refused, now by the strict decode of an unknown field. OQ1 would add the field as an omit-when-absent member without changing any existing hash. OQ1 and OQ2 are each stated as: ratified behaviour (built), proposed amendment (not built), cost if adopted, and a recommendation for the owner. OQ3 keeps its non-amending build (a).

**Found while writing §1.5 (not raised by the review).**
- r3's local caps allowed 256 live records of up to 256 KiB each (64 MiB) inside a 32 MiB file cap. → live bytes ≤24 MiB (E54, SC-1.R).
- "Steps that reached `Start`" was ambiguous for an exec request rejected inside `Start`, which E28 classes `failed_not_started`. → `executed_mask` is defined by step status (SC-1.X, E29).
- Deny-all and cancel snapshots had a lifetime but no crash rule between confirm and the per-item cancels. → §1.5 SC-2.D, with D1a and D2 resume tests.
- r3's tombstone budget of 512 bytes could not hold the report fields a stub needs. → ≤768 bytes, with the local tombstone file cap raised to 48 MiB, and B1's latency check now covers stub commits.

The review's confirmations (the `/v2` routing correction, the reserved-verb checks, the role-fixture change, the migration-ledger bump and the named frozen-response fixture changes) need no change.

Owner questions after r4: OQ1 and OQ2 are optional amendments, not built and with no hook in the build; OQ3 concerns frozen v1, and the build uses (a).

### 9.1 Independent check of r4 (r4.1, 2026-10-03)

Each independent review 2 finding was re-checked against r4's text, the ratified K-rulings and the code at `fb515f9`. All eight findings and both precision fixes are resolved by r4, each with a contract and a test that would catch the regression. These r4.1 edits close what r4 left open or contradictory:

| # | Gap in r4 | r4.1 change |
|---|---|---|
| 5 (introduced by r4) | SC-1.C applies SC-0.4's lazy checks, whose step (d) records signer-derived `expired_unclaimed`, before E25's answers. Answer 5 then caught it, so a first Claim after the window answered `execution_reported{expired_unclaimed}` and answer 6 (`expired`) was unreachable for a never-claimed record. The executor's SC-4 row "adopts the stated state" has no `expired_unclaimed` job state, so E74's negative `expired_unexecuted` report was never sent, contradicting the A6a "both orders" test and C2's expectations. | E25 answers 5 and 6, SC-1.C: `expired_unclaimed` answers `expired`. SC-4 splits the `execution_reported`/`pruned` row: adopt a client-reported final class; otherwise end `expired_unexecuted` (`error` for `not_executable`) with a negative report. A6a and C2 tests, C2 against the real memory lifecycle. |
| 6 | SC-1.L deleted the legacy record right after the response, yet the tick must later audit the prepared vote as uncounted (A6b, A8 tests). | SC-1.L keeps a reduced entry until S2 completes; growth is bounded by human taps; an unknown handle answers "expired". |
| 6 | SC-0.2 said local legacy v1 keeps a fail-open audit, while SC-1.L and its tests need the legacy vote audit to be fail-closed. | SC-0.2 states that only the v1 response audit stays fail-open; the legacy vote is audit-first. New §1.3 item 20 (owner-visible behaviour change). |
| 2 | Only the MCP client typed `storage_uncertain`; neither the synchronous coordinator (A13b) nor the executor (C2, SC-4) said what to do with it. | SC-4 row and A13b: retry the same request with bounded backoff; never a refusal or not-found. A13b and C2 tests. |
| 2 | B1's "replacement not installed" uncertain case had no seam: `afterStoreUpdate` runs only after a successful replacement. securestate really can return `ErrCommitUncertain` before the callback runs (the pre-transaction directory fsync in `withLock`). | B1 adds a `beforeStoreUpdate` seam. |
| 2 | A10's "exactly one signature set ever exists" is not true: `SigPayload` carries a nonce, so a re-mint after an unlanded `ready` commit produces different bytes. | "exactly one signature set ever leaves the signer". |
| P1 | "Kept at least 24 h after first delivery" and "age expiry at 7 d" had no precedence. | Age expiry wins; pressure eviction needs delivery plus 24 h (SC-4, E75 row). |
| — | E27 still listed "any requested validity" in the draft, contradicting r4's removal of the field (E26, §1.2). | E27 corrected. |

Owner questions are unchanged: OQ1 and OQ2 are optional amendments, not built and with no hook in the build; OQ3 concerns frozen v1, and the build uses (a).

## 10. r5 redesign: disposition (2026-10-03)

### 10.1 Why r5 redesigns instead of patching

independent review 3 (X-High, reviewing r4.1, review note 3-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)) returned NO-GO with 7 findings, after 11 and 8 in the first two rounds. `STATE-MODEL-76-65.md` mapped all 30 review items onto 41 cross-record obligations and found that five seams had drawn a finding in every round (executor evidence, the execution report, the vote protocol, retention, identity and ownership), generated by seven structural properties: many writers per fact (G1), several copies of one outcome (G2), identities minted at different hops (G3), memory capabilities shadowing durable decisions (G4), many durable intermediate states (G5), reads that write (G6), and three regimes in one record (G7). The owner's stop rule fired, and three architectures were proposed and judged.

### 10.2 How the three proposals compared, and what was grafted

| Criterion | P1 Signer ledger | P2 Event logs | P3 Write-once facts |
|---|---|---|---|
| K-rulings (hard gate) | **Fails in substance** (below) | Passes | Passes |
| Removes the reviewed defect classes (G1–G7) | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Durable record types (r4.1 has 13) | about 6 | about 7, plus a view table | 3 |
| Recovery is easy to reason about | Medium: recovery spans two processes per step | Medium: chains, compaction, forgettable payloads | High: one reconciler per owner, over write-once slots |
| Build size | Medium | Largest (a framework) | Smallest |
| Frozen v1 and current code | Good | Worse: local v1 becomes durable; the hosted detail GET writes | Best: its MAC also closes the hosted-v1 gap between consuming a challenge and `SubmitVote` |

**Why P1 fails the K gate:** K10 says the executor "owns the execution journal" and the "atomic job claims", and P1 moved the authoritative execution state, including spec §6.2's "durably write executing", to the signer. Every step would cost one signer write, which with `backend=hosted` is an HTTPS round trip plus an SQLite commit out of each step's 60 s window (K16), and a signer outage would stop the batch. With no delivery tracking, budget pressure could evict an undelivered result, weakening K11 bullet 4.

**Verdict: P3, with grafts.**
- *From P1:* the pure function set (Decide, View, Next, Class) in `approvalplan`; the monotonic `now` (r5.1: narrowed to a guard-only high-water mark; stamps are the wall clock, SA-1 rule 3); shedding a record in place to its header instead of deleting it; idempotent edits re-run until confirmed; delivered-card challenge validity with no reattach; hosted derived columns as a same-transaction cache; snapshot items applied as idempotent Cancel commands.
- *From P2:* the property-test regime (deterministic replay, idempotent commands, no re-send or re-dispatch for any crash prefix under a new epoch); and one additive securestate primitive, which r4.1 already needed silently for result eviction.
- *From P1 and P3 together:* `sub_principal`, MCP-minted ids, mint at the first Claim, MAC hosted challenges, `registry.ReadCurrent`, and the agent tools' source of truth (signer List joined with executor jobs; await reads the job or the signer).
- *Rejected from P1:* per-step signer intents, HMAC-derived tokens, an executor without a job journal, budget eviction of undelivered bodies, keyed shards and their secret, a persisted MAC key, durable snapshots.
- *Rejected from P2:* hash chains, forgettable-payload chain semantics, durable legacy and snapshot logs, lifecycle event and view tables, a detail GET that writes, progress reports.
- *Rejected from P3:* separate body blobs (r5 commits bodies with their step result), deleting at `submit_not_after` plus clock skew (r5 keeps +24 h), and a single-snapshot fallback.

### 10.3 Every review finding, and where r5 resolves it

"Structural" means the defect class cannot be expressed in r5's architecture; "rule" means one explicit, tested rule; "kept" means r4.1's resolution stands unchanged.

| Item | Finding (short) | r5 resolution | Where | How |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1.1 | v2 signing path dropped the byte-level reserved-verb guards | `ValidateStepBytes` at every intake, outermost before grant matching, and again at the first Claim | E71, §1.5 SA-4, A1a, A6, A7, A10, A12 | kept (rule) |
| R1.2 | Proxy ownership index not atomic; owners overwritable; index loss | the hosted key includes `sub_principal`, which the stateless proxy sets from `SO_PEERCRED`; no local index exists | E19, E66 (deleted), A12, C-17 | structural |
| R1.3 | Challenge consumed before the vote; stranded approvals; unaudited vote blocks expiry and cancel | a v2 vote is one commit (no intermediate states); hosted v1 keeps the frozen engine and its resume; v1 expiry and cancel CASes ignore unaudited votes | E72, E4c, E7, §1.5 SA-4 | structural |
| R1.4 | Pruning erases outstanding execution and resurrects denied ids | Shed keeps the header and its answers; Delete only settled, audited, past `submit_not_after + 24 h`; Submit refuses a new id after its own window; (r5.1) pruning a superseding grant can no longer revive the older grant (newest-grant mark), and a shed grant keeps its alias, duration and host in the header | E73, §1.5 SA-6.4, SA-9, A6, A10, B1 | rule (r5.1 strengthened) |
| R1.5 | First-non-success aggregation hides `indeterminate` | `AggregateJobClass` | E29 | kept (rule) |
| R1.6 | Result file written, index still `executing` → `indeterminate` | one file per job; a step result commits with its bodies; recovery reads one record | §1.5 SA-3 R2, E74 | structural |
| R1.7 | Negative reports needed a claim; Claim after zeroing undefined | report acceptance allows an empty mask without a claim; Claim is a total function of the View | §1.5 SA-4, E25 | rule |
| R1.8 | No recovery for notification `not_started` | notify step 1 runs from the Submit effect, the tick and Startup | §1.5 SA-4, SA-8, E63 | rule |
| R1.9 | Hosted challenge not session-bound | the MAC covers `session_binding` | E7, A11 | rule |
| R1.10 | Unbounded executor results, hosted rows, challenges, proxy index | one bound and reclamation per store; challenges and the index no longer exist; (r5.1) every bound is on encoded bytes, executor bodies are `[]byte`, orphan temp files are removed at store open | E75, §1.5 SA-3, SA-9, B0 | rule (r5.1 strengthened) |
| R1.11 | OQ1/OQ2 built as defaults; legacy direct path without challenge or label | amendments carry no hook (§1.2); production never reaches the direct path; legacy cards use the shared renderer and a challenge | §1.2, E8e, SA-10 | kept (rule) |
| R1.s1 | `assist_deadline` missing from the hosted schema | it is an R1 header field, inside `record_json` | §1.5 SA-3, A9 | structural |
| R1.s2 | A6a lacked the A4 edge | A4 → A6 | §5 | kept |
| R2.1 | Recovery resumed a batch after an abort status | `JobView`/`JobNext` is one function live and in recovery, finality first; (r5.1) single-flight per job, and a current-epoch marker without a result is in flight, so a tick or nudge during a live step never dispatches it again | §1.5 SA-5, E29, E74, C2, C-34 | structural + rule (r5.1) |
| R2.2 | `ErrCommitUncertain` treated as a refusal | an uncertain commit is a crash of the store's owner (poison and reopen under a new epoch; executor exits; hosted re-reads) | §1.5 SA-8, E18, B1, C1, A10 | structural |
| R2.3 | Assist completion raced the multipart burst | one actor per record; the card is sent only after the parts commit, at the View's revision; edits only to delivered cards | §1.5 SA-4, E48, E63, A6, A8 | rule |
| R2.4 | A claimed record settled ten minutes after its window while still running | `awaiting_report` until `report_horizon`; reports accepted while the header exists | §1.5 SA-6.5, SA-9, E73 | rule |
| R2.5 | Contradictory report matrix; progress cutpoint | one final report, `report_sequence` 1; no progress reports | §1.5 SA-4, E25, E28 | structural |
| R2.6 | Local legacy deadline vs prepared-vote recovery (K12) | local v1 is an in-memory `LegacyRequest` with today's first-of semantics, not a lifecycle record | §1.5 SA-10, E8b | structural |
| R2.7 | Hosted ceiling counted tombstones, so tombstoning could not lower it | Delete removes rows; both ceilings counted inside the Submit transaction; `queue_full` when only protected rows remain | §1.5 SA-9, E75, A10 | rule |
| R2.8 | A7's acceptance needed A8's notifier | A8 is the first writer of signer `main.go` | §3 A7, A8, §5 | kept |
| R2.p1 | Seven-day expiry vs "undelivered never evicted" | pressure never removes undelivered bodies; age removes any body at 7 d | §1.5 SA-9, E75 | kept (rule) |
| R2.p2 | `Session.Start` errors carry the signed command | `PhasedError` with fixed strings; sanitized debug log | E28, A13a, A13b, C2 | kept (rule) |
| R3.1 | `claimed` recovery ignored a durable check-failure final file | a check failure is a step result; `JobNext` checks finality before any dispatch | §1.5 SA-5, E74, C2 (exact-cut test) | structural |
| R3.2 | Universal audit-first contradicted prepare-first votes | one rule: events commit in the record's outbox and are flushed after; signatures wait for the flush | §1.5 SA-7, E23, E72 | structural |
| R3.3 | Stub close wrote `unreported` over an accepted final report | the final report is a write-once slot; `unreported` is a view, shown only while the slot is empty | §1.5 SA-4, SA-6.2, E25 | structural |
| R3.4 | Report answered `pruned` before checking the owner | The owner scope is part of the storage key and the load comes first; there is no lazy work before it; (r5.1) operator surfaces, which have no owner key, address v2 records by record handle and refuse ambiguous ids | §1.5 SA-2, SA-6.6, C-28, C-37 | structural (machine plane) + rule (operator plane, r5.1) |
| R3.5 | Grant activation had no lifecycle | a grant is its record: id = `request_id`, expiry from the decision, liveness a view, revoke a slot, death with the epoch; (r5.1) supersession from the newest-grant mark, which pruning never lowers; per-principal coverage; no v1 bridge | E9, E24, §1.5 SA-6.4, C-29, C-36 | structural + rule (r5.1) |
| R3.6 | Registry `Snapshot()` failed open through the last-known cache | `registry.ReadCurrent` reads and validates the file on every step and errors on any failure | A13a, §1.5 SA-5, C-30 | rule |
| R3.7 | No idempotency for `exec_submit`; a lost reply loses the id | the MCP mints the `request_id`; `exec_submit` is idempotent on it and `input_hash`; (r5.1) an id minted by one MCP process cannot be known to another, so a dead MCP still leaves the §1.3 item 25 residual, now with agent guidance | §1.5 SA-2, SA-5, C1, C3, C-31 | rule (narrowed; r5.1 reclassified) |
| F-1 | The executor's report sequence was not persisted | `report_sequence` ≡ 1 and the body is a canonical function of the job record; (r5.1) `result_digest` comes from per-step digests that outlive the bodies, so the body stays stable after the 7 d body removal | §1.5 SA-3 R2, SA-5, E38 | structural |
| F-2 | Claim's tombstone-first answer; lazy work before the owner check | no tombstones and no lazy writes; owner-scoped load first | §1.5 SA-1 rule 3, SA-6.6 | structural |
| F-3 | The agent tools had no source of truth | List = signer List joined with executor jobs; await = the job, else signer Get | §1.5 SA-5, C3 | rule |
| F-4 | `revoke_grant` did not reach records approved under the grant | the first Claim re-checks grant liveness (`grant_not_live`) | E9, §1.5 SA-4 | rule |
| F-5 | Hosted audit marks rested on a non-durable sink | on hosted the committed row is the audit fact; stderr is a projection | E23, §1.5 SA-7 | rule (accepted sink) |
| F-6 | `unreported` vs the executor's known result, permanently | narrowed: a late report is accepted while the header exists and replaces `unreported` | §1.5 SA-4, §1.3 item 17 | rule (narrowed) |

**Counts (r5.1).** Of the 30 review items, 10 are closed structurally; 3 are structural with an r5.1 rule for the part the structure left open (R2.1 live dispatch, R3.4 operator plane, R3.5 supersession); 9 are closed by one explicit rule (R1.4 and R1.10 strengthened in r5.1); 1 is narrowed (R3.7, reclassified from structural); and 7 keep r4.1's resolution (R1.1, R1.5, R1.11, R1.s2, R2.8, R2.p1, R2.p2). r5 counted 14 structural and 9 rules; critique pass 1 showed that four "structural" claims were not fully closed and two rules needed strengthening (§12). Of STATE-MODEL's six found-here items, two are structural, three are rules, and one (F-6) is narrowed, not removed. Of STATE-MODEL's 41 obligations, about 17 remain as explicit rows (§1.5 SA-11); the rest hold by construction or disappear with their record.

## 11. Before A1a: critique pass 1, the X-High review, and owner items

### 11.1 What critique pass 1 must check against the code (read-only, before any build)

**(r5.1) Done.** Two critiques ran these checks; every result and the fix it led to is in §12 (items 1–10 map to critique 1's items 9–18). The list stays as the record of what was asked.

1. **`securestate` additions (B0):** that `ListNames` and `Remove` fit the existing pinning, flock and `validateDataName` model (`internal/securestate/store_linux.go:186-240,370-438,542-557`), and that an unlink followed by a failed directory fsync is correctly `ErrCommitUncertain`.
2. **Hosted mint inside the Claim transaction (E21):** `Service.SignApproved` (`pkg/signerkit/hosted_sign.go:56`) inside a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction on the `_txlock=immediate` DSN (`sqlite.go:192`), including the busy timeout and the signing cost under the write lock.
3. **`ExpireLegacy`/`CancelLegacy` bypassing the unaudited-vote barrier (E4c):** the exact behaviour of a `SubmitVote` that loses the race (`approval_engine.go:212-281`, `persistedOutcome` returning `DecisionPending` for an `error` row) and what `handleVote` then answers; that no signature is persisted and `/v1/poll` reports `error`.
4. **The MAC check before `SubmitVote` (E7):** the new order against `human_handlers.go:117-131,723-790` and every named test change; and whether "one vote per operator" plus an unstored MAC meets K15's "random one-time vote challenge" (§1.5 SA-12 argues it does; the critic decides).
5. **Telegram offset-0 redelivery under derived validity (SA-8):** `telegram.go:339` and `handleCallback` (`:455-473`); that a redelivered tap is judged at processing time and that a tap made during downtime on a still-valid card counting once is acceptable under K15 and spec §6.1.
6. **B1's rewrite and startup cost against the p99 ≤250 ms gate:** per-commit cost (one file, three fsyncs) and the startup load of 65 536 files; whether the 10 s startup bound is right.
7. **Grant semantics left as today:** v2 grant coverage stays per alias across principals, as v1 grants are today (`daemon.go:1456-1500`); confirm nothing in K17 or L7 requires per-principal grants.
8. **Executor write volume:** a worst-case job rewrites its file once per step (§1.5 SA-3 R2; C2 measures it); confirm this is acceptable against the 256 MiB budget and disk wear.
9. **`registry.ReadCurrent` (A13a):** that reusing `Load`'s checks (`servers.go:97-196`) on every step is enough, and that an unreadable registry should end the step as `stale_target` rather than wait.
10. **The notifier's `status_edits` set (E49, SA-4 step 4):** that one best-effort edit per display state is bounded and never resends a review burst.

Then one **independent review of the r5.1 contract** (§1.5 with the rewritten errata and §12), read-only, following the reviewer recipe. Fix every NO-GO and re-review. A1a starts only after GO.

**(r5.3) Critique pass 1 of r5.3, against the code, read-only, before the X-High re-review:**
1. **Clock-first against hosted's mint outside the transaction (E21):** the pre-minted set is discarded whenever Clock or SA-1 rule 3.2 refuses inside the Claim transaction, and no new work runs under the write lock (`pkg/signerkit/hosted_sign.go:56`, `sqlite.go:192`).
2. **The OA-5 interplay (X1):** the revision check is judged after the Vote's own Clock pass, on both planes, and a Deny on a stale local card is refused like an Approve (SA-6.1); hosted's MAC rule already agrees.
3. **The `internal/elapsed` closure:** `go list -deps` of sshgate-mcp, signer-telegram, signer-server and the executor gains no module beyond `golang.org/x/sys/unix`; `verify-gate-closure` is unaffected; `unix.ClockGettime(unix.CLOCK_BOOTTIME, …)` works under the user and system units. (r5.3, critique pass 1) The signer's system unit does set `SystemCallFilter=@system-service` (`scripts/install.sh:206`), and x/sys `ClockGettime` is a raw `SYS_CLOCK_GETTIME` syscall (`zsyscall_linux.go:585-586`), so the filter applies; it allows the call, because `clock_gettime` is in `@default`, which `@system-service` includes (`systemd-analyze syscall-filter @default`). The executor's user unit sets no filter today; C4 keeps `@default` in any filter it adds.
4. **The admission Clock pass (X5):** its commits run before counting, each for one record, under the same store lock; on hosted, each in an earlier transaction, before the counting `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`.
5. **Re-derive the judged time model's scenarios** ((r5.3, critique pass 1) twelve: S1 to S6, S9 to S13 and S15; S7, S8 and S14 were never defined) from `local-workspace/release-2026-10-02/TIME-MODEL-JUDGE.md` (§12.8) against A1b's harness design, and the paths that now commit (X3): an idempotent re-Submit, a Claim answering `not_ready`, and E7 step 3's transaction.
6. **The inverted view tests (X4)** in A6, A10 and B2, and that no other test still expects a view to lead Clock.

Then the **independent review re-review of the r5.3 contract** (§1.5, the changed errata, §12.8 and Appendix L), read-only. A1a starts only after GO.

**(r5.4)** That re-review (independent review 5) returned NO-GO with 7 findings. r5.4 folds all 7 (§12.9). The next independent review re-review is of the r5.4 contract (§1.5, the changed errata E8, E10, E23, E28, E50, E61, E74 and E75, §12.9, and C-49 to C-54), read-only. A1a starts only after GO.

### 11.2 Owner items

- **No new owner decision is needed to build r5.1.** K1–K20 are implemented as ratified (§1.5 SA-12); every departure from r4.1 is either a spec-body erratum (E21, E25, SA-3 R2) or an owner-visible consequence listed for information in §1.3 items 15–32.
- The owner should read §1.3 items 21–26, which are new in r5: grants reach approved-but-unclaimed work; an uncertain commit drops every grant; no card reattach; snapshots are lost on restart; the accepted residual of a second request when the MCP dies after `exec_submit`; hosted v1 expiry and cancel no longer wait for an interrupted vote. Item 16 restores today's local v1 behaviour, withdrawing r4.1's item 20.
- (r5.1) The owner should also read §1.3 items 27–32, new in r5.1: a v2 grant belongs to the uid that requested it and an old (v1) MCP never runs under it; the grant-covered throughput limit; hosted v2 rows kept by default (`--approval-max-age` off); clock errors fail closed; AI advisory on legacy cards; operators name v2 requests by handle. Each is the conservative default, and none waits on an answer. Two of them are settings the owner may later change: hosted v2 retention (set `--approval-max-age`) and, outside this build, a gate-side future-timestamp check (§1.4 item 5, with #22 or Phase E).
- OQ1 and OQ2 are unchanged: optional amendments, not built, with no hook in the build. (r5.2) OQ3 is ruled (b) by the owner and built (A7 hunk 5, §1.3 item 33).
- **(r5.3) Owner items from the time redesign.** None blocks the build; each has a default that is built.
  - **OI-4 (§1.3 item 25): a second job after the MCP dies.** If the MCP dies after `exec_submit` reached the executor but before it replied, the agent's retry creates a second request, and under a live grant both run without a tap. **Owner ACCEPTED (2026-10-03).** An agent holding a live grant can already re-issue the same command at will, so this adds no capability. Without a grant, the second request shows a second card and needs its own tap. The agent guidance of §1.3 item 25 stays. The alternative, an agent-supplied idempotency key, would change the tool surface (K9) and is not built.
  - **R-2: deleted ids.** After both stores delete an id, a step back of more than a day lets its exact bytes in as a new request, which needs a tap or a live grant. **Recommendation: accept as a residual.** Closing it takes a durable per-store floor (the largest deleted `submit_not_after`, committed before each Delete): a migration-7 table on hosted, a new name in the local store grammars, and a new refusal mode in which an RTC reset refuses every new id until NTP syncs. That cost buys nothing that a same-uid holder of the bytes could not get by submitting afresh.
  - **The gate future bound (§1.4 item 5).** Schedule it with #22's gate release, at 60 s or 300 s.
  - **The v1 grant suspend bug (§1.4 item 6).** It is live in shipped signers; it needs a patch-release decision.
  - **(r5.6, critique of the fold) OI-5: the 256 quorum ceiling at startup (§1.3 item 37).** No K-ruling requires it. Every v2 record freezes the server's quorum and its vote set is sized from it (SA-3), so a quorum above 256 cannot be frozen. **Built default: the signer-server refuses to start with `--required-approvals` above 256, in every mode.** Who it affects: a deployment that serves `--ui` with more than 256 eligible operators (the roster check, `main.go:845-866`; policy mode already refuses above 256), or one that passes such a value without `--ui`, where no human plane exists and the value decides nothing today. The alternative, refusing only v2, would leave the v2 routes mounted and refuse every v2 Submit at runtime, or unmount them so clients see a misleading `signer_incompatible`; a loud startup refusal was judged better. Nothing waits on an answer.
  - **Behaviour changes to see (§1.3 item 30):** reads can lag a deadline by up to one tick; a clock step back no longer kills grants, and a forward step ends them early; pending requests stretch under any step back; a clock corrected by more than 5 s strands approvals not yet claimed, and the next signed step of an async job restarted after the correction ((r5.3, critique pass 1) that step ends `expired_unexecuted` and the rest of the job does not run, E29).
