## 12. Dispositions: critique pass 1 (r5.1), independent review 4 (r5.2), the time redesign (r5.3), independent review 5 (r5.4), independent review 6 (r5.5), independent review 7 (r5.6), independent review 8 (r5.7) and independent review 9 (r5.8), 2026-10-03

Two critiques checked r5 against the code at `53c2ed6`, read-only. Both returned GO-WITH-FIXES: critique 1 with 2 blockers, 6 majors and 14 minors (its minors 9–18 are the ten checks of §11.1), critique 2 with 1 blocker, 5 majors and 5 minors. Every item was re-checked against the code and the ratified spec before it was folded in. None was rejected. Where both raised the same issue it is folded once. The state architecture of §1.5 stands; each fix is a rule inside it, marked "(r5.1)". Code cites were read at `53c2ed6`; the working tree also carries the separate `src/gate/confine` lane's edits (`Makefile`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `src/gate/**`), none of them in a file this plan cites except `Makefile`, whose cite is pinned to `53c2ed6`.

### 12.1 Critique 1 (GO-WITH-FIXES)

| # | Finding | Verdict | Checked against | Where folded |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Blocker:** `JobNext` had no rule for an in-flight step and was not single-flight, so a tick or waiter nudge during `RunPhased` re-picked and re-dispatched the running step | **Accepted** | SA-1 rule 4 runs `JobNext` after every commit, every tick and every nudge; SA-5 step 2 caught only markers from another epoch; E29 defined `executing{k}` but SA-5 never read it | SA-1 rule 4 (single-flight I/O work), SA-5 (one actor per job; step 3 "in flight"; `already_marked` never reaches `Start`), E29, E74, SA-8, SA-11 (Marker's repeat answer); A1b, C2 (10 ms nudge storm during a 5 s step), C5; new C-34; §10.3 R2.1 |
| 2 | **Blocker:** grant supersession scanned surviving records for the same alias, but the alias lived in the sheddable plan, so shedding or deleting a revoked or expired superseder revived the older grant | **Accepted** | SA-3 header had no alias; SA-6.4's supersession rule; SA-9 Shed and Delete; B1's stress profile | SA-3 (grant fields in the header), SA-6.4 (newest-grant mark per `(principal, alias)`, raised in commit order, never lowered by Revoke, Shed or Delete, empty at each epoch because every earlier grant is dead), SA-4 Revoke, SA-9, E9; A1b and A6 out-of-order-then-shed tests; C-36; §10.3 R1.4, R3.5. The mark is not "rebuilt at Startup" as suggested: a new epoch kills every grant, so starting empty is exact |

### 12.3 Found while folding (not raised by either critique)

- **R2 bodies must be bytes, not strings** (12.1 #4): a correctness defect, not only a size one.
- **A clock refusal must not cost a step its result** (12.2 #1): r5.1 refuses only commands that write a stamp, so a result, Flush, Report or Claim still commits during a clock step back.
- **Idempotency property with audited duplicates** (12.1 #8): auditing every duplicate means a repeated Vote appends a noise event, so SA-11's idempotency is stated up to noise events, and Marker's repeat answers `already_marked`.
- **`live` narrowed for reported records** (12.2 #11), which also keeps synchronous grant-covered writes from consuming live slots for their whole window.
- **The approved-but-unreleased synchronous outcome** (12.1 #5): when the budget ends while Claim still answers `not_ready` (a failing sink), the call returns `approval_pending` with wording that says the request was approved, so the Denial set does not grow.
- **A `Makefile` cite** had drifted in the working tree because of the separate confine lane; §1 cites stay pinned to `53c2ed6`.

### 12.4 Owner items after critique pass 1

- **No new owner decision is needed to build r5.1.** No fix changes a K-ruling; four make the build match a K-ruling it had drifted from (K8 digest, K12 TS, K15 audit, K19 error).
- **For information, with conservative defaults (§1.3 items 27–32):** v2 grants are per uid and never used by v1 `sign`; the grant-covered throughput limit; hosted v2 rows are never deleted unless `--approval-max-age` is set; clock errors fail closed; local legacy cards keep the AI advisory summary; operators name v2 requests by handle.
- **Settings the owner may change later, neither blocking:** hosted v2 retention (set `--approval-max-age` to delete old rows; the default keeps them, as v1 rows are kept today); and a gate-side future-timestamp bound (§1.4 item 5), a gate change that belongs with #22 or Phase E.
- **Next:** the independent review of the r5.1 contract (§11.1), read-only. A1a starts only after GO. (r5.2) Done: independent review 4 returned NO-GO, folded in §12.5.

### 12.5 independent review 4 disposition and the OQ3 ruling (r5.2, 2026-10-03)

independent review 4 (read-only, against r5.1 and the code at `0c2ac0c`; review note 4-plan-76-65 (not in the repository)) returned NO-GO with 9 findings, 3 of them P1. Each finding was checked against this plan's text and the code at `4314626`, whose `internal`, `pkg`, `src/signer` and `src/mcp` trees are byte-identical to `53c2ed6`. **All 9 hold. None is rejected.** Every fix is a rule inside §1.5. The three record types (R1, R2, R3) stay, as do the write-once slots, the pure views, and one commit function and one reconciler per owner. **No durable record type is added.** Five fields are added inside existing records: `rationale_redacted` in the R1 header; `assist_bound` in R1's decision slot; the `window_closed` reason in R1's mint slot; the `refused` codes in R2's `report_result`; and `submit_not_after` in the `exec_submit` input. The store ownership lock is a held file descriptor, not a file.

Startup applies the same floor from the largest loaded stamp (per record since the fold critique, §12.7). Claim answers `expired` for `window_closed` and for a repeat claim whose bytes are erased | A1b and A6: grant approved 12:00 for 1 h, step back at 13:01 to 12:54, then a Submit → no `grant:` decision and no signature; also a request shown expired refuses a vote, an unclaimed closed window answers `expired`, and erased bytes answer `expired`. **Checked for consistency.** Every changed rule was searched for where else it is restated, and each such place now agrees: the clock reset (SA-1, SA-11, A1b, A6, §1.3 item 30); the R2 bound (SA-3, SA-9, E56, E75, A1a, C1, C2); `submit_not_after` (SA-2, SA-3, SA-5, E73, A13a, C1–C3); the job-file deletion and token removal (SA-9, E75, C2); the edit re-run (SA-4, SA-8, E48, B1, B2); the Claim before every step (SA-5, E74, C2, A13a); v1 grant matching (E8b, E9, E24, SA-6.4, SA-10, §1.2, §1.3, §1.4, A7, A12, D3, §11.2). §7–§9 and §10 are history and keep their r2–r5 wording, including their OQ3 "(a)" notes. Where they disagree, §1.5 and this section win.

**On the stop rule (§4.4).** §4.4 says a fourth round of state findings means returning to `STATE-MODEL-76-65.md`, not adding rows. Review 4 is that fourth round. r5.2's first wording argued that the rule does not fire because no finding shows a seam *between* records. **(r5.2) That argument is withdrawn (§12.7, fold critique 2 #3).** #2 is STATE-MODEL OR-1, idempotency memory across the MCP→executor→signer hops, which SA-11 had listed as an explicit row since r5 and which was still open. #6 is R2's deletion and token lifetime against R1's report acceptance (OR-3, OR-6). #1 spans the grant projection, R1's mint slot and the executor's K16. What remains true is narrower: every fix stays inside §1.5's shape, with no new record type or intermediate state, and three fixes make more state write-once. Whether that is enough is the owner's ruling, not this plan's; §12.6 puts it to him with a recommendation.

### 12.6 Owner items after review 4

- **OQ3 is settled:** (b) is built (§1.3 item 33). No other new owner decision is needed to build r5.2. No fix changes a K-ruling. Two of them make the build match rulings it had drifted from: K8 and spec §8.3 (the advisory binding), and K16 (signature windows bind only signed steps).
- **For information (§1.3 items 30, 33, 34):** a clock step back of more than 5 min drops every grant; v1 grants no longer auto-sign; one process per store directory.
- **(r5.2) §4.4 stop rule: needs the owner's word before the re-review.** Review 4 was the fourth round of state findings. The fold critique (§12.7) then found two more clock-state majors, the mark across restarts and the Startup floor. **Recommendation: apply the rule in scoped form.** Re-score STATE-MODEL §5's 41 obligations against r5.2, with OR-1 checked across all three hops, OD-3 for the new report-owed and edit-supersession states, OS-2 with `window_closed`, and a written lifecycle for the clock mark and the K16 floor, before the the reviewer re-review. That is a write-down, not a redesign. It either confirms that no other hop lacks a horizon, or finds the next defect more cheaply than review 5 would. If the owner waives the rule instead, the waiver is recorded here as his word.
- **Next:** the owner's §4.4 ruling, then (if applied) the scoped re-score, then an independent review re-review of the r5.2 contract (§1.5 with the changed errata and this section), read-only. A1a starts only after GO.

### 12.7 Critique of the r5.2 fold (r5.2, 2026-10-03)

Two critics checked the r5.2 fold read-only. Both returned GO-WITH-FIXES: critique 1 with 2 majors and 5 minors, critique 2 with 3 majors and 5 minors. Three items were raised by both (1.1 = 2.1, 1.3 = 2.5, 1.6 = 2.8 b) and are folded once. Each was checked against this plan and the code at the working tree's `pkg/signerkit`, `internal/securestate`, `src/signer` and `src/mcp` (unchanged from `4314626`). **All 15 hold. None is rejected.** §1.5's shape is unchanged: no record type, slot or intermediate state is added. The clock rules move in three places: the mark survives an in-process reopen, the Startup floor is per record, and the executor gets a memory-only K16 floor at a break.

### 12.8 The r5.3 time redesign and the memo's non-time gaps: disposition (r5.3, 2026-10-03)

**Inputs.** `STATE-REDESIGN-NEEDED.md` (D1–D8, constraints C-a to C-g, gaps OA-5, OD-3, OR-5, the first-window note, owner item OI-4), and the judged time model: three proposals (P1 "durable time", P2 "received time", P3 "edge-anchored") scored against C-a to C-g and attacked with twelve scenarios ((r5.3, critique pass 1) S1 to S6, S9 to S13 and S15; the judge's numbering skips S7, S8 and S14), with P3 the winner (8 / 9 / 9 for correctness, simplicity and cost; the synthesis 9 / 8.5 / 8.5) and grafts from P1 and P2. The judge read the plan and the code at `4314626`; nothing was run, and darwin was not tested. (r5.3, critique pass 1) The three proposals, the judge's verdict and its scenario table are filed verbatim at `local-workspace/release-2026-10-02/TIME-MODEL-JUDGE.md`. This fold re-checked the load-bearing cites at `4314626` and at `366ba07`: `src/gate/verify.go:82-91` (unchanged), the gate's `VerifySigned` caller (moved by #22 phase 1, row F-1), `pkg/signerkit/daemon.go:856`, `:958`, `:1488`, `:1641-1646`, `pkg/signerkit/hosted_sign.go:56`, Go 1.27.1 `runtime/sys_linux_amd64.s:270`, `time/time.go:54` and `runtime/sys_darwin.go:286`, x/sys v0.45.0 `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` (`zerrors_linux.go:736`) and `ClockGettime` (Linux and darwin), and `go list -deps` for `x/sys/unix`. **Shape:** no record type, slot, writer, durable object or wire field is added; the record architecture of §1.5 stands.

**Why P3, in one line each.** P1 closes D8 with a durable floor at the cost of a fourth durable artefact (a migration-7 table, two store grammars and a new refusal mode) against a harm that only a same-uid holder of the bytes could cause. P2 decides durable facts from per-process monotonic deadlines, which is STATE-MODEL G4 again: it fails C-f at every restart (S1, S9) and lets two hosted processes close each other's windows (S10). P3 adds nothing durable and mostly deletes clock code; its weaknesses (S3's suspend gap, an uncapped pair, Go's monotonic reading lost through `Round(0)`, JSON or Unix seconds) are closed by P1's grafts.

### 12.9 independent review 5 disposition (r5.4, 2026-10-03)

independent review 5 of r5.3 (review note 5-plan-76-65 (not in the repository), static and read-only) returned NO-GO with 7 findings (1 P1, 6 P2). Each was re-checked here against the plan text and the code at `366ba07`. All 7 hold, and none needed a change outside §1.5's architecture or r5.3's time model. R1 gains one monotone counter (`renotify_gen`) and one write-once burst field (`gen`); R2 gains one write-once slot (`final_at`); `RunPhased` gains a guard argument. No record type, writer, durable object, wire field or clock-held state is added.

| # | Finding | Verdict and evidence | Fix (where) | Acceptance test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **P1.** The v2 vote contract calls `Decide(policy, votes)` and A10 required only function-level equivalence with `Decide`, dropping the engine's effective-policy binding of `RequiredApprovals` and `Requester` | **Holds.** `SubmitVote` overrides both before `Decide` (`approval_engine.go:197-204`); production's policy carries only the two flags (`main.go:281`, `human_handlers.go:776`); `Decide` floors N to 1 (`approval.go:95-98`) and matches no self-vote for an empty `Requester` (`approval.go:113`). So a straight port approves on one vote and lets the requester approve its own request | `approvalplan.EffectivePolicy(rec, flags)`: N from the header's frozen `required_approvals` (written at Submit from `Config.RequiredApprovals`, as `handlers.go:237`), requester from the record key (`client_id` on hosted, the principal locally), flags from configuration only; the Vote input type has no N or requester field; `sub_principal` never enters the requester (K17) (SA-3, SA-4 Vote, SA-11, SA-12 K15, §2, A1b, A10, A11, L6) | A1b pure corpus; A10 the v2 port of `TestEngine_SelfApproveOnVsOff` (stored N = 2, N frozen across a flag change, self-approval off and on for `sub_principal` empty and `local-uid:1000`, veto, a lying policy struct) and v2 vs `SubmitVote` equivalence; A11 over HTTP; C-49 |
| 2 | **P2.** Notify step 1 depended on "no burst since the last `/renotify`", which no durable fact recorded; after a crash during the parts there is no card to retire, so a confirmed `/renotify` followed by a crash left `Next` unable to tell an authorized resend from a prohibited one | **Holds.** SA-3's record held no re-notify fact; step 5 only retired card entries | Monotone `renotify_gen` in the header; each burst records `gen`; step 1 sends exactly when no burst carries the current `gen`; Renotify raises it and retires cards in one commit, is inert while a resend is already due, and is refused while a current-epoch send is in flight (SA-3, SA-4 steps 1, 2 and 5, SA-6.3, SA-8, SA-11, B2) | A1b crash-prefix model; A6; B2 the cut after the Renotify commit and before the burst's append, landed and not landed, with an empty card set, and the cut after the append; C-50 |
| 3 | **P2.** Executor retention counts from the job's end, which R2 did not store | **Holds.** R2 stored `created_at`, markers' `at` and `first_delivered_at` only; `created_at` and `at` are too early for a long job, and every later commit rewrites the file, so its mtime moves | Write-once `final_at`, the wall `now` of the commit that first makes `JobView` final, set in that commit; ages count from it (SA-1 rules 3.1 and 3.5, SA-3 R2, SA-8, SA-9, E75, C2, Appendix L) | A1b pure; C2 (a) an 8 d step, (b) a kill right after finalization, (c) later report, delivery and token writes and a touched mtime, (d) outcome-ended jobs; C-51 |
| 4 | **P2.** The promised K16 check "immediately before `Start`" could not be expressed: `RunPhased` had no pre-start hook, and the transport loads the key, dials, handshakes and opens a session before `Start` | **Holds.** `src/mcp/ssh/client.go:89,117,145`, with dial and handshake bounded only by the 30 s `Client.Timeout` (`:84-87`); `Session.Start` sends the exec request (`x/crypto@v0.52.0/ssh/session.go:278`) | `RunPhased(…, preStart func() error)`, called after `NewSession` and immediately before `Session.Start`; a refusal sends no exec request and returns `guard_refused`, which the runner records as `expired_unexecuted`; the runner's guard is the K16 re-check on the held pair (E10, E28, E74, SA-1 rule 3.4, SA-5 step 6, sync coordinator, A13a, A13b, C2, Appendix L) | A13a a delayed handshake and a delayed session open, each with only the wall half and then only the elapsed half moved: zero exec requests and `expired_unexecuted`; A13b sync; C2 through the executor, with no re-dispatch after a restart; C-52 |
| 5 | **P2.** Notify step 2 committed only `msg_id`, while SA-6.3 reads `outcome`, so a delivered card read `sending` for ever and `uncertain` after any restart | **Holds** (plan-internal: SA-4 step 2 against SA-6.3 and SA-8) | Step 2 commits `msg_id` and `outcome = sent` in one commit; SA-6.3, SA-8 and E50 read only bursts and send entries (`first`, `replacement`), never edit entries (SA-3, SA-4 step 2, SA-6.3, SA-8, E50, B1) | A1b; A6 and A8 Get shows `sent` after delivery; B2 `sent` before and after a restart, also with an unconfirmed edit entry pending; C-53 |
| 6 | **P2.** E8b's "a tap after resolution records nothing" contradicts K15's "duplicates … audited", which E7 applies to v1 decisions and E61 to every duplicate | **Holds.** Today's handler answers "expired or already resolved" and writes nothing (`telegram.go:484-490`), and the legacy teardown keeps no trace of the request (`telegram.go:762-795`) | A bounded memory-only tombstone per resolved `LegacyRequest`; every non-resolving tap (a duplicate, an opposite replay, the `sync.Once` loser, a post-deadline tap, a stale challenge, an unknown handle) writes one fail-open `legacy_vote_replay` or `legacy_vote_refused` line through the `AuditWriter`, capped like the noise fold; the answers, the v1 response and the first-of semantics are unchanged (E8b, E23, E61, SA-3 memory list, SA-10, SA-12, §1.3 item 16, A6, A7, A8, C-25) | A6 duplicate approve, opposite replay, race loser, post-timeout, stale, evicted and post-restart taps, the 32-line cap, a failing writer; A7 at the socket; A8 against the fake Bot API with the production adapter; C-54 |

**Checked for consistency.** After the fold, a search for `records nothing`, `no burst since`, `every command is idempotent`, `Decide(policy, votes)`, `commit its \`msg_id\``, `after the job ends`, `job ended`, `just before \`Start\``, `immediately before \`Start\``, `RunPhased(ctx` and `burst or card entry` finds each one either in history (§7 to §12.8.1), in text marked "(r5.4)" that states the new rule beside it, or in owner-facing summaries (§1.2 OQ2, §1.3 item 12) whose plain "after the job ends" now has a stored meaning (`final_at`). #### 12.9.1 Critique of the r5.4 fold: disposition (r5.4, 2026-10-03)

Two critics checked r5.4 against the code at `366ba07` (both GO-WITH-FIXES: 16 findings, 4 major and 12 minor; three are shared between the critics, so 13 are distinct). Each was re-checked here against the plan text and the code. All hold and are fixed in place inside §1.5's architecture and r5.3's time model; none needed anything outside them. Nothing durable is added: no record type, slot, counter, writer or wire field. The fixes are rules (a fail-open refusal audit line, a noise commit, a cache-only refresh, guard and view rules) and one stamp choice for R1 ages from an existing stamp. "C1-n" and "C2-n" name critic 1's and critic 2's findings in order.

#### 12.10.1 Critique of the r5.5 fold: disposition (r5.5, 2026-10-03)

Two critics read r5.5 against the plan and the code at `366ba07`, and both returned GO-WITH-FIXES: critic 1 raised 8 issues (1 major, 7 minor) and critic 2 raised 6 minor issues. Three pairs overlap (C1-2 = C2-4, C1-3 = C2-3, C1-6 = C2-2), so there are 11 distinct findings. Each was re-checked here against the plan text and the code. All 11 hold, and each is fixed inside §1.5's architecture (three durable records, write-once slots, pure views, one commit function and one reconciler per owner) and r5.3's time model. No record type, durable slot, counter, writer, wire field or clock-held state is added. The additions are: an in-memory retry timer in the notifier actor; three pure functions or constants of the records (`R2FinalCharge`, `OutboxMax` and `ShedMax`); a noise-fold cap of 1 on shed records; one hosted flusher goroutine; and per-instance placement of E26's sentences. No owner item arises. Text changed by this pass is marked "(r5.5, critique of the fold)".

| # | Finding | Verdict and evidence | Fix (where) | Acceptance test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1-1 (major) | An in-epoch edit that ends in a possible send had no driver: "re-runs until confirmed" with no schedule, so the card had no valid challenge until expiry, a restart or `/renotify` | **Holds.** SA-4 step 3, E48 and SA-8 said only that the actor re-runs the edit. `next_due_at` has deadline terms only, and once assist has filled, a pending record's next term is `request_expires_at`. A8's `EditCard` reports a possible send, but only a known failure had a path. SA-6.1 refuses both the revision-0 challenge and the unconfirmed edit's challenge, and no test covered the case. Hosted has no cards (SA-6.3), so this is local only | SA-4 step 3: after a possible send the actor arms an in-memory retry, 1 s doubling to 30 s, while the entry is the latest unretired one, still unconfirmed, and the record is pending; the timer only nudges the actor and dies with the process, after which the other-epoch rule applies; Renotify stops it by retiring the entry. Also E48, SA-8 and SA-11. A per-owner retry list was not chosen, because only the local notifier has cards and the actor already serialises the record | A6: one possible send then success with no other command → confirmed within 2 s, then an Approve tap decides; 70 s of possible sends → backoff spacing, never two in flight, no replacement; `/renotify` and a terminal decision stop the retries. A8 (4b) against the fake Bot API. SA-11 edit-driver property |
| C1-2 = C2-4 | r5.5's mapping of a Claim `expired` after `approved` was written and tested only for `run`/`run_batch`; `revoke_server` (TTL 60) meets the same case | **Holds.** `RevokeTTLSec = 60` (`src/mcp/tools/revoke_server.go:34`). | C1-4 | A final executor job counted at its actual encoded size, but it still grows through `report_result`, `first_delivered_at` and `bodies_removed`; C2 also said a job's share drops to its "header size" | **Holds.** SA-9 and E75 counted a final job at its encoded size; SA-3 R2 commits those slots after finality, and they must not be refused; R2 keeps per-step metadata and both digests after body removal (SA-3 R2) | `R2FinalCharge(job)`: the encoded size plus the encoded maximum of the still-empty post-final slots, a pure function of the record; it is at most the reservation and never rises after finality (SA-9, E75, C2 content); C2's wording becomes "its encoded size without bodies, plus its post-final allowance" | C2 reservation lifecycle: the budget in use equals live reservations plus the finished jobs' `R2FinalCharge`; the post-final commits of 100 finished jobs never raise it and none is refused |

Repeats under the *exact* and *counter only* replay rows append nothing: a repeat Claim, an acked duplicate Report, and a Renotify answered "already pending". Exempting Flush from single flight was not chosen: duplicates are recognisable, but SA-1 rule 4 is binding, and one goroutine costs nothing | A10: 50 concurrent approve POSTs during a retry pass never write an `event_key` twice while the sink works; a 5 s sink stall holds no SQLite lock; the sync flush test reads "the flusher's nudge" |
| C1-8 | For local legacy taps that do not resolve the request, the plan claimed to keep "today's strings" while its tests required "expired" and "already resolved", which today's handler never sends | **Holds.** Today such a tap finds no pending entry, because `resolve` and `timeout` delete it (`src/signer/backend/telegram.go:767,780`), and it is answered "expired or already resolved" (`:484-490`). A tap that loses the `sync.Once` race has already loaded the entry: it is answered with its own verb and edits the footer again (`:508-523`) | E8b: every non-resolving tap is answered "expired or already resolved". The race loser's answer is named as the one deliberate change: today's footer re-edit can show "Denied by …" under an approval, so now it gets the same string and no edit. | C2-1 | Shed released the reservation although a shed record can regrow an outbox (late Report, `invalid_claim`/`invalid_report` noise), and quarantined files had no budget charge | **Holds.** The outbox is sheddable payload (SA-3); E73 accepts a late Report on a shed `awaiting_report` record; SA-6.6 audits `invalid_claim`/`invalid_report` on any found record; SA-8 counts quarantined files only against the live caps. §12.10's "guaranteed bound" overstated it | SA-9: on a shed record the noise fold is 1, and the late Report's slot is the only one it can still fill (decision and mint are filled; Revoke touches only live grants). So each shed record is ≤ `ShedMax`, and shed records together are ≤ 65 536 × `ShedMax`, a bound separate from the budget. A quarantined file counts as a full record at its encoded size until it is removed (SA-3, SA-7, SA-9, A1a, B1). §12.10's sentence now covers full records only | A1a `ShedMax` proof; B1: 40 invalid Claims and Reports plus a late Report on a shed record with a failing writer stay within `ShedMax`; a 400 KiB quarantined file lowers the free budget; Startup rebuilds the counter; SA-11 shed property |

### 12.11 independent review 7 disposition (r5.6, 2026-10-03)

independent review 7 of r5.5 (review note 7-plan-76-65 (not in the repository), static and read-only) returned NO-GO with 3 findings (all P2). Each was re-checked here against the plan text and the code at `366ba07`. All 3 hold, and each is fixed inside §1.5's architecture (three durable records, write-once slots, pure views, one commit function and one reconciler per owner) and r5.3's time model. No record type, durable slot, counter, writer, durable object or clock-held state is added. The additions are one closed-set wire code (`busy`) with its typed client error, two constants (`MaxRequiredApprovals`, `VoteSlack`), four pure functions (`VoteCap`, `VoteRoom`, `ExecutedOrdinals`, `Partial`), one report-acceptance check and one startup check. One owner-visible consequence arises (§1.3 item 37); it needs no ruling.

| # | Finding | Verdict and evidence | Fix (where) | Acceptance test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **P2.** Hosted "busy, retry" has no wire or retry contract: E21 promises a retryable 503 for `SQLITE_BUSY` at `BEGIN`, but A5's closed code set has no `busy`, SA-5 makes every unlisted code definitive, and A13b retries only `storage_uncertain` and `not_ready` | **Holds.** The DSN takes the write lock at `BEGIN` with a 5 s busy timeout (`pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/sqlite.go:192`, `_txlock=immediate`, `busy_timeout(5000)`), and the policy worker, v1 handlers and maintenance writers share the file, so contention is reachable. E21 and A10 named the answer; A5's list, SA-5's split and A13b's rule omitted it. A literal build either violates the wire or ends the job with `error{busy}` | `busy` joins A5's closed set: HTTP 503, `Retry-After: 1`, envelope `{approval_proto:1, ok:false, error:{code:"busy", …}}`, meaning not applied (so resending the same bytes is always safe, unlike `storage_uncertain`); detected as modernc `*sqlite.Error` primary code 5 at `BeginTx` or on a plain read; a BUSY at `COMMIT` stays `storage_uncertain`; every v2 machine route and the human-plane POSTs answer it with no state change; the worker and the flusher skip the row until the next pass; the client's typed `ErrBusy`; A12 passes it through (and audits it as SA-7 requires); transient in SA-5's split; retried by A13b inside the budget with the same Submit tuple, claim token and report body (E21, E26, SA-5, SA-7, A5, A10, A12, A13b, C2, Appendix L.2) | A5 golden for the envelope, `ErrBusy` distinct from `ErrStorageUncertain` and `ErrResponseLost`, a bare 503 a transport error; A10 on a file-backed store, a second connection holding `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for 6 s across Submit, Claim and Report in turn: 503 `busy`, the row byte-unchanged and no signature released, then the same bytes succeed exactly once; BUSY at `COMMIT` still `storage_uncertain`; vote POST and worker under the lock ((r5.6, critique of the fold) the vote POST case moved to A11, §12.11.1); **A12 end to end through the proxy** (sync coordinator and executor; (r5.6, critique of the fold) A12 now resends by hand at client level, and the coordinator cases moved to A13b and C2, §12.11.1) with a recording transport: each retried Submit, Claim and Report body byte-identical, one record, one signature set, one accepted report, no Denial; A13b `ErrBusy` once on each call for all five tools, and until the budget ends → `approval_pending`; A1b and C2 `busy` transient, never `error{busy}`; C-61 |

| 3 | **P2.** The partial-execution acceptance case contradicts its definition: E29 counts `indeterminate` as executed and defines `partial` as some execution plus an approved step that did not execute, yet A13a expects `partial` for the whole job `[failed read, indeterminate write]` | **Holds** (plan-internal). #### 12.11.1 Critique of the r5.6 fold: disposition (r5.6, 2026-10-03)

Two critics read r5.6 against the plan and the code at `366ba07`, and both returned GO-WITH-FIXES: critic 1 raised 7 issues (3 major, 4 minor) and critic 2 raised 7 (1 major, 6 minor). After overlaps there are 8 distinct issues. Each was re-checked against the plan text and the code. Seven hold and one holds in part; all are fixed in place inside §1.5's architecture and r5.3's time model. No record type, durable slot, counter, writer, durable object or held deadline is added: the Submit-phase bound is a retry bound, not held authority (Appendix L.2), and the assist commit's resend holds a result the job already has in memory.

| # | Finding (critics) | Verdict and evidence | Fix (where) | Acceptance test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-1 | **Major** (C1-1, C2-1 part 1). A12's contention test drives the A13b coordinator and the C2 executor, but `A12 → A13b` is an edge and C2 is in Phase C, so neither exists when A12 merges, and L-core owns neither `src/mcp` nor `src/executor`; A13b pointed the case back to A12 | **Holds.** DAG `A12 → A13b`, `(Phase B end) → C1 → C2` (§5); A12's lane owns `tests/hostedproxy` and the proxy files only | A12 keeps a client-level test that resends by hand through the proxy (recording transport, one record, one signature set, one accepted report, one `refused{busy}` audit row per pass-through) and is the reference harness; A13b owns the sync coordinator through the proxy (`run` and `run_batch`); C2 owns the executor through the proxy (test-only imports, closure unaffected); A13b's pointer to A12 is deleted; C-61's sub-phase list, the A11, A12, A13b, C2, C3 and D2 ledgers and §12.11 row 1 updated | A12, A13b and C2 tests as listed |
| F-2 | **Major** (C1-4, C2-1 part 2, C2-2). The human-plane `busy` work sits in A10, which owns no human handler (the v2 vote route is A11's, cancel and deny-all are D2's); "nothing was committed, so the challenge is still valid" is false under step-up, because `ConsumeTOTPStep` is its own autocommit write before the Vote transaction, and a busy there comes back as 401 "step-up required"; the deny-all confirm applies items one commit at a time, so a whole-request 503 is wrong; the hosted `AssistResult` commit had no `busy` rule | **Holds.** `pkg/signerkit/sqlitestore/operators.go:255-265` (`s.db.ExecContext` upsert), `pkg/signerkit/hosted/auth.go:315-318` (wrapped with `%w`), `auth.go:598-603` (`StepUp` returns it), `human_handlers.go:769-770` (any step-up error → 401); D1a's per-item AdminCancel; E68's single post-commit assist job | E21 *Where* and E7 spell `busy` per transaction for v2: steps 3 and 5 → 503 "busy — retry" before step-up; a `SQLITE_BUSY` from `ConsumeTOTPStep` → 503 "busy — retry with a fresh code" (a write that may have landed, by E21's detection rule), never 401; step 7 after a consumed step → 503 "busy — retry with a fresh code"; v1 unchanged. A11 builds it and `request.js` shows both messages; D2 answers 503 for cancel and snapshot and reports a busy confirm item "not canceled (busy)" while the rest run; A10 resends the `AssistResult` commit with E25's backoff until `assist_deadline`, with no second provider call (critic 1's "drop it" was not taken: it loses a ready advisory and leaves Approve unavailable until the deadline) | A11 (a)–(e) under a held lock and store wrappers; D2 cancel, snapshot and a three-item confirm with item 2 busy; A10 assist commit resent after a 6 s lock, and dropped past the deadline |
| F-3 | **Major** (C1-2, C2-5). Nothing pinned a report's step list to ordinals 1..`step_count`, so a report omitting its `not_run`/`skipped` tail was either refused when its `partial` was computed correctly over the full list, or accepted with a `partial` that disagrees with `JobView` | **Holds.** E38 named only per-step fields; SA-4 Report compared the functions over "the report's steps"; the header already stores `step_count` | E38: one entry per ordinal 1..`step_count`, ascending; unrun steps carry `JobView`'s derived status and no exit code or bytes; the sync coordinator builds the same list. E25 and SA-4 Report check that and E29's shape (a `succeeded`/`failed` prefix that may end in one abort status, then a `not_run` tail after an abort or a `skipped` tail after `failed`) before the class and the two functions, else `invalid_report`; SA-5 step 7 sends that list | A1b: 2-step, misordered, duplicate and ill-shaped reports for a 3-step record refused; `[failed, indeterminate, not_run]`, `[succeeded, failed, skipped]`, the negative report and `[stale_target, not_run, not_run]` accepted; A13b and C2 accept the 3-step and stop-policy reports end to end |
| F-4 | **Major** (C1-3), minor (C2-4). E29 derived statuses only "after the step that ended the job", so a job ended by an `outcome` after steps ran had no status for its unrun steps: step 1 ran, Claim `record_corrupt` before step 2 → `[succeeded]`, `partial: false`, against SA-5, A1b and C2 | **Holds.** E29 step-status bullet; SA-5's definitive split; A1b's and C2's `error{record_corrupt}` cases | E29: for a final job, `JobView` derives every step without a stored result: `skipped` after a stop-policy stop, otherwise `not_run` (after an abort, or when any `outcome` ended the job); the partition paragraph and C-63 read through it | A1b: `[succeeded]` then `error{record_corrupt}` over 3 steps → `[succeeded, not_run, not_run]`, `[1]`, `partial: true`; outcomes before any step → all `not_run`, `partial: false`; C2's case shows `[succeeded, not_run]` and survives a restart |

| F-6 | **Minor** (C1-6, C2-3 part 2). `list_pending_approvals`, `await_approvals` and the `approval_status` join call List and Get, which can now answer `busy`, with no mapping | **Holds.** C3 mapped only an unavailable executor; A5's client never retries | C3: List resent once after 250 ms, Get resent inside the await deadline; then "unknown (signer busy — retry)" per item or column, plus a note when List could not be read; never a tool error. E21 and E29 point to it | C3 fake-client cases for List and Get, once and always |
| F-7 | **Minor** (C1-7). (b) Holds, with corrected evidence.** (a) No K-ruling requires the refusal. Refusing only v2 is rejected: A10 mounts the v2 routes in every mode, so a v2-only refusal means a runtime refusal of every v2 Submit or unmounting the routes, which clients read as a misleading `signer_incompatible`; the affected deployments are `--ui` with more than 256 eligible operators (policy mode already refuses above 256), or a value passed without `--ui`, where no human plane exists. (b) The binary cannot freeze a negative N: `hosted.New` copies only a positive value (`config.go:126-128`) over `NewServer`'s default 1 (`server.go:124`); a direct `NewServer` caller can (`server.go:37-40`) | (a) The refusal stays; §1.3 item 37 says it is a plan choice, and §11.2 files it as owner item OI-5 with its built default. independent review 8 of r5.6 (review note 8-plan-76-65 (not in the repository), static and read-only) returned NO-GO with 4 findings (all P2). All 4 hold, and each is fixed inside §1.5's architecture (three durable records, write-once slots, pure views, one commit function and one reconciler per owner) and r5.3's time model. Nothing needed a change outside them.

The gate classifies the verified inner command itself and runs a write through `execAndAudit`, unjailed, with `reveal` set (`src/gate/cmd/sshgate-gate/main.go:257,278`); the child's output reaches the session (`:289-292`). `readJailDenied` checks only exit 77 and a substring, and its comment says a command can forge the line (`run.go:313-319`). r5.3 cited `main.go:492` ("a revealed read is still jailed"), which holds only when the gate classifies the command as a read | E10: the read-jail exception is narrowed to `unsigned_read`, the only route the gate can never run as a write (`main.go:273-276`; Tier-1 `:182-191`). On every signed route, exit 77 is `failed` and executed whatever stderr says; the line selects the remediation only (`read_jail_unavailable`, not retryable, no retry advice, never "nothing ran"). E28's `failed_not_started` bullet says the same. A13a adds `jobplan.StepOutcome`, the one route-gated mapping both paths use. A13b rewords the class `Summary` (named change). §1.3 item 38, C-64 | A13a: `StepOutcome`'s route × exit × stderr table, plus a `signed_reveal` step that prints the line and exits 77 → `failed`, `executed_ordinals [1]`, and `[unsigned_read with the line, signed_write]` → `[failed_not_started, not_run]` with no exec request for step 2. A13b (sync): a revealed write (`systemctl restart app`) via `run` → `failed`, `executed_ordinals [1]`, `read_jail_unavailable` not retryable, no "did not run", report accepted by the real A6 lifecycle; a revealed read gives the same; (r5.7, critique of the fold) the batch case is a `signed_write` step 1 of a `run_batch`, since no batch can reveal (§12.12.1 F1); the parity row for `TestRun_GateReadJailDeny77` is rewritten. | 2 | **P2.** Hosted version skew has no valid local wire form: A12 had to translate an older hosted server's non-envelope 404 into `signer_incompatible`, which A5's closed code set omits, and the client recognized only legacy `unsupported kind` and an HTTP 404 | **Holds** (plan-internal). A5's error-code list ends at `lifecycle_unavailable` and has no `signer_incompatible`, while SA-5 already listed that code as definitive and A12 named it. The legacy answer is a v1 `respondError` frame (`pkg/signerkit/daemon.go:519`), not a v2 envelope. At `366ba07` an older hosted server answers `/v2/approvals*` with the policy plane's JSON 404 `{"error":"not found"}` when attached (`pkg/signerkit/hosted/server.go:250-252`, `policy_runtime.go:79-93`), or the mux's plain-text 404 | `signer_incompatible` joins A5's closed set: a local-only frame with fixed bytes, sent only by the A12 proxy, definitive, never retried. A12: fixtures of `366ba07`'s answers on all six v2 routes, with and without the policy plane, captured at A0's base → current proxy → A5 MCP client: each kind gets the golden frame and `ErrSignerIncompatible`, with one audit row per call. A13b: the same fixtures through the proxy → sync `run` and `run_batch` → the `signer_incompatible` Denial, no Wait or Claim, zero SSH dispatches |
| 3 | **P2.** A10's acceptance needs A11's routes: the v2 vote POST, approve-POST-driven flushing and `/ui/audit`'s `v2_entries` | **Holds** (plan-internal). A11 owns `human_handlers.go`, the v2 vote routes and `/ui/audit`'s `v2_entries`, and depends on A10 (§5: `A10 → A11`). ### 12.12.1 Critique of the r5.7 fold (r5.7, 2026-10-03)

Two critics read r5.7 against this plan and the code at `366ba07` and returned GO-WITH-FIXES: 8 issues (3 major) and 6 issues (1 major), 10 distinct after overlaps. Each was re-checked against the plan text and the code. All 10 hold, and each is fixed inside §1.5's architecture and r5.3's time model. The fixes add no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object, held deadline or clock-held state. `StepOutcome` gains two pure inputs (the step kind and `earlierExecuted`), which are never stored or sent. E3 pins the bytes of a 404 the approvals plane already answers. §1.3 gains item 39 and widens item 38.

### 12.13 independent review 9 disposition (r5.8, 2026-10-03)

independent review 9 of r5.7 (review note 9-plan-76-65 (not in the repository), static and read-only) returned NO-GO with 3 findings (all P2). Each was re-checked here against the plan text and the code at `366ba07`. All 3 hold, and each is fixed inside §1.5's architecture (three durable records, write-once slots, pure views, one commit function and one reconciler per owner) and r5.3's time model. The fixes add no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object, wire field, held deadline or clock-held state. The stop latch is memory-only, one flag per process, like the store's poison flag, and recovery after a stop uses only the existing crash rules. Nothing needed a change outside the architecture.

| # | Finding | Verdict and evidence | Fix (where) | Acceptance test |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **P2.** Hosted v1 replays of resolved requests are not audited: E7 step 3 answers 409 for a resolved row and audits only v2; the v1 duplicate line is at step 7, which a resolved row never reaches, so an N=1 approve followed by a second approve or a deny is unaudited, and the engine also returns before auditing | **Holds, and wider than stated.** r5.7's step 3 named a `vote_refused` event "For v2" only, and step 5's refusal audit is also "For v2". Today `handleVote` (`human_handlers.go:742`) calls `SubmitVote` and maps `ErrAlreadyResolved` to 409 (`:781-782`); `SubmitVote` returns it at `approval_engine.go:147-149`, before `PrepareVote` and before `Core.Verdict` (`:178`), and again for `PrepareVote`'s `ErrRequestResolved` (`:165-166`). With N=1 the first approve flips the row (`:213-218`), so every replay meets a resolved row. `ListVotes` has no status filter (`sqlitestore/operators.go:473-477`), so the earlier vote is visible on a terminal row. Found while folding: the sink and encoding of r5.1's HTTP-layer line were never stated, and the hosted core's sink writes every `AuditVerdict` as kind `verdict` (`audit_sink.go:309-311`) and `AuditLog` as `approved` or `denied` (`:399-428`), so a replay line carrying `Approved` would read as a second resolution | E7 steps 3, 5 and 7: every authenticated v1 POST refused on a resolved row, at the challenge check, or by `SubmitVote`'s `ErrAlreadyResolved` writes one inert line before today's answer: `vote_duplicate` (the operator holds a listed vote) or `vote_refused{resolved \| expired \| canceled \| stale_challenge \| expired_challenge \| session_mismatch}`. The line is an `AuditVerdict` with `Approval{transition, decision, replay}` through the core's `Verdict`, fail-open, one per POST. E23: such a verdict encodes as kind `verdict_inert` (append-only) and status `approval-<transition>` (`AuditLog`); other verdicts keep their bytes (A6 owns `audit_sink.go`). E61, SA-10 and SA-11's noise row follow. The engine and its test are untouched. C-66 | A11 (kept apart from the pending N-of-M duplicate cases): (a) N=1 approve → approve: 409 bytes unchanged, one `vote_duplicate{approve}`; (b) approve → deny: one `vote_duplicate{deny}`; (c) another operator on the approved row: `vote_refused{resolved}`; (d) after expiry, with the worker paused: `vote_refused{expired}`, twice for two POSTs, and `vote_duplicate` for an operator who voted before the expiry under N=2; (e) after `CancelLegacy`: `vote_refused{canceled}`; (f) stale, expired and cross-session challenges: one line each, no TOTP step; (g) an `ErrAlreadyResolved` race: `vote_refused{resolved}`; (h) a failing sink changes no answer; no refused POST adds a `verdict` line, and `approval_engine.go` and its test have an empty diff. Today SIGTERM ends the signal context (signer `main.go:219`), `srv.Listen` returns (`:321`), and the deferred `svc.Close` runs after (`:288-292`). A Bot API call takes no context and is bounded only by the client timeout (`src/signer/backend/telegram.go:247-263`), so a send in flight at `Close` returns whatever the network gives. The same cancellation reaches the actors at B1's uncertain-commit reopen (harmless there only because the poisoned store refuses the `error` commit, and effects follow only confirmed commits, which the plan never tied to the stop), the assist job (a provider failure is a committed `unavailable`, E68) and the executor's `JobNext` (a dial cut before `Start` could commit `failed_not_started`) | §1.5 SA-8 gains the owner stop, in five steps: intake stops; a memory-only `stopping` latch, set under the store lock, refuses every later commit (`lifecycle_unavailable`, transient; executor `executor_unavailable`); the owner's own effect context, which no signal context derives, is cancelled and the effects joined; anything an effect observes after the latch is an interruption that commits nothing and makes no further external call; and recovery is the crash rules (`uncertain` and `/renotify`, a superseding edit, a repeated status edit, Clock's assist fill, a duplicate-safe re-flush, an orphan marker's `indeterminate`). ### 12.13.1 Critique of the r5.8 fold (r5.8, critique of the fold, 2026-10-03)

Two critics read r5.8 against the code at `366ba07` and returned GO-WITH-FIXES with 19 issues (critic 1: 7; critic 2: 12). Three overlap (C1-2 = C2-1, C1-3 = C2-4, C1-5 = C2-8), leaving 16. Each was re-checked here against the plan and the code. All 16 hold and are fixed in place, marked "(r5.8, critique of the fold)". Every fix stays inside §1.5's architecture and r5.3's time model: no record type, slot, counter, writer, durable object, wire field or held deadline is added. The new state is memory-only (the pool's `closed` flag, like the stop latch), and one audit-sink convention (`Approval.replay`, hosted v2 events through `Call`) is fixed where r5.8 left it unstated.

| C2-2 | An `exec_submit` whose job commit landed was answered `executor_unavailable` at the stop, an `escalate_to_human` Denial inviting a resubmit while the job runs after the restart | **Holds.** C2 item 6 as written; E11 makes `executor_unavailable` a Denial; C3 retries the same id only on `ErrResponseLost` | C2 item 6, SA-8 step 2 and E63: such an `exec_submit` answers with its committed job (`request_id`, `JobView`) at once; `executor_unavailable` only when the latch refused that call's own commit | C2 (e): SIGTERM in E63's poll → the job is answered, not `executor_unavailable`; one signer record; the job runs once after the restart. C5: the same through the MCP, whose output invites no resubmit |
| C2-3 | Interruption was keyed on "its context cancelled" and A8's "returns the context error, which the actor treats as an interruption", so a budget built as a context deadline would read as a stop and leave the request stuck `sending` | **Holds.** A6's `Close` text named the lifecycle's own context, but SA-8 step 4 said "its context" and A8 classified by the error; E50 has the budget select on the context | SA-8 step 4, A6 `Close`, A8 `SendParts` and E50: an effect is interrupted exactly when the latch is set when its call returns; never by error type or a derived context; the budget ending is returned as the budget failure | A6: with no `Close`, a part returning `context.DeadlineExceeded` or `context.Canceled` still gives `error` and INCOMPLETE edits. A8: 429 `retry_after=61` with no stop → budget failure → `error` with INCOMPLETE edits |
| C2-5 | A11 (h) failed the sink for the setup too, but `SubmitVote`'s vote audit is fail-closed | **Holds.** `approval_engine.go:178-190`; `human_handlers.go:785-786` maps it to 500 | (h): the sink fails only after each case's setup, and only for verdicts carrying `Approval` | A11 (h) as reworded |
| C2-6 | C-66 overclaimed (400, 403, 401 are unaudited); SA-11 put hosted v1 under "until the fold" while E7 says no fold; step 3 runs before the role check, so any authenticated user could append lines | **Holds.** E7 step 5 leaves the 400 unaudited; `allowAuth` limits the vote POST only with step-up (`human_handlers.go:763-766`) | C-66 is narrowed to the enumerated refusals by an operator-role session. E7 step 3 writes the v1 line only for an operator-role session (another role's POST is not a vote and keeps today's silent 409, as it keeps today's 403 on a pending row). SA-11's noise row: hosted v1 has no fold and at most one line per POST. **No cap is added**: every line answers one authenticated operator's HTTP request, the bound SA-7 already accepts for local refusal lines, while E8b's cap answers Telegram redelivery from offset 0, which HTTP has no analogue of | A11 (g''): an admin-role POST on a resolved row → today's 409, no line |
| C2-7 | For a v1 row, `stale_challenge` and `session_mismatch` cannot be told apart, so A11 (f) asked for an impossible split | **Holds.** A v1 challenge binds fixed values (E7), so a MAC failure is a session, restart or forgery case with no way to distinguish them | E7 step 5, v1: `expired_challenge` when the MAC verifies and `expires_at` has passed; `stale_challenge` for every MAC failure; `session_mismatch` is v2-only | A11 (f): expired → `expired_challenge`; cross-session, pre-restart and garbled → `stale_challenge`; one line per POST |
| C2-9 | §1.3 item 23 still said only a crash or an uncertain commit makes a send `uncertain` | **Holds.** r5.8's searches covered no §1.3 term | Item 23: a signer stop or upgrade during a send leaves the request pending and `uncertain`, and `/renotify` resends it | none (owner-facing text) |
| C2-10 | At the reopen, the poison's `storage_uncertain` and the latch's `lifecycle_unavailable` competed; an actor whose own commit was uncertain could start the reopen and join itself | **Holds.** Notify steps commit from the actor (SA-4), and B1 injects faults at every command's commit; the plan never said where the reopen runs | SA-8 and B1: the poison and the latch are set in one critical section before `ErrCommitUncertain` returns; the poison's answer takes precedence until the reopen; the reopen runs on the reopen loop's own goroutine | B1: the actor's own `sent` commit uncertain (landed and not) → no self-join (10 s timeout, goleak), no further Bot API call, concurrent callers get `storage_uncertain`, and the burst is `uncertain` after the reopen |
| C2-11 | The executor's join was bounded only for Bot API calls; nothing said cancelling ended `RunPhased` after `Start` | **Holds.** C2 tests an 8-day step; SA-8 step 3 bounded only `tgbotapi` | SA-8 step 3 and C2 item 6: `RunPhased` runs under the effect context, whose cancel closes the SSH client as today's watcher does (`src/mcp/ssh/client.go:133-141`); a dial or handshake ends within `Client.Timeout` (30 s, `:84-87,101-113`). C4 keeps systemd's default 90 s stop timeout | C2: exit within 5 s of SIGTERM in (b)–(e), within `Client.Timeout` + 5 s in (a) |
| C2-12 | §5 had "C2 → C3 (integration tests only)" under a rule that a sub-phase starts only after every predecessor merges, and also said C2 runs in parallel with C3 | **Holds.** Both statements were in §5; r5.8's §0.5 rule made the edge binding | §5 defines a **merge-only** edge (it gates the merge and its acceptance, not the start); C2 → C3 is merge-only; C3's Depends and §5's C bullet say C3 starts after C1 and merges after C2 | none (schedule) |

**Checked for consistency.** After this fold, searches for `Startup is trivial`, `B1's \`startup.go\``, `decision slot is empty`, `repeat Claim and runs`, `one line per authenticated POST`, `integration tests only`, `context cancelled`, `treats as an interruption`, `calls in flight answer`, `then legacy requests end` and `the service \`Close\` that closes the lifecycle` find no hit outside history (§7–§12.13, kept verbatim, and the r5.8 revision summary). The remaining `startup.go` hits are history or say A6. The remaining `assist busy` hits describe a full pool, which `closed` is not. The remaining `session_mismatch` hits are v2 or history. The other `decision slot is empty` hits (E7's challenge validity, SA-4, SA-6) are validity rules, not B2's assertion, and the one other `treats as an interruption` hit is SA-8 step 2's `closed` pool, which is the fix.

## Appendix L. How r5.3's time state actually lives: Clock's facts, held authority on two clocks, the store-ownership lock and `submit_not_after` (2026-10-03; rewritten in r5.3)

**Status:** this appendix is descriptive, not binding; §1.5 SA-1 rule 3 binds. r5.2's version recorded the clock mark and the executor's K16 floor, and `STATE-REDESIGN-NEEDED.md` found them structurally flawed (D1–D8). r5.3 withdrew both (§12.8). L.1 and L.2 now record what replaced them; L.3 is unchanged; L.4's deleted-id residual is now SA-1 rule 3's R-2. Each claim was checked against this plan's text and, for the two clocks, against `golang.org/x/sys` v0.45.0 and Go 1.27.1 (SA-1 rule 3.4).

**Terms:**
- A *stamp* is a past-event field written from the wall clock. On R1: `created_at`, a vote's `ts`, `decided_at`, `revoked_at` and a burst's `started_at`. On R2: `created_at`, a marker's `at`, `first_delivered_at` and (r5.4) `final_at`, from which the executor's retention ages count.
- `now` is the one wall-clock reading of an evaluation (SA-1 rule 3.1). There is no `t` and no mark.
- *E* is the elapsed clock of `internal/elapsed`: `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` on Linux, Go's monotonic reading elsewhere.
- A *held deadline* is a pair (W, `elapsed_end`) acquired once (SA-1 rule 3.4).
- *snaf* is `submit_not_after`.

### L.0 Events

| Event | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Step back | The wall clock moves backwards by X, while the owner runs or while it is stopped. No owner detects it as a state change; the tick only reports it (E59). |
| Forward step | The wall clock jumps forward by J. |
| Suspend | The machine sleeps. Wall and E (on Linux) keep counting; Go's `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` reading does not. |
| In-process reopen | Local signer only: an uncertain commit, then poison ((r5.8) and SA-8's owner stop of the lifecycle's effects), then backoff, then `OpenDirectory(holderFD)`, then Startup under a new epoch. It carries only the ownership descriptor. The executor has none, because it exits on an uncertain commit. Hosted has none: it keeps no cache, answers 503 and re-reads. |
| Process restart | Any exit (a crash, SIGTERM, or the executor's exit on an uncertain commit) followed by a fresh start. (r5.8) SIGTERM first sets the owner's `stopping` latch, so nothing commits after it, and every effect it interrupts is a crash cut (SA-8). |
| Pruning | Erase (signature bytes), Shed (payload), Delete (a record or job file), body removal and token removal. |

### L.1 Clock's facts (local signer and hosted)

**Holders.** None in memory. Every time-based closure on R1 is a write-once slot: decision `expired`, assist `unavailable`, mint `window_closed`, and erased bytes. Only Clock writes them, and only at the `now` of the evaluation that commits them. Its single writer is the store's commit function, which applies Clock first in every command except Delete, and alone on the tick (SA-1 rule 3.3).

| Event | Local signer | Hosted |
|---|---|---|
| Process start | Startup loads every record and runs `Next`, which applies Clock at the wall clock. Due facts commit; nothing is written for a deadline still ahead of the wall clock. No floor, no mark. | The worker's first pass applies Clock over `DueV2(now)`. |
| Normal run | The tick applies Clock each second and at each `next_due_at`. Every command applies it first, in its own commit. A read shows only committed slots, so it can trail a deadline by one tick (R-3) and never leads it. | Same, inside each `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. The vote POST's step 3 applies Clock in its own transaction before the MAC and step-up checks (E7). |
| Live cap full | Submit applies Clock to the records whose `next_due_at` has passed, each in its own commit, before it counts (SA-9). | Same, each in an earlier transaction. |
| Step back | Nothing committed changes. Deadlines not yet passed stretch by up to X (R-1). Commands keep running: no epoch change, no actor restart, and (r5.3, critique pass 1) no refusal beyond SA-1 rule 3.1's list (3.2's release check catches a set decided before a large enough step). | Same. |
| Forward step | Due facts commit at the next evaluation and never reverse. | Same. |
| In-process reopen | Startup again; nothing is carried, and nothing is needed, because every closure any view showed is a committed slot. An uncertain Clock commit answered `storage_uncertain` and showed nothing (SA-8). | n/a |
| Process restart | Same as process start. | Same. |
| Pruning | Shed keeps the header, so the decision and mint slots survive. Delete removes the record; R-2 covers the replay of its exact bytes after a step back of more than a day. | Same; Delete is off by default. |

**Guaranteed:**
- Every time-based refusal on an existing record is committed in its own answer's commit (C-b), and a closure shown by any view is a committed slot. (r5.4) A submit-window refusal of a new id stores nothing at all, so nothing needs committing (SA-11 item 3). Neither reverses under any clock event, reopen or restart (OC-1, SA-11 items 3, 4 and 8).
- Clock never writes a fact due after the wall clock of its own commit, so no fact appears that no view could have shown (C-f; D1 and D2 cannot occur).
- No clock event changes an epoch, makes a notification `uncertain`, or refuses Revoke, AdminCancel or deny-all (C-d, C-e; D6 and D7 cannot occur). (r5.3, critique pass 1) The clock-based refusals are exactly SA-1 rule 3.1's list, and a Deny tap is refused on a clock reading only as OA-5's stale view, until the edited card is confirmed. r5.3's "no clock event refuses a command" overclaimed.

**Not guaranteed (SA-1 rule 3's residuals):** R-1 (a step back stretches deadlines nobody has judged yet; a forward step ends them early), R-2 (deleted ids), R-3 (reads trail the tick).

### L.2 Held authority and the (wall, elapsed) pair

**Holders.** Three, each with a single driver:
- the local signer's grant projection writer (v2 grants);
- the executor's per-job actor (claimed signature sets);
- the MCP synchronous coordinator (its claimed set and its sync wait budget). r5.2 gave it no time rule at all (D5).

| Event | Grant G (local signer) | Claimed set, step k (executor, sync coordinator) | Sync wait budget |
|---|---|---|---|
| Acquire | When the commit that decides G approved in the current epoch is confirmed. W = `decided_at + duration_seconds`; `elapsed_end = E_acq + max(0, min(duration_seconds, W − wall_acq))`. | Once per step per process: at the first Claim whose answer carries step k's window; both clocks read just before that Claim is sent; a repeat Claim never re-acquires (r5.3, critique pass 1). W = `decided_at.Unix() + TTL_k` from the answer; `elapsed_end = E_acq + max(0, min(TTL_k, W − wall_acq))`. | When Submit returns. W = `sync_claim_deadline − 5 s`; nominal 300 s. |
| Use | Submit's grant match and a grant-decided first Claim's liveness check read projection membership. | K16 for the step about to be dispatched, if signed: `wall + 2 s < W` and `E + 2 s < elapsed_end`, before the marker and again before `Start`, (r5.4) inside `RunPhased` as its pre-start guard, after the handshake and session setup (E28). | The Wait loop ends at the first end. |
| Passed | The writer removes G at the first finding (tick, grant match or Claim check) and never re-adds it in that epoch. | result(k) = `expired_unexecuted` (executor) or the run ends with that status (sync). | `approval_pending` per E26. |
| Step back | Ended by E; the wall half cannot extend it (review 4's case). | Ended by E. | Ended by E. |
| Forward step | Ends early by the wall half, permanently (R-1). | Same. | Same. |
| Suspend | On Linux E counts it, so the grant ends on time. Outside Linux it may stretch by the smaller of the suspend and a coincident step back (R-5). | Same. | Same. |
| In-process reopen | Every grant dies with the epoch; none is acquired from loaded records. | n/a (the executor exits instead). | n/a |
| Process restart | Every grant is dead. | The set is lost. A repeat Claim re-acquires from the new process's clocks; the signer answers from its committed facts (`window_closed`, erased bytes, or 3.2's 5 s check), so no closed window reopens. R-4 covers a wall clock wrong at re-acquisition. | Dies with the call. |
| Drop | Revoke, supersession, either end passing, or the epoch ending. | The first of: the last signed step's `Start`, a K16 failure, the job or run becoming final, or process exit. | The call returns. |

(r5.5) The sync budget is not the only thing that ends a synchronous call before it runs. The signer's Clock closes an approved record's window at `claim_end` (L.1), which after a timely decision comes about one TTL later, long before the budget. A Claim answered `expired` after the coordinator saw `approved` is that close, so the run ends `expired_unexecuted` with a negative report and the non-retryable Denial (E26); the budget's `approval_pending` is only for an approval late enough that its first window outlasts the budget.

(r5.6) A hosted Claim answered `busy` (E21) carries no window, so it acquires nothing. The coordinator resends the same Claim inside the budget, and the first answer that carries step k's window acquires the pair. If contention outlasts the window, that answer is `expired`, because Clock commits `window_closed` in the Claim's own transaction. (r5.6, critique of the fold) A Submit answered `busy` comes before the sync wait budget is acquired, so its resends are bounded separately, at the draft's `submit_not_after` by the wall reading or 125 s of E, whichever is first (E21). That bound is a retry bound, not held authority: the signer judges the submit window itself (SA-1 rule 3.5), so a wrong local clock only stops the resends early or late and never admits anything. It is not a fourth holder, and the table above is unchanged.

**Guaranteed:**
- No held deadline is extended by any clock event, and none outlives either of its ends (C-c; SA-11 items 5 and 6; D4 cannot occur, because nothing is frozen).
- K16 binds only the first step with no result, and only when it is signed, so an unsigned step before it is never skipped (D3 cannot occur; E29's "at most one abort step, the last attempted" holds).
- A signature's own `Exp` is the bound the gate enforces; the pair only makes the holder stop earlier, never later.

**Not guaranteed:** R-4 (an acquisition under a wrong wall clock; the span is still capped at the nominal elapsed span) and R-5 (clocks wrong at the source).

### L.3 The store-ownership lock (local signer B1, executor C1)

| Event | Effect |
|---|---|
| Process start | Before anything is loaded: `mkdirat` and a parent fsync, then `openat(O_RDONLY\|O_DIRECTORY\|O_NOFOLLOW\|O_CLOEXEC)`, then `flock(LOCK_EX\|LOCK_NB)`. `EWOULDBLOCK` makes the process exit non-zero, naming the directory; the signer does this before `Listen`. Any other error (`EBADF`, `ENOLCK`, `EOPNOTSUPP`, `EINVAL`) is also fatal: the store must be on a local filesystem, and NFS is refused. The lock is taken once. |
| Every store open | `securestate.OpenDirectory(holderFD)` duplicates the descriptor with `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC` (`store_linux.go:112-113`), which shares the open file description, so the opened store is the locked inode. securestate's own per-transaction flock is on the separate `lockFD`. `Store.Close` closes only the duplicate (`:161-170`), so the lock persists. B0's `ListNames` uses a fresh descriptor and never flocks it. |
| In-process reopen | (r5.3: r5.2's break restart is withdrawn.) It goes through the held descriptor and never calls `flock`. Even if `journal_root` was renamed and replaced in the meantime, the reopened store is the locked inode (B1 test). |
| Exit or crash | The kernel releases the lock. If systemd starts the new process while the old one is still alive, the new one exits and is restarted. Exclusivity holds at every instant, and a cache is only ever loaded under the lock. |
| Clock events; pruning | No interaction. `Remove` and Delete act inside the locked directory. |
| Hosted | No lock: there is no cache, and every command re-reads inside `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. There is also no process singleton; under r5.3 two hosted processes on one database stay consistent, because every closure is a committed row (§12.8, S10). |

**Load-bearing for:**
- OS-1: two caches would mint two signature sets.
- OX-1: an "other-epoch" marker must mean a dead process.
- OR-5: admission must be atomic.
- OD-1: a reopen must reload the store whose commit was uncertain.

**Residuals:**
- A same-uid process can hold the lock and stop the owner from starting. That is inside E17's trust boundary.
- If the directory is renamed and a new one is created at its path, a second process can own a *different* store. No records are shared, so no compare-and-swap breaks.
- A store rolled back from a backup or a VM snapshot would revive empty mint slots and drop markers. That is outside the crash model and is not stated as an assumption.

**No defect.**

### L.4 `submit_not_after`

| Hop | Set or checked | What deduplicates | How long that lasts |
|---|---|---|---|
| MCP, async | Call time + 3600 s, inside the `exec_submit` input and `input_hash`. An `ErrResponseLost` retry re-sends the same input. | MCP process memory. | Dies with the MCP (§1.3 item 25). |
| MCP, sync | Submit + 120 s, inside the draft. | The call. | — |
| Executor | New id: `now ≤ snaf ≤ now + 3605 s` (wall clock, SA-1 rule 3.5), checked before admission and before any signer call. Otherwise `submit_window_closed`, or `invalid_request` when it is too far ahead. An existing id is returned whatever the time. The value is copied into the draft unchanged. | The job file's header. | SA-9's predicate: final, bodies gone or none, report settled or never owed, and at least snaf + 24 h. |
| Executor to signer | Resubmit while there is no `signer_ack`, until snaf, then `approval_lost`. | — | — |
| Signer, local or hosted | New id: `now ≤ snaf ≤ now + 3600 s`, plus 5 s locally and 300 s on hosted; too far ahead answers (r5.3, critique pass 1) `submit_window_ahead`, which `JobNext` retries. An existing record, shed or not, is returned whatever the time. | The immutable R1 header. | Delete: settled, audited, at least snaf + 24 h, and either the age limit or count pressure. Hosted Delete is off by default. |
| A12 proxy | Stateless; hosted keys on `sub_principal`. | — | — |

**Holds:**
- All four guards read one immutable value, and neither store forgets an id before snaf + 24 h. So an exact replay inside the window is deduplicated, and a replay after it is refused (C-40).
- (r5.3) A step back can reach it only for an id no store holds (R-2). While either store holds the id, the stored header answers whatever the time.
- Cross-host skew of more than 5 min ends in `invalid_request` or `submit_window_closed`, which fails closed.

**Residuals:**
- **(a) The agent hop.** The agent is the real outermost retrier, and it has no idempotency key. Under a live grant, a retry after the MCP dies executes twice (§1.3 item 25; an owner item in the memo).
- **(b) R-2 (r5.2's D8): deleted ids.** Now stated once as SA-1 rule 3's R-2. Deletion is irreversible and judged at one wall clock, while the window is judged again at a later one. If the clock later lands at or before snaf (after a step back of more than a day, or after the correction of a forward excursion that drove the Delete), the exact bytes are accepted as a new request. It needs a fresh tap or a live grant of the same principal, and it gets fresh windows that the gate judges. E73 and SA-11 now say "while either store holds the id". Only a same-uid process holds those bytes, and it could submit a fresh request anyway. Closing it is an owner item (§11.2).

### L.5 What the four lifecycles show

The ownership lock and `submit_not_after` are durable facts, each with one owner, and their lifecycles close except for R-2.

r5.2's mark and K16 floor were time kept only in process memory, and every event needed its own rule for them (STATE-MODEL G4 returning through time, against §8 properties 1 and 4). r5.3 removes that class of state rather than adding rules to it:
- every time-based closure that a view, an answer or an effect reflects is a committed write-once slot (L.1), or the end of a held deadline that its single driver latched (L.2);
- held deadlines are never extended, so no event needs a rule to keep them dead; they die with their epoch or process, and the durable facts answer afterwards;
- nothing is carried across a reopen or a restart except the ownership descriptor (L.3).

What remains is stated once, as SA-1 rule 3's R-1 to R-5, and STATE-MODEL §5 gains OC-1 for it.
